The payrun containing a day is `payePayrunPeriod(org, dayKey)` (it never crosses a payday change; null before the first payday or when none is set); **Past PAYE payruns** is `payePastPayruns(org, todayKey)`, newest first, back to the first payrun after the payday was set. **1.32.0 (owner decisions 4 Nov): the PAYE payday is required.** Settings → Payment runs and timesheets saves it with the screen's main **Save**, in the same partial update as the payment runs (`payePaydayWriteFields` beside the invoicing fields; there is no separate PAYE payday button). Organisation setup asks for it and does not go on until one is picked (`payePaydaySetupError`, `PAYE_PAYDAY_REQUIRED_MESSAGE`). While an organisation has none (`payePaydayMissing`), a banner says so (`payePaydayBanner({ organization, loaded, viewerIsAdmin })`: admins `PAYE_PAYDAY_MISSING_BANNER`, which says where to set it; everyone else `PAYE_PAYDAY_NOT_SET_MESSAGE`) on Settings, Payment runs and timesheets, and the Overtime, Expenses & Price Work screens. Existing organisations are never written to. **Until a payday is set** every PAYE screen says `PAYE_PAYDAY_NOT_SET_MESSAGE` ("Ask an admin to set the PAYE payday") and only a custom date range works (`payeCustomRange`); nothing else changes for a company that never sets one.

### 4.6 Warning settings
`warningDetection { detectClashes, clashLookaheadMode, clashLookaheadDays, includeWeekendsForUnbookedLabour, excludedUserIdsFromUnbookedWarnings, weekendCoverage }`, parsed by `parseWarningDetection`. See §5.

**Weekend hours to cover** (1.19.0, owner design). When "Include weekends in unbooked labour" is on, Warning settings shows two more toggles, **Saturday** and **Sunday**; each, when on, shows an "Hours to cover" start–end (e.g. Saturday 07:30–14:00, Sunday 08:00–13:00). Stored as `warningDetection.weekendCoverage = { saturday: { enabled, start, end }, sunday: { enabled, start, end } }` (`parseWeekendCoverage`; `start` / `end` `HH:MM` or null). Defaults when missing: `enabled` = `includeWeekendsForUnbookedLabour`; the window = that day's working-hours set window if it has one, else the standard day (`resolveWeekendCoverageDay(coverage, includeWeekends, policy, day)`, read under the policy in effect that day) — so existing settings behave as today. Turning include-weekends on starts with both days on. Include-weekends off hides the toggles and scans no weekend day. The settings save writes the whole `weekendCoverage` map as one field of the partial update (`warningDetectionFirestoreFields` → `warningDetection.weekendCoverage`).

### 4.7 Material order cut-off
`settings.materialCutOff { materialOrderCutOff, materialCutOffHour, materialCutOffMinute, materialCutOffOnSaturday, materialCutOffOnSunday }` (`parseMaterialCutOff`, `resolveMaterialCutOffClock`). A missing hour defaults to 16:00; a saved 15:30 stays 15:30. A user's notification preferences never hold the clock.

### 4.8 Schedule options
`settings.myScheduleOptions { showOffice, showWorkingFromHome, showSiteSurvey, customItems[], customItemEnabled{name: bool} }`. Missing flags mean on. Every location picker offers exactly `enabledScheduleLocations(options)`. A save writes exactly the list on screen, with names de-duplicated ignoring case (`scheduleOptionsWriteFields`) — it never merges back a deleted item.

## 5. Warnings

Warnings refresh whenever bookings, leave, settings or users change, and when the day changes (midnight in the organisation zone, and when the app or tab returns to the foreground).

**Detection period** (`coverageWindow`):
- **Set number of days:** today through N calendar days including today (2 = today and tomorrow). N is a whole number 1–365; a missing, blank, non-numeric or below-1 value falls back to 7 and both the settings screen and the Warnings screen show the notice `warningLookaheadFallbackNotice(reason)` (`coverageWindow` → `fallbackReason`, `parseWarningDetection` → `clashLookaheadDaysFallback`, never saved) — never a silent failure.
- **Invoicing period:** every past, present and future day of the active payment run. Selecting it shows the **invoicing schedule summary** (`activePaymentRunSummary`); with no payment run configured the screen asks the user to set one up. The summary is hidden for the other two options.
- **Full week:** Monday–Friday of the current week, including days already past. Saturday and Sunday are added for unbooked labour only when "Include weekends in unbooked labour" is on.
- **Unbooked days** (`unbookedScanDays`): the window above; in Full week with include-weekends on it runs to Sunday. Saturday and Sunday are scanned only when include-weekends is on, in every mode. 1.19.0: a Saturday or Sunday whose `weekendCoverage` toggle is off is not scanned (`unbookedScanDays` takes `weekendCoverage`); the weekend "counts as" hours no longer decide anything here.
- **Clash days** (`clashScanDays`): clashes follow the scan window. In Full week, a Saturday or Sunday of the current Monday–Sunday week is also checked when it has a booking. Set number of days and Invoicing period are calendar days, so their weekends are always checked. The include-weekends toggle never affects clashes.

**Warning types and severity:**
- **Booking clashes** — a person double-booked on the same date. Operative clashes are **High** (`operativeClashGroups`); manager/admin overlaps are **Medium** (`managerClashGroups`). Who counts as a manager or admin for that split is one rule (1.23.0, audit finding 20): `clashManagerIds(roster)` — active people at the admin level (super admin, `adminAccess`, the admin role) or with the manager flag (stored top level or in `permissions`; with neither stored, the manager role), the rule both apps already used; a person with no level flag is on the Managers list (§11) but their clashes stay operative clashes. A named slot (Full day, AM, PM) is read under the working hours in effect on the booking's own day (1.23.0: the scans take the organisation's working-hours state, `standardDayOn`), never today's. Both group a person's bookings per day (the person is `bookingPersonKey` / the roster account, so former ids, same-email ids and catalogue rows are one person) by overlapping clock intervals; touching times do not clash, and A–B plus B–C is one cluster. **Overnight bookings** (1.13.0, owner decisions round 4, Q5): a booking whose finish is before its start crosses midnight, and its interval is the real clock time on its start day (`slotInterval`: 22:00–06:00 is 22:00 to 30:00). It also sits on the next morning (`overnightCarryInterval`), so it clashes with that day's bookings that start before it ends (a 05:00 start clashes with a night shift ending at 06:00; a 06:00 start only touches). Such a clash is listed on the later day; two overnight bookings on one night are one clash on their own day. The same applies to the timesheet overlap flags (`overlappingBookingIds`), which also read each booking's named slot under its own day's working hours (1.23.0). Weekend bookings (custom hours only from 1.13.0, §4.3) clash on their clock hours; a stored weekend FULL DAY with no clock times occupies that weekend day's own Full day window (1.23.1, `bookingStandardDayOn`: `weekendFullDayWindow` — the set window, or the standard day when the day pays every hour at the multiplier), an AM / PM its half split at that day's own lunch break; never the Monday–Friday day. The timesheet overlap flag and leave cover read a weekend day the same way. An operative cluster lists every booking id and one pair row per overlapping pair, each with its `clashPairKey` and `clashResolutionKey`. A clash can be **approved** and **un-approved** (online, managers and admins only): `clashApprovalWrites` writes `acceptedBookingClashes/{clashPairKey}` and `warningResolutions/{clashResolutionKey}` with status `approved` — the key is the booking-pair key for operative clashes and `mgr-clash-…` for manager clashes; approving an operative cluster writes every pair. Approving writes, and un-approving deletes, both documents (every pair of a cluster) in one batch (1.9.0), so one never exists without the other. A clash is approved when either document exists for it (`isClashApproved`); an operative cluster is approved when every pair is (`isClashGroupApproved`), so a third booking re-opens it. Only approvals count as approved: `approvedWarningKeys` (status `approved`) and `acceptedBookingClashes`; a dismissed clash is hidden but is not approved. Approved clashes are listed separately on the weekly report and no longer count as open. Whether any row still counts is `isWarningActive` over `resolvedWarningKeys` (approved and dismissed). Keys compare without case (§1). Resolutions are read live (a snapshot listener on `warningResolutions`) through `warningResolutionRecords`, which puts older iOS keys in their shared form (`sharedWarningResolutionKey`: `mgr-clash-{user}-{seconds}-{i}` → `mgr-clash-{user}-{YYYY-MM-DD}-{i}`, `materials-{projectId}-{seconds}` → `materials-{projectId}-{YYYY-MM-DD}`, organisation zone). The one-off legacy migration (D3) is `warningResolutionMigrationWrites({ docs, legacyKeyForms, resolvedByUserId, timeZone })`: the `warningResolutions` documents to write under the shared keys (approved beats dismissed; nothing when the shared key is already resolved; old documents are left in place); staff only, idempotent.
- **Unbooked labour (High)** — every active user (operative, manager, admin and Super Admin) who is not booked on a working day in the window (`unbookedLabourRows`; owner decision 4 Nov — the organisation chooses whom to leave out with Excluded users). Skipped: excluded users (silently), inactive users (1.33.0: also when the person is matched through an `operatives` row on their email — an inactive account is never picked for that row, `preferFinishedAccount`), pending users who have not finished sign-up (1.34.0: a ghost operative, §11, has no login by design and counts as signed up — `LabourPerson.ghost`; people with no email are one row each, keyed by id, and an inactive one is never listed), days on approved annual leave, weekends unless included, anonymised deleted profiles (`listedCurrentLabourPeople`). 1.19.0: each day of the window is read under the working hours in effect on THAT day — the caller passes the organisation's policy state as `payrollPolicy`. **Weekend requirement** (owner design, replacing the "counts as" hours): with include-weekends on, a Saturday or Sunday is scanned only when its `weekendCoverage` toggle is on, and a person is unbooked there when their bookings that day do not cover every minute of the day's "Hours to cover" window (`resolveWeekendCoverageDay`, `windowCoverage`); missing hours = the uncovered minutes of the window; time inside that day's lunch-break window (its own, else the weekday one; paid or not, `payrollBreakOnDay`) never needs covering, so two bookings either side of lunch cover the day (e.g. Saturday 07:30–14:00 with lunch 12:00–12:30 booked 07:30–11:30 → "… is missing 2h on Saturday …"; 07:30–12:00 plus 12:30–14:00 → covered). A weekday (1.20.0) must be covered for the whole working day, start to finish, and time inside the lunch-break window never needs covering, the same as a weekend day (`standardDayCoverage`; owner example 06:30–14:30, break 10:00–10:45: nothing booked → 7.25h missing; 06:30–10:00 plus 10:45–14:30 → covered). A day with approved half-day leave is not an unbooked-labour row; it is the leave-cover rule (`leaveCoverageRows`, §9; 1.23.0: each day of the window under the working hours in effect on it — the caller passes the policy state as `day`), where the other leave half must be booked minute for minute except the lunch-break window, which never needs covering there either. `unbookedLabourRowsOnDay` (Daily Overview) follows the same rule. Every id match in `unbookedLabourRows`, `unbookedLabourRowsOnDay` and `leaveCoverageRows` — leave to person, booking to person, former and same-email ids, exclusions — ignores case (`normalizeDocumentId`, §1); returned rows keep the ids as stored.
- **Low:** materials not ordered by the cut-off (`materialCutOffWarningRows`: once the organisation clock passes the cut-off, each job with an active booking tomorrow warns if tomorrow has no material lines or any line is not ordered — a line counts as ordered when its status is `ordered` or an order send record lists it (`materialLineCountsAsOrdered`); a Saturday or Sunday tomorrow only if that day's flag is on; key `materials-{projectId}-{YYYY-MM-DD}` for tomorrow); qualification expiry including already expired (`qualificationExpiryRows`; any count of them — a menu subtitle or Home badge — is `qualificationExpiryCount`, 1.23.0: the same rows on the organisation day, expiring on or before today plus one calendar month, split `expired` / `expiringSoon`; never a device-calendar 30-day window); unverified accounts (`unverifiedOperativeRows`; 1.34.0: never a ghost operative, invited or not).

**Scope** (1.10.0, §17; **1.31.0 owner decision**). `warningScopeFor({ viewer, jobs, managers, roster, people, operatives, enforced })` → `WarningScope`, passed as `scope` to `unbookedLabourRows` / `unbookedLabourRowsOnDay`, `qualificationExpiryRows`, `unverifiedOperativeRows` and `materialCutOffWarningRows`, and tested with `isWarningRowInScope` for clashes and leave cover. **Admins and super admins outside operative mode see every warning.** A manager — whatever their Projects / Small works toggles — sees only (a) warnings about themselves under any of their ids (`personIdSet`), (b) warnings about their reports (the active people whose `lineManagerIds` include them) and (c) warnings on the jobs they are an assigned manager of (`isJobManager` over the stored `managerIds` / `managerUserIds` / `managerId`, or a `managers` row on their email). Per type: unbooked labour, qualification expiry and unverified accounts → (a) + (b); operative and manager clashes and leave cover → any person involved in (a) + (b), or any booking of the row on a job of (c) (`managerBookingJobId` for a manager site booking); materials cut-off → (c). A viewer who cannot read every booking still gets no unbooked-labour rows (`readsEveryBooking`); **1.33.0:** every manager reads every booking (§17), so a manager's unbooked rows come back with `enforcedReads` on — only operatives and an unknown viewer get none. Operatives: nothing. The warning list, its counts (Home badge) and Book labour use this scope; Daily Overview uses `dailyOverviewScopeFor` (§6), its parts (a) and (b), so a manager's Daily Overview unbooked people equal their Warnings and Book labour unbooked people. `visibleJobIds`, `bookings` and `managerBookings` are no longer read; a caller that passes no `jobs` gives a manager no job rows. Before 1.31.0 the scope was the jobs the viewer could see and everyone booked on them, so a manager who saw every job saw every warning.

**Annual leave when booking** (1.16.0, owner rule). Every booking flow that lists double bookings before it saves also lists each (person, day) of the booking that falls on that person's annual leave: `bookingLeaveConflicts({ people, dayKeys, holidays, timeZone, timeSlot, workStartTime, workEndTime, day, roster })` → `{ personId, dayKey, kind: 'annual_leave', leaveId, leaveSlot: 'FULL DAY' | 'AM' | 'PM', status, label }`, one row per person-day. Approved leave conflicts when its window overlaps the booking: full-day leave with anything; AM leave with a booking that touches the AM leave half (1.20.0: `leaveHalfDayWindows`, the exact middle of the day — on 06:30–14:30 AM leave conflicts only with bookings overlapping 06:30–10:30), PM likewise; a stored AM / PM booking is that leave half (`leaveComparableInterval`), so AM leave and a PM booking never conflict; custom hours conflict with half-day leave when they overlap that half. Pending leave is listed too (`status: 'pending'`, `ANNUAL_LEAVE_REQUESTED_LABEL`, shown softer); rejected leave never. People match leave under every id they go by (`personIdSet`, their `userId` and operative ids), without case, from `parseHolidayDocument` / `labourHolidayInput` day keys. Leave rows are purple and read `ANNUAL_LEAVE_BOOKED_LABEL` ("Annual leave booked"); double bookings stay orange; the heading is `BOOKING_CONFLICTS_HEADING` ("Some selected days clash. Tick to confirm, or ✕ to drop that day."). Each row is ticked (book anyway) or ✕ (drop that day for that person), and the save waits until every row is one or the other — the same as a double booking. The warning after saving is still `leaveCoverageRows`.

**Double bookings when booking** (1.19.0, owner decisions). Every booking flow lists a person already booked on a day as an orange row only when the new booking's times actually overlap an existing booking that day (`slotInterval` intervals, `intervalsOverlap`; touching times do not clash) — My Schedule self-booking included (it used to warn for any booking that day). 1.23.0: the check is `bookingRequestClashIds({ request, existing, day })` — the existing bookings (labour or manager schedule) whose `slotInterval` overlaps the booking being made, both read under the working hours in effect on the **booked** day (never today's: pass the policy state), an overnight booking from the day before reaching that morning, cancelled bookings never; every booking flow uses it — the Schedule wizard, Book labour, the Daily Overview booking sheet, Quick Add and booking edit on web (1.23.1), Select Operatives on iOS — and `bookingLeaveConflicts` reads each booked day's working hours the same way; a weekend day's named slot is that day's own window (`bookingStandardDayOn`). A manager or admin who is double-booked may book anyway: the orange row is ticked (keep) or ✕ (drop) like the annual-leave rows, and the overlap is then a Medium manager clash warning (`managerClashGroups`).

**Replace existing booking** (1.41.0, owner decision 11 Nov, built before launch). Every orange double-booking row — web: the Schedule wizard, Book labour, the Daily Overview booking sheet, Quick Add and booking edit; iOS: Select Operatives (the Scheduling wizard), Quick Add and Book labour — has a third choice beside ✓ (book both) and ✕ (drop that day): **Replace** (`BOOKING_REPLACE_LABEL`), which removes the existing overlapping booking(s) on that row and saves this one in their place. It is offered only when the viewer may delete every overlapping booking on the row (`canDeleteBooking`) and each is a labour or manager schedule booking — `bookingReplaceOffer({ clashing: [{ bookingId, collection, mayDelete }] })` → `{ offered, bookings }`. The save is one batch, `bookingReplaceWrites({ organizationId, booking: { collection, bookingId }, replaced, deletedBy, deletedFrom })` → `{ bookingPath, deletes: [{ path, tombstonePath, tombstone }] }`: the new booking (its usual create, or the edit being saved) and, for each replaced booking, its delete and its tombstone (`bookingTombstoneWriteFields`), so nothing is half applied; null (nothing written) when a replaced booking is the booking itself, a collection is not one Replace may delete, or an id is unusable. The security rules accept the batch as it is (no rules change). Replace is online only on iOS and Android (`booking.replace`, §2): disabled offline with `OFFLINE_ONLINE_ONLY_MESSAGE`. The decisions are `BOOKING_CONFLICT_DECISIONS` (`confirm`, `drop`, `replace`); a flow saves only when every row has one.

**Excluded users** are a per-user, all-or-nothing exemption from unbooked-labour warnings, for people who are never bookable (office PAYE staff, directors). Temporarily unavailable operatives use the **inactive** flag instead.

Display order (Oldest/Newest) is a per-device preference (`sortWarningsByDate`).

## 6. Daily Overview

One entry per day listing booked people (with project or small work), unbooked people (`unbookedLabourRowsOnDay`; on a Saturday or Sunday the "Hours to cover" rule of §4.6 / §5) and people on approved annual leave. It respects the organisation working hours in effect on the day shown (`effectivePayrollTimePolicy`, history first, 1.19.0), including that weekend day's own lunch break, the fixed working week, include-weekends, exclusions and active/inactive status. Hours (1.20.2) are the pay rule's for the day shown (`payrollHoursResult` under the policy in effect that day): each booking row shows its times ("Custom 07:30–14:00") and `bookingHoursLabel(result).label` — the paid hours with the multiplier sum when hours are at a multiplier (Saturday 07:30–14:00 on an 8h set window → "8h"; Sunday 07:30–14:00 every hour × 2 → "13h (6.5h × 2)"; weekday 07:30–18:00 with 2h at 1.5 → "11h (8h + 2h × 1.5)"), never the clock duration; the day's total is the sum of `totalPaidHours`, split into standard hours (`standardHours`) and hours at the multiplier (`outsideHours`; 1.20.3: shown as their clock hours with any unpaid break as plain hours, `payrollMultipliedHours`). Clock hours worked (`workedHours`, `workedHoursSplit`) are never Daily Overview's figures. Before 1.20.2 the rows showed clock hours worked (`workedHours`), so a Saturday or Sunday 07:30–14:00 read 6.5h on every list. 1.29.0: a booking with its own overtime rate (`otMultiplierOverride`, §25) shows that rate in its label ("9.5h (7.25h + 2h × 1.5)" becomes "10.5h (7.25h + 2h × 2)"); without one, the organisation's rate as before. A Saturday or Sunday booking shows the custom hours entered (1.13.0, §4.3); a stored weekend FULL DAY / AM / PM shows as stored. It is identical on every platform for the same day and viewer. **Who is shown** (1.33.0, owner decision 5 Nov, Q-D1 agreed 6 Nov): `dailyOverviewScopeFor({ viewer, roster, people, operatives, enforced })` → `DailyOverviewScope` (the `WarningScope` shape with no job part) and `isDailyOverviewRowInScope(scope, { personIds, email, subcontractor })`. Admins and super admins outside operative mode see every person and every subcontractor booking. A manager — whatever their Projects / Small works toggles — sees only themselves and their reports (the people whose line managers include them), under every id and on their email: their booked rows, leave, unbooked people (`unbookedLabourRowsOnDay` with this scope, so the list equals their Book labour list) and the day's hours and counts, which are taken from those rows only. A non-report booked on a job they manage, and subcontractor bookings, are not on a manager's Daily Overview (they still are in the job's scheduling). The Home team panels and the Home on-site / on-leave figures (`homeOverviewMetrics` `scope`, §23) follow the same scope. Before 1.33.0 a manager saw everyone booked and only their unbooked list was scoped.

**Booked cover for one person on a day** (1.22.0) is `managerCoverageHours({ bookings, dayKey, policy, timeZone })` → `{ paidDayHours, paidHours, blocks, requiredHours, coveredHours, missingHours, covered }`, over all their labour and manager site bookings that day (every id they go by). `covered` is `standardDayCoverage` — the Daily Overview and unbooked-labour rule — and decides who Book labour still offers on both apps. `paidHours` — despite its name, **hours worked, not pay** (1.36.0 wording; the field name is kept because both apps read it) — merges the clock intervals (`slotInterval`; overlaps count once) and prices each merged block once with `payrollHoursResult` under the policy in effect that day (hours worked, never multiplied; the unpaid break off only where a block covers the break window); a FULL DAY with no clock times, or a block spanning the standard day, is capped at `weekdayPaidHours`. It is not the Daily Overview figure (that is the pay rule's `totalPaidHours`, above) and not a person's real hours on a day (`bookedWorkedHours`, §12). Before 1.22.0 iOS (`ManagerScheduleInterval.combinedPaidBookedHours`) took the standard break off any partly booked day and Book labour on iOS compared those hours with the paid day, while web used `standardDayCoverage`.

**Moving a booking between collections** (1.39.0, owner answer 11 Nov). The Daily Overview booking sheet moves a manager site booking onto a job as a labour booking, or a labour booking to Other (a manager site booking), as **one batch**: the new booking (a fresh id, write metadata), the old booking's delete and its tombstone (`bookingTombstoneWriteFields`, §2) — so a refused or failed write leaves both bookings as they were. The live rules allow the batch for the job's managers within the lookup budget (emulator test). A source already deleted on another device writes nothing and shows `EDIT_OF_MISSING_DOCUMENT_MESSAGE`. Before 1.39.0 web wrote the new booking and then deleted the old one in a second write, which could leave both (each paid, §8) or neither.

## 7. Weekly Report

**Admins only** (1.32.0, owner decision 5 Nov): the Weekly report is opened only by admins and super admins (outside operative mode; an admin has none) — `canOpenWeeklyReport(user)`, read from the level, so a promotion gives it and a demotion takes it away. The `weeklyReports` toggle is admin-only (`ADMIN_ONLY_PERMISSION_FIELDS`): locked on for admins, never offered to managers or operatives on Add User, Edit User or setup, and a manager's stored `weeklyReports: true` reads off and is never rewritten. Every way in — menu, Home tile and quick action, help links, a deep link and the page itself — asks `canOpenWeeklyReport`; a direct URL shows no access. Managers with the toggle on before 1.32.0 lose the report. No security rule depends on it (the report is built on the device from data the person already reads and is never stored).

Generated live for any chosen date range from current bookings, Daily Overview, warnings and timesheets. Leave days in the range are `leaveDaysInRange(record, range, timeZone, nonWorkingDayKeys)` (1.9.0, on `leaveRecordDayKeys`; a half day counts 0.5) on every platform. Contents: hours, overtime, warnings including approved clashes, totals. 1.19.0: every pay row, the timesheet feed and each row's day units (the standard day and overtime rate) use the working hours in effect on the row's own day (the whole policy state), and rows on days with different rules are separate rows, never averaged; unbooked-labour warnings read each day under its own rules (§5); signed lines keep their signed amounts. The PDF, XLSX and HTML exports show the same rows. A warning counts as open only while `isWarningActive` over `resolvedWarningKeys` (1.10.0): a dismissed clash is in neither the open warnings nor the approved clashes; approved clashes (`isClashApproved` / `isClashGroupApproved`) are listed separately. Reports are **not stored** and **never write** (1.10.0): a signed sheet with no `weeklyReportOverride` is summarised in memory for the report only — the report never writes anyone's timesheet (`syncWriteAllowed('weeklyReport')` is false). The people in the timesheet feed are `timesheetParticipant(user, day)` (§8). **The approved-timesheet feed** (1.22.0) is `weeklyReportTimesheetFeed({ weeks, people, range, timeZone })` on every platform (`mergeTimesheetFeedWeeks`, `timesheetFeedCovers`, `timesheetFeedLabourLines`, `timesheetFeedMoneyLines`): approved weeks of accounts that share an email (roster email, without case; else the user id) and the same week start are one person's week — the first week keeps its fields and its lines are the union by identity (a booking line by `bookingId`, else its day, place, job, hours, days, amount, overtime flag and details; a money line by `id`, else its day, job, title, amount and details; each identity as many times as any one week has it), so a booking both accounts carry counts once and hours on only one account stay; a person's live bookings on a day inside an approved week (organisation-zone days, inclusive) are replaced by that week's lines; labour lines in the range count except declined ones, price work and expenses except declined ones and amounts of 0.0001 or less. Behaviour is unchanged from web 1.21; iOS kept its own copy until 1.22.0. **PAYE people** (1.29.0, owner decision): a PAYE person's days (`weeklyReportPayeDay(person, dayKey)`, effective-dated `employmentTypeOnDay`) leave the normal hours and pay rows; instead a **PAYE overtime** section lists, per PAYE person per job, their overtime hours in the range — name, job number, overtime hours, e.g. "Sam · J-1042 · 1.5h" (`weeklyReportPayeOvertime({ people, bookings, organization, timeZone, range })`, the same lines as the Overtime screen, §25). **1.32.0 (owner decision 4 Nov):** rows are per person, job and multiplier and show the multiplier — "2h at ×1.5 = 3h" (`overtimeMultiplierText`; with an unpaid break "6.5h at ×2 − 0.5h break = 12.5h") — and a row whose bookings carry their own rate different from the organisation's is marked "changed from organisation rate (×1.5)" (`changedFromOrganization`, `OVERTIME_RATE_CHANGED_MARK`). Each person's lines are matched under `overtimePersonIds`. **Pay on the weekly report** (1.32.0, owner decisions 5 Nov, review fix r274): the Pay Summary on screen and in every export (PDF, HTML, XLSX) shows each person's rate and pay only when `canViewPersonPay` allows the viewer (default context — the weekly report is not a timesheet review): admins every person, a manager with the Operatives toggle operatives, everyone themself. Anyone else sees the hours with the rate and pay cells `PAY_HIDDEN_CELL_LABEL` ("—"), and the grand total is over the visible rows only with `PAY_HIDDEN_NOTE` (`reportPayForViewer(rows, { viewer, roster })` → `{ rows, grandTotal, hiddenPeople }`); a row with no known person is shown to admins only. Price work and expense amounts in their own sections are claims, not rates, and are unchanged — always live from the bookings, whatever the lines' review state; hours only. A report is produced and downloaded (PDF/XLSX/HTML or the share sheet) and that is all. Producing a report again for the same range reflects any changes made since.

**Weekly report pay per booking** (1.46.0, rates audit): each booking is priced on its own — `bookingPaySlices({ hours, standardDayHours, payBasis, dayRate, hourlyRate, isPaye })`, its standard and overtime part each `payForHours` rounded to pennies (a PAYE day 0) — and a pay row is the sum of its bookings' pennies (`mergeBookingPaySlice`), never one price of the summed hours, so the weekly report pays exactly what each booking's timesheet line pays. Before 1.46.0 iOS priced each row's summed hours once and could differ from web and from the timesheets by a penny or two.

**Payrun Snapshot** (1.33.0, owner decisions 6 Nov, `lib/canonical/payrunSnapshot.ts`). The Weekly report's **Payrun Snapshot** button (admins only, `canOpenWeeklyReport`; read-only, nothing stored, no export) opens a period picker — **Current PAYE period** (`payePayrunPeriod`, §4.5), **Current payment run period** (the run self-employed timesheets use, `activePaymentRunSummary`, §8) or **Custom range** (`payrunSnapshotCustomPeriod`); `payrunSnapshotPeriods` gives the first two — and one row per person (`payrunSnapshotRows({ roster, organization, period, sheets, overtimeRequests, overtimeReports, payeClaimReports, priceWork, expenses, timeZone })`). Every active person (`rosterSegment` "active") is listed whatever the period; an inactive, pending or deleted person only when they have a timesheet, overtime request or report, expense, price work item or claim report in the period. Roster rows that are the same person (`personIdSet`) are one row and records under any of their ids count. Columns: Name; **User level** (`permissionLevelOf`: Admin / Manager / Operative; the Super Admin "Admin (Super Admin)"); **Employment type** on the period's last day (`weeklyReportPayeDay`; a note when it differed on the first day); then four cells `{ state, label, tone, link }`:
- **Timesheet** (self-employed): Not started (no sheet or unsigned) / Submitted (signed by the person, waiting for their line manager) / Signed (fully signed or exported) — `timesheetListBucket` with `hasActiveLineManager`, one state per payment run overlapping the period (`timesheetPaymentRunPeriodsFromWindow` plus the runs stored on their sheets, `pickTimesheetRowForPeriod`). Several runs: the least-advanced state with a count ("Submitted · 2 of 4 signed"). A PAYE person's cell is red "PAYE"; a ghost operative's (1.34.0, §11) is grey "No app login" (`PAYRUN_SNAPSHOT_GHOST_CELL`, no link).
- **Overtime** (PAYE): with an active line manager, Not started / Submitted (a request overlapping the period still waiting) / Returned (returned by the line manager; withdrawn requests ignored); with none, "Report generated: Yes / No" (an overtime report overlapping the period; a report marked out of date reads "No (out of date)"). A self-employed person's cell is red "Self Employed".
- **Expenses** and **Price work** (PAYE): "Report generated: Yes" when a saved report of that kind overlaps the period, else the item count in the period (`payeClaimsInRange`: "2 items, no report" / "No items"). Self-employed: red "Self Employed".
A cell's `link` (`{ kind: timesheet | overtime | expenses | priceWork, personId, start, end }`) opens that person's timesheet for that payment run, or Users' Overtime / Expenses / Price work for that person and range; red cells have none. Timesheets are read with `parsePayrunSnapshotSheet`.

## 8. Timesheets and employment profile

**Profile fields** on `users/{uid}`: employment status (`employmentType`: PAYE or self-employed), pay basis (`payBasis`: day or hourly), `dayRate`, `hourlyRate`. A rate save writes exactly one basis and removes the other; both apps show `PAY_BASIS_SAVE_NOTE` ("Pay is a day rate or an hourly rate, not both. Saving a day rate removes the hourly rate, and saving an hourly rate removes the day rate.") with `PAY_BASIS_DAY_HELP` / `PAY_BASIS_HOURLY_HELP` under the pay fields (1.11.0; iOS copy that said both rates are kept was wrong). A PAYE person's rate is kept for days they are self-employed and shown with `PAYE_RATE_NOT_USED_NOTE`. The same applies to roster rows: an `operatives` or `managers` row with `payBasis: hourly` is hourly (`parseOperativeDocument`, `parseManagerDocument`). `timesheetsEnabled` is still read and written (`defaultTimesheetsEnabled` when not stored) but, from 1.11.0, no longer decides who has a timesheet: only self-employed days do (below). Annual leave fields not stored on the user (`annualLeaveDaysPerYear`, `annualLeaveYearStartMonth`, `annualLeaveYearEndMonth`, `annualLeaveCarriesOver`) take the organisation `annualLeaveDefaults` (§4.4), never a platform constant (`parseUserDocument` with `annualLeaveDefaults`); a save never writes the default onto the user.

**Effective dating.** Status and rate are both effective-dated; a change asks when it takes effect. Status: `employmentTypeTransitionFrom` / `employmentTypeEffectiveAt` (`applyEmploymentTypeChange`, `employmentTypeOnDay`). Rate: `organizations/{orgId}/operativeDayRateHistory` rows `{ userId, operativeId, dayRate, payBasis, effectiveAt, createdAt }`, built by `payRateHistoryWriteFields`: `effectiveAt` is midnight of the effective day in the organisation zone (never London unless that is the organisation's zone, never the device zone). Clearing a rate writes a row `{ dayRate: null, payBasis: null, effectiveAt }`, meaning "rate not set from that day". History is never rewritten. **A pay change** (1.10.0) writes its rows with `payRateChangeWrites({ previousProfile, nextProfile, history, effectiveDayKey, seedEffective })`: nothing when the amount and basis are unchanged; a seed row of the previous rate from the account's creation only when the person's history loaded and is empty; then the new row. The rows are written in the same batch as the profile (`users/{uid}`), never as a best-effort second write; when the history did not load the save is refused (`history_not_loaded`, `PAY_HISTORY_NOT_LOADED_MESSAGE`) instead of treating it as empty.

**Rate on a day** (`payRateOnDay`; 1.10.0: given `person` and `roster`, history rows match every id of the person — `personIdSet`: own id, former ids, same-email ids — so each app passes the whole history): a PAYE day has rate 0 (the line keeps its hours and is flagged PAYE). Otherwise the rate is the latest history row for the person whose effective day (organisation zone) is on or before the day; an explicit 0 is a real rate. If the live profile is hourly, an hourly row stays in force until a day-rate row falls on a later day. A "rate cleared" row in force means "rate not set" (it ends an hourly row too). With no history row the live profile applies (`users/{uid}`, then the operative row). With nothing set the rate is "rate not set".

**Pay rules.**
- **PAYE has no timesheet** (1.11.0, owner decisions round 3). A PAYE day produces no timesheet line, no pay and no invoice row; it is still a booking for warnings, Daily Overview and Weekly Report hours (where its pay cell reads PAYE, `reportPayCell`). The days that count on a person's sheet for a period are `timesheetDaysForPerson(user, period, timeZone)` — every organisation-zone day of the period on which they are self-employed (`employmentTypeOnDay`, effective-dated); `showsMyTimesheets(user, period, timeZone)` is whether that list is not empty. A person sees **My Timesheets** for a period only then, gets the submission reminder only then, and their owner sheet, its invoice (`timesheetInvoiceRows` with `timesheetDays`: on an unsigned sheet, live labour lines, price work and expenses dated on another day are left out; a signed snapshot is decided only from its own stored rows — signed is final, 1.46.0) and the User Timesheets row for that period exist only then. A PAYE manager or admin still reviews other people's timesheets (User Timesheets is `canReviewUserTimesheets`, not this). PAYE overtime is §25 (1.29.0): it is never a timesheet line and never money; PAYE price work and expenses are §26 (1.30.0): not timesheet lines, no line-manager approval, the person's own report. `isInvoiceLine` still drops a line flagged PAYE (older data).
- Self-employed days pay the effective day or hourly rate.
- Example: PAYE → self-employed changed on Tuesday, effective Wednesday: the sheet for that period shows Wednesday onwards only; Monday and Tuesday are not on it.
- **An unpaid break is never multiplied** (1.20.3, owner decision, final): on hours at a multiplier (weekend every hour at N×, or a set-window day's outside hours) the hours are multiplied first and the break comes off as plain hours — Sunday 07:30–14:00 every hour × 2 with 30 min unpaid = 13 − 0.5 = 12.5h (§4.3, `payrollHoursResult`). Booking rows show it as "12.5h (6.5h × 2 − 0.5h break)" (`bookingHoursLabel`); (1.20.4) timesheet lines, invoices, the weekly report and Daily Overview show "6.5h × 2 − 0.5h break = 12.5h" (`multipliedHoursText` over `payrollMultipliedHours`; a signed line's stored text is read back with `parseMultipliedHoursText`), never the back-calculated base hours ("6.25h × 2"), and the line's pay is unchanged.
- **Overlapping bookings are each paid** and never merged. An overlap is a warning managers should have caught; on the timesheet each overlapping line is shown in amber for the person and their line manager with "Overlaps another booking — check with your manager" (`overlappingBookingIds`, `OVERLAPPING_BOOKING_NOTE`). Times that only touch do not overlap. A Full day, AM or PM line is the standard day in effect on that line's own day (1.23.0: the collector passes the working-hours state, as pay has since 1.19.0), so a working-hours change inside the pay run moves the overlap flag with it.

**Who may see pay** (1.13.0, owner decisions round 4, Q1; narrowed 1.32.0, owner decisions 5 Nov; `canViewPersonPay(viewer, subject, roster, { context })`). A person's pay — day or hourly rate, pay basis, employment type (with its effective-dated transition), rate history, timesheets and invoices — is seen only by: the person (under any of their ids; their own rate read-only on My Profile); admins and super admins of the organisation (every person); a manager with the Operatives toggle, for an operative only; and the platform owner. **A line manager who is neither** sees a report's pay **only on the timesheets they review** — the rate, the overtime calculation and the invoice, so every timesheet looks the same — and nowhere else (no Operatives page, no profile rate, no rate history): the timesheet review screens pass `{ context: 'timesheetReview' }`; every other screen passes nothing (`context` `profile`). It is read live from the roster, so a level change recalculates it (no rights carry over). Before 1.32.0 every active line manager saw the rate everywhere. The read plans (`payProfileReadPlan`, `rateHistoryQueryPlan`) still load a report's pay for their line manager, and the security rules (`canViewPayOf`) still allow those reads, because the timesheet review prices from them; the narrowing is what each screen shows. **1.33.0 (owner decision 6 Nov, option C1 — accepted for launch):** this stays as it is. A line manager who is not an admin and has no Operatives toggle reads their reports' pay documents (`payProfiles`, rate history, the `operativeProfiles` mirror) **only for timesheet review**: the read plans ask for the viewer's own id and their reports' ids — never anyone else's, never a catalogue row — and every other screen shows them nobody's pay (`canViewPersonPay` with no context). The screens enforce that; the security rules allow the line manager's read of a report's pay documents, because they cannot tell which screen asked. A server-checked alternative (the rate given only for a sheet the line manager reviews) can follow after launch if the owner wants it. Every screen showing pay (the people profile, Manage Users, timesheets, User Timesheets, invoices, the weekly report's pay cells) asks `canViewPersonPay` first — from 1.13.0 the people profile on web; the others with the release that reads pay per person (step E of the move). Security rules cannot hide fields, and every member reads the roster's `users` documents, so pay moves to a private document per person per organisation: `organizations/{orgId}/payProfiles/{subjectId}` (`payProfilePath`; `subjectId` = the person's users id, or an `operatives` row id for a catalogue person with no account), fields `PAY_PROFILE_FIELDS` (`payBasis`, `dayRate`, `hourlyRate`, `employmentType`, `employmentTypeTransitionFrom`, `employmentTypeEffectiveAt`) plus `userId` and write metadata, read with `parsePayProfileDocument` and laid over the person with `overlayPayProfile` (the pay profile wins once it exists). A pay save writes the changed pay fields to the pay profile in the same batch as the profile (`payProfileWriteFieldsFromProfileWrite`), by whoever `canChangePersonPay` allows. **The move** (Deploy notes 1.13.0): apps write pay to both places and read the pay profile first; `scripts/backfill-pay-profiles.mjs` copies existing pay (`payProfileBackfillWrites`); a later release reads pay only per person; the platform owner then sets `platformConfig/app.privatePay` true (the rules then limit rate history, the `operativeProfiles` mirror and timesheets to pay viewers and refuse any pay value written back onto `users`, `operatives` or `managers`); the backfill's `--strip` step then removes pay from those documents. Only after the strip can no colleague read another's rate. **1.26.0, reading and writing with the switch:** while `privatePay` is on for the organisation (`securitySwitchOn`), a save writes pay only to the pay profile and the rate history — the `users` / `operatives` / `managers` write leaves every `PAY_PROFILE_FIELDS` key out (`sharedDocumentPayWrite`; Add User writes the invited pay to the placeholder's pay profile, and accepting the invitation writes the account without pay); rate history is read whole by admins and otherwise with one `userId ==` query per person whose pay the viewer may see (`rateHistoryQueryPlan`); and pay profiles are read whole by admins and otherwise by id (`payProfileReadPlan`), each laid over the roster person under their id, else a former invite id (`overlayPayProfile`). **1.26.1, one starting value for display and editors** (`payEditorStartingValues(person, profile, { privatePay })`): no single-person pay display or pay editor starting value reads `payProfiles` while `privatePay` is off for the organisation — it is the person's `users` / `operatives` document exactly as stored, because an older build may have left the pay profile stale. While it is on, the display (the people profile) and every pay editor (Manage Users / Edit User, the catalogue operative form, the rate dialogs) start from the same value, the person with their pay profile laid over (`overlayPayProfile`; own id, else a former invite id; the person's document when none is readable), and that same record is the baseline of the save: the changed-fields diff, the "rate changed?" test and the rate history's previous rate (`payRateChangeWrites` `previousProfile`). An editor never starts from, or saves back, an old rate left on a shared document. **1.27.0, catalogue operatives** (owner decision 7): a catalogue operative is an `operatives` row with no app account — no stored `linkedUserId` / `userId` / `uid`, a row id that is no roster member's id and no roster member on its email (`catalogueOperativePaySubjects(operatives, roster)` → `{ id: <row id>, email, catalogueOperative: true }`; the security rules: no users document of that id, no stored link and no `userEmails/{email}` claim of the organisation on its email). Its pay is seen by admins, the person on its email and managers with the Operatives toggle (`canViewPersonPay` with `catalogueOperative: true`; it has no line manager). Both apps pass the catalogue operatives with the roster to `payProfileReadPlan` (so an operatives manager reads `payProfiles/{rowId}` by id and the row is laid over with it, `overlayPayProfile`) and to `rateHistoryQueryPlan`, whose `people` plan now also lists `operativeIds` — one query `operativeId ==` <row id> `and userId == ''` each (`payRateHistoryWriteFields` stores an empty `userId` for a catalogue row; rows saved with no `userId` field at all are read by admins only). An `operatives` row that belongs to an account is that person's and is read through their users id. **1.27.0, the invited rate on accept** (owner decision 6): while `privatePay` is on, accepting an invitation copies the pay fields of the placeholder's pay profile, exactly, to the account's own `payProfiles/{authUid}` (`invitePayProfileCopyWrite`; nothing when the switch is off, there is no placeholder, the account already has a pay profile, or the placeholder's profile holds no pay field). It is written after `users/{authUid}` and before the email claim moves to the account; the security rules allow this one create only then (§16). With the switch off nothing changes. Known limit while it is on: an invitation accepted by an existing account of another company keeps the rate on the placeholder's pay profile (the placeholder stays; admins see it).

**Who may change pay** (`canChangePersonPay`). A person never changes their own pay, line manager or employment type on any path — `users/{uid}`, `operativeProfiles/{uid}`, `operativeDayRateHistory`, or the pay fields of `operatives/{id}` and `managers/{id}`. An admin may (including their own rate). A manager with Operatives access may change an operative's pay in the same company, never their own or another manager's; a manager's pay is changed by admins only. Resending an unchanged value is not a change.

**A self-employed person must have a pay rate** (1.14.0, owner decision). Self-employed people are paid from their rate, so a save that leaves a self-employed person (`normalizeEmploymentType`; a missing type reads as self-employed) without a rate is refused: `payRateRequiredError({ employmentType, payBasis, dayRate, hourlyRate })` returns `PAY_RATE_REQUIRED_MESSAGE` ("Set a day rate or hourly rate — self-employed people are paid from it.") when the rate for the chosen basis (`hourly` → `hourlyRate`, `day` → `dayRate`, no basis → either) is missing, blank, not a number, 0 or below. PAYE never needs a rate. Pass the person as they will be saved — a change to self-employed, immediate or effective-dated, is checked with the new type. Add User refuses the invite and Edit User refuses the save on every platform, with the message on the rate field, whenever the writer may set that person's pay (`canChangePersonPay`). People saved before this rule are listed for admins by `usersMissingPayRate(roster)` (active, not deleted, once per person by `personIdSet`).

**Evidence on timesheet entries** (1.45.0, owner decisions 14 Nov; `lib/canonical/timesheetEntryEvidence.ts`, `lib/canonical/claimEvidence.ts`). Before 1.45.0 both apps stored only a placeholder `receiptName` ("receipt.jpg" / "receipt-uploaded") on an expense entry and uploaded nothing. A `priceWorkEntries[]` or `expenseEntries[]` entry may now carry `evidence[]` in the PAYE claim evidence shape (§26: `{ path, name, kind: file | photo | personalSiteAudit, contentType?, auditId? }`, `payeClaimEvidenceWriteFields`, stored per entry by `timesheetEntryEvidenceWriteFields` and only when the entry has evidence, so an older entry's stored shape is unchanged), uploaded by the sheet's owner into their own claim folder (`payeClaimEvidencePath` → `organizations/{orgId}/payeClaims/{uid}/{uid}_{ms}.{ext}`, §24) — the folder the person, their active line managers and active admins read, exactly the sheet's reviewers, so no new Storage rule. **Reading:** `parseTimesheetEntries(sheetDocId, sheet, { timeZone, organizationId?, ownerUserIds? })` returns each entry's `evidence`, the sheet owner's own claim files only (`timesheetEvidenceOwnerIds`: the stored `userId`, the id the document is filed under, `timesheet_{uid}_…`, and the ids the app passes — every app passes every id the person goes by, `personIdSet`, so a sheet filed under an older id keeps evidence uploaded under the current one) — another person's file, a URL or junk is dropped; an older entry's `receiptName` is kept read-only and shown with `CLAIM_EVIDENCE_LEGACY_RECEIPT_NOTE`. **Saving:** the owner's save (`timesheetOwnerWriteFields`, unchanged) carries the entry arrays with their evidence until the line manager signs. The entries the owner added or changed (`timesheetOwnerTouchedEntries`, matched by id without case or by content; the evidence list is an owner field, so adding or removing evidence is an edit) are checked by `timesheetOwnerEntriesError({ previous, next, organizationId, ownerUserIds })`: price work needs a job number (1.30.1); **a new or edited expense needs at least one piece of evidence** (`EXPENSE_EVIDENCE_REQUIRED_MESSAGE`); price work evidence is optional; at most `PAYE_CLAIM_EVIDENCE_MAX` (20) per entry, each in the owner's own folder. Entries the owner did not touch — older ones with only a receipt name or without a job number — are never checked, rejected or rewritten, and a reviewer's decision is not an edit. **Edit and delete:** the owner edits or deletes their own entries while they may add one — `canEditTimesheetEntry(sheet, { viewerIsOwner, selfSigned })`, the same test as `timesheetOwnerCanAddExtras` (not once the line manager has signed; a signed self-signed sheet asks the person to sign again); an edit keeps the entry's id, and any change to its own fields or evidence reopens a review that carries a fingerprint (above). Removed evidence is deleted from Storage only when the owner uploaded it within the §24 undo window (`timesheetEntryEvidenceToDelete`), otherwise kept for recovery. **Shown:** on the sheet, the review lists (links and `claimEvidenceCountText`), Users' read-only rows (`timesheetClaimRows` rows carry `evidence`), the weekly report price work and expense rows (the count beside the description) and **the invoice PDF: one page per piece of evidence after the invoice** for its price work and expense rows (`timesheetEvidencePages({ rows, entries, currency })`, the PAYE report's page shape and drawing, `payeClaimEvidenceDrawAs`; a declined entry is not on the invoice, so its evidence is not either). Labels are the rulebook's on both apps: `EXPENSE_AMOUNT_LABEL` ("Amount inc VAT") on every expense form, `PRICE_WORK_AMOUNT_LABEL` ("Amount"), `CLAIM_EVIDENCE_ADD_PHOTO_LABEL` / `CLAIM_EVIDENCE_ADD_SITE_AUDIT_LABEL` / `CLAIM_EVIDENCE_ADD_FILE_LABEL` (web; iOS has `CLAIM_EVIDENCE_TAKE_PHOTO_LABEL` and `CLAIM_EVIDENCE_CHOOSE_PHOTO_LABEL` in place of Add photo), `TIMESHEET_ENTRY_EDIT_LABEL`, `TIMESHEET_ENTRY_DELETE_LABEL`, `TIMESHEET_ENTRY_SAVE_CHANGES_LABEL`, `TIMESHEET_ENTRY_DELETE_CONFIRM_MESSAGE`. **There is no security rule for it on timesheet documents** (as for the job number): older iOS builds save the entry arrays whole from their own model and do not know `evidence`, so **an older iPhone that saves the sheet drops the evidence of every entry** — `minimumIosBuild` is raised before people attach evidence (Deploy notes 1.45.0).

**Timesheet review** (`timesheetEntryReviews`). Only the person's line manager or an admin writes `payrollLineReviews` and `extraEntryReviews`. The decision for a price-work or expense entry is `extraEntryReviews[entryId] { decision, revisedAmount, reviewedAmount, reviewedByUserId, reviewedAtMs }` (`timesheetEntryDecision`, written with `timesheetEntryReviewWriteFields` as dotted partial updates); a review stored under the entry id in another letter case is found too. Entries are read with `parseTimesheetEntries(sheetDocId, sheet, { timeZone })`: every entry with a title is kept whatever its id, a stored id keeps its stored case (UUID or not), and an entry stored without an id gets `timesheetEntryId(sheetDocId, kind, index, entry)` — a UUID-shaped id derived from the sheet id, the array, the position and the content, the same on every read and every platform, never random — and the next save writes it onto the entry. A review made against a different amount is stale and the entry is pending again; an entry nobody reviewed counts at its own amount. **1.45.0 (review #322):** a review written from 1.45.0 also stores `reviewedFingerprint` — `timesheetEntryFingerprint(row, kind)` of the entry **as stored**: its own name, description, job number, agreed manager (price work), dates, amount and every stored evidence path, sorted (a reorder is no change); nothing is defaulted (a missing date fingerprints as missing) and `kind` is the array the row is in — and is stale when the stored entry's fingerprint differs, so **any owner change reopens the line manager's review, as PAYE does** (`timesheetEntryDecision`, and so `timesheetTotals` and `timesheetInvoiceRows`). One input everywhere: `parseTimesheetEntries` always computes each entry's `reviewFingerprint` from the stored row (a `reviewFingerprint` field stored on an entry is never read, and the owner's save strips one, `timesheetOwnerWriteFields`); an app keeps it on its loaded copy and passes it explicitly — `timesheetEntryDecision(sheet, entry, kind, trustedFingerprint)`, `entryFingerprints` on `timesheetInvoiceRows` / `timesheetTotals` (`timesheetEntryFingerprintMap`), and each entry's `reviewFingerprint` to `timesheetEntryReviewWriteFields` — so a display copy (dates filled in, evidence filtered) never decides and a forged stored value never keeps a review. A review stored under the entry id in another letter case is updated under that same key. A review stored before 1.45.0 has none and keeps the amount-only rule, so no older decision is reopened. **A reviewer's save writes only the reviews it changes** (§1): a current review with the same decision and revised amount is never rewritten (its reviewer, time and fields stay; an older review is not stamped with a fingerprint), and an entry left pending with no current review records nothing. The inline `managerDecision` / `managerRevisedAmount` on an entry is still written for older builds and counts only when it lowers what is paid, or on a legacy sheet (no map) the line manager already signed. Owner saves go through `timesheetOwnerWriteFields`: it always removes the review maps and the manager sign-off and export fields (`TIMESHEET_MANAGER_SIGN_OFF_FIELDS`); the person writes `weeklyReportOverride` only when they have no line manager (self-signed), cannot change price work or expenses once the line manager has signed, and writes `managerNote` (their own note to the manager). The owner then sends only the fields that changed, as a partial update — never a full merge-set of the sheet. Every timesheet save, owner or reviewer, writes `timesheetChangedFields(base, next)` (1.9.0): the fields that differ from the sheet **as this editor loaded it**, never every difference from the server copy, so a signature, expense or price-work entry saved on the other platform after the load is kept; and whether the line manager has signed is read from the stored sheet, never a local copy. Whether a writer is a reviewer is `timesheetWriterIsReviewer`. **Nobody reviews their own timesheet** (1.30.2): the person whose sheet it is — under any of their ids (`personIdSet`: their id, `formerUserIds`, `sameEmailUserIds`, roster rows on the same email) — is never its reviewer, admins and the Super Admin included, and `userTimesheetSubjects` never lists the viewer (User Timesheets, its counts and the review page). Their own sheet is the owner flow (My Timesheets): a self-signed owner (`timesheetSelfSigned`) completes it with their own signature through `timesheetOwnerWriteFields` with `subjectHasLineManager: false` (writing `weeklyReportOverride`); anyone else, an admin with Timesheet self-sign off included, waits for their line manager (or, with none, the other admins). **The export notice** (1.32.0, owner decision 4 Nov): because an export never includes the exporter's own sheet, User Timesheets → Signed off shows `timesheetExportOwnSheetNotice({ ownSheetSigned, ownSheetExported })` — `TIMESHEET_EXPORT_OWN_SHEET_NOTICE` ("Your own timesheet is not in this export — nobody exports their own sheet. Your line manager or another admin exports it.") — when the viewer's own sheet for that period is signed and not exported.

**Extras after sign-off** (1.43.0, owner bug 14 Nov): the person adds price work or expenses to their own sheet only until their line manager has signed it — `timesheetOwnerCanAddExtras(sheet, { selfSigned })` → `{ allowed, message }`, false once `managerSignedAt` (or the older `approvedAt`, the fields the security rules test) is set, with `TIMESHEET_SIGNED_OFF_EXTRAS_MESSAGE` ("This timesheet is signed off — add it to your next timesheet."); the add controls are not offered and no signature is cleared. A self-signed sheet stays open (adding asks the person to sign again). Before 1.43.0 web cleared only the person's signature, the line manager's signature and snapshot stayed (the rules keep them), and the sheet was left half signed. A sheet already half signed (`operativeSignedAt` empty, `managerSignedAt` set) reads `unsigned` in `timesheetListBucket` (neither Awaiting nor Signed off); how to show it is an owner question (1.43.0 hand-off). **Agreed-manager suggestions** (1.43.0): "Manager who agreed this" on a timesheet price work entry offers `agreedManagerSuggestions(query, person, roster, { organizationId })` — the people `priceWorkApproverCandidates` offers, whose name contains the typed text anywhere without case, at most `AGREED_MANAGER_SUGGESTIONS_MAX` (6); the field stays free text. **Price work job number** (1.30.1, owner decision 4 Nov; §26 has the shared rule). A price work entry (`priceWorkEntries[]`: `id`, `title`, `details`, `jobNumber`, `agreedManagerName`, `startDate`, `endDate`, `amount`) added on My Timesheets needs a job number (`priceWorkJobNumberError`, 1–60 characters, `PRICE_WORK_JOB_NUMBER_REQUIRED_MESSAGE`). An owner save is checked with `timesheetPriceWorkJobNumberError(previous, next)` against the sheet as the editor loaded it (else as stored): every entry the owner added, or whose own fields (`title`, `details`, `jobNumber`, `agreedManagerName`, dates, `amount`) they changed, needs one, so an edit of an older entry without a job number asks for it before it saves; an entry they did not touch — matched by id without case, or by content for one stored without an id — is never checked, so older entries never block a signature or a save, and a line manager's review (decision or revised amount) is not an edit and never asks. Entries are never rewritten or rejected on read. The sheet, its sign and review lists, the invoice (`timesheetInvoiceRows` rows carry `jobNumber`, 1.30.1), the weekly report price work rows and exports show the job number, "—" when an older entry has none (`priceWorkJobNumberText`). **There is no security rule for it on timesheet documents**: older iOS builds save price work entries without `jobNumber`, and a rule would refuse their whole timesheet save (signatures included), so the check is the rulebook and the apps only. Expense entries keep their optional job number.

**Pay arithmetic** (1.10.0) is `payForHours({ payBasis, dayRate, hourlyRate, paidHours, standardDayHours, otMultiplier })` — hourly: rate × hours × multiplier; day: day rate × (hours ÷ the organisation's paid day, `orgDayHours`) × multiplier — rounded with `roundPennies`; no app keeps its own. 1.12.0: a day rate with a paid day below 1 hour (`PAY_STANDARD_DAY_MIN_HOURS`), missing or not a number pays 0 (rate not set), never the day rate divided by a 0.01 floor; a multiplier that is not a positive number is 1; a rate that is not a number is 0.

**The unpaid break** (1.12.0, `payrollHoursResult`). On a weekday the break taken off a booking is the part of the break window (`breakWindowStart`–`breakWindowEnd`) the booking's clock time covers, up to `unpaidBreakMinutes`: a booking across the whole window loses the whole break; 11:50–12:20 against a 12:00–12:30 break loses 20 minutes. Nothing comes off when the break is paid (`breakPaid`), removed on the booking (`isBreakRemoved`) or the slot is a named AM / PM. **A booking whose clock time lies entirely within the unpaid break pays 0 hours** (12:00–12:30, 12:05–12:25) — when a booking has clock times it never falls back to a full standard day; only a booking with no clock times at all is paid by its slot (FULL DAY, AM, PM, EVENING, OVERTIME). **Lunch warning** (1.13.0): before a custom-hours booking is saved, every app shows `customHoursBreakWarning(policy, dayKey, booking)` — "You are booking custom hours that cover the unpaid lunch break. Turn off Unpaid break to make sure they are paid for this time." — as a confirm step when the booking is custom hours, the organisation break is unpaid (`breakPaid` not true, an unpaid length above 0), the booking's Unpaid break toggle is on (`isBreakRemoved` not true) and its hours overlap the break window that day (1.19.0: on a Saturday or Sunday only when that day's break is unpaid, `unpaidBreakAppliesOnDay`). The pay rule above is unchanged.

**Weekday pay** (1.20.0, owner decisions). A FULL DAY pays the weekday paid day — the standard day less the covered part of the unpaid break (`payrollHoursResult`; owner example 7.25h). Custom weekday hours pay as before: the hours inside the standard day less the unpaid-break overlap, and the hours outside it at the weekday overtime multiplier (06:30–16:30 → 7.25h + 2h × 1.5). Custom weekday hours are never snapped to a half day (11:00–14:30 pays 3.5h, not half the day) — 1.44.0: under every policy; before 1.44.0 a policy saved before 1.20.0 kept the 20-minute half-day snap, which went with the stored paid day (owner to confirm, part of decision A = 1). A stored AM / PM booking pays half `weekdayPaidHours` as before. The day a day rate is divided by is `weekdayPaidHours` of the policy in effect that day (`payForHours`, `reportDayValue`); every live timesheet line carries it (`standardDayHours`, required from 1.44.0 — no 8-hour default anywhere). A signed line is shown from its stored days, hours and amount, never re-read on today's paid day (1.44.0).

**Weekend pay** (1.13.0, Q2). Weekend bookings are custom hours (§4.3) and are paid by `payrollHoursResult` on that day's rule: with a set window, the hours inside it count as its "counts as" hours (pro rata when only part of it is worked) and the hours outside it are at that day's outside multiplier; with every hour at one rate, each hour × that multiplier. 1.19.0: when that day's lunch break is unpaid, that day's own break (length and window, the weekday ones when not set) comes off as §4.3 says (set window: off the credited hours for the part inside the window, off the outside hours for the part outside; every hour: (clock − break) × N). Every day is priced under the policy in effect on it; **a signed timesheet is never re-priced** — its `weeklyReportOverride` lines (and the signed-sheet invoice, `timesheetInvoiceRows`) keep their signed hours and amounts whatever the working hours later become. Stored weekend rows keep being paid as before and are never rewritten: an AM / PM pays half the "counts as" hours; a FULL DAY with no clock times pays the set window at its "counts as" hours with nothing outside it (`weekendFullDayWindow`; before 1.13.0 the weekday standard day plus weekend overtime), and on an all-hours day the pre-1.13.0 calculation (the standard day × that day's multiplier, e.g. 07:30–16:00 at 2× = 17).

**Overnight pay** (1.13.0, Q5): a booking that crosses midnight is paid wholly at its start day's rules (`payrollHoursResult`): on a weekday the hours inside that day's standard window count once (less the unpaid break they cover) and every other hour, after midnight too, is at that day's weekday multiplier; on a Saturday or Sunday that day's weekend rule. A weekday night is paid at the weekday overtime multiplier for every hour outside the standard window (owner confirmed). Before 1.13.0 it was paid as plain hours at 1×.

**Who takes part in timesheets** is `timesheetParticipant(user, dayOrPeriod, timeZone)`: never an inactive person, an anonymised profile or (1.34.0) a ghost operative (`timesheetDaysForPerson` is empty for `ghost`; `userTimesheetSubjects` and `exportedTimesheetHistorySubjects` never list one); given a period, `showsMyTimesheets`; given a day, self-employed that day (1.11.0: `timesheetsEnabled` and operative mode no longer bring a PAYE person in). It decides the Weekly Report timesheet feed (§7, per payment run), the `timesheet_submission_reminder` recipients (§13) and the User Timesheets rows.

**Invoices** (1.10.0). The rows of a generated invoice are `timesheetInvoiceRows({ sheet, payrollLines, extrasMode })` on every platform: a signed sheet's `weeklyReportOverride` first (its signed amounts; declined and zero rows, and PAYE / leave lines, left out); otherwise the live lines (`invoiceLines`) with the line manager's review once signed (`payrollLineReview(sheet, lineId)`, found under the line id in any letter case), then price work and expenses with their decision (`timesheetEntryDecision`) in `decided` mode or at their own amounts in `raw` mode. 1.30.1: each row carries its `jobNumber` (the stored entry's or override row's, trimmed; labour: the override row's, else the live line's; '' when none — price work shows "—", `priceWorkJobNumberText`). 1.11.0: given `timesheetDays`, the live rows of an unsigned sheet dated on a day the person was PAYE are left out (the PAYE-extras path is removed). A signed snapshot is decided only from its stored rows (1.46.0, signed is final): never by `timesheetDays`, today's employment or the live lines — a row is left out only when it is declined, 0, or flagged PAYE / leave (`isPayeDay`, `isLeave`) on the signed row itself; every app writes both flags on every snapshot line. **The signed snapshot waits for the bookings** (1.43.0, owner bug 14 Nov): `weeklyReportOverride` is built once, at full approval, from the bookings the screen holds, so signing, counter-signing, Generate invoice, Export (User Timesheets → Signed off) and every save that builds it wait until the period's bookings and the pay inputs have loaded — `timesheetSnapshotReady({ bookingsLoaded, bookingsFailed, payProfileLoaded, rateHistoryLoaded })` → `{ ready, failed, message }` (the buttons read `TIMESHEET_SNAPSHOT_LOADING_MESSAGE`, "Loading bookings…"; a failed load `TIMESHEET_SNAPSHOT_FAILED_MESSAGE`). Before 1.43.0 a counter-signature made before the bookings arrived froze a snapshot with no labour lines and the invoice showed only price work. "Loaded" means a server answer of the whole query (every part of a read plan), never rows replayed from a cache or an earlier, narrower listener; a new query, a re-plan or a cache snapshot clears it until the server answers again (a server confirmation of unchanged rows counts). Export re-checks this before each person and stops when it closes, re-reads each sheet, leaves one already exported elsewhere exactly as it is, builds the snapshot (rebuilding an empty one as below) before it stamps `exportedAt` — an exported snapshot is never rebuilt — and builds the invoice from that same saved copy. While a sheet could not be loaded, the period page shows only the error and Retry (nothing to edit, sign or add to). A snapshot already frozen that way is rebuilt from the live bookings, with its signatures and approver as stored, by whoever may write it (the line manager or an admin; a self-signed owner): `timesheetSnapshotNeedsRebuild(sheet, liveLabourLines)` — a snapshot with no labour lines, not exported, and a live labour line in the period (PAYE days left out) whose booking was created and last written on or before the sheet's approval instant — the later of its `createdAt` and `updatedAt` (each line passes `bookingCreatedAt` and `bookingUpdatedAt`) ≤ the snapshot's `approvedAt`, else `managerSignedAt` / `approvedAt`. A snapshot signed with price work or expenses only is therefore never re-priced by a booking added to, moved into or changed in the period later, nor by one whose times are unknown, and a snapshot with any labour line is never rebuilt.

**A line manager's "Edit hours"** (1.46.0, rates audit). Changing the hours of a timesheet line prices the whole booking again at the edited times — `revisedPayrollLineAmounts({ dayKey, timeZone, policy | policyState, startTime, endTime, isBreakRemoved, otMultiplierOverride, payBasis, dayRate, hourlyRate, bookingHasOvertimeLine, overtimeLineMinHours })`: `payrollHoursResult` under the policy in effect that day in the organisation zone, with the booking's own overtime rate, then `payForHours` for the standard and the overtime part, each rounded to pennies — the same figures the timesheet collector gives a booking with those times. The result is stored one review per line, `payrollLineReviews[lineId] = { decision: 'edited', revisedAmount, revisedStandardHours, revisedOvertimeHours, revisedOvertimeClockHours, revisedOvertimeMultiplier, revisedStandardAmount, revisedOvertimeAmount, reviewedBookingFingerprint }` (`reviewFields`, `PayrollLineEditReviewFields`): the booking's standard line its standard hours and pay and its overtime line its overtime hours and pay; when the booking has no overtime line on the sheet, the standard line carries both, so the invoice and the totals pay the overtime the edit added. Only the edited line and a line whose amount the edit changes are written. **The review is tied to the booking it was made against**: `reviewedBookingFingerprint` is `overtimeBookingFingerprint` of the live booking (each app puts it on every payroll line as `bookingFingerprint`); once the booking changes, `payrollLineReview` (and so `timesheetInvoiceRows` and `timesheetTotals`) reads the review as stale — the line is pending at its live amount, like a stale price work or expense review — so a booking later changed to the edited times is never paid twice. A standard-line review that carries overtime (made when the booking had no overtime line) is also stale once the booking has its own overtime line (`bookingHasOvertimeLine`, e.g. after a working-hours change — the fingerprint does not cover the working hours). A review stored without a fingerprint (before 1.46.0, approve / decline) is never stale. The fingerprint does not cover the person's rate either: a rate change after an edit keeps the edited amount, as before 1.46.0. **A signed sheet is never re-priced by a stale review**: each signed snapshot line keeps the booking as signed — `weeklyReportOverride.lines[].bookingFingerprint`, `bookingHadOvertimeLine` (the booking had its own overtime line on the sheet; a part an Edit hours review added is not one) and `appliedReviewKey` (`payrollLineReviewKey` of the review applied at signing, '' for none). A later save tests each review against the line as signed with the same rule as at signing (`payrollLineReview` with those values): a review stale then is never applied, a review that is the one applied at signing changes nothing, and a line signed before 1.46.0 (no fingerprint) never takes a fingerprinted review. A `{booking}-ot` line that a standard line's review added (`reviewPartOf` = that line's id) follows that line's current review: recomputed with it, 0 when it is declined or no longer pays overtime, unchanged for an approval; a booking's own overtime line keeps its own review. Lines group by booking with `payrollLineBookingKey` (collection — a line without one is `bookings`, or `managerSiteBookings` for a `mgr-` id — and booking id), so a labour and a manager site booking sharing an id stay apart. A review written after the line manager has counter-signed still re-prices the signed sheet, as before 1.46.0 (an owner question). **The signed copy decides its own invoice rows** (1.46.0): `timesheetInvoiceRows` leaves a signed row out only by its own stored data (declined, an amount of 0, or a PAYE / leave flag on the signed row), never by the live line or today's employment and never by `timesheetDays`, so a later back-dated change to PAYE never removes a signed row from the invoice or the totals. Before 1.46.0 a signed row whose live line had become a PAYE day (or a period with no self-employed days left) was dropped. **Hours follow the money**: `timesheetTotals` counts an edited booking's hours from its review (standard hours, overtime clock hours, weighted hours; a declined line of that booking counts none) and splits its money into standard and overtime labour; a signed snapshot takes the parts (`payrollLineReviewSnapshotParts`: a standard line carrying overtime becomes the standard line and `{booking}-ot` at the multiplier), so the weekly report reads 8.5h at the day rate and 3h × 2, never one day at the whole amount. Times that cannot be priced — blank, not `HH:mm`, or start equal to finish — are refused (`revisedPayrollLineTimesError`, `PAYROLL_LINE_EDIT_TIMES_MESSAGE` "Enter a start and a finish time that are different."; `revisedPayrollLineAmounts` returns null) and Save stays off; a finish before the start is overnight. The collector's overtime-line cut-off (`overtimeLineMinHours`) applies whether or not the booking has an overtime line, and a PAYE day (`employment`, `employmentTypeOnDay`) keeps its hours and pays 0. Owner case: working day 07:00–16:00 with 30 min unpaid 12:00–12:30, a line edited to 07:00–19:00 at ×2 on £250 a day → 250 + 176.47 = **426.47**. Before 1.46.0 both apps scaled the line's own amount by the ratio of new to old hours, so a standard line never paid the hours the edit put outside the working day. **Revised amounts are money in pennies** (1.46.0): every revised amount is written rounded (`roundPennies`) and read rounded wherever it is counted (`timesheetEntryEffectiveAmount`, `timesheetInvoiceRows` — a live review and an edited row of a signed snapshot, which is rounded, never re-priced), so the invoice rows add up to the invoice total. The export total of the invoice lines is rounded too.

**Timesheet totals** (1.22.0, owner rule: a summary never disagrees with the invoice). Every figure that sums a sheet — the User Timesheets and My Timesheets list rows (hours, overtime, price work, expenses), the detail screen (hours subtotal, overtime, price work, expenses, extras and the total), the sign sheet and the signed-sheet value — is `timesheetTotals(input)`, where `input` is exactly the input of `timesheetInvoiceRows` and the money is the sum of its rows, so the summary total is the invoice total for the same inputs by construction. `{ hours, standardHours, overtimeHours, labour, standardLabour, overtimeLabour, priceWork, expenses, extras, total, submittedPriceWork, submittedExpenses, submittedExtras, pendingEntries, source, invoiceRowCount }`, money rounded to pennies. In `decided` mode a declined entry counts 0, an edited one its revised amount, a stale review (made against another amount) is pending and counts the entry's own amount, an unreviewed entry its own amount (`timesheetEntryDecision`, `timesheetEntryEffectiveAmount`); a signed snapshot (`weeklyReportOverride`) wins; PAYE days and leave lines never count. List rows use `decided` (the export invoice). A detail screen uses `decided` once the line manager has signed or while the viewer is reviewing, else `raw` (the owner's draft at the amounts entered); a reviewer before signing passes `applyLabourReviews: true` (new on `timesheetInvoiceRows`; missing = once the line manager has signed, as before), so their screen shows the invoice their signature will produce. Hours come from the live payroll lines: each booking's standard line carries `bookingPaidHours` (`payrollHoursResult.totalPaidHours`) and `bookingOvertimeHours`; without them a line's `paidHours` (× `otMultiplier` on an overtime line). Before 1.22.0 list rows summed every entry at its own amount (a declined expense still counted) and both apps re-implemented the entry decision on the detail screen.

**Calculation.** Hours, overtime and pay come from bookings, the working-hours policy in effect that day and payment runs (`payrollHoursResult`, `workedHoursSplit`). **A booking's own overtime rate** (1.29.0, §25): `otMultiplierOverride` on a labour or manager site booking (1–3 in 0.25 steps; missing = the organisation's rate, so every existing booking prices exactly as before) replaces the day's multiplier for the booking's overtime part only — weekday hours outside the working day, a set-window weekend day's outside hours, every hour of a weekend paid at a multiplier only — never its standard hours, in `payrollHoursResult` and so on every screen that prices or labels a booking (timesheets, invoices, Daily Overview, the weekly report, booking breakdowns). A self-employed person's overtime lines are priced at it. A signed timesheet is never re-priced: a rate changed after signing does not alter the signed sheet or its invoice. Timesheets are grouped by payment run (`timesheetPeriodFromInvoicing`, `selectableTimesheetPaymentRunPeriods`); status Awaiting / Signed off / Exported is `timesheetListBucket`. **Line times** (1.43.0, owner rule 14 Nov): a booking with clock times shows, on its standard line, the standard-window times and, on its overtime line, the overtime times — `payrollLineClockRanges(payrollHoursResult)` → `{ standard, overtime, standardText, overtimeText }`, from the pay rule's own segments (working day 07:00–16:00, booked 07:00–19:00 → standard "07:00–16:00", overtime "16:00–19:00"; an early start and a late finish are two overtime ranges, "05:00–07:00 + 16:00–19:00"; a set-window weekend day's window is standard and the hours outside it overtime; every hour of an every-hour day is overtime; an overnight range runs past midnight as stored).
