The standard line keeps " · no break" when the booking removes the break; an overtime line with an unpaid break keeps its multiplied text after the times ("16:00–19:00 · OT 6.5h × 2 − 0.5h break = 12.5h", still read by `parseMultipliedHoursText`), and screens show only its clock part (`timesheetLineClockText`) beside the rate line. A booking with no standard part keeps its own times on the standard line; a named slot (FULL DAY, AM, PM) keeps its name. Wording only: no hours or pay change, and a signed sheet's `weeklyReportOverride` keeps its stored text. **Booking previews price the booking's own rate** (1.43.0): every booking editor and create screen passes the rate on screen (`otMultiplierOverride`) to the hours preview, the timeline, `bookingHoursExplanation` and `weekendBookingBreakdown` (new optional last argument) — owner example: working day 07:00–16:00, 30 min unpaid break, organisation rate 1.5, booking 07:00–19:00 at ×2 → "8.5h + 3h × 2 = 6h → 14.5h".

**Annual leave on timesheets.** Each approved leave day in the period appears as an information line "Annual leave (full day / AM / PM)" for the person and their line manager, with no edit control and no hours or pay (`timesheetLeaveLines`, `timesheetLeaveLabel`); 1.20.0: given the working day in effect on each day, the line carries its clock range from `leaveHalfDayWindows` (`timeRange`, e.g. "Annual leave (AM) · 06:30–10:30"). Leave never appears on a generated invoice.

**Admin and operative mode** (1.11.0). An admin or super admin has no operative mode: a stray `operativeMode` flag on an admin is ignored everywhere — people lists (`parseUserDocument` → `operativeMode`, `accountKind`), permissions (`effectivePermissions`), the approval rules (`isAdminOutsideOperativeMode`, `adminOutsideOperativeMode`) and the security rules (`callerOrgRole`, `hbCallerIsOperative`). Only operative-level users get operative mode, at setup. (Before 1.11.0 such an admin decided only as a line manager.)

**Line manager.** A person's line managers are `assignedManagerUserIds` (else the single `assignedManagerUserId`; `hasNoLineManager` means none) — `lineManagerIds(user, roster)`. Given the roster, only active members count, an old invite id the roster knows as someone's `formerUserIds` / `sameEmailUserIds` is that person, and the person themself never counts under any of their ids (`personIdSet`: id, `formerUserIds`, `sameEmailUserIds`, roster rows with the same email). `hasActiveLineManager` is whether anyone is left. Line managers receive that person's timesheets for signing, annual leave requests and other approval items (`userTimesheetSubjects`, `leaveApproverIds`). With no active line manager (legacy data only), the active admins and super admins outside operative mode receive them — never "any manager". Nobody approves their own items. Routing is read at the time — changing the line manager on any platform re-routes everything, nothing is stored on the request.
- **Every operative and every manager needs an active line manager**, whatever the annual-leave self-book permission and whether annual leave is switched on for them. **An admin needs one too unless their self-manage toggles are satisfied** (1.28.0, `selfManageToggles(user).satisfied`): Annual Leave Management (`annualLeaveSelfBook`) on **and** Timesheet self-sign (`timesheetSelfSign`) on — or, for a PAYE admin (no My Timesheets, `isPayeAccount`: stored `employmentType` `paye`), Annual Leave Management alone. **The Super Admin** of the organisation being worked in always counts as having both toggles on and never needs one. Who that is is judged per organisation, the same as the security rules (`callerIsSuperAdminOf`): its creator (`creatorUserId`), whether or not the users document has `isSuperAdmin`, or a users document of that organisation with `isSuperAdmin` — never a Super Admin of another company working here as an admin (`isOrganizationSuperAdmin(viewer, org)`). Apps pass the result as `organizationSuperAdmin` on the person (`isSuperAdminAccount` falls back to the stored flag only when it is missing). `lineManagerRequired(user)`; an inactive person is not checked. `lineManagerRequiredError(user, roster)` refuses the save when a required line manager is missing and says what fixes it: an operative or manager `LINE_MANAGER_REQUIRED_MESSAGE` ("Set a line manager — this person's timesheets and annual leave need approval."); an admin `ADMIN_LINE_MANAGER_REQUIRED_MESSAGE` ("Set a line manager, or turn on both Annual Leave Management and Timesheet self-sign — this person's timesheets and annual leave need approval."); a PAYE admin `PAYE_ADMIN_LINE_MANAGER_REQUIRED_MESSAGE` ("Set a line manager, or turn on Annual Leave Management — this person's annual leave needs approval."). Manage Users on every platform refuses that save. People who fail it are listed for admins by `usersMissingLineManager(roster)` (Manage Users and Settings → Data health); until fixed, their timesheets and requests go to the other active admins (`leaveApproverIds`, `timesheetSubmittedRecipients`), so nothing is stranded — an organisation always has its Super Admin, who is always satisfied.
- **Self-manage toggles** (1.28.0, admins): **Annual Leave Management** (`annualLeaveSelfBook`; on = their own leave saves approved, "Submit"; off = they request it, §9) and **Timesheet self-sign** (`timesheetSelfSign`, label "Timesheet self-sign", help "Sign your own timesheet and generate the invoice without a line manager's signature."). The two are independent of each other and of the line manager: either may be on while the admin has a line manager (leave self-booked but the timesheet signed by the line manager, or the reverse). **Self-signed timesheets** (`timesheetSelfSigned(user)`): an admin with Timesheet self-sign on, or the Super Admin — line manager or not. Their timesheet has no "note to manager" and no "pending manager signature" state: their own signature completes the sheet (generate invoice is ready), and their owner save may write `weeklyReportOverride` (`timesheetOwnerWriteFields` with `subjectHasLineManager: !timesheetSelfSigned(...)`). With the toggle off their line manager signs (or, with none, the other admins). **Having no line manager no longer self-approves anyone** (before 1.28.0 it self-signed and self-booked every admin without one). Timesheet self-sign exists for admins only (`ADMIN_ONLY_PERMISSION_FIELDS`): it is shown and editable only on admin profiles (`ADMIN_EDITABLE_PERMISSION_FIELDS`), shown greyed out for a PAYE admin (`TIMESHEET_SELF_SIGN_PAYE_NOTE`), on and locked for the Super Admin (`SUPER_ADMIN_SELF_MANAGE_MESSAGE`), never shown and always read off for a manager or operative (`effectivePermissions`). Demoting an admin keeps its stored value underneath (like every toggle since 1.15.0, `accountTypePermissions`), so a promotion back restores it; after the demotion the person needs a line manager. **Per organisation** (1.30.2, `organizationSelfManageFlags`): both toggles are read for the organisation being worked in — the person's users document when its `organizationId` is that organisation, otherwise their membership there (`users/{uid}/orgMemberships/{orgId}`, `parseOrgMembershipDocument`, the same source as `organizationLevelFor`), otherwise off; the organisation's creator reads both on. Never the users document of another company: a person's toggles in one company never apply in another. Admins set both toggles in Manage Users; nobody changes their own (`permissionToggleRows({ viewerIsSubject })` → `OWN_SELF_MANAGE_MESSAGE`, enforced by security rules, §16). No notification is sent.
- **Line managers and overtime** (1.29.0, §25): being a person's line manager also lets one apply that person's approved overtime to their bookings filed under their users id (§16). Whoever may set the line manager — admins, and operatives managers for operatives — therefore also decides who gets those writes.
- **`hasNoLineManager`** is a line-manager field: it is written only by whoever may change the line manager (admins; operatives managers for operatives), never by the person, together with the line-manager ids in one partial update. It is only valid for an admin or super admin, and (1.28.0) for an admin only with their self-manage toggles satisfied (`lineManagerRequiredError`). "No line manager" is offered to every admin (`lineManagerOptions`); the save says which toggles to turn on.

## 9. Annual leave

One document per request at `organizations/{orgId}/holidayBookings/{id}`: a run of calendar-consecutive days with the same slot is one record from `startDate` to `endDate` (`holidayRequestRanges`, `holidayRequestWriteFields`), with `timeSlot` (`FULL DAY` / `AM` / `PM`, applying to every day) and `status` (`pending`, `approved`, `rejected`). Cancelling approved leave is a request (`cancellationRequestedAt`, `cancellationRequestedByUserId`) that an approver accepts (the record is removed) or refuses. Either way the requester is notified (`holidayDecisionRecipients`: `holiday_request_approved` when accepted, `holiday_request_declined` when refused, 1.9.0), as its own notice that never reuses the original decision's row. Older one-document-per-day records stay valid. **Reading a record** is `parseHolidayDocument(docId, data, timeZone)` on every platform (no app keeps its own parser): it accepts the keys either app has written (start `startDate`/`start_date`/`start`/`from`/`dateFrom`/`holidayStart`/`date`; end `endDate`/`end_date`/`end`/`to`/`dateTo`/`holidayEnd`, else the start day; person `userId`/`user_id`/`uid`/`userID`/`linkedUserId`/`employeeUserId` or nested `user.id`, and `operativeId`/`operative_id`/`operativeID`; status `status`/`holidayStatus`/`approvalStatus`; slot `timeSlot`/`slot`/`halfDay`; note `decisionNote`/`note`), uses the document id as the record id, keeps ids in their stored case (an `operativeId` that is not a UUID is kept), turns dates into organisation-zone day keys (a calendar-date string is that day) and swaps a reversed range. Status words map through `normalizeHolidayStatus` (`HOLIDAY_STATUS_SYNONYMS`: requested/awaiting/submitted → pending; accepted/confirmed → approved; declined/denied/cancelled → rejected); a missing or unknown status is approved. Warnings, Daily Overview, leave coverage and timesheets take the parsed day keys as they are (`labourHolidayInput`) and never recompute them from the stored dates. A decision writes `status`, `approvedByUserId`, `approvedAt` and an optional trimmed `decisionNote` (blank clears it) shown to the requester (`holidayDecisionWriteFields`, `parseHolidayDecisionNote`). Day counting (`leaveRecordDayKeys`, `annualLeaveBalance`): a single-day record counts its day; a multi-day record counts each day except weekends and bank holidays, each in its own leave year. Submitting a request notifies its approvers (`holiday_request_submitted`, `holidayRequestRecipients`), and the requester is notified of the decision (`holidayDecisionRecipients`); see §13. Approved leave affects Daily Overview, unbooked-labour warnings (`leaveCoverageRows`), timesheets (information lines) and My Schedule. Booking someone onto a day of their approved or pending leave asks first (`bookingLeaveConflicts`, §5, 1.16.0). Editing an existing record is a partial update of the changed fields.

**Leave halves** (1.20.0, owner decision). Annual leave keeps AM / PM / Full day = 0.5 / 0.5 / 1 day (`leaveRecordDayKeys`, `annualLeaveBalance` unchanged). The halves split the Monday–Friday working day at its exact middle, ignoring the break: `leaveHalfDayWindows(policy)` (06:30–14:30 → AM 06:30–10:30, PM 10:30–14:30; 10:00–18:00 → AM 10:00–14:00, PM 14:00–18:00; floored to the minute), read under the working hours in effect that day. They are used only for leave: the Daily Overview, Schedule and My Schedule leave labels (`leaveHalfDayLabel`, e.g. "AM 06:30–10:30"), `bookingLeaveConflicts` (purple rows), `leaveCoverageRows` and timesheet leave lines (`timeRange`). A stored AM / PM booking compared with leave is that leave half (`leaveComparableInterval`); custom hours are their clock times. **Leave cover:** on a half-leave day the other half must be booked minute for minute, except that time inside the lunch-break window never needs covering (owner rule, as for unbooked labour, §5; the break does not move where the halves split): AM leave on 06:30–14:30 with lunch 10:00–10:45 plus a booking 10:30–14:30 → covered; plus 10:45–14:30 → covered; plus 11:00–14:30 → "10:45–11:00 (0.25 hours) is not booked"; on 10:00–18:00 (paid break 13:00–13:30) AM leave plus 14:00–18:00 → covered. Bookings keep `halfDayWindows`; no app splits a leave day itself.

**Self-booking** (`canSelfBookAnnualLeave(user, roster)`, 1.28.0): a person's own leave saves approved when they have the `annualLeaveSelfBook` permission (Annual Leave Management; 1.30.2: in the organisation being worked in, `organizationSelfManageFlags`) outside operative mode, or they are the Super Admin. Having no line manager never makes anyone a self-booker, admins included (before 1.28.0 an admin without one self-booked). Everyone else sends a pending request to `leaveApproverIds`. The submit button reads "Submit" when the leave saves approved and "Submit request" otherwise (`leaveSubmitLabel`, `LEAVE_SUBMIT_LABEL`, `LEAVE_SUBMIT_REQUEST_LABEL`). Self-booked leave notifies nobody. **A ghost operative's leave** (1.34.0, §11) is booked for it by whoever decides its leave (`canBookLeaveFor` = `canDecideLeave`: its line manager or an admin, never another manager), saved approved, and nobody is notified (`ghostLeaveBooking` → `{ allowed, status: 'approved', notify: false }`). **Never delete leave when the toggle changes** (owner decision 4 Nov): switching Annual Leave Management on or off for a person only decides whether their *future* leave saves straight away or is sent as a request; their existing approved leave and pending requests stay exactly as they are, on every platform.

**Who decides** (`canDecideLeave(viewer, requester, roster)`): never the requester under any of their ids, admins included. An admin or super admin not in operative mode may decide any request in the organisation; anyone else only when they are in `leaveApproverIds(requester)` (an admin in operative mode decides only as a line manager). The list a person sees to decide — pending requests and cancellation requests — is `leaveDecisionQueue(viewer, requests, roster)`. Enforced by security rules.

**Annual leave → Team** (1.40.0, owner decisions 11 Nov F18–F23; `lib/canonical/annualLeaveTeam.ts`). Who a viewer's Team list holds is `annualLeaveTeamPeople({ viewer, people, roster })`: admins and the Super Admin outside operative mode — every current person, people who book their own leave included, never themselves (their own leave is on My leave); anyone else — only the people they line-manage (`lineManagerIds` over the roster, matched under every id the viewer goes by, `personIdSet`; an operative-level person is never a line manager, 1.31.0). Never an anonymised deleted profile, an inactive person or a pending invite (`annualLeaveTeamPersonIsCurrent`); a catalogue `operatives` row with no account is listed for admins while active. The Team tab shows for admins and for anyone who line-manages at least one current person (`annualLeaveTeamTabVisible`) — the Operatives toggle plays no part. Header figures and counts come from that list only; the header year is the organisation's leave year (`leaveYearBounds` from `annualLeaveDefaults.startMonth`), never the viewer's own. Each person's leave is matched under every id they go by (`leaveRecordsForAccount`); away today / away soon use `leaveDayKind`. The empty state is `annualLeaveTeamEmptyMessage`. Before 1.40.0 both apps had their own list builder, hid everyone who books their own leave and listed deleted people; iOS listed everyone for every manager, and web gave a line manager without the Operatives toggle no Team tab. **Leave filed only under an `operatives` row** (no users document on `userId`; owner decision 13 Nov, item 17): only admins decide it. A decision is judged on the users document the record's `userId` (else `operativeId`) names (`leaveDecisionRequester`), never on the person a row's email points at, because an Operatives manager may change a row's email (§16 Roster rows). **Notices** (§13): a manager or admin booking approved leave for someone writes `holiday_booked_for_you`, removing someone's leave writes `holiday_cancelled_by_manager`, to `holidayDecisionRecipients` — web from 1.40.0 (iOS already). iOS offers "book leave for someone" only when `canDecideLeave` allows it (`canBookLeaveFor`); otherwise it sends a pending request, never a save the rules refuse afterwards.

**The leave calendar** (1.23.0, audit finding 19). What a person's annual-leave calendar shows on a day is `leaveDayKind(records, dayKey, timeZone, nonWorkingDayKeys)` → `{ kind, recordId, isLocked }`: a record covers the day only when the day is one of its leave days (`leaveRecordDayKeys` — a multi-day record skips weekends and the bank holidays given, the days the allowance counts); rejected records never show; approved leave with a pending cancellation request still shows as approved (as everywhere in §9); and the order is fixed, never the list order — a full day beats a half day, and at the same length pending beats approved (pendingFull, approvedFull, pendingHalf, approvedHalf, none). A full day is locked (`isLocked`): no more leave is requested on it. Before 1.23.0 web read the whole start–end range on the browser day and iOS hid leave with a pending cancellation.

**Holiday Report** (1.22.0) — one person's leave in one leave year — is `leaveReportRows({ records, personIds, onDayKey, startMonth, timeZone, nonWorkingDayKeys, statuses })` → `{ startDayKey, endDayKey, yearKey, rows: [{ id, startDayKey, endDayKey, timeSlot, status, days }], totalDays }` on every platform, with the year picker from `leaveReportYears({ firstDayKey, todayDayKey, startMonth })` and one record's days from `leaveReportDayCount(record, range, timeZone, nonWorkingDayKeys)` (`leaveDaysInRange`). The year is the organisation leave year (`leaveYearBounds` from `annualLeaveDefaults.startMonth`), never the calendar or device year; a multi-day record counts its working days only (weekends and the region's bank holidays out, `leaveRecordDayKeys`), each inside the year; AM / PM count 0.5; records are `parseHolidayDocument` rows matched under every id the person goes by (`personIdSet` plus their operative ids) without case; approved only unless `statuses` says otherwise. The report's total equals the `taken` figure of `annualLeaveBalance` for the same year. Before 1.22.0 iOS counted every calendar day from start to end, a half day as 1, the device's January–December year and exact ids only. Web has no Holiday Report screen.

## 10. My Schedule

Every person sees their own bookings and annual leave (approved, and pending marked as pending), matched by account (`bookingIdsForAccount`, with the roster's same-email ids as `aliasIds`; leave with `leaveRecordsForAccount`, 1.9.0, which from 1.10.0 also matches leave whose `userId` is the account's email, as older iOS builds stored it) — never by name. Every My Schedule screen shows it — on web both the read-only screen and the self-booking screen admins and managers get (before 1.12.2 the self-booking screen showed no leave, while Daily Overview did). It updates live when a booking or leave changes on any platform. Hours as in §6 (1.20.2: each row shows its times and `bookingHoursLabel`, the pay rule's hours for that day, on every list — My Schedule, Schedule, the project / small works week, Daily Overview).

**Booking clash marker** (1.39.0, owner answer 11 Nov; My Schedule only, on web and iOS; `myScheduleClash.ts`). Each of the viewer's own bookings that overlaps another of their bookings shows a small triangle — red while the clash is not approved, grey once it is. It is the Warnings rule exactly (§5): `myScheduleClashMarkers({ operativeBookings, managerBookings, people, managerIds, day, decisions, canOpenWarnings, scope, viewerIds, viewerEmail })` → `{ bookingId, state: clash | approved, otherBookingIds, canOpenWarnings, title, message, actionLabel }[]` groups with `operativeClashGroups` and `managerClashGroups` (`clashManagerIds` decides which), each booking's named slot under the working hours in effect on its own day (pass the policy state), people under every id (`personIdSet`); approved is `isClashGroupApproved` / `isClashApproved` over `approvedWarningKeys` and `acceptedBookingClashes` (keys without case), so a third overlapping booking re-opens it (red). While the approvals have not loaded (`decisions` null) nothing reads as approved and "Open Warnings" is not offered; screens listen to `warningResolutions` and `acceptedBookingClashes` (§1 live rule), so an approval or dismissal elsewhere changes the marker at once. An overnight booking clashes with the next morning (§5), so a screen passes the viewer's bookings of the day before as well (web passes every loaded booking of the viewer). Tapping it opens a popup in the app's own style (never a stock alert): not approved — `MY_SCHEDULE_CLASH_TITLE` "Booking clash", the other overlapping booking(s) (day · place · times) and `MY_SCHEDULE_CLASH_OPEN_WARNINGS` "Open Warnings" only when the viewer may open Warnings, the clash is in their real warning scope (`warningScopeFor` / `isWarningRowInScope`: admins everything, managers themselves, their reports and their jobs, operatives never) and Warnings lists it now — its day is one of today's `clashScanDays` (`scanDayKeys`) and it is not dismissed (operative clusters `isClashGroupApproved` over the resolved keys, manager clashes `isWarningActive`, as Warnings filters them); a staff viewer whose clash Warnings does not list reads `MY_SCHEDULE_CLASH_NOT_IN_PERIOD` "This clash isn't in the current Warnings period." or `MY_SCHEDULE_CLASH_DISMISSED` "This clash was dismissed in Warnings."; anyone else `MY_SCHEDULE_CLASH_ASK_MANAGER` "Ask your manager to review this clash."; then Close; approved — `MY_SCHEDULE_CLASH_APPROVED_TITLE` "Clash approved", `MY_SCHEDULE_CLASH_APPROVED_MESSAGE` "This clash was already approved. The booking will have to be adjusted manually." and Close. Display only: nothing is written. Every member of the organisation reads `warningResolutions` and `acceptedBookingClashes` (§16), operatives included, so the marker is never wrongly grey for lack of a read.

## 11. Manage Users

All Manage Users changes — role, line manager, employment status and rates with effective dates, inactive flag, exclusions, invites — are online-only, sync to every platform through live listeners, and are written as partial updates of the changed fields only (`userProfileWriteFields`; a linked `operatives` / `managers` row with `operativeRowWriteFields` / `managerRowWriteFields`). Changing a person's pay from an operative or manager record writes only the pay fields of their `users/{uid}`, never the whole document. People lists use `rosterPeople` / `listedRosterUsers`; Active / Inactive / Pending is `userRosterSegment`. **Which list** (1.23.0, audit finding 9): `rosterListFor(kind, roster, claimedEmails)` with `kind` `operatives` | `managers` | `admins` — each person is on exactly one list, their level (`rosterListKind` = `permissionLevelOf`): admins (super admin, `adminAccess`, the admin role) on Admins only, never Managers; the operative level (operative mode, or the operative role) on Operatives; everyone else on Managers — the manager flag, the manager role, and a person with no level flag at all, who already reads as manager level for their toggles, the line-manager picker and Change user type. A `managers` row never moves a person between lists and never sets `manager: true` on a person (1.23.1, owner decision: web's roster no longer does; iOS never did). One document per email (`rosterPeople`), sorted by name. **Every user has a level** (1.23.1, owner decision): every create path writes one — Add User and re-invite (refused without one), accepting an invitation (the invitation's stored level), guided setup and sign-up (admin). A person with none (`userMissingLevel`: no `isSuperAdmin`, `adminAccess`, `manager` or `operativeMode` stored true and not an admin by role) is a data fault: Settings → Data health lists them (`usersMissingLevel`) with `USER_MISSING_LEVEL_MESSAGE` ("Has no user level: open in Manage Users and pick one") and a link to each person, where Change user type sets the level. Until then they read as manager level, are listed on Managers, and their clashes stay operative (High) clashes (owner decisions 1A, 2A). Before 1.23.0 web listed a person with no flag on Operatives and admins by role on Managers; iOS listed a person with no flag nowhere unless a `managers` row placed them on Managers. A save that leaves an operative or manager with no active line manager is refused with "Set a line manager — this person's timesheets and annual leave need approval." (`lineManagerRequiredError`, §8) — the self-book permission does not waive it. **1.28.0:** an admin may have none only with their self-manage toggles satisfied (Annual Leave Management and Timesheet self-sign on; PAYE: Annual Leave Management); otherwise the save is refused with the admin message, which says to set a line manager or turn on both toggles. The Super Admin never needs one. Admins see the people already in that state (`usersMissingLineManager`, Manage Users and Settings → Data health). **Admin permission rows** (1.28.0, `permissionToggleRows`): an admin's rows are all on and locked except the two self-manage toggles, Annual Leave Management and Timesheet self-sign (`ADMIN_EDITABLE_PERMISSION_FIELDS`); the Super Admin's two are on and locked; a PAYE admin's Timesheet self-sign is greyed out; the viewer's own two are never editable; Add User starts both off (`addUserPermissionDefaults`). A self-employed person needs a day or hourly rate above 0 (`payRateRequiredError`, §8): Add User and Edit User refuse the save with "Set a day rate or hourly rate — self-employed people are paid from it." on the rate field, and admins see an amber list of self-employed people without a rate (`usersMissingPayRate`). The line manager, `hasNoLineManager`, the self-book flag and (1.28.0) Timesheet self-sign are written by admins (operatives managers for operatives, never Timesheet self-sign) only. The live roster is the `users` query on `organizationId` (§1). A user may not change their own rates, pay basis, employment type, active flag, line manager or permissions — enforced by security rules. **Roles & permissions** opens Manage Users directly.

**Permission toggles by user level** (1.11.0, owner decisions round 3). The toggles in effect are `effectivePermissions(user)`; every permission check on every platform and in the security rules reads them, never the stored flag. The level is `permissionLevelOf(user)` (admin for a super admin, `adminAccess` or the admin role; else operative for operative mode or the operative role; else manager).
- **Admins and super admins:** every `USER_PERMISSION_FIELDS` toggle reads on and is locked (`lockedPermissionFields`) — Admin Access and Operatives included — except **Annual Leave Management** (`annualLeaveSelfBook`), the only toggle an admin can change (`ADMIN_EDITABLE_PERMISSION_FIELDS`). `operativeMode` reads off and is not shown. The level is changed only with "Change user type". The annual leave allowance fields (`annualLeaveEnabled`, days per year, leave year, carry-over) are not permission toggles and stay editable.
- **Managers:** the stored toggles, editable as before, except **Materials** and **Site audit**, always on and locked (`MANAGER_LOCKED_ON_PERMISSION_FIELDS`; both apps already saved them on). **Wholesalers** stays an editable toggle: a manager can have it off. **Operatives** off: they still schedule live active operatives onto the jobs they manage (`canScheduleOperatives`, `canWriteBooking`) but cannot add or manage operatives (`canManageOperatives`). **Projects** and **Small works** (1.42.0, owner decision 13 Nov, option B) are add-and-edit permissions, never view permissions — every manager sees every project and small works job (§17): with the toggle a manager adds jobs of that kind, edits any job of that kind and chooses its managers (`canCreateJob`, `canEditJob`, `canChangeJobManagers`); without it they still see every job and edit the details of the jobs they manage, but add none and change no manager list; bookings are `canWriteBooking` either way (the jobs they manage). The rows read `PROJECTS_TOGGLE_LABEL` "Projects — add & edit" / `PROJECTS_TOGGLE_HELP` and `SMALL_WORKS_TOGGLE_LABEL` "Small Works — add & edit" / `SMALL_WORKS_TOGGLE_HELP` (`PERMISSION_TOGGLE_CATALOGUE`); both apps show this text and keep no copy of their own. Before 1.42.0 the help still said a manager without the toggle sees only the jobs they manage (true only before 1.33.0). **Materials catalogue** (1.33.0, owner decision 4 Nov): adding, editing and deleting catalogue items and prices is **admins only** (`canManageMaterialCatalogue`: admins and super admins outside operative mode) on both apps; managers keep the Materials toggle (job material lists, quotes and orders, `canSendMaterialOrders`) and still pick from the catalogue when adding material to a job, but no longer open the catalogue page.
- **Operatives:** Materials and Site audit, editable.
- Existing user documents are never rewritten for this: locked values are derived when read (`parseUserDocument.permissions` is `effectivePermissions`), and `userProfileWriteFields` never writes a locked toggle (`keepStoredLockedPermissions`: top-level, dotted and `permissions` map entries keep their stored value), except when "Change user type" changes the level.
- **One set of rows** for Add User, its summary and Edit User on both apps: `permissionToggleRows({ level, user?, mode: 'add' | 'edit' | 'summary' })` → ordered `{ field, label, help, visible, editable, value }` (`PERMISSION_TOGGLE_CATALOGUE`). Admin: every row shown on and greyed out except Annual Leave Management. Manager: every row but Admin Access; Materials and Site audit on and greyed out. Operative: Materials and Site audit. Add User starts from `addUserPermissionDefaults(level)` (1.14.0, owner decision): **every toggle the adder can change starts off** and the adder turns them on one by one; locked toggles keep their locked value (admin and super admin: every toggle on and locked except Annual Leave Management, which starts off; manager: Materials and Site audit on and locked; operative: Materials and Site audit both start off). This applies to new users only — changing an existing person's user type keeps every saved toggle (`accountTypePermissions`, 1.15.0) and never applies these defaults.

**Change user type** (1.11.0) is `accountTypeChangeWrites({ person, kind, organizationId, roster, permissions? })`. **A type change keeps every saved toggle** (1.15.0, owner decisions round 6): `accountTypePermissions(kind, previous)` returns the person's stored toggles (top level, else `permissions.{key}`; a toggle never stored keeps the value it reads as) and changes only the level flags (`adminAccess`, `operativeMode`, `manager`); the screen's `permissions` are taken only for the toggles the new level can change (`accountTypeWritePermissions`: an admin only Annual Leave Management). An admin still reads every toggle on and locked except Annual Leave Management (`effectivePermissions`); the stored values stay untouched underneath and read again after a demotion, so manager → admin → manager returns the exact toggles and operative → manager keeps the operative's. (Before 1.15.0 a change kept Projects, Small Works, Weekly Report and Daily Overview and preset the rest.) One batch writes every document that decides the type in this organisation — `users/{uid}` (role, level flags and every toggle, top level and `permissions.{key}`), same-email users documents of this organisation, `organizations/{orgId}.members.{uid}` and `users/{uid}/orgMemberships/{orgId}` (role and nested permissions). When the person's `users/{uid}.organizationId` is another company, that profile is not rewritten: only `members.{uid}` and the membership here are written (their level here is read from them, `organizationLevelFor`) and the screen shows `ACCOUNT_TYPE_OTHER_COMPANY_NOTE`. It is refused with `ACCOUNT_TYPE_SUPER_ADMIN_MESSAGE` for a super admin who would stop being an admin. The security rules allow these writes to an admin or super admin of the organisation only. The plan is written as **one atomic batch** (1.12.2) — never the users document first and the rest after — so a refusal leaves nothing half applied; an admin may read the person's membership (`users/{uid}/orgMemberships/{orgId}` get) to build it. A type that is not the same on the users document of this organisation and `members.{uid}` (or the membership) is **half applied** (`accountTypeSyncState`: an older iOS build wrote only `users/{uid}`, or a save stopped half way); the users document is the source, Manage Users lists those people and Edit User says `ACCOUNT_TYPE_OUT_OF_SYNC_MESSAGE`, and the next Save re-applies `accountTypeChangeWrites` with that type. One save runs at a time (a second press is ignored) and a saved profile returns to the people list. A profile save syncs names to same-email users documents of this organisation only (a users query on email alone is never allowed by the rules).

**Job managers** (1.30.3, owner bug 4 Nov). The manager picker on a project or small works — create and edit, on every platform — lists `jobManagerCandidates(roster)` and takes **as many managers as are picked** (1.42.0, owner decision 13 Nov: several managers per project or small works — on create, and on edit by adding and removing; each pick is added to the list, the same person never twice, saved as `managerIds` with `managerId` the first and `managerUserIds` beside them through `jobWriteFields`; no new field): the active admins, super admins and managers, one per person (rows that are one person by `personIdSet` fold together and the active, signed-up users document speaks for them, as in `lineManagerCandidates`; level read from that row, shown as Admin / Manager, `JOB_MANAGER_LEVEL_LABELS`), never an operative, an inactive person, an invite not accepted yet (`passwordSet` false or status `pending`) or an anonymised deleted profile; with none, `JOB_MANAGER_NO_CANDIDATES_MESSAGE`. The roster passed is every users document of the organisation, **not** the email fold (`rosterPeople` keeps an admin document first, so a stale admin copy left by repeated Change user type saves stood in for a live manager and hid them on web, while iOS listed them). Each manager stored on a job (`jobManagerIds`) reads through `jobManagerAssignments(job, roster, catalogue)` → `{ storedId, state, candidate, name, reason, note }`: `current` (a candidate; matched by any of their ids without case or `user:` prefix, or a `managers` catalogue row — its id or `stableManagerId` — on their email), `former` (a known person who is no longer a candidate — `not_manager`, `inactive`, `pending`, `deleted` — or a catalogue row with no users document, `no_account`; the picker shows them with `JOB_MANAGER_FORMER_NOTES[reason]`, e.g. "Former manager — no longer a manager", and a remove action, never silently), or `unknown` (nothing loaded matches; kept so a save never drops an assignment). Job screens name only current managers as the job's managers. Change user type updates every users document of the organisation on the person's email (`accountTypeChangeWrites` with the unfolded roster), so the copies cannot disagree again.

**People and access** (1.31.0, access audit and owner decisions 4 Nov; `peopleAccess.ts`).
- **No rights carry over on a level change.** An operative is nobody's line manager: `lineManagerIds` (and so `leaveApproverIds`, `canDecideLeave`, `canViewPersonPay`, `canViewPayeClaims`, `userTimesheetSubjects`) skips a roster member at the operative level (`rosterMemberIsOperativeLevel`), even when an old id is still stored on the report; the security rules hold the same (`hbCanDecide`, timesheet review, `canViewPayOfUser`, `callerLineManagesUser` — overtime, price work and expense reads — and the claim-evidence storage rule).
- **A line manager is not made an operative, deactivated or deleted while anyone reports to them**: `lineManagerReports(person, roster)` (active people whose stored line manager ids name any of the person's ids, every users document of the organisation) and `lineManagerChangeBlock({ person, roster, change })` → `{ blocked, reports, message }` with `LINE_MANAGER_REPORTS_BLOCK_MESSAGE` ("This user is the line manager for the users below. Give each of them another line manager first, then try again."); the screen lists the reports and refuses the save. Admin ↔ manager never blocks.
- **Job managers on a level change**: admin ↔ manager keeps every job. Made an operative, deactivated or deleted (`changeRemovesJobManager`), the person is taken off every job they manage — `jobManagerRemovalWrites({ organizationId, person, change, jobs, roster, managers })`: every id of the person (`personIdSet`, the `user:` form, `managers` rows on their email by id and `stableManagerId`) out of `managerIds`, `managerId`, `managerUserIds` and `assignedManagerUserIds`; a list left empty is removed and `managerId` moves to the next manager or is removed. The job stays, with no manager when none is left, and the admins get `job_manager_removed` (company activity, `jobManagerRemovedNoticeText`). **A new project or small works needs a manager** (`jobManagerRequiredError`, `JOB_MANAGER_REQUIRED_MESSAGE` "Please assign at least one manager"; the security rules refuse a create without one).
- **Every Manage Users write reaches every users document of the person** in this organisation (the unfolded roster, not the email fold): Change user type (`accountTypeChangeWrites` — the person's document gets only the fields whose stored value differs, so a type change never writes back a toggle another admin changed since; same-email documents get the level flags and role; a same-email document already listed in `members` gets the new role there too, never a new entry; leaving admin clears `hasNoLineManager`), deactivate / reactivate (`userActiveWrites`: `isActive` on each users document of this organisation, `accountActive` on the membership; 1.33.0: the person's own users document is skipped when it names another company — they are deactivated here through the membership — and the membership is only ever updated, never created: `membershipExists: false` leaves it out, and the apps update it rather than merge-set it, so no bare `{ accountActive }` membership is made), and the Super Admin transfer (`superAdminTransferWrites`). `rosterListFor` folds before it classifies (one list per person; a live admin document wins the fold, never a stale admin copy), so the copy a list shows is the one the type change wrote.
- **One flag reader**: `userPermissionFlag(user, key)` — the top-level value when stored, else `permissions.{key}` — for every toggle and self-manage rule (`effectivePermissions`, `selfManageToggles`, `canSelfBookAnnualLeave`); firestore.rules `staffFlag` reads the same way. Before 1.31.0 the self-manage rules and the security rules counted a flag on when it was on in either place.
- **Change Super Admin** is one batch, `superAdminTransferWrites({ organizationId, fromUserId, toUserId, previousCreatorUserId, roster })`: the organisation's `creatorUserId` and `members.{to}` admin; the new Super Admin `isSuperAdmin` with both self-manage toggles on (their card shows Annual Leave Management and Timesheet self-sign on and greyed out, `SUPER_ADMIN_SELF_MANAGE_MESSAGE`); every outgoing Super Admin (and the previous creator) `isSuperAdmin` false, admin — and, when they have no active line manager, both self-manage toggles written on, so they keep the self-approval they had (owner to confirm; the alternative was to refuse the transfer until they had a line manager). Their memberships here are written in the same batch.
- **A demoted admin keeps Annual Leave Management** as they had it (`accountTypePermissions`; a Super Admin's, never stored, reads on). **Wholesalers** stays visible and editable for managers (`permissionToggleRows`).
- **Line manager set once, then locked** (1.33.0, owner decision 6 Nov): `canChangeLineManager(actor, target, { isCreate, invitation })`. Admins and super admins change anyone's line manager at any time. A manager with the Operatives permission sets an operative's line manager only when adding them (`isCreate`: Add User, where a line manager is required, `lineManagerRequiredError`) or on an invitation they sent that is not accepted yet (`invitation` `{ accepted, invitedBy }`); after that only an admin changes it (deleting and re-adding the operative is the accepted way round). Managers without the toggle, operatives and the person never do. A save that changes it (`lineManagerChanged(before, after)`: the stored ids without case and order, or `hasNoLineManager`) by someone who may not is refused with `LINE_MANAGER_LOCKED_MESSAGE` ("Only an admin can change the line manager once this person has been added."); Edit User shows the picker read-only to them. `LINE_MANAGER_FIELDS` = `assignedManagerUserIds`, `assignedManagerUserId`, `hasNoLineManager`.
- **"No line manager" is for admins only**: the security rules refuse setting `hasNoLineManager` on anyone who is not an admin (resending a stored true passes); operatives managers never set it.
- **Operatives managers** (owner decision 7): a manager with the Operatives permission adds, edits, deactivates and deletes operatives — operative level only, with or without a login — never a manager, an admin, themselves, or their own pay. Delete is `canDeleteUser` / `userDeleteRefusal` with `actorManagesOperatives` (`operatives_only` otherwise). The security rules allow them: the operative's users document (names, contact, trade, active flag, Materials / Site audit, leave and pay — the person stays an operative; the line manager only as below, never a privilege or a self-manage toggle); the delete plan's documents (users document, tax ids, membership, email claim, the one `members` entry with value `operative`, the operatives row, and — once the users document is gone — the person's labour bookings **dated after today** in the organisation zone, each with its tombstone (`operativesManagerMayDeleteBooking`; owner decision: today's and past bookings the delete leaves as they are, as history; 1.33.0: there is no cancel, and the Operatives permission adds no booking power — only an admin or the job's manager deletes those, `canDeleteBooking`). Admins are unchanged (today on, `bookingsToRemoveOnOperativeDelete`). Because of the rules' document-lookup budget, their delete runs the account step as separate writes in the plan's order (tax ids, membership, email claim, then the users document, then the members entry); an admin's stays one batch. Memberships are admin writes, so their deactivate writes the users documents only. The admins get `operative_created` and `operative_deleted` (company activity).
- **Atomic saves**: Edit User writes the profile, its pay history and pay profile, the email claim, the same-email names, the linked `operatives` / `managers` row names and the `operativeProfiles` mirror in one batch; Add User writes the invitation, the placeholder and the email claim in one batch (the pay profile and tax ids follow, as their rules read the placeholder).
- **Exported timesheets** (owner decision; §8): the Exported list and its hub count show `exportedTimesheetHistorySubjects({ viewer, members })` → `{ userId, own }[]` — everyone on User Timesheets (`userTimesheetSubjects`, unchanged: nobody reviews their own sheet), **the viewer's own exported sheets** under any of their ids (`own: true`, read-only — `timesheetWriterIsReviewer` is unchanged), and **inactive or anonymised people in scope** (admins: every timesheet user; managers: the people they line-manage). Before 1.31.0 a reviewer's own exported sheets vanished from the list (a PAYE admin's showed nowhere) and inactive people's exported sheets never showed. A sheet stored without `userId` is matched by its document id (`timesheetDocumentSubjectId`), never dropped. The security rules' reads are unchanged (the person, their line manager and admins already read these sheets).
- **Unbooked labour on web** reads the roster fold's same-email ids (`userIdAliases`) as the person's (`labourPersonFromUser`), as iOS already did.
- **PAYE price work, expenses and their reports and evidence (§26)** follow the same people rules: a demoted manager stops reading a report's claims and evidence (`canViewPayeClaims` over `lineManagerIds`; rules `callerLineManagesUser` and the storage rule); `priceWorkApproverCandidates` never offers an operative (unchanged); claims match the person under every id (`personIdSet`); deactivating the person stops their approvals (inactive members never count); an operatives manager gains no claim rights; the price-work job number rule is unchanged.

**Ghost operatives** (1.34.0, owner design 6 Nov "ghost: all recommended", answers rounds 1–2; `lib/canonical/ghostOperatives.ts`). A ghost is a bookable operative with **no app login** — no email and no phone — so managers know where someone without the app is and the weekly report shows their cost. It is a `users/{ghostId}` document of the organisation with `ghost: true` (`isGhostUser`: the boolean `true` only, as the security rules test it), `passwordSet: false`, `email: ''`, the operative level (`role` operative, `operativeMode`, `ghostPermissions` — every toggle the adder could change off), always self-employed (`employmentType: 'self_employed'`, `timesheetsEnabled: false`), one pay basis with a rate above 0 and at least one line manager; no `userEmails` claim, invitation, membership or `members` entry, no `isSuperAdmin` key. It reuses every users-document rule (line manager, leave deciders, pay, the delete plan, notices).
- **Who adds one:** admins and managers with the Operatives toggle (`canCreateGhost` = `canManageOperatives`); the admins are told with `operative_created` (company activity, §13) and, on delete, `operative_deleted`. **Add user / Add new operative** (both apps) opens with a **Ghost account** toggle at the very top (`GHOST_TOGGLE_LABEL`, `GHOST_TOGGLE_HELP`) — shown only while the `ghostAccounts` switch on `platformConfig/app` is on for the organisation (owner decision 7 Nov "1b", §18; `ghostToggleVisible({ canCreateGhost, ghostAccountsEnabled })`: the permission and the switch) — and a small expandable explanation (`GHOST_OPERATIVE_EXPLANATION`); with it on, only the fields a ghost needs stay (`ghostAddFormRows({ ghost, canCreateGhost, ghostAccountsEnabled })`; with the switch off or not passed the toggle is hidden and the form is the ordinary invite form): first name and surname, line manager and pay (required), trade (optional) and the annual-leave allowance; the account type (always operative), email, phone, permissions, tax ids, employment type and timesheets disappear. The save is `ghostCreateWrites({ organizationId, ghostId, draft, actorUserId, platform, privatePay, roster })` → `{ user, payProfile }`, refused with `ghostCreateError(draft, { roster })` (names, then the rate above 0, then a line manager from `lineManagerCandidates`; an email or phone is refused). While `privatePay` is on the pay goes to `payProfiles/{ghostId}` (`sharedDocumentPayWrite`). The old web "Create New Operative" page (an `operatives` row with no login) redirects to Add user with the toggle on.
- **Everywhere else:** a ghost is never Pending and never a leftover invite (`rosterSegment` Active / Inactive, `isIdentifiablePendingLeftover` false); it is on the Operatives list with the badge `GHOST_BADGE_LABEL` "No app login" (`ghostBadgeLabel`; "No app login · Invited" once an admin gave app access). It is bookable on every booking screen and counts as signed up for unbooked labour (§5), Daily Overview and Book labour; every picker folds people on `personDedupeKey` (the email, else `id:` + the id) — before 1.34.0 several pickers folded on the email alone, so only the first person with no email was offered. Its leave is booked by its line manager or an admin, saved approved, nobody notified (`canBookLeaveFor`, `ghostLeaveBooking`, §9). It never gets a login, notifications (§13), a timesheet (§8), overtime, price work, expenses (§25, §26), My Schedule, toolbox talks or deadlines (J2; task pickers list `operatives` catalogue rows, so a converted ghost's row is still offered there — a task notice still never reaches it). The Payrun Snapshot (§7) lists it with a grey Timesheet cell "No app login" (`PAYRUN_SNAPSHOT_GHOST_CELL`). Edit User hides email, phone, last active, employment type, timesheets, password and Change user type. Deleting a ghost runs the ordinary plan (`userDeleteWrites`): its users document, the tax ids and membership deletes (no-ops when missing), the `members` entry only when listed (a ghost has none) and a converted row's `operatives` row — the plan never skips a document because of `ghost` (review fix: anyone could otherwise mark themself a ghost to keep their company access through a delete). Unbooked labour reads an `operatives` row whose id is one of a person's ids as that person, never a second person (a converted ghost's row keeps its users id with the email cleared).
- **Line manager** (§16 R1): an Operatives manager sets it only when adding the ghost; afterwards only admins change it (the 1.33.0 unaccepted-invitation exception never applies to a ghost). Being a ghost's line manager gives no overtime booking write on it (§16 R2).
- **Give app access** (owner decision round 2, option 1; admins only): `canUpgradeGhost(viewer, ghost)`, `ghostUpgradeRefusal(viewer, ghost, email)` (`GHOST_UPGRADE_REFUSAL_MESSAGES`), `ghostUpgradeWrites(...)` → one batch: the ghost's `email` (and `inviteEmailHint` removed), an invitation for the operative level with its line managers and pay (`invitationCreateFields`) and the email claim naming the ghost; the email must be free as for Add User (`resolveInviteEmailClaim`). The ghost stays a ghost — bookable and listed, "No app login · Invited" (Q-G3) — until the person accepts; accepting (the existing flow, `inviteAcceptWrites`) copies the placeholder onto their account without `ghost` / `inviteEmailHint` (`dropPlaceholderFields`, `inviteAcceptPlaceholderFields`) and the ghost id goes on `formerUserIds`, so its bookings, leave and pay history follow.
- **Converting existing no-login rows** (Q-G2): `scripts/convert-no-login-operatives-to-ghosts.mjs` (owner-run, dry run first). `ghostConversionCandidates({ organizationId, operatives, users, memberIds })` lists active `operatives` rows with no users document of the organisation on their id, stored link or email (1.36.0: a users document counts as the organisation's when its `organizationId` is the id, a path or a reference, `documentOrganizationId`, or when it is one of `memberIds` — the `members` keys and the creator, the anchored people of §1 — so a member from another company is never converted; the script reads those documents too and writes no `updatedBy` / `updatedFrom`, having no signed-in user) (one per email; other rows on that email are `duplicateOperativeIds`; `issues` name, rate). The dry run writes the owner's check list (CSV + Markdown) with the line managers to fill; `--apply --line-managers=<file>` writes `ghostConversionWrites` per filled row: `users/{rowId}` (the row keeps its id, so bookings, leave and pay history stay attached) with the row's email kept aside as `inviteEmailHint`, duplicates on `formerUserIds`, the row linked (`linkedUserId`) with its email cleared, duplicates linked and made inactive. Settings → Data health lists `ghostsToCheck(roster)` (missing name, rate or active line manager, and every converted ghost).

**Line managers on Manage Users** (1.11.0): the picker lists `lineManagerCandidates(person, roster)` — the active admins, super admins and managers, never the person under any of their ids, never a pending invite or a deleted profile, by name; **one option per person** (1.14.0): roster rows that are the same person by `personIdSet` (two users documents on one email, an id in another letter case, a former invite id) are one option whose value is the person's live users document (active, signed up, not listed as another row's alias), and the level and status are read from that row; the selected values are `lineManagerSelectedIds(person, roster)` — a stored id that names the person's other document (or another letter case) shows that one option as selected; "No line manager" is offered only when `lineManagerOptions(person).allowsNoLineManager` (`lineManagerRequired` false: admins); the row reads `lineManagerSummary(person, roster)` → `{ label, ids: [{ id, name }] }` — each stored id resolved on the roster (an unknown id reads "Unknown person", never dropped), the label the first name with "+N more" only for N ≥ 1, "Not set" when none is stored, "No line manager" when chosen. **Changing the account type on Add User** (1.24.0): the picks become `lineManagerSelectionAfterLevelChange(picks, newLevel, roster, person?)` → `{ lineManagerIds, hasNoLineManager, dropped }` — each picked id that is still a `lineManagerCandidates` row is kept (as that candidate's id, once per person, in picked order) and the rest are dropped; "No line manager" is kept only when the new level allows it (admins, `lineManagerOptions`). Who may be picked does not depend on the level, so a type change keeps valid picks and drops "No line manager" when leaving admin. Picking the same type again changes nothing. The save is still checked by `lineManagerRequiredError`. **The invitee is never their own line manager** (1.27.0, owner decision 1): Add User passes the invite as it will be saved — `inviteDraftLineManagerSubject({ email, level })` → `{ id: INVITE_DRAFT_PERSON_ID ('invite-draft'), email, role, … }` — as `person` to `lineManagerCandidates`, `lineManagerSelectionAfterLevelChange` and `lineManagerRequiredError`, so the roster rows on the typed email are never offered or counted as the invitee's own line manager. Before 1.27.0 web passed no person (iOS already passed the draft). **One level for every roster shape** (1.44.0): a roster row's level is `permissionLevelOf` whether its `role` is the stored string, the level flags, or the resolved `StaffAccountRole` object (web `rulebookRoster` / `staffAccountRole`, iOS `RulebookPeople.person` / `LineManagerRulebook.roster`) — admin for the admin or super admin flag, operative only for operative mode or the operative role, everyone else manager level, a person with no level flag included (1.23.1). Before 1.44.0 an object role without `isManager` read as operative, so a flagless legacy manager was offered by web's Add user picker (stored rows) but refused by the ghost save check (`ghostCreateError` over `rulebookRoster`: "Choose a line manager from the list"), was missing from every iOS line-manager picker (Add user, Edit user, the ghost form) and from the job manager picker given resolved roles, while `lineManagerIds` / `lineManagerRequiredError` already counted them as a line manager.

**Duplicate names** (1.36.0, owner decision 10 Nov). A person is identified by their email (their login), never their name (§8 `personIdSet`), so two people may share a name. Add User / Add new operative — the Ghost account path included — asks `usersWithSameName(roster, { firstName, lastName }, { claimedEmails })` before it saves: the people of the organisation whose first and last name match, trimmed, without case and with inner runs of spaces read as one (a blank first or last name matches nobody), one per person (rows that are one person by `personIdSet` count once; an active row speaks for them) → `{ id, displayName, email, level, active, segment, ghost }[]`. Active, inactive and pending people and ghosts count; anonymised deleted profiles (`isAnonymisedDeletedProfile`) and leftover invites whose email claim is gone (`isIdentifiablePendingLeftover`) do not. When it returns anyone, the app shows `DUPLICATE_NAME_WARNING_TITLE` and `DUPLICATE_NAME_WARNING_MESSAGE` ("A user with this name already exists. Are you sure you want to add this user?"), lists the matching people with `sameNamePersonContactLabel` (their email; else "No app login", `GHOST_BADGE_LABEL`, for a ghost only; else `DUPLICATE_NAME_NO_EMAIL_LABEL` "No email"), and offers `DUPLICATE_NAME_CONFIRM_LABEL` ("Add anyway": the save goes on unchanged) and `DUPLICATE_NAME_CANCEL_LABEL` ("Cancel": back to the form). A warning only, never a block, and no security rule.

**Accepting an invitation** (1.10.0; 1.11.0 below) is `inviteAcceptWrites({ existingProfile, placeholder, invitation })`: the role comes from the invitation (its `role`, else the kind its permissions give), never from another company's profile; with no account yet, `users/{authUid}` is written (from the placeholder when the claim names this invite's) and only then is the placeholder moved and removed and the claim pointed at the account; an account that already exists in another company keeps its profile, the placeholder stays (the in-organisation row with the invited pay) and its role there goes on its membership. Add User cannot tell that an email already has an account in another company (no account lookup by email). **1.11.0 (owner decisions round 3):** accepting applies exactly what the invitation stores, nothing more — the invitation is the only source of the person's access in that organisation. Add User stores the level as `role` on the invitation (`invitationCreateFields`) beside its permissions, pay and line manager, and writes the same on the placeholder. A new account gets `invitationProfileFields(invitation)` (role, permissions, employment type, one pay basis, line manager). An account in another company accepts with one batch, `inviteMembershipWrites({ invitation, invitationId, authUid, now })`: the membership created with the invitation's role (`invitationRole`) and `permissions` exactly as stored plus `invitationId`, `members.{uid}` = that role, and the invitation marked used; the placeholder keeps the invited pay and line manager. The security rules accept it only for a genuine, unused invitation to the signed-in email, marked used in the same batch (§16). **A new account** (1.31.1, F5) is accepted the same way: `users/{authUid}` carries the invitation's `invitationId`, its membership (`inviteMembershipWrites` shape) is written and the invitation is marked used, all in the same batch; the claim move, the placeholder's removal and its tax ids then need that account (§16). **Who may invite** (1.12.0): admins invite any level; a manager with operatives access creates, changes and holds only an operative-only invitation (`invitationIsOperativeOnly`: `invitationRole` operative, operative mode on, no admin, super admin, manager or operatives toggle at the top level or in `permissions`) — the same test as their operative placeholder on `users/{uid}`. The rules refuse any other; an app can call `invitationIsOperativeOnly` before writing to show the reason.

**Deleting a person** (1.10.0). `canDeleteUser(actor, target, { roster, creatorUserId })` (`userDeleteRefusal` gives the reason, `USER_DELETE_REFUSAL_MESSAGES`) is the same test as the security rules: only an admin or super admin outside operative mode deletes; never themselves, the super admin or the organisation creator; an admin who is not super admin deletes only people who are not admins. Both apps check it before any write and show Delete only when it passes. The delete is one ordered plan, `userDeleteWrites({ orgId, user, actorUserId, platform, bookings, managerSiteBookings, operativeRows, managerRows, invitations, nowIso, tz })`: (1) one batch deleting `users/{uid}`, `users/{uid}/private/taxIds`, `users/{uid}/orgMemberships/{orgId}`, `organizations/{orgId}/userEmails/{email}` and `members.{uid}` — a refusal there changes nothing; (2) their labour and manager site bookings from today on, each with its tombstone; (3) their `operatives` / `managers` rows (by id or email); (4) their pending invitations in the organisation. Past bookings and their `bookedBy` names are never rewritten.

**Delete my account** (1.15.0, owner decisions round 6; web now, iOS later) is decided per organisation by `accountDeletionPath(viewer, roster, org)`:
- `handover_required` — the person is the organisation's super admin (`isOrganizationSuperAdmin`: its creator, or a users document of it flagged `isSuperAdmin`) and another active member is listed (`rosterSegment` active, not the person under any of their ids). They first make another admin the super admin with the existing ownership transfer (`creatorUserId`, Change Super Admin): the app offers the active admins (web `superAdminSuccessors`) or makes an active member an admin first (`accountTypeChangeWrites`) and then transfers. The account is not deleted until then (`ACCOUNT_HANDOVER_REQUIRED_MESSAGE`).
- `delete_with_company` — the super admin is the only active member: deleting the account deletes the company. They type the company name and DELETE (`organizationDeleteConfirmationError`, `ACCOUNT_DELETE_WITH_COMPANY_MESSAGE`); the company enters the 30-day scheduled state of §20 with `restoreBy: 'platform_owner'` (nobody is left to restore it; only the platform owner can) and the account is deleted straight away.
- `delete` — everyone else, and an organisation already scheduled for erasure: the account is deleted as before (company records stay; the name becomes "Deleted user").
Web `/api/account/delete` answers 409 `handover_required` or `company_delete_required` instead of the old `sole_admin`.

**Bookings not linked to a person** (1.44.0, owner decision 14 Nov, question H option 1: "make it very clear and easy to sort in data health"). Settings → Data health (admins) lists every stored person id on a labour or manager site booking that no one in the company resolves to (`unresolvedBookingPeople({ accounts, operatives, bookings })` → one row per id, without case: `{ personId, idHint, count, firstDayKey, lastDayKey, jobs: [{ jobId, jobName, count }], bookedBy, bookingIds, collections }`, most bookings first), with help text (`UNRESOLVED_BOOKINGS_HELP`) and one action per id, **Link to a person…** (`LINK_BOOKING_PERSON_ACTION`). The picker is `linkBookingPersonCandidates(roster, organizationId, { viewerIds })` over every users document of the organisation (never the email fold): one option per person (`rosterPersonGroups`), the person's live, signed-up, active document speaking for them and being the one written; only Active people (`rosterSegment`: never a pending invite, an inactive person or an anonymised deleted profile; a ghost operative is Active) whose document belongs to this organisation; **never the viewer** under any of their ids — nobody writes their own identity links, admins included (§16), so the picker says `LINK_BOOKING_PERSON_SELF_NOTE` ("To link bookings to yourself, ask another admin."). Linking an id makes **everything** filed under it the person's through `personIdSet`, not only bookings — pay-rate history (`payRateOnDay`, so the pay of unsigned timesheets may change), annual leave, timesheets, overtime, price work and expenses: before the confirm the app counts them (`operativeDayRateHistory` and `holidayBookings` by `userId` / `operativeId`, `settings/timesheet_<id>_*` and sheets by `userId`, `overtimeRequests`, `priceWorkItems`, `expenseItems` by `userId`) and `linkBookingPersonImpact(filed)` → `{ parts, alsoText, requiresPayAck }` words them ("Also linked: 2 pay-rate history rows, 1 leave record. Pay for unsigned timesheets may change."); when a pay-rate history row is filed under the id the Link button waits for the tick `LINK_BOOKING_PERSON_PAY_ACK_LABEL` ("I understand pay may change"). After the confirm `linkBookingPersonConfirmMessage` ("All 3 bookings under this ID will show as Farnie Nel.", followed by `alsoText`) the app reads the organisation's users documents and `operatives` rows fresh and, in **one transaction** that re-reads the chosen person's users document, asks `linkBookingPersonRefusal({ personId, targetId, roster, operatives, organizationId, viewerIds })` — refused (with its message) when the id now resolves to someone (`scheduleRowPerson`), is already on any users document's `formerUserIds` / `sameEmailUserIds`, or the person is no longer a candidate — and otherwise writes `linkBookingPersonWriteFields`: an `arrayUnion` of the id onto that person's `formerUserIds` plus write metadata (a partial update; stored ids never rewritten; an admin identity-link write, §16). Success shows `linkBookingPersonDoneMessage` ("Linked: 3 bookings now show as Farnie Nel.") only when that write happened, else `LINK_BOOKING_PERSON_ALREADY_MESSAGE` ("Already linked."). Every booking under the id then resolves to that person everywhere (`resolveBookingPerson`, `personIdSet`), on both apps; nothing on the bookings changes. Bookings store no name of the booked person: the row shows the id, its jobs, its dates and who booked it (`bookedBy`).

## 12. My Profile

Matches on every platform and updates live: profile photo (`profilePhotoURL`, with `profilePhotoUpdatedAt` set on every upload so other devices refresh the cached avatar), contact details, day/hourly rate and employment status (read-only), and billing details — VAT number and UTR — stored privately at `users/{uid}/private/taxIds` (`sensitiveProfile`). A photo upload stores a fresh download URL; a stale profile copy never removes or rolls back the photo. **Delete my account** (1.15.0) is on My Profile / Settings; what it does is `accountDeletionPath` (§11): a super admin with other active members hands super admin over first, a super admin alone in the organisation deletes the company with the account (§20), everyone else deletes only their account.

**Personal figures** (1.35.0, owner decisions 7 Nov). My Profile on every platform shows a "Your figures" card for the signed-in person, from `myProfileSummary({ nowMs, timeZone, viewer, user, roster, operatives, managers, leaveDefaults, holidays, nonWorkingDayKeys, tasks, bookings, managerSiteBookings, policyState })`, which only composes existing rules:
- **Annual leave left this leave year** — `annualLeaveBalance` over the person's records (`leaveRecordsForAccount`: every id they go by, same-email ids, catalogue rows on their email and the email itself), with the region's bank holidays for `myProfileBankHolidayRange` (below); the figure is `remaining`, which already takes pending requests off (Q4). With no allowance (`hasAllowance` false) the card shows "Days taken this leave year: N" (`usedThisYear`).
- **Hours booked this week** — the Monday–Sunday organisation week containing today (`organizationWeekDayKeys`, Q2), days later in the week included; each day is `bookedWorkedHours` over the person's labour bookings that occupy the day (`bookingOccupiesDay`) and manager site bookings, matched under every id (`personIdSet`, `bookingIdsForAccount`), under the working hours in effect that day (`payrollTimePolicyForDayFromState`). These are worked hours (Q1): clock time less the unpaid break, never multiplied, overlapping bookings once — `payrollHoursResult.workedHours`, with overlapping bookings merged (`mergeMinuteIntervals`) and priced once. Unlike `managerCoverageHours.paidHours` (§6) a block that spans the standard day is its real hours, not the weekday paid day. (Daily Overview's row labels show paid hours with the multiplier since 1.20.2, §6; the figure here is the hours worked.)
- **Open tasks** — tasks assigned to any of the person's ids (`tasksAssignedToViewer`, the Home rule, §23) that are not completed, tasks with no due date included (Q3); underneath, the number overdue (`isTaskOverdue`) and due this week (`homeOverviewMetrics.tasksDueThisWeek`, today and the next six days).
- **Qualifications expiring soon** — `qualificationExpiryCount` on the person's own `operatives` rows (by id or email): expired and expiring within a calendar month; "None" with no row or nothing due.
- **Line manager** — `lineManagerSummary`; every line manager is listed by name (Q5), never "First +N more"; "No line manager", "Not set" and "Unknown person" as §11.
Each figure links to its screen (Annual leave, My Schedule, Tasks, qualifications, the line manager's profile when the viewer may open it). The rest of My Profile and Delete my account are unchanged.

**Bank holidays for a leave balance** (1.35.0, §4.4, §9). `annualLeaveBalance` needs the region's bank holidays from the first day of the **previous** leave year (carry-over counts the leave taken in it, and a bank holiday inside a multi-day record is not leave there either) to the last day of the current one: `annualLeaveBankHolidayRange({ annualLeaveEnabled, startMonth, endMonth, orgStartMonth, orgEndMonth, onDayKey, timeZone })` → `{ startDayKey, endDayKey, years }` (the balance's own year inputs; `myProfileBankHolidayRange` takes My Profile's `user` and `leaveDefaults`). Every screen that shows a balance — My Profile, Annual leave and Edit User on web, My Profile and the leave screens on iOS — loads bank holidays for `years` and shows no figure until they have loaded (iOS offline: after a short wait, with the bank holidays it already has, then again once they load), so the same person reads the same figure everywhere.

## 13. Notifications

- **My notifications** toggles are personal (`users/{uid}.notificationPreferences`), written as dotted partial updates, and sync across platforms (`parseUserNotificationPreferences`, `userNotificationPreferenceFieldPatch`).
- Which toggles a person sees depends on role and features (`visibleNotificationPreferenceKeys`). People who can see variations (`canSeeVariations`: admins and managers) have a **Variations** toggle (`variations`, on by default) covering all five variation types. Admins and super admins have a **Company activity** toggle (`companyActivity`, on by default: "People, clients, jobs added, warnings removed and annual leave adjusted.") covering the company activity types (1.40.0: `annual_leave_adjusted` too); the older per-type toggles for them (`projectCreated`, `smallWorksCreated`, `operativeCreated`, `managerCreated`, `clientCreated`, `warningDismissed`) are retired — still parsed, never shown. 1.29.0: everyone has an **Overtime** toggle (`overtime`, on by default: "Overtime requests and updates, and hours changed on bookings you manage.") covering `overtime_request_submitted`, `overtime_request_reviewed` and `booking_hours_changed_by_owner` (§25); like every toggle it stops the push only.
- A toggle turned off stops that notification type's **device push** everywhere (`notificationPreferenceAllowsDelivery`, `devicePushAllowed`; enforced by the push Cloud Function). It **never hides the inbox row**: every notice addressed to the person stays in the notification centre and the unread count, on every platform (`notificationShowsInInbox`, `NOTIFICATION_TOGGLE_HIDES_INBOX_ROW = false`). The app-icon badge counts the same unread rows.
- **Inbox-only detail** (1.40.0, review r306 #10): an inbox row may carry `detail`, text shown under its message in the notification centre and never pushed (the push sends `title` and `message` only; `notificationCreateFields` writes it when not blank, at most 300 characters; `parseNotificationDocument` returns it, null when none). Used for the reason of an annual-leave adjustment.
- **Reading inbox rows** is `parseNotificationDocument(docId, data, { role })` on every platform: the type is kept as the stored text (a type the app does not know is still listed, `known: false`), `relatedId` keeps any shape (UUID, day key, uid, job id), title / message / read flag default instead of dropping the row, and `destination` is `notificationDestination` for the role. Every app's type list (the iOS enum) accepts every type in `KNOWN_NOTIFICATION_TYPES` (the table below, the push allow-list and the legacy `material_added`); no row is dropped for its type. Marking read writes `isRead` only (`notificationReadWriteFields`).
- Types that send a device push are `DEVICE_PUSH_NOTIFICATION_TYPES`, including every variation type (`variation`, `variation_renumbered`, `variation_added`, `variation_from_tracker`, `variation_numbers_updated`).
- Organisation settings (material cut-off, schedule options) never appear in My notifications.
- Web never sends browser or OS notifications.

**Who receives each notification** (`lib/canonical/notificationRecipients.ts`). One rule per type, the same on every platform, and **never the person who caused it** (the actor) under any of their ids. Every recipient function is pure over the roster (`id`, `email`, `isActive`, `role`, `formerUserIds`, `sameEmailUserIds`, line-manager fields), drops inactive roster members, and de-duplicates ids without case. `personIdSet(person, roster)` is every id of a person (id, `formerUserIds`, `sameEmailUserIds`, roster rows with the same email); `excludeActor(ids, actor, roster)` removes them.
- Timesheets: `timesheetSubmittedRecipients(subject, roster)` — the subject's active line managers, else the active admins (never the subject); nobody when the sheet is self-signed. `timesheetSignedRecipients(subject, signer, roster)` — the subject, unless they signed. `lineManagerPeerRecipients(subject, actor, roster)` — the subject's other active line managers (no admin fallback).
- Annual leave: `holidayRequestRecipients(requester, roster)` — `leaveApproverIds` without the requester; nobody when the leave is self-booked. `holidayDecisionRecipients(requester, decider, roster)` — the requester, unless they decided.
- `bookingCreatedRecipients`, `deadlineAssignedRecipients`, `toolboxTalkRecipients`, `ideaRecipients` — the given ids without the actor.
- **Company activity** (`operative_created`, `manager_created`, `client_created`, `project_created`, `small_works_created`, `warning_removed`, `COMPANY_ACTIVITY_NOTICE_TYPES`): `companyActivityRecipients(actor, roster)` — the active admins and super admins outside operative mode, without the actor. One row per admin with `userId` and `actorUserId`; a row without `userId` is never written.
- **PAYE overtime** (1.29.0, §25): `overtimeRequestRecipients(requester, roster)` — the requester's active line managers and every active admin outside operative mode, never the requester (push; `deepLinkUserId` the requester, opens Users' → that person → Overtime, `notificationDestination` kind `overtimeUser`); `overtimeReviewedRecipients(requester, decider, roster)` — "Overtime update", the requester unless they decided (push; opens their Overtime page, kind `overtimeMine`); `bookingHoursChangedRecipients(owner, jobManagerIds, roster)` — when a PAYE manager or admin changes their own booking directly, the job's managers and their line managers, never them (inbox only; opens the booking day). `relatedId` is the request id (`overtimeRequest`) or the booking id (`booking`).
- **Push test** (`push_test`, Settings "send a test notification"): `pushTestRecipients(actor)` — the signed-in user only. It is the one type pushed to its actor (`notificationPushesToActor`); no toggle turns it off. On web its only effect is the inbox row.
- Job notices keep `materialOrderRecipients` and `receivesJobNotification`, then `excludeActor`. Neither has an "all admins" fallback: a job with no managers notifies no manager.
- **`relatedId`** (1.9.0) is the stored id of the record the notice is about, chosen per type by `NOTIFICATION_RELATED_ID` and written with `notificationRelatedId(type, ids)`: leave notices the holiday booking id; job, booking, material and project / small-works notices and `variation_renumbered` / `variation_numbers_updated` the job id; `variation` / `variation_added` / `variation_from_tracker` the variation id; `client_created` the client id; `operative_created` / `manager_created` the user id; tasks the task id; deadlines the deadline id; toolbox notices the issue id; `warning_removed` the warning key; `timesheet_submission_reminder` the payment day key; feedback notices the idea id; `line_manager_peer_update` the holiday booking id when it is about leave. Timesheet notices and `push_test` carry none (null) — timesheets are identified by `deepLinkUserId` + `deepLinkWeekStart`. Never a random id. A new row is `notificationCreateFields` (§1). 1.10.0: an app holding the one id the type names writes `notificationRelatedIdFor(type, id)` (null for a type that carries none) — the single source for every web and iOS write. `notificationDestination` opens a variation notice on the variation (`variationDetail`, relatedId the variation id) or the job's variations (`variations`, relatedId the job id); operatives get the inbox.
- **Leave notices other than a decision** (1.10.0). A manager or admin removing someone's approved leave writes `holiday_cancelled_by_manager`; a manager or admin booking approved leave for someone writes `holiday_booked_for_you`; both go to `holidayDecisionRecipients(person, actor)` (never the actor) under the annual-leave-decision toggle, and neither reuses `holiday_request_approved` / `holiday_request_declined`. Switching someone's self-book permission off writes no notice. No leave notice goes to every admin.
- Every inbox row an app writes carries `actorUserId` (the signed-in user who caused it; security rules require it to be that user when present). Scheduled notices nobody caused (`timesheet_submission_reminder`, `deadline_reminder`, `deadline_due`) leave it out. Notice dates shown in leave notices (`annualLeaveDecisionDateText` on web, `AnnualLeaveNoticeText.decisionDateText` on iOS) use the organisation business time zone. The push Cloud Function never pushes a row whose `userId` is its `actorUserId`, except a type whose push is for the actor (`notificationPushesToActor`: `push_test`).
- **The inbox is the rows addressed to the person, from their `createdAt`.** `inboxNotificationVisibleToUser(row, ids, now)` and `unreadNotificationBadgeCount(rows, ids, now)` count only rows whose `userId` is one of the person's ids; a scheduled row (`SCHEDULED_NOTIFICATION_TYPES`: `deadline_reminder`, `deadline_due`, `timesheet_submission_reminder`) only once its `createdAt` is not after now (1.10.0), any other row unless its `createdAt` is more than `INBOX_CREATED_AT_SKEW_MS` (5 minutes) after now (1.16.0); a row whose server time is not back yet is listed; the push function neither pushes nor badges a hidden row. A row with no `userId` is not an inbox row and is never listed, counted or pushed.
- **`createdAt` is server time** (1.16.0). A row due now is written with the app's SDK server timestamp, passed to `notificationCreateFields` as `serverTimestamp` (`notificationCreatedAtIsServerTime`); only a scheduled type written ahead of time keeps its own due instant. A writer's or reader's clock no longer hides a row or drops it out of the newest 150.
- **Recipients are accounts** (1.16.0).
