Every recipient function returns signed-in account ids only, one per person: each given id — an operatives catalogue id, an invite placeholder or former id, a same-email id, any letter case — goes through `notificationRecipientAccountId(id, roster, catalogue)` (the person's active users document that has signed up, not another row's former id, first in roster order) inside `excludeActor`; an id the roster cannot resolve is dropped; 1.34.0: a ghost operative (§11) is never a recipient (no inbox, no device). `bookingCreatedRecipients(bookedIds, booker, roster, catalogue)` takes the operatives rows. No app resolves recipients itself. (The inbox reads `userId == auth uid`, so a row addressed to any other id was pushed to whatever device held that document's token and never listed.)
- **Push payload** (1.16.0): `pushPayloadFields(row)` — `notificationId`, `type`, `relatedId`, `dayKey`, `catalogue`, `userId`, `organizationId` (strings, empty left out) — so a tapped push routes like the row in the centre.
- **Rows in another company** (1.16.0, interim until the owner decides on a cross-company inbox): the push carries `organizationId`; tapping a push or row of another organisation switches to it first when the person is a member (`notificationOrganizationSwitch({ rowOrganizationId, openOrganizationId, membershipIds })` with `sessionMembershipIds`), otherwise it routes in the open company. Each inbox still lists only the open organisation's rows. The push Cloud Function's app-icon badge counts the recipient's own unread rows the same way (bounded read).
- **Scheduled push types are not pushed** (1.10.0). The push Cloud Function runs when a row is created and has no schedule, so a row written ahead of time with a future `createdAt` (`timesheet_submission_reminder` written early, `deadline_reminder`, `deadline_due`) is never pushed — it appears in the inbox when its `createdAt` arrives. A row written when it is due is pushed as usual (its type's push flag).
- Timesheet notice document ids are each app's synthetic UUID of `timesheetNotificationDedupeKey` — the period start as unix seconds of midnight in the organisation zone (never the device calendar).
- **Device push tokens belong to exactly one account** (1.16.0): the last account to register it. A token is written to `users/{uid}.pushTokens` of the account signed in on that device (its Firebase Auth uid), never copied to other users documents and never to an invite placeholder users document (a UUID-shaped id, `isPushTokenPlaceholderUserId`, 1.17.0); signing out removes this device's token from every users document it was written to. The server enforces it: when a users document gains a token (`addedPushTokens`), the `pushTokenSingleOwner` Cloud Function removes it from every other users document (`pushTokenOwnerWrites`, an `array-contains` query and `arrayRemove`), so a forced or offline sign-out can no longer leave it behind. **Every iOS sign-out goes through one path** (iOS PR #166, `FirebaseBackend.signOutRemovingPushToken`, a bounded 5-second wait): a forced sign-out (expired session, trial blocked) removes this device's token wherever the app is still signed in to do so; a sign-out Firebase starts itself, when the app can no longer write, relies on the server's single-owner rule (`pushTokenSingleOwner`). `scripts/dedupe-push-tokens.mjs` (dry run by default, `--apply`) cleans existing data once (`pushTokenDedupePlan`, 1.17.0): a placeholder users document always loses its tokens; a token on more than one signed-in account stays on the one with the newest `pushTokenUpdatedAt` (`updatedAt` is compared only when none of them has `pushTokenUpdatedAt`); a token found only on placeholders is removed everywhere and the phone registers it again on its signed-in uid at next launch. A placeholder is never the owner in `pushTokenSingleOwner` either: when a placeholder gains a token, the token is removed from that placeholder (`pushTokenOwnerWrites`) and the signed-in accounts keep it. The push function also skips any recipient token that is on the actor's own users document (`pushTokensExcludingActor`).
- **Recipients are members** (1.12.0). An inbox row is written only for a member of the row's organisation (`organizationMemberActive`: a key of `members`, the creator, a users document of that organisation, or a membership there); the security rules refuse any other `userId` and a row without one. The push Cloud Function pushes a row only to an active member of its organisation (the same function; a person that organisation deactivated gets no push). A stranger's own company can no longer push text to a uid elsewhere.
- `NOTIFICATION_CATALOG` is the table below (`notificationCatalogRow(type)`). Push follows `DEVICE_PUSH_NOTIFICATION_TYPES`. `variation_from_tracker`, `variation_numbers_updated`, `booking_clash`, `booked_onto_job` and `material_order_created` are on the push allow-list but nothing writes them. Status is the divergence each platform must fix to meet this section.

| Type | Trigger | Recipients | Push | Written by | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `holiday_request_submitted` | Leave request, or a request to cancel approved leave | `holidayRequestRecipients` | yes | web, ios | web: pass a roster with aliases (PersonalLeave). |
| `holiday_request_approved` | Leave request approved (or a cancellation accepted) | `holidayDecisionRecipients` | yes | web, ios | iOS: stop writing it for a manager-booked confirmation (that is `holiday_booked_for_you`) and to every admin (`notifyAdminAnnualLeaveApproval`). |
| `holiday_request_declined` | Leave request declined (or a cancellation refused) | `holidayDecisionRecipients` | yes | web, ios | iOS: a manager cancelling approved leave writes `holiday_cancelled_by_manager`; switching self-book off writes no notice (1.10.0). |
| `holiday_cancelled_by_manager` | A manager or admin removes someone's approved leave | `holidayDecisionRecipients` | yes | web, ios | 1.10.0: was `holiday_request_declined` on iOS. The person, never the actor. |
| `holiday_booked_for_you` | A manager or admin books approved leave for someone | `holidayDecisionRecipients` | yes | web, ios | 1.10.0: was `holiday_request_approved` on iOS. The person, never the actor (nobody when they booked it themselves). |
| `timesheet_pending_manager_signoff` | Owner signs their timesheet (not self-signed) | `timesheetSubmittedRecipients` | yes | web, ios | both: use `timesheetSubmittedRecipients` and `timesheetNotificationDedupeKey`. |
| `timesheet_signed_by_manager` | Line manager or admin signs a timesheet | `timesheetSignedRecipients` | yes | web, ios | ok |
| `line_manager_peer_update` | One line manager signs a timesheet or decides leave for a shared report | `lineManagerPeerRecipients` | no | web, ios | web: not written for leave decisions (iOS is). |
| `timesheet_submission_reminder` | Scheduled reminder before the submit deadline | `dueTimesheetSubmissionNotices` (schedule); recipients are each participating user | yes | web, ios | scheduled: rows carry `actorUserId`: null (nobody caused them). Who participates is still decided in each app; no shared recipient rule yet. |
| `material_order_placed` | A line is added to a job material list | `materialOrderRecipients`, then `excludeActor` | yes | web, ios | iOS: falls back to every admin when the job has no managers; there is no fallback — write nothing. |
| `variation` | Variation created | `receivesJobNotification`, then `excludeActor` | yes | web, ios | ok (iOS reads the type and passes the job managers). |
| `variation_renumbered` | Variation renumbered | `receivesJobNotification`, then `excludeActor` | yes | web, ios | ok. relatedId is the job id. |
| `variation_added` | Variation added to a job | `receivesJobNotification`, then `excludeActor` | yes | web, ios | iOS: relatedId is the job id; write the variation id (`notificationRelatedIdFor`). |
| `toolbox_talk_issued` | Toolbox talk issued | `toolboxTalkRecipients` | yes | web, ios | web writes it from 1.10.0. iOS: relatedId is the job id; write the issue id (`notificationRelatedIdFor`). |
| `hs_toolbox_reminder` | Reminder to the people who have not signed a toolbox talk | `toolboxTalkRecipients` | yes | web | ok |
| `booking_created` | People booked onto a job | `bookingCreatedRecipients` | yes | web, ios | web writes it from 1.10.0. iOS: the "New Booking Created" batch row has no `userId` (never an inbox row) — stop writing it. |
| `task_created` | Task assigned | `excludeActor` (the assignees) | yes | web, ios | web writes it from 1.10.0. The Settings diagnostic writes `push_test`, not this type. |
| `push_test` | Settings "send a test notification" | `pushTestRecipients` (the signed-in user) | yes | web, ios | pushes to its actor (`notificationPushesToActor`); no toggle turns it off. |
| `task_completed` | Task completed | `taskCompletedRecipients` (the task creator) | yes | web, ios | tasks store `createdByUserId` from 1.10.0 (`taskFields`, never rewritten); older tasks fall back to the one active person with the stored `createdBy` name. iOS: write `createdByUserId` on create and address `taskCompletedRecipients` (it addressed the completer). |
| `deadline_assigned` | Assignees added to a deadline | `deadlineAssignedRecipients` | yes | web, ios | both: the actor is not removed; use `deadlineAssignedRecipients`. |
| `deadline_reminder` | Scheduled deadline reminder | the assignees (on-device schedule, no shared rule) | no | ios | iOS only. Scheduled: no actorUserId. Listed from its createdAt (1.10.0). |
| `deadline_due` | Deadline due | the assignees (on-device schedule, no shared rule) | no | ios | iOS only. Scheduled: no actorUserId. Listed from its createdAt (1.10.0). |
| `operative_created` | Operative added | `companyActivityRecipients` | yes | ios | one row per admin (`userId` + `actorUserId`); never written without `userId`. Toggle: `companyActivity`. |
| `manager_created` | Manager added | `companyActivityRecipients` | yes | ios | one row per admin (`userId` + `actorUserId`); never written without `userId`. Toggle: `companyActivity`. |
| `client_created` | Client added | `companyActivityRecipients` | yes | web, ios | one row per admin (`userId` + `actorUserId`); never written without `userId`. Toggle: `companyActivity`. |
| `project_created` | Project created | `companyActivityRecipients` | yes | ios | web: not written yet. One row per admin (`userId` + `actorUserId`). Toggle: `companyActivity`. |
| `small_works_created` | Small works created | `companyActivityRecipients` | yes | ios | web: not written yet. One row per admin (`userId` + `actorUserId`). Toggle: `companyActivity`. |
| `warning_removed` | Warning dismissed | `companyActivityRecipients` | yes | web, ios | one row per admin (`userId` + `actorUserId`); never written without `userId`. Toggle: `companyActivity`. |

Toggle: `companyActivity`. |
| `idea_status` | Feedback idea status changed | `ideaRecipients` | no | web | web: the actor is not removed; use `ideaRecipients`. |
| `idea_shipped` | Feedback idea shipped | `ideaRecipients` | no | web | web: the actor is not removed; use `ideaRecipients`. |

Toggle: overtime. |

Toggle: overtime. |

## 14. Authentication and organisations

- Sign-in and passwords use Firebase Auth email/password on every platform; a password change applies everywhere. Every password — sign-up, invite set-up, reset, change — must be at least 8 characters; the platform owner's developer account needs 10 (`PASSWORD_MIN_LENGTH`, `passwordMinLength`, `passwordPolicyError` → "Password must be at least N characters."). The platform owner is `isPlatformOwnerEmail(email)` (`PLATFORM_OWNER_EMAIL`, compared without case); `passwordMinLength({ email })` applies the 10 for it. No complexity rule.
- **The platform owner** (1.12.0) is the signed-in Firebase Auth account whose token email is `PLATFORM_OWNER_EMAIL` and is verified (`isVerifiedPlatformOwner`; security rules `isPlatformOwner`, owner API routes `requireOwner`). A users document's `email` never counts.
- **Deactivated** (1.12.0): a person is deactivated in an organisation when their users document is that organisation's and says `isActive: false`, or their membership there says `accountActive` (else `isActive`) false (`organizationMemberActive`). The security rules give them no access to that organisation's data and the push function sends them nothing; they still read their own users document and memberships, so the apps show the deactivated screen and Switch organisation.
- **Changing a sign-in email** (1.12.0): the person's own users document `email` changes only to their signed-in Auth email — after Firebase Auth has changed it (iOS `sendEmailVerification(beforeUpdatingEmail:)` / web `verifyBeforeUpdateEmail`, then the next sign-in). An admin changes a person's email from Manage Users (both apps), or the platform owner from the support tool. **1.13.0:** a person's own profile save decides with `selfEmailChangeAction({ storedEmail, nextEmail })` — `verifyBeforeUpdate` asks Auth to verify the new address (the message is `selfEmailChangeSentMessage`) and the rest of the save goes ahead with the stored email; nothing else is written until Auth has changed it. On the next sign-in with a verified token email that differs from `users/{uid}.email`, `signInEmailSyncWrites` gives the writes: `users/{uid}.email` = the token email, the new `userEmails/{email}` claim for the account, and the old claim deleted when it is the account's. The rules allow exactly these (§16).
- **Switching organisation is per device.** Each platform remembers the last organisation it opened (web `pp.webActiveOrg:{userId}`, iOS `cached_organizationId`) and reopens to it (`chooseSessionOrganization`). Switching on one platform does not change another platform, and does not write `users/{uid}.organizationId`.
- **Membership is shared.** The list of organisations a person belongs to is shared data (`sessionMembershipIds`).
- **One membership shape** (1.10.0). `users/{uid}/orgMemberships/{orgId}` is read with `parseOrgMembershipDocument` (nested `permissions.{key}` first, else the legacy flat key, else its default — `ORG_MEMBERSHIP_DEFAULT_ON`; operative mode turns manager and operatives off; `accountActive`, else `isActive`) and written with `orgMembershipWriteFields({ organizationId, stored, next, writer, now })`: nested permissions only, changed fields only, and an admin update removes the legacy flat keys. Only an admin of that organisation writes role, permissions, super admin or the active flag (`ORG_MEMBERSHIP_ADMIN_FIELDS`); the person writes only acceptance (`status: 'active'`, `acceptedAt`). No app copies its users document onto a membership on a switch.
- **A role belongs to an organisation** (1.10.0). Whether someone is an admin or manager *of an organisation* comes from that organisation: their `users/{uid}` flags only when that document's `organizationId` is this organisation, otherwise this organisation's `members` map entry. A manager in company A who is an operative in company B is an operative in B (security rules `callerOrgRole`).

## 15. Platform-specific (not synced)

- Light and dark mode.
- Warning display order (Oldest/Newest).

**The rules version in Settings** (1.32.0, owner decision 4 Nov): the bottom of Settings shows everyone, on web and iOS, the rulebook version the app runs — `rulebookVersionLabel(version)` → "Rules 1.32.0" (iOS from `rulebookStamp()`; web from the version it was built with). Hidden when unknown.

## 16. Server-side permissions (Firestore security rules)

Deployed only from `project-planner-web/firestore.rules`. Apps hide controls too, but these rules are the guard:
- **Who the caller is: Auth custom claims** (1.32.4; `authClaimsForPerson`, design `docs/rules-claims-design.md`). The rules read the caller's own role and company facts from their ID token's custom claim `pp`, not from their documents: per organisation they belong to (their users document names it, its `members` map lists them, or they created it) the role (`callerOrgRole` before billing), the `members` entry, member-and-active, home, creator, admin (users document or `members`), Super Admin, the Projects / Small works toggles and annual-leave self-booking for that organisation; and from their users document the admin-like, operative, operatives-manager, Super Admin, exists and inactive flags. Each is the 1.32.3 rule computed once from the same fields. The Cloud Function (`authClaimsOnUserWrite`, `authClaimsOnMembershipWrite`, `authClaimsOnOrganizationWrite`) recomputes them when `users/{uid}` (`AUTH_CLAIMS_USER_FIELDS`), a membership, or an organisation's `members` / `creatorUserId` changes, sets them only when they changed, and writes the marker `users/{uid}/private/authClaims` `{ v }` (read by the person, written by the server only). **Staleness:** a change reaches the rules on the person's next ID token — at once for an app that listens to the marker and refreshes (`authClaimsTokenIsStale`: web from 1.32.4, iOS from the build that ships the hand-off), otherwise within an hour. Deactivation and removal also revoke the person's refresh tokens; a token issued before the change still works until it expires (at most an hour). Without claims (none set yet) every role-decided rule refuses. Organisations that do not fit in the claims (Firebase's 1,000-byte limit; home first, then by role and id) are refused for that person. **Still read from documents** (claims cannot exist yet, or the data is not the caller's role): self-create, founder bootstrap and invite acceptance; the caller's alias ids and names; billing and deletion (`orgBillingOpen`); everything about other documents (job managers, line managers, subjects, placeholders).
- **Organisation:** only a current admin edits `organizations/{orgId}`; billing is never written by an app; ownership (`creatorUserId`) passes only from the current Super Admin to another admin (or by the platform owner). A demoted creator cannot edit. **1.12.0:** `isSuperAdmin` (on `users/{uid}` and on a membership) changes only by that organisation's Super Admin (its creator, or a users document of it that is already super admin) or the platform owner — never on one's own document by anyone else, and a self-created profile is super admin only for the creator; an admin who is not the Super Admin never changes the role, permissions, active flag or company of the Super Admin or the creator (names and other fields stay editable).
- **Platform owner** (1.12.0): `isPlatformOwner` is the Auth token email `info@projectplanner.us` with `email_verified == true`, never a users document's `email`.
- **Deactivated people** (1.12.0, §14): `canAccessOrganization`, `callerOrgRole`, the admin tests and co-member reads refuse a person deactivated in that organisation (their users document of that organisation `isActive: false`; for a member from another company, their membership `accountActive` false). 1.32.4: read from the auth claims (above), so it applies on the person's next token (refresh tokens are revoked). Their own users document and memberships stay readable. Known limit: an admin from another company deactivated only on their membership here keeps the members-map admin test.
- **Identity links on users** (1.12.0): on their own document a person never changes `formerUserIds` or `sameEmailUserIds`, and changes `email` only to their signed-in Auth email; a self-created profile carries alias ids only from the invite placeholder it copies plus that placeholder's id (`inviteMigrationFormerIdWriteFields`), and none when re-created by a returning member or a founder. Admins write them (Manage Users, the admin backfill). Rules read the caller's alias ids only from a users document of that organisation.
- **Company settings documents** (`settings/{id}` such as job types and trades): admins and managers. Deadlines and health & safety documents stay open to the team.
- **Pay reads** (1.13.0, Q1; rules `canViewPayOf` = `canViewPersonPay`): `payProfiles/{subjectId}` is read by id only by the person (their uid, or a users document on their signed-in email), their line manager (listed on their users document), admins of the organisation, a manager with operatives access for an operative of the company, and for a catalogue row admins, operatives managers and the person on its email — 1.27.0: operatives managers only for a catalogue operative (`payOperativeRowIsCatalogue`: no users document of the row id, no stored `linkedUserId` / `userId` / `uid`, no `userEmails` claim of the organisation on the row's email), the same for its rate history rows (a query `operativeId ==` + `userId == ''`); the whole collection only by admins and the platform owner. It is written by those `canChangePayOf` allows, only `PAY_PROFILE_FIELDS`, `userId` (= the document id) and write metadata. **1.27.0, the invitee's own copy** (`inviteeCopiesOwnInvitedPay`): while `privatePay` is on for the organisation, the accepting person may create `payProfiles/{their uid}` once (create only, never update) when the email claim `userEmails/{their token email}` still names a pending placeholder of this organisation on that email (`inviteClaimIsPendingPlaceholder`), that placeholder is not them, `payProfiles/{placeholderId}` exists, and every pay field written equals that profile's. They can never set or change their own pay. While `privatePay` is on for the organisation (`privatePayOn(orgId)`, 1.26.0: platform-wide or listed, §18): `operativeDayRateHistory` rows, `operativeProfiles/{uid}` and `settings/timesheet_*` are read only by those people (a non-admin query of history must name the person), and no write puts a pay value (not null) on `users`, `operatives` or `managers` (`payStaysPrivate`; removing one is allowed). The organisation catch-all read never re-opens `payProfiles`, `operativeDayRateHistory`, `operativeProfiles` or `settings`. A person never changes their own `employmentStartDate` (§4.4).
- **Lapsed subscription** (1.13.0, Q3; §19): while `orgBillingOpen` is false, organisation data is refused to everyone but the platform owner — `canAccessOrganization`, the admin tests (`isOrganizationAdmin`, `isOrgAdminViaMembersMap`), `callerOrgRole` and co-member users reads (`anchoredInCallerCompany`) all refuse. Not closed: the organisation document itself (read, and its admin edits), the caller's own users document and memberships. Billing stays server-only.
- **Email claims** (1.13.0, §14): a person deletes their own `userEmails/{email}` claim only when their verified token email is another address.
- **Ghost operatives** (1.34.0, §11): created through the existing users create rules (an Operatives manager's plain-operative create accepts a ghost: no privilege flag, no alias ids, no "No line manager", `email: ''`). **R1:** an Operatives manager's update of an unaccepted placeholder may change its line manager (1.33.0) only when it is not a ghost — a ghost's line manager is changed by admins only. **R2:** being a ghost's line manager gives no overtime booking write on it (`callerLineManagesUserOrRow` refuses a users document with `ghost: true`); the job's managers and admins still write its bookings (`canWriteBooking`). Leave, pay and delete follow the users-document rules unchanged. **Nobody changes `ghost` or `inviteEmailHint` on their own users document** (`selfUpdateKeepsIdentityLinks`, admins included) and a self-created profile carries neither; an Operatives manager never changes them either (their field list); only an admin changes them on someone else's document.
- **Profile and pay:** a person cannot change their own pay (`dayRate`, `hourlyRate`, `payBasis`), line manager, active flag, employment type or permissions — on `users/{uid}`, `operativeProfiles/{uid}`, `operativeDayRateHistory`, `operatives/{id}` or `managers/{id}`. Pay changes are admins, or operatives managers for operatives (§8). Resending an unchanged value always passes. The self-employed pay-rate requirement (`payRateRequiredError`, §8) is an app form rule: the security rules do not enforce it, so older app builds can still save a self-employed person without a rate (`usersMissingPayRate` lists them).
- **Annual leave:** a person creates and edits their own pending request and asks to cancel. Nobody decides their own request, admins included (`canDecideLeave`). The person's line manager or an admin (not in operative mode) approves or rejects; a person with no line manager waits for an admin — never any manager. Only a self-booker (`canSelfBookAnnualLeave`, 1.28.0: the `annualLeaveSelfBook` permission outside operative mode, read for this organisation (1.30.2: their users document of this organisation, else their membership here — `orgSelfManageFlag`), or the organisation's Super Admin — its creator, or a super admin users document of it, rules `hbCallerSelfBooks`) saves their own leave approved; having no line manager allows it for nobody. 1.40.0 (owner decision 13 Nov, item 17): leave filed only under an `operatives` row (no users document on `userId`) is decided by admins only — never through the row's email claim, which an Operatives manager could repoint at someone they line-manage (rules `hbCanDecide`: the line manager listed on `users/{userId}` only; apps `leaveDecisionRequester`); a line manager never changes a record's `userId` or `operativeId` (moving leave to another person is admins only). **Reads are unchanged** (every member reads every leave record): a manager legitimately needs the leave of anyone they may book (booking leave conflicts, the scheduling grid, Daily Overview and warnings on jobs they manage), so the only narrowing possible is "operatives read only their own", which needs both apps to query their own leave first and `minimumIosBuild` raised before the rules change — a follow-up, not in 1.40.0.
- **Annual leave adjustments** (1.40.0, §4.4): `annualLeaveAdjustments` is created only — never updated or deleted — by an admin of the organisation (never for one of their own ids: their uid, `formerUserIds`, `sameEmailUserIds` or an `operatives` row on their sign-in email) or the person's line manager (`callerLineManagesUser`), never the person; the fields are exactly the create shape (`days` a non-zero number of half days, at most 365 either way; `reason` 1–200 characters with at least one that is not a space; `yearKey` a day key whose calendar year is the request's year, the year before or the year after (the apps offer the current leave year only); `createdBy` the caller; `createdAt` the request time; no author name) plus write metadata. Read by the person, their line managers and admins (`overtimeViewer`: the organisation access test first — never a person deactivated in the organisation, never while the subscription has lapsed or the company is scheduled for deletion, §19, §20); the catch-all read never re-opens it.
- **Timesheets:** a person never counter-signs or exports their own timesheet (their saves never carry those fields, `timesheetOwnerWriteFields`); only their line manager or an admin writes the sign-off and export fields and the review maps (`payrollLineReviews`, `extraEntryReviews`); after sign-off the person cannot change price work or expenses. The owner writes `weeklyReportOverride` only when their sheet is self-signed (1.28.0, rules `timesheetSubjectSelfSigns`): the organisation's Super Admin, or an active admin of the organisation whose users document has `timesheetSelfSign` on — never because they have no line manager. 1.30.2: `isTimesheetReviewer` never counts the sheet's subject (the stored or incoming `userId` is one of the caller's ids — their uid, `formerUserIds` or `sameEmailUserIds` on their users document — or the `timesheet_{uid}_` id prefix names them), admins and the Super Admin included, so an admin's own sheet follows the owner rule: without `timesheetSubjectSelfSigns` they write no sign-off or export field, review map or `weeklyReportOverride` on it. The owner never files a sheet under someone else or changes its `userId` (`timesheetOwnerKeepsSubject`). A self-signed subject — the sheet's `userId` is one of their ids, the doc id names that same id, and `timesheetSubjectSelfSigns` — may write their whole sheet (the doc id prefix alone never grants it), because iOS builds before 1.30.2 save a self-signed admin's own sheet as the reviewer's (sign-off fields and `extraEntryReviews`). `timesheetSubjectSelfSigns` reads Timesheet self-sign for this organisation (`orgSelfManageFlag`, below). Known limit: a same-email document the caller's users document does not list in `sameEmailUserIds` is invisible to the rules; the apps leave it out (`timesheetWriterIsReviewer` with the roster). **1.43.0:** a settings document whose `userId` is the caller is readable to them first, so a person's own query `userId ==` their id (or `in` a list of only their id) is allowed — before 1.43.0 the read first required staff for `variations_*` / `variationItem_*` documents and a query cannot exclude them, so every operative's own-timesheet query was refused. The clause is `userId == caller && (request.method == 'list' || the id starts timesheet_)`: a direct read of any other settings document is never opened by it, and (review r318) no settings document other than a timesheet may be written with a top-level `userId` (a create without one; an update keeps the stored value). Known limit: a query cannot test the document id, so a legacy non-timesheet settings document already carrying a top-level `userId` naming the caller would be returned to them by their own `userId` query; no app writes one and both apps keep only `timesheet_` ids. Review r318 also closes an older gap: the owner may add a missing `userId` to a sheet only when the sheet's id names that id (`timesheet_{uid}_…`), so nobody claims another person's legacy sheet stored without `userId`, `privatePay` on or off (emulator suite `tests/timesheetSettings.rules.test.ts`). When the history query fails, the period page shows `TIMESHEET_HISTORY_LOAD_FAILED_MESSAGE` ("Couldn't check older timesheets — try again.") with Retry and saves nothing until it loads — never a blank new sheet, which could become a second copy under another id.
- **Self-manage toggles for this organisation** (1.30.2, `organizationSelfManageFlags`): `hbCallerSelfBooks` and `timesheetSubjectSelfSigns` read `annualLeaveSelfBook` / `timesheetSelfSign` with `orgSelfManageFlag` — the person's users document when its `organizationId` is this organisation (the Super Admin flag there counts as both on), else their membership here (`users/{uid}/orgMemberships/{orgId}`: `permissions.{key}`, else the flat key; its role, else its flags, for admin and operative mode; `accountActive` not false), else off; the creator always both. Before 1.30.2 they read the caller's users document whatever company it named: an admin whose toggles were on only in their membership here was refused approved leave, and a manager visiting with the flag on at home was allowed it.
- **Self-manage toggles** (1.28.0): nobody changes their own `annualLeaveSelfBook` or `timesheetSelfSign` (top level or `permissions`) — the admin self-repair exemption and the admin branch exclude them on one's own document; the Super Admin's own document (flagged `isSuperAdmin`, or the organisation's creator) is exempt (both read on). A non-admin can never turn either on for themself. Resending an unchanged value passes. **Create paths too:** accepting an invite copies the toggles only as the admin set them on the placeholder (`noMorePrivilegeThan`); re-creating one's own deleted profile (returning member) or the bootstrap path never switches them on, except for the organisation's creator (`selfCreateSelfManageOff`).
- **Notifications:** any member creates inbox rows; `actorUserId`, when present, is the signed-in user. Reads and updates are the addressee's own rows (or an admin's). 1.12.0: the row's `userId` must be a member of that organisation (members key, creator, a users document of it, or a membership there); a row without `userId` is refused.
- **Roster rows** (1.12.0): `operatives/{id}` and `managers/{id}` are created by staff (admins and managers) only. Changing an existing row's `email` — which `jobListsCaller` and `callerOwnsBookingId` trust — is admins only, and on an operatives row also a manager with operatives access for a row that is not their own. Pay keys as before (§8).
- **Catalogues** (1.12.0): `qualifications` and `skills` are written by admins and managers only. A person's own qualification assignments and expiry dates stay on their operatives row (iOS My Qualifications).
- **Account confirmations** (1.12.0): `accountConfirmations` are never listed; a founder creates one only for themselves (`userId` = the signed-in account); marking it used needs the signed-in account whose token email is the one it names (the confirm page asks a signed-out visitor to sign in first).
- **Platform config** (1.12.0, §18): `platformConfig/app` is readable by anyone, signed out included; other `platformConfig` documents by signed-in users; only the platform owner writes.
- **Job and booking reads** (§17) are enforced only while `enforcedReads` is on for the organisation (1.12.1; 1.26.0: platform-wide or listed, `enforcedReadsOn(orgId)`, §18); otherwise every member of the organisation reads them (a deactivated person never does). 1.33.0: with it on, admins and every manager outside operative mode (`callerOrgRole` admin or manager) read every job (`get` and `list`, the health & safety subcollection too) and every labour, manager site and subcontractor booking; operatives keep their own bookings (`callerOwnsBookingId`) and jobs by id only.
- **PAYE overtime** (1.29.0, §25). **Bookings:** today's writers are unchanged (`canWriteBooking`). In addition, the booked person's line manager (listed on the users document of this organisation whose id is the booking's person id — `operativeId` / `userId` exactly as stored; an active manager or admin here) may change **only** `workStartTime`, `workEndTime`, `timeSlot`, `otMultiplierOverride` (1–3 in 0.25 steps, or removed) and `status` → `Cancelled` (plus write metadata; `Cancelled` is for 1.32 builds only — 1.33.0 apps never write it, and a later rules release drops it) on that person's labour or manager site bookings — never their own booking (rules `overtimeLineManagerBookingUpdate`, `callerLineManagesUser`); the clock times are short text (at most 8 characters) and the slot one of the known slots. **1.33.0 (owner decision 6 Nov, "a line manager can always approve"): also a booking filed under one of the person's own `operatives` rows** — the row's `email` (lower case) names, through the organisation's `userEmails/{email}` claim, a users document of this organisation that lists the caller as line manager (rules `callerLineManagesUserOrRow`, `callerLineManagesRowPerson`, `rowPersonLineManaged`; the same rows `overtimePersonIds` reads). The row's account link (`linkedUserId`) still grants nothing, a row with no email (a 1.34 ghost operative) never matches, and a row on someone else's email gives the line manager of that someone else, not this person's. Accepted risk (Q-B1): a row's email is changed only by admins and, for an operatives row that is not their own, Operatives managers (§16 Roster rows), so an Operatives manager could point a row at a user they line-manage — the same class as the line-manager risk below. The rules check the line manager's write in its own allow, before `canWriteBooking`, and the users-id path first (a `get` / `exists` budget of 10 per write: the row path costs three lookups). Before 1.33.0 a booking filed under an `operatives` row id was never written through this path (it stayed with admins and the job's managers, "Needs an admin"). **1.33.0 review fix — a Return reaches every id the person goes by:** a booking change written by an overtime Return carries `overtimeReturnRequestId` (`OVERTIME_RETURN_REQUEST_ID_FIELD`, write-only, set by `overtimeReturnWrites`); the rules then accept the line manager's overtime-field change when that request, as it stands after the same transaction (`getAfter`), is `approved`, was decided by the caller and lists the booking in `appliedBookingIds`, the caller line-manages the request's person `U` (`callerLineManagesUser`), and the booking's person id is `U`, one of `users/{U}.formerUserIds` / `sameEmailUserIds` (an old invite placeholder id whose users document was deleted on accept, a same-email account that does not list the line manager — fields the person cannot change on their own document), or an `operatives` row whose account they line-manage (rules `overtimeReturnFor`, `overtimeReturnPersonOk`). Ids compare exactly as stored. Without the field the 1.33.0 path above applies unchanged (iOS 1.32 and web 1.32.4 writes). Because line managers are what grants it, **a manager with the Operatives toggle, who may set an operative's line manager (§11), can thereby give themselves (or another manager) these overtime-field writes on that operative's bookings** — accepted (owner decision: operatives managers manage operatives). An operative never writes a booking. **`overtimeRequests`:** the person creates their own (`userId` = them, `status: 'pending'`, at least one line, only the request fields) and withdraws it while pending (status → `withdrawn`, nothing else); only their line managers and admins decide it (1.33.0 Return: status → `approved` with `decisions`, `decidedBy` = the decider, `decidedAt`, `appliedBookingIds`, `droppedBookingIds`, `staleBookingIds`, `deletedBookingIds` and write metadata, nothing else; a 1.32 build's partial approval may still leave it `pending` until the phase-2 rules release after `minimumIosBuild` is raised) and nobody decides their own, admins included; once approved it is final; never deleted. The rules cannot check that every line was decided (a list cannot be walked); `overtimeReturnReady` / `overtimeReturnWrites` are the guard. **Delete on Return** (1.33.0, item f): the decider of an approved request deletes a labour booking it lists in `deletedBookingIds` — the person's line manager (the request's person under any id, as for a Return change above, an active manager or admin here) or an admin — when the booking's tombstone, written in the same batch, names the request (`overtimeRequestId`); the booking delete itself only checks that this tombstone is new in the batch and was written by the caller (the tombstone's own rule holds the request test). Manager site bookings are never deleted this way. The rules cannot check that the booking was all overtime or that the person asked for it (Q-F4), nor that `deletedBookingIds` holds only lines the person struck (`lines` is a list of maps, which the rules cannot search); the app and the decider are the guard, and the person test above limits a decider to that one person's bookings, which as their line manager they could already change. **`overtimeReviews/{userId}_{bookingId}`:** the id is the person and booking; `reviewedBy` is the writer; a person writes their own only when they may edit that booking (admins every booking, managers the bookings on jobs they manage — never an operative); line managers and admins write anyone's; `decision` is `approved` | `edited` | `trimmed` | `cancelled` (1.29–1.32) | `changed` | `declined` | `deleted` (1.33.0); admins delete. **`overtimeReports`:** the person creates their own (`userId` and `generatedBy` = them, `kind: 'overtime'`), never updated or deleted by an app. All three are read only by the person, their line managers and admins (rules `overtimeViewer`), and the organisation-wide catch-all read never re-opens them. **Reads** (1.33.0): every manager reads every booking (§17), so the 1.29.0 known limit (a line manager who does not manage the job could not read a report's booking with `enforcedReads` on) is gone. The create rule cannot check each line's booking (a list cannot be walked in the rules); `overtimeRequestWriteFields` and the Return's re-check (`overtimeReturnWrites`) are the guard.
- **PAYE price work, expenses and personal site audits** (1.30.0, §26). `priceWorkItems` and `expenseItems`: the person creates, edits and deletes their own (`userId` = the signed-in account, active in the organisation; never moved to anyone else); their line managers (listed on the person's users document of this organisation — never an `operatives` row) and admins of the organisation read them; nobody else, and nobody but the person writes them — no approval. **1.33.0 (owner decision 6 Nov, a line manager can always review):** an item may carry `personUserId`, the creator's current users id (`payeClaimPersonFields`; only the signed-in account on create and update, never changed to anyone else), and the line manager of that users id reads the item through it too (a query `personUserId ==`), so an item still filed under a former id is readable once `scripts/backfill-paye-claim-person-user-id.mjs` has stamped it (`payeClaimPersonUserIdBackfill`). Price work carries a job number (1.30.1: required on create, a string of 1–60 characters; an update of an item stored before 1.30.1 without one passes, and one that has it keeps 1–60) and names an approver whose users document names this organisation, active, an admin or with the manager flag or role (not an operative), never the person; dates in order; amount above 0 (at most 1,000,000); at most 20 pieces of evidence, the first five checked by the rules to be files in the person's own claim folder of this organisation and not SVG (the expression budget; the readers drop any later entry that is not the person's own); an expense needs at least one. A person whose users document stores `hasNoLineManager` is not read through a listed line manager (1.30.0; this also applies to the §25 overtime documents). `payeClaimReports`: the person creates their own once (`kind` `priceWork` | `expense`, `generatedBy` themself, at most 500 items), read as the items, never changed or deleted. `personalSiteAudits`: the owner only — read, create, edit (the create shape: job name, day-key date, 1–100 items), delete; admins included cannot read them. **`payeClaimReviews/{kind}_{itemId}`** (1.32.0): read as the items (`overtimeViewer`); created or updated by the person's line manager (`callerLineManagesUser`, the users document named by the review's `userId`) or an admin of the organisation; the item must exist and be that person's (`expenseItems` / `priceWorkItems` `userId` = the review's `userId`), an update keeps `userId`, `itemId` and `kind`, and the writer is never the person under any of their ids (their uid, `formerUserIds` / `sameEmailUserIds` on their users document, or a users document on their sign-in email; review fix r274), with `reviewedBy` = the writer, `kind` `priceWork` | `expense`, the id `{kind}_{itemId}`, `decision` `approved` | `rejected` and only the review fields plus write metadata; never deleted. **1.33.0:** a review of an item filed under another of the person's ids carries `personUserId` (the person's users id, `payeClaimReviewWrites`); the line manager of `personUserId` writes it when that users document lists the item's `userId` in `formerUserIds` or `sameEmailUserIds` (fields the person cannot change on their own document), the writer is never the person under either id, `personUserId` never changes on an update, and the line manager reads such reviews through `personUserId` too. There is no "Needs an admin" any more. The catch-all read never re-opens these five. **Storage:** `payeClaims/{userId}/…` is written only by that person (images other than SVG, and PDFs, 10MB) and read by them, their active line managers (users document; an inactive line manager reads nothing) and active admins of the organisation (a stray `operativeMode` on an admin is ignored, 1.11.0, as in Firestore and `canViewPayeClaims`); `personalSiteAudits/{ownerUserId}/…` is the owner's only (images other than SVG, 10MB). Emulator tests: `tests/payeClaims.rules.test.ts`, `tests/storage.rules.test.ts`.
- **Warnings:** only admins and managers accept or un-accept a clash (create and delete `acceptedBookingClashes`), dismiss a warning (`dismissedWarnings`, enforced from 1.9.0) and approve, dismiss or undo a warning resolution.
- **Jobs and bookings** (1.10.0, owner decisions; `staffAccess.ts`; reads in §17). Creating a project or small works job and changing a job's manager list (`managerIds`, `managerId`, `assignedManagerUserIds`, `managerUserIds` — `JOB_MANAGER_LIST_FIELDS`) is admins, and managers with that kind's add/edit/manage permission (`permissions.projects` / `permissions.smallWorks`) in that organisation (`canCreateJob`, `canChangeJobManagers`). With it they also edit any job of that kind (`canEditJob`). A manager without it edits the details of a job they manage, never its manager list (`canEditJob`, `jobWriteWithoutManagerList`); 1.42.0: they still see every job (§17) — the toggle is never a view permission, and its row text says so (§11). Creating, editing and deleting labour (`bookings`), manager site (`managerSiteBookings`) and subcontractor (`subcontractorBookings`) bookings, and writing their tombstones, is `canWriteBooking` / `canDeleteBooking`: admins every booking; managers bookings on jobs they manage (a manager site booking with no job: their own) — a toggle does not extend it, and a manager never books themselves onto a job they do not manage; operatives never. The rules read the job document (`projects/{id}` or `smallWorks/{id}`, the stored id, then upper, then lower case) and accept the signed-in account in `managerUserIds`, `managerIds` or `managerId`, or `managerId` naming a managers row with the signed-in email. The toggle in an organisation is that organisation's: `users/{uid}` flags when it is the person's own company, else `users/{uid}/orgMemberships/{orgId}.permissions`. A job save writes `managerUserIds` = `jobManagerUserIds(job, managers, accounts)` beside `managerIds`; jobs saved before 1.10.0 get it from `scripts/backfill-job-manager-user-ids.mjs` (Deploy notes). Tombstones as in §2, carrying only their own fields.
- **Invitations** (1.12.0): admins of the organisation (and the platform owner) list, create, change and delete them; a manager with operatives access lists and deletes their company's, and creates or changes only an invitation that is operative-only before and after the write (`invitationIsOperativeOnly`). 1.33.0: they change its line manager (`assignedManagerUserIds`, `assignedManagerUserId`, `hasNoLineManager`) only on their own invitation (`invitedBy` = them) that is not used yet.
- **Line manager locked once set** (1.33.0, owner decision 6 Nov, §11 `canChangeLineManager`): on an existing users document, a manager with operatives access changes `assignedManagerUserIds`, `assignedManagerUserId` or `hasNoLineManager` only while the person has not finished sign-up (`passwordSet` is not true, the invite placeholder); afterwards only admins do. Resending the same line managers passes. The rules do not check who invited a placeholder (users documents do not store it); the app's `canChangeLineManager` does.
- **My Projects / My Small works picks** (1.33.0, §21): `worksListPicks/{uid}` is read, written and deleted only by that person (`uid` = the signed-in account, a member of the organisation), with only `projects`, `smallWorks` (lists of at most 500 ids) and `updatedAt` (the request time); admins included never read another person's list, and the catch-all read never re-opens it.
- **Materials catalogue** (1.33.0, `canManageMaterialCatalogue`): `materialCatalogue` is written by admins of the organisation (and the platform owner) only; every member still reads it.
- **Memberships:** only an admin of that organisation writes another person's role, permissions, super admin or active flag on `users/{uid}/orgMemberships/{orgId}`; the person may only accept and set `role` to what the organisation's members map lists. **1.11.0:** the person accepting an invitation may create their membership and add their own `members.{uid}` with exactly the invitation's role (`invitationRole`) and permissions, only for an invitation in `invitations/{id}` to the signed-in email and this organisation that was unused before the batch and is marked used by it (`inviteBeingAccepted`); nothing else on the organisation document changes, and an existing members entry is never replaced.
- **Booking kind** (1.11.0): `jobCollection`, when a booking carries it, is `projects` or `smallWorks` and only on a booking with a job; a manager's write must name the collection the job they manage is in (`callerManagesJobIn`).
- **Deleting a person:** as `canDeleteUser` (§11); the account batch is refused whole when the users delete is refused.
- **Material quotes and orders** (1.15.0, `canSendMaterialOrders`): only admins and super admins outside operative mode and managers send a quote or order and see quote / order history; the per-user Materials toggle does not gate them (it is locked on for managers and admins), and for an operative it only opens the job's material list. `materialSendRecords` are created and read by admins and managers of the organisation only. The `material_request` email (email function) and the web send route refuse anyone else (`MATERIAL_ORDER_SEND_REFUSED_MESSAGE`); recipients are saved wholesaler contacts, the sender's own copy and at most 3 typed addresses not saved on a wholesaler per send (`materialOrderRecipientCheck`, `MATERIAL_ORDER_TYPED_RECIPIENTS_MAX`). The web route sends the copy to the signed-in email only. **The email envelope** (1.24.0) is `materialRequestEmailEnvelope({ requestType, jobNumber, companyName, caller })` → `{ subject, fromName, replyTo, cc }`, used by the web send route and the email function's `material_request`: subject `Quote request — <job number> — <company>` or `Material order request — <job number> — <company>` (`materialRequestEmailSubject`; the sender's own separate copy, when no CC is applied, starts `Copy: `); From name `<caller name> via Project Planner` from the verified caller's own profile name (none when the name is blank or is the email: the organisation From), always from the service's verified address; Reply-To and CC the verified caller's email. A name, address or CC in the request is never used. The company is the organisation name in the function and the send sheet's company (the organisation name) on web. **Job check** (1.24.0): `material_request` takes an optional `jobId`; when sent, the job must be a project or small works of the caller's organisation (`organizations/{orgId}/projects|smallWorks/{jobId}`) or the email is refused. A request without `jobId` (iOS today sends only the job number) is still accepted. **1.27.0 (owner decision 2):** with `jobId`, both the email function's `material_request` and the web send route (which now sends `jobId`) also check `materialRequestJobCheck({ viewer, job, kind, managers, jobNumber })`: the sender must see the job (`canSeeJob`, §17, the same rule as every job list — `viewer` from `materialRequestJobViewer`: the verified uid and email, the level and toggles in that organisation, alias ids; `managers` the organisation's `managers` rows on the sender's email) else 403 `MATERIAL_REQUEST_JOB_NOT_VISIBLE_MESSAGE`, and the sent job number must be the stored `jobNumber` (trimmed, spaces collapsed, without case) else 409 `MATERIAL_REQUEST_JOB_NUMBER_MISMATCH_MESSAGE`; a job not in the organisation is 404 `MATERIAL_REQUEST_JOB_NOT_FOUND_MESSAGE`. Nothing is sent and no rate-limit count is used when a check fails.
- **Company deletion** (1.15.0, §20): while `organizations/{orgId}.deletion.status` is `scheduled`, organisation data is refused to everyone but the platform owner, exactly like a lapsed subscription (`orgBillingOpen` also checks it): not closed are the organisation document (read), the caller's own users document and memberships. No other edit of the organisation document is allowed while scheduled. `deletion` is written by the server only, except the super admin's restore — removing `deletion` (with write metadata only) when `restoreBy` is not `platform_owner` (`canRestoreOrganization`). The platform owner may restore any.
- **Write metadata** (`updatedBy` = the signed-in user, `updatedFrom`, `updatedAt`) is validated when present and never required; tombstones never carry it. A staff material status update (Send: `status`, `lastSentAt`, `lastSentRequestType`) may carry it too (1.9.0; the rule's field list refused `updatedBy` / `updatedFrom` before).

## 17. Job access and reads (1.10.0)

Reads are enforced by the security rules, not only filtered on screen. Firestore rules are not filters: an app may only run a query whose every possible result the viewer may read, so both apps build their queries from one plan.

- **Who sees which job** (`seesEveryJob(role, kind, toggles)`, `canSeeJob(viewer, job, managers, kind)`; **1.33.0, owner decision 6 Nov: "every manager sees every project and small works"**): admins, super admins and every manager outside operative mode see every project and every small works job, whatever their Projects / Small works toggles; operatives (and operative mode) see no job list, only the jobs of their own bookings, tasks and deadlines, read one by one. The toggles now only govern adding jobs, editing any job of that kind and changing a job's manager list (`hasWorkCatalogueToggle`, `canCreateJob`, `canChangeJobManagers`, `canEditWorkCatalogue`; 1.42.0: the toggle rows are labelled "Projects — add & edit" / "Small Works — add & edit", §11); editing a job's details stays `canEditJob` (with the toggle any job of that kind, without it a job they manage) and booking writes stay `canWriteBooking` / `canDeleteBooking` (the jobs they manage). Before 1.33.0 a manager without a toggle saw only the jobs they manage.
- **Who reads which bookings** (1.33.0): admins, super admins and every manager outside operative mode read every labour, subcontractor and manager site booking (`readsEveryBooking`), with `enforcedReads` on or off; operatives read only their own labour bookings (their account id, a former or same-email id, or an `operatives` row with their email). Every labour, subcontractor and job-linked manager site booking still carries `jobCollection` (`projects` | `smallWorks`, `bookingJobCollectionFields`; the collection decides the kind, `jobKind`), written on create and edit by both apps. Before 1.33.0, with the switch on, a manager read only the bookings of their toggled kinds (`jobCollection ==`), of the jobs they manage and their own.
- **Job queries** — `jobQueryPlan(viewer, { documentIds })` → `{ seesEvery, queries }`: per collection `{ kind: 'all' }` for admins and managers, or for operatives `{ kind: 'documents', ids }` (single-document reads of each id spelling, `jobIdQueryValues`; a job list is refused to them). The `managed` query (`managerUserIds array-contains`) is gone (1.33.0).
- **Booking queries** — `bookingQueryPlan(viewer, visibleJobIds, { ownBookingIds })` → `{ readsEveryBooking, queries }`: whole collections for `readsEveryBooking`; otherwise (operatives) their own labour bookings: `bookings` `operativeId in` (`OWN_BOOKING_IDS_PER_QUERY` = 4 per query). Each query also takes the collection's download bound (`date >= bookingLoadStart`, §1); the composite indexes are in `firestore.indexes.json`. `visibleJobIds` and `enforced` no longer change the plan (1.33.0: the per-kind and per-job manager queries are gone).
- **Rules limits.** A query may look up 10 documents (`get` / `exists`) in total across all its `in` values, and a rule evaluation stops at 1000 expressions, so own-booking queries carry at most four ids and the rules compute the caller's role once per rule. `FIRESTORE_IN_QUERY_LIMIT` (30) is Firestore's own cap, not the plan's chunk.
- **Daily Overview and warnings** (1.31.0; 1.33.0): admins everything; on Warnings a manager sees only themselves, their reports and the jobs they manage (`warningScopeFor`, §5), on Daily Overview only themselves and their reports (`dailyOverviewScopeFor`, §6) — seeing every job is not seeing its warnings or its people.
- A viewer whose role changes re-plans on the next snapshot (an operative's job documents follow their own bookings, tasks and deadlines).
- **Job kind** (1.11.0): `jobKind(doc, collection)` — a `projects` document is a project and a `smallWorks` document is a small works job, whatever `jobType` says; `jobType` decides only when the collection is unknown. A project and a small works job sharing one id (older iOS builds) are both kept, each in its own list; `jobIdsInBothCollections` lists them for the owner and the booking backfill reports their bookings.
- **Switched by data in the rules** (1.12.1): the security rules apply this section's read scoping only when `enforcedReads` is on for the organisation (§18; written by the platform owner only, read by the rules — one document read per rule). While it is off, every member of the organisation reads every job and booking, as before 1.10.0, so the rules deploy does not wait for the backfills. Writes (§16: job create and edit, `canWriteBooking` / `canDeleteBooking`, tombstones, `jobCollection`) and every other rule are never switched by it. **1.26.0: the apps follow the same switch.** Each app reads `platformConfig/app` and passes `enforced: securitySwitchOn(config, organizationId, 'enforcedReads')` to `jobQueryPlan`, `bookingQueryPlan`, `readsEveryBooking` and `warningScopeFor`, so it plans whole-collection reads while the switch is off and the scoped queries while it is on — no release has to flip a constant first, and an app that supports 1.26.0 works either way. Web listens to the document and re-plans its job and booking listeners the moment the switch changes; iOS reads it on launch and on return to the foreground (as for `minimumIosBuild`) and re-plans then. Rollback is switching it off. An app build older than its 1.26.0 support still plans whole collections and loses the job and booking lists of managers without a job toggle and of operatives while the switch is on, so `minimumIosBuild` is raised to that build first.
- **Switched off until the backfill** (1.10.1; 1.11.0 kind reads too; from 1.26.0 only the default when no switch value is passed): `ENFORCED_READS_ACTIVE` is false. From 1.33.0 the value no longer changes `jobQueryPlan`, `bookingQueryPlan` or `readsEveryBooking` (staff read whole collections either way). It becomes true in the release after the owner has run the backfills (`managerUserIds` and, from 1.11.0, `jobCollection`), and that release ships before the rules deploy. 1.11.0 keeps it false: its by-kind booking queries are planned only once it is true.
- Known limit: an operative can read a job document by id without the rules knowing whether they are booked on it (the rules cannot query their bookings); listing jobs is refused to them.

## 18. Forced app update (1.12.0)

One platform document says the oldest build each platform may run:

`platformConfig/app` `{ minimumIosBuild: number, minimumWebVersion?: string, enforcedReads?: boolean, enforcedReadsOrganizationIds?: string[], privatePay?: boolean, privatePayOrganizationIds?: string[], ghostAccounts?: boolean, ghostAccountsOrganizationIds?: string[], organizationErasureEnabled?: boolean }` (`organizationErasureEnabled`, 1.15.0: the §20 eraser's kill switch, read only by the server); 1.38.0 also `dataCheckPanel?: boolean, dataCheckPanelOrganizationIds?: string[]` — the §27 Data check panel, a screen switch (`dataCheckPanelEnabled`), missing = hidden

- `minimumIosBuild` is an iOS `CFBundleVersion` (whole number). `minimumWebVersion` is dotted numbers (e.g. `1.12.0`). Both optional.
- An app reads that one document (`PLATFORM_CONFIG_APP_PATH`), before and after sign-in, and calls `appUpdateRequired({ platform, runningBuild }, config)`: true only when the running build is older than the minimum for its platform. A missing document, a missing field, a value that is not a number (iOS) or not a dotted version (web), or an unreadable running build is false — the gate never blocks by mistake. When true the app shows only the update screen.
- Anyone may read it (signed out too); only the platform owner writes it (§16). It is not organisation data and is not live: an app reads it on launch and when it returns to the foreground.
- **The security switches** (1.26.0; `SECURITY_SWITCHES`, `securitySwitchOn(config, organizationId, name)`, `securitySwitches(config, organizationId)`). `enforcedReads` (1.12.1) turns on the §17 job and booking read scoping; `privatePay` (1.13.0) limits rate history, the `operativeProfiles` mirror and timesheets to the people who may see that person's pay and keeps pay values off `users`, `operatives` and `managers` (§8, §16). Each is on for every organisation when its field is exactly `true`, or for one organisation when its id (exactly as stored) is in `enforcedReadsOrganizationIds` / `privatePayOrganizationIds` (`SECURITY_SWITCH_ORGANIZATION_FIELDS`), so the owner can switch one company on first; anything else is off. The security rules (`enforcedReadsOn(orgId)`, `privatePayOn(orgId)`) and the apps read the same value: from 1.26.0 the apps plan their reads and pay writes from it (§8, §17), so they work with each switch on or off. Order and rollback: `docs/SECURITY_SWITCHES.md`.
- **The ghost accounts switch** (1.34.0, owner decision 7 Nov "1b"; `ghostAccountsEnabled(config, organizationId)`, `GHOST_ACCOUNTS_SWITCH_FIELD`, `GHOST_ACCOUNTS_ORGANIZATION_IDS_FIELD`). `ghostAccounts` shows the **Ghost account** toggle on Add user / Add new operative (§11) on both apps; on for every organisation when it is exactly `true`, or for one organisation when its id (exactly as stored) is in `ghostAccountsOrganizationIds` — the same test as the security switches. A missing document or field is off, so the toggle stays hidden until the platform owner turns it on after the iPhone 1.34 update is out and `minimumIosBuild` is raised. It is a screen switch only: the security rules do not read it (no `firestore.rules` change), and existing ghosts — converted by the script or created while it was on — are listed, booked and work the same whatever it says.

## 19. Lapsed subscription (1.13.0)

Owner decisions round 4, Q3. When the organisation's subscription has lapsed (`hasAccess(billing)` false: canceled, unpaid, paused, incomplete, or past_due more than 7 days after the failed payment), both apps are blocked — not read-only — and show only `SUBSCRIPTION_LAPSED_MESSAGE`: "The organisation's Project Planner subscription has lapsed. Please contact your system administrator and ask them to renew." and Sign out (`subscriptionAccess({ billing, viewerIsAdmin, viewerIsPlatformOwner })` → `open` | `adminBillingOnly` | `blocked`). Nothing is ever deleted; access returns as soon as Stripe marks the subscription paid. Admins and super admins (`adminBillingOnly`) also reach the billing screen — on web Settings → Billing, and nothing else (`SUBSCRIPTION_LAPSED_ADMIN_NOTE`, web only). **On iOS everyone, admins and super admins included, sees only `SUBSCRIPTION_LAPSED_MESSAGE` and Sign out** (1.14.0, owner decision, App Store guideline 3.1.3): neither `SUBSCRIPTION_LAPSED_ADMIN_NOTE` ("renew in Settings → Billing") nor `SUBSCRIPTION_LAPSED_ADMIN_WEB_NOTE` is ever shown on iPhone, and the iOS app links to no billing or renewal. Missing billing is open (trials and organisations created before billing). The platform owner is never blocked.

The security rules enforce it (§16) from two fields the Stripe webhook writes into `billing` on every sync (`billingAccessFields`): `accessOpen` (= `hasAccess` at the time) and `accessClosesAt` (the end of the 7-day grace while a payment has failed), so the grace ends on time without another Stripe event. The rules close the organisation when `accessOpen` is false or `accessClosesAt` has passed; missing fields are open, so an organisation whose billing has not synced since 1.13.0 is closed by the apps only until its next Stripe event.

## 20. Company deletion (1.15.0)

Owner decisions round 6. The canonical module is `lib/canonical/organizationDeletion.ts`.
- **Who and where.** Web only: Settings → Billing → **Delete organisation**, shown to the super admin only (`isOrganizationSuperAdmin`). iOS has no delete control (it reaches the company only through "Delete my account", §11, later). The form explains what happens (`ORGANIZATION_DELETE_BODY`) and asks for the company name and DELETE (`organizationDeleteConfirmationError`; the server checks both again). There is no data export in the apps yet; the form says to email info@projectplanner.us for a copy first.
- **What deleting does** (server, admin SDK): cancels the Stripe subscription at the end of the paid period (`cancel_at_period_end`, no refund — refunds are made by hand in Stripe), writes `organizations/{orgId}.deletion = { status: 'scheduled', requestedAt, requestedBy, eraseAfter: requestedAt + DELETION_RESTORE_DAYS (30; 60 before 1.27.0) days, restoreBy: 'super_admin' | 'platform_owner' }` (`organizationDeletionWriteFields`), and emails the platform owner (info@projectplanner.us) and the person who deleted it through the email function's server mode.
- **Locked for 30 days** (1.27.0, owner decision: the same as file recovery; 60 before — a deletion already scheduled keeps its stored `eraseAfter`, so it is not erased sooner) (`organizationDeletionState(org, now)` → `active` | `scheduled` (locked, `due` once `eraseAfter` passed) | `erased`). Every member of the organisation, on every platform, sees only `organizationDeletedMessage(eraseAfter, timeZone)` — "This organisation has been deleted and will be erased on {date}." (organisation zone) — and Sign out; the security rules refuse its data (§16). The super admin also sees **Restore** when `canRestoreOrganization` (the deletion was theirs, `restoreBy` `super_admin`): restore removes `deletion` and, when the subscription is still in its period, resumes it (`cancel_at_period_end` false); a subscription that has already ended is started again from Billing. A company deleted with its last member's account (`restoreBy` `platform_owner`) is restored only by the platform owner. The platform owner is never locked out.
- **Erased on day 61** by a daily scheduled Cloud Function (`eraseDeletedOrganizations`, `functions/organizationEraser.js`; `organizationEraseDue`): users whose users document names the organisation and who belong to no other organisation (their tax ids, memberships, users document and Firebase Auth account; never the platform owner), every membership of the organisation, its invitations, its Storage files under `organizations/{orgId}/`, and the organisation document with every subcollection (bookings, timesheets, `userEmails` claims, …). People who also belong to another organisation keep their account and lose only this membership. An audit row `erasedOrganizations/{orgId}` `{ erasedAt, name, status }` is kept (`erasedOrganizationAuditFields`; no personal data). The eraser is idempotent and can run as a dry run. **Kill switch:** the eraser deletes nothing unless `platformConfig/app.organizationErasureEnabled` is `true` (written by the platform owner only, §18). While it is missing, false or unreadable, every daily run is a dry run that only logs each due organisation's id, name and counts (people deleted and kept, memberships, invitations). It is deployed off; the platform owner turns it on after reviewing the first dry-run log. `functions/organizationEraser.emulator.test.js` runs the eraser against the Firestore emulator (not in CI; how to run it is in the file).

## 21. Job lists (1.21.0, 1.21.1)

Which jobs appear where — every Projects and Small works list, their tabs and counts, the Home "active projects" figure, Clients, Site Audit, the weekly report's job lookups and every booking picker — is decided by `lib/canonical/worksLists.ts` on every platform, never by a store, listener or screen (owner rule after small works vanished on iOS: an iOS store kept live small works only, and a web store flag dropped inactive projects depending on which screen loaded last). Inputs are plain data (jobs as read by `parseJobDocument`, the viewer as `JobAccessViewer`, arrays of rows), so iOS calls the same functions through `CanonicalBusinessEngine`.

- **Stores keep every job.** A job store or listener keeps every document the reader accepts (`parseJobDocument`, `workDocuments`), live or not. Lookups by id (task job, weekly report rows, timesheet labels) use that full list, never a filtered one.
- **List membership** — `worksListMembership(jobs, kind)`: a Projects (`projects`) or Small works (`smallWorks`) list holds every document of that kind, once by id (two jobs with one job number both stay), inactive ones included. The collection decides the kind (`jobKind`), never `jobType`.
- **Who sees which job** — `visibleJobs(jobs, viewer, context)` (§17, `canSeeJob`): admins, super admins and (1.33.0) every manager outside operative mode see every job; an operative sees the jobs of their own labour bookings (a status that occupies the day, `bookingOccupiesDay`), of tasks assigned to them and of their deadlines (`operativeJobIds`; also the documents their `jobQueryPlan` reads); an account with no staff role sees the jobs it manages or is booked onto. **1.33.0 (owner decisions 6 Nov): the managers' per-job hide list `hiddenManagerUserIds` is no longer read or written** (`parseJobDocument` drops it; values already stored are left in place and ignored) — every manager sees every job. **The operatives' hide list stays:** an operative named in the job's `hiddenOperativeUserIds` (ids without case) does not see it, even when booked on it (`visibleJobs`), and the job's **View** tile (admins and managers with that kind's toggle) shows only the operatives list, "Hide this job from these operatives". The viewer is matched by every id and by email (`worksListPersonIds`: account ids, `operatives` and `managers` rows on their email), never by name. `context` = `{ managers, operatives, bookings, managerSiteBookings, tasks, deadlineJobIds }`.
- **My Projects / My Small works** (1.33.0, owner decision 6 Nov). Admins, super admins and managers (`hasMyWorksList`; never operatives) get a first filter on the Projects and Small works lists, and it is their default (`defaultWorksListTab` → `mine`; operatives `active`). Its jobs are `myWorksList(jobs, picks, viewer, context, kind)`: the jobs of that kind they see (`visibleJobs`) whose id is on their list (ids without case), in list order, every status; its count is that list's length. The list is the person's own picks, per person per organisation, stored in `organizations/{orgId}/worksListPicks/{uid}` (`myWorksPicksPath`, `parseMyWorksPicks` → `{ projects, smallWorks }`, each a list of job ids or `null`), so web and iPhone show the same list. A kind never saved (`null`) is pre-filled with the jobs of that kind they manage (`myWorksDefaultIds`, `isJobManager`) until their first save; a saved empty list stays empty and shows `myWorksEmptyTitle` ("No projects in your list yet" / "No small works in your list yet") with a button (`myWorksEmptyActionLabel`) that opens the ⚙. The ⚙ lists every job of the kind they see with a tick (`myWorksPickerRows`); ticking gives `myWorksPicksAfterToggle(current, jobId, picked)` and Save writes `myWorksPicksWriteFields(kind, ids, updatedAt)` (merge; the other kind left as stored; at most `MY_WORKS_PICKS_MAX` ids). A saved id of a deleted job is not shown and stays stored. Labels: `myWorksTabLabel` ("My Projects" / "My Small works").
- **Tabs and counts** — `jobWorkStatus(job, now, timeZone)` (`workStatusOn` on the organisation-zone day; the zone is required, never London or the device by default), `worksTab(status)` (inactive → Completed), `worksInTab(jobs, tab, now, timeZone)`, `worksTabCounts(jobs, now, timeZone)` → `{ all, active, upcoming, completed }`. All is every job listed, and every job is in exactly one of Active, Upcoming and Completed, so the tiles add up to All. A job's days come from `startDayKey`/`endDayKey`, else `startMs`/`endMs`, else `startDate`/`endDate`.
- **Bookable jobs** (owner decision) — `bookableJobs(jobs, viewer, context)`: the jobs a person may book onto — visible (`visibleJobs`), not inactive (`isLive` not stored false) and writable (`canWriteBooking` for `context.bookingCollection`, default `bookings`; a manager site booking for My Schedule). **The job's start and end dates are never checked**: a job past its end date, and a job with no end date, are bookable on any day. Optional trailing `dayKeys` / `timeZone` arguments are accepted and ignored. Operatives book onto no job. Every booking picker uses it: Book labour, My Schedule self-booking, the Daily Overview booking sheet, the job scheduler (Schedule booking) and Quick Add. A picker's tabs only sort its bookable list.
- **Home "active projects"** — `activeJobsCount(jobs, viewer, context, now, timeZone)`: projects and small works together that `visibleJobs` shows and that are active today — the same jobs the lists show under Active (1.33.0: every job for a manager — the managers' hide list is not read; an operative's jobs, less the jobs hidden from them, are their bookings, tasks and deadlines; manager site bookings count only for an account with no staff role). Before 1.21.0 iOS also counted an operative's manager site bookings and web ignored hide lists.
- **Job number** — `parseJobDocument` reads a numeric `jobNumber` as its text (4521 → "4521"); it read as blank on both apps.
- **Site map** (1.23.0, audit finding 17) — `siteMapPins({ jobs, bookings, managerSiteBookings, dayKey, timeZone, people })` → `{ jobId, bookingCount, peopleCount }[]`: one pin per job that is not inactive, counting that organisation day's labour bookings whose status occupies the day (`bookingOccupiesDay`; cancelled never) and manager schedule bookings on the job (project or small works, `isSiteLocationType`); `peopleCount` counts each person once (`personIdSet` over users, `operatives` and `managers` rows). The map opens on today in the organisation zone (`dayKeyInOrganizationZone`), never the device day. Before 1.23.0 web counted cancelled bookings.
- **Job tile badges** (1.23.0, audit finding 22) — `jobTileBadges({ jobId, bookings, managerSiteBookings, subcontractorBookings, tasks, people })` → `{ tentativeBookings, openTasks, activeUsers }`: Scheduling = the job's Tentative labour bookings (all time); Tasks = the job's tasks not completed (`normalizeProjectTaskStatus`); Active users = the people booked on the job (labour bookings not cancelled, manager schedule bookings on it), each person once under all their ids, plus subcontractor firms with an open (Confirmed or Tentative) booking. Before 1.23.0 a person under two ids, or with both a labour and a manager booking, counted twice. Job ids compare without case.

- **Annual leave on a job's scheduling week** (1.37.0, owner decisions 10 Nov). The project and small works week grid — web and iOS, every way in (the job's Scheduling page and its hub panel) — lists only the people booked on that job (labour, manager site and subcontractor bookings) somewhere in the visible days; leave never adds a person. For each person on it, every visible day of their annual leave is marked by `jobScheduleLeaveMarks({ people, holidays, dayKeys, timeZone, nonWorkingDayKeys, roster, day })` → `{ key, days: [{ dayKey, kind, onLeave, pending, slot, label, title, recordId, am, pm }] }[]` (`am` / `pm`: how each half is shaded, approved, pending or not leave): a day is leave when it is one of the record's leave days (`leaveDayKind` over `leaveRecordDayKeys` — a multi-day record skips weekends and the region's bank holidays), in the organisation zone, with `leaveDayKind`'s order (full beats half, then pending beats approved); leave is matched under every id the person goes by (`personIdSet` over the users documents and `operatives` rows, and the email of every roster row that is the person, as older iOS builds stored it — the function adds those emails itself), without case, never by name. An AM record and a PM record on the same day are the whole day ("AL", or "AL?" when both are pending; one approved and one pending shows both, "AL AM · AL? PM", each half in its own shade, the approved half's record as `recordId`). Quick add from a grid column books that column's organisation day (organisation-zone midnight of its calendar date), whatever the device zone. Both apps bucket the grid's days by the calendar date of each column (a stored booking by its organisation-zone day), so bookings and leave stay on the same column whatever the device zone, and a multi-day record is marked only once the region's bank holidays have loaded. Approved full day `JOB_SCHEDULE_LEAVE_LABEL` "AL", half day "AL AM" / "AL PM"; pending requests paler, `JOB_SCHEDULE_LEAVE_PENDING_LABEL` "AL?" ("AL? AM" / "AL? PM"); rejected never. `title` is "Annual leave · Full day" / "Annual leave requested · AM 06:30–10:30" (`leaveHalfDayLabel` under the working hours in effect that day). A day with no booking on the job shows a brown leave cell (a half day: the leave half brown); a day that also has a booking keeps its booking cell with a thin brown band across the top marked with the label (overtime keeps its own marking, the band on top). The leave mark never changes a booking's figures, a count, a warning or a write. **One booking colour scheme** (owner decision 10 Nov): booking cells on both apps use the iOS scheme and legend `JOB_SCHEDULE_LEGEND` — Standard (blue), Std + OT (blue with an orange hatched overtime part), OT only (orange hatched), Ann. leave (brown), Half day (a stored AM / PM, split) — one rule for both apps, `scheduleCellKind({ paidHours, overtimeHours, timeSlot })` → `{ tone, standardHours, overtimeHours, halfLabel }` over `scheduleCellHours(payrollHoursResult)` (`paidHours` = `totalPaidHours`, `overtimeHours` = the paid hours of the segments that are not the standard window; a day of several bookings adds them up first): a stored AM / PM is Half day, a stored OVERTIME slot or hours at a multiplier only is OT only, both is Std + OT, else Standard; a booking that pays 0 hours is a Standard 0h tile (never hidden, the leave band still drawn on it). The person's role pill (Op green, Admin / Manager blue, Sub purple) stays. Before 1.37.0 web coloured cells by the person's role and showed no leave; iOS showed approved full-day leave only on a day without a booking, matched by exact id. **One tile per person-day** (1.41.0, owner decision 11 Nov, option a: one scheme on both apps): the grid shows one tile per person per day, `scheduleDayCell({ bookings: [{ paidHours, overtimeHours, timeSlot, overtime }], onLeave })` → the `scheduleCellKind` of the summed hours (a stored AM / PM or OVERTIME slot decides the colour only when it is the day's one booking) plus `headline` — the day total and, with more than one booking, their number: "26h · 2 bookings" (`hoursText`, `countText`) — `standardText` / `overtimeText` underneath ("18h Std", "8h OT") and `overtimeLines`, the clock overtime at each rate (`multipliedHoursText` of `payrollMultipliedHours`, bookings at one rate added: "4h × 2 = 8h"). On a day with the brown leave band (`onLeave`) the "Std" / "OT" words are dropped so the figures fit ("18h", "8h"; "AM" / "PM" stay). Every booking stays reachable: tapping a tile with one booking opens it, with several a chooser of that day's bookings (each with its times and hours) opens the one picked.
