Before 1.41.0 web drew one tile per booking and iOS combined the day but opened only its longest booking (subcontractor firms: only the first). **A booked person is never unknown** (1.41.0): a row's person is `resolveBookingPerson` (former ids, same-email documents, `operatives` rows on their email, ghosts and catalogue rows with no email all resolve); **A booking is never hidden** (1.44.0, owner decision 14 Nov, question H option 1; replaces the 1.41.0 "left off the grid"): a booking whose id names nobody the device has loaded — usually a former or second users document the viewer cannot read (another company's document a manager is not allowed to open) — is its own row, `scheduleRowPerson(personId, { accounts, operatives })` → `{ key, accountId, operativeId, unresolved, label, idHint }`: `UNKNOWN_BOOKING_PERSON_LABEL` and the first eight characters of the stored id ("Unknown person · 6A9632BA"; bookings store no name of the booked person, `bookedBy` is who booked), marked with the amber chip `UNRESOLVED_BOOKING_PERSON_CHIP` "Not linked to a person" instead of the role pill, with no Quick Add; its booking tiles and their edit sheet work as any other. Labour and manager site bookings on both apps; web's grid now asks `scheduleRowPerson` (it kept its own id lookup). Admins link the id to a person in Settings → Data health (§11).

- **Subcontractor bookings on the week grid** (1.43.0, owner rule 14 Nov) — `subcontractorScheduleCell({ bookings, day, dayKey })`: a subcontractor's tile shows only its working window, start to finish (custom hours as stored, an overnight finish as stored; a named slot is its clock window on that day under the working hours in effect, `slotInterval` on `bookingStandardDayOn`) and, with several bookings, their number — never paid hours, the standard / overtime split or overtime lines (a firm is not priced by the organisation's working hours). One plain colour; the day's booking chooser shows the times only.

**Job edits** (1.31.0). A job's picker type is stored by `jobTypeStoredFields(name, collection)` → `{ jobType, customJobType }` (small works always `Small Works`; a project its default type, else `CAT A`; `customJobType` the name when it is not the collection's default, else null — a save removes it, so a custom type switched back to CAT A is cleared); `jobTypeDisplayName(job, collection)` reads it back. An edit of an existing job writes only what changed (`jobWriteFields` with the job as opened): the manager group (`managerIds`, `managerId`, `managerUserIds`) is not written unless the managers were changed, and then it keeps every stored account id the user did not remove; clearing the managers removes all three fields; switching from a map pin to an address removes `latitude` / `longitude`; dates are organisation-zone day keys (`organizationTimeZone`); a client id derived from the client name (`jobClientId`) is kept. `parseJobDocument` reads a legacy `assignedManagerUserIds` as `managerUserIds` when that is missing.

## 22. Variation numbers (1.22.0)

Variation (VO) numbers are written by one rule on every platform (audit finding 8: the two apps wrote different numbers for the same job).
- **A label's number is its trailing digits only** (owner decision): `variationNumberValue` — "VO-003" → 3, "V2-003" → 3; a label not ending in digits is 0. Before 1.22.0 iOS joined every digit ("V2-003" → 2003) and wrote "V2-2004" where web wrote "V2-004".
- **The next number** is `nextVariationNumber(existingNumbers, prefix, padding)`: one higher than the highest number among every variation of the job — open, submitted, closed and **deleted** — with the tracker's `prefix` and `padding` (`variationTrackers/{parentId}`; default "VO-", 3; `formatVariationNumber`). Gaps stay empty.
- **Duplicate check** (`variationNumberTaken(existing, voNumber, exceptId)`): a number is taken when another variation of the job has it, trimmed and without case, **deleted variations included** — a number that was ever issued is never reused (decision: before 1.22.0 iOS skipped deleted ones, so it could re-issue a deleted variation's number). The variation being edited is skipped by id without case; a blank number is never taken.
- Renumbering previews (`previewRenumber`) and tracker order use the same trailing-digit value.
- **One list from every source** (1.23.0, audit finding 21): `mergeVariationSources(collectionRows, fallbackRows, itemDocs)` — the `variations` collection, the older iOS settings-log fallback and the one-document fallbacks, one row per id (without case); the newer `updatedAtMs` wins and, on a tie, the collection row, then the item document, then the settings log. The open-variations badge is `openVariationCount(rows)`: rows not deleted whose status is `open` (any case). The rule both apps already used, written once.

## 23. Home, tasks and deadlines (1.23.0)

Audit findings 12 and 18: each app counted these on its own day (web London or the browser, iOS the device) and by raw ids.
- **One day.** Every figure uses the organisation-zone day of now (`organizationTimeZone`); a stored due date is turned into that zone's day with `dueDayKey` (any stored shape `holidayDocumentDayKey` reads).
- **Home overview** — `homeOverviewMetrics({ nowMs, timeZone, viewer, roster, operatives, managers, tasks, bookings, managerSiteBookings, holidays, nonWorkingDayKeys, scope })` → `{ dayKey, tasksDueToday, tasksDueThisWeek, tasksOverdue, outstandingTasksAllUsers, operativesOnSiteToday, managersOnSiteToday, peopleOnSiteToday, operativesOnALToday, managersOnALToday }`. A task is the viewer's when any assignee (operative or manager ids) is one of the viewer's ids (`personIdSet` of their account and email over the users, `operatives` and `managers` rows); due this week is today and the next six days; open tasks counts every task given that is not completed. People on site are the people with a labour booking today that occupies the day, or a manager schedule booking today on a project or small works — each person once (`peopleOnSiteToday` is not the sum of the two when one person has both). People on leave are active people with approved leave covering today (`leaveRecordDayKeys`), on the Operatives list (or with only an `operatives` row) or on the Managers list (§11); admins are not counted. **1.33.0:** with `scope` (the viewer's `dailyOverviewScopeFor`, §6) the on-site and on-leave figures count only people in it — a manager's own team; admins everyone. The "active projects" figure stays `activeJobsCount` (§21).
- **Tasks** — `isTaskOverdue(task, nowMs, timeZone)`: not completed (`normalizeProjectTaskStatus`) and due before today; a task due today is not overdue.
- **Deadlines** — `deadlineUrgency(item, nowMs, timeZone)`: complete → overdue (before today, even when blocked) → blocked → today → tomorrow → soon (2–3 days) → this week (4–7 days) → later; `deadlineDaysRemaining` for the days. The rule both apps used, on the organisation day.

## 24. Uploaded files (1.25.0)

Launch checklist 2.2: an uploaded file is never deleted outright or lost by someone who did not upload it. `lib/canonical/storedFiles.ts`; `storage.rules` enforces the same rule on the server.
- **Where files live.** Every upload is under `organizations/{orgId}/…` (`storedFileKind(path)` → `{ kind, organizationId, objectName, ownerUserId }`, `STORED_FILE_KINDS`): profile photos `userProfiles/{userId}/profile.jpg`; the company logo `branding/company_logo/{name}`; timesheet exports `timesheetExports/{seconds}_{name}`; qualification certificates `operatives/{operativeId}/qualifications/{qualificationId}/certificates/{name}`; variation evidence `healthSafety/{jobId}/variations/{name}` (web), `healthSafety/{jobId}/variationEvidence/{name}` and `variations/{variationId}/{name}` (iOS); site audit photos `siteAudits/{auditId}/images/{name}`; task files `tasks/{taskId}/{name}` (web), `tasks/{taskId}/files|images/{name}` (iOS); health & safety files (talks, RAMS, other, deadlines) `healthSafety/{jobId}/{category}/{name}`; (1.30.0, §26) PAYE price work / expense evidence `payeClaims/{userId}/{name}` (`paye_claim_evidence`, the person's own folder: they upload; they, their line managers and admins read; deleted as any other file; 1.45.0: also the evidence on their own timesheet price work and expense entries, §8) and personal site audit photos `personalSiteAudits/{ownerUserId}/{auditId}/{name}` (`personal_site_audit`: the owner only reads, writes and deletes — `fileDeletePermission` gives admins `owner_only`). Any other prefix is refused for everyone.
- **Every upload is a new object.** Its name is `storedFileObjectName({ uid, unixMs, extension })` → `{uid}_{unixMs}.{ext}` (the uploader's Auth uid; the original file name is never copied in), so a replacement — a new certificate, a new RAMS, a new logo — never overwrites the file it replaces and every stored link keeps working. `storedFileUploader(path)` reads the uploader and upload time back from the name. Two exceptions keep their fixed names because both apps read them: the profile photo (`profile.jpg`, replaced by the person or an admin; the replaced version is kept by the bucket's soft delete) and timesheet exports (`{seconds}_{name}`, no uploader in the name).
- **Who may delete** — (1.27.0, owner decision 3: kept as it is — an admin or super admin outside operative mode may still delete any file of their organisation; a deleted object is recoverable for `FILE_RECOVERY_RETENTION_DAYS`.) `fileDeletePermission({ storagePath, organizationId, viewerUserId, viewerRole, nowMs, uploadedAtMs? })` → `{ allowed, reason, undoEndsAtMs }`: an admin or super admin of that organisation outside operative mode (`reason: 'admin'`); for a profile photo also the person it belongs to (`own_profile_photo`); company logos and timesheet exports admins only (`admin_only`); every other file also its uploader, within `FILE_UNDO_WINDOW_MINUTES` (15) of the upload (`uploader_undo`; after that `undo_expired`) — for a retried upload or a file that uploaded but failed to link. Nobody else, managers included (`not_uploader`); never a file in another organisation (`other_organization`). Uploader ids compare exactly (Auth uids are case-sensitive). The server uses the object's `timeCreated`; an app passes the stored upload time when it has one, else the time in the name. A file whose name does not follow the pattern (uploaded before October 2026) has no provable uploader: admins only.
- **Overwriting an existing object** is allowed only to its uploader inside the same window (a retry of the same path), and for a profile photo to the person or an admin. An admin deletes but never overwrites another person's file.
- **Removing a file from a record** (variation evidence, a health & safety row, a task attachment, a certificate) deletes the Storage object only when `fileDeletePermission` allows it; otherwise the record drops its link and the object is kept, and the app says `FILE_KEPT_ON_REMOVE_COPY` ("Removed from this list. The file itself is kept so an admin can restore it."). Deleting a job, task, audit or H&S row never deletes its files.
- **Removed variation evidence is recorded** (1.27.0, owner decision 4). Unlinking an evidence file from a variation is one partial update (`variationEvidenceRemovalWriteFields(stored, { evidenceId, removedBy, removedAt })` → `{ set: { evidence, evidenceCount, removedEvidence } }`, built from the stored document, on `variations/{id}` and its `settings/variationItem_{id}` copy): the entry leaves `evidence` and `{ path, name, removedBy, removedAt }` is appended to the variation's `removedEvidence` list (`path` the Storage path, else the download URL). Nothing is ever taken off that list and no other save writes it. (Known gap: the apps keep this list append-only and web removes evidence in a transaction; the security rules do not yet refuse a staff write that shortens it.) `parseVariationDocument` returns it as `removedEvidence` (oldest first). Admins and super admins outside operative mode (`canViewRemovedEvidence`) see it in the variation detail under `removedEvidenceHeading(n)` — "Removed evidence (n)" — with the name, path, who removed it and when, to find and restore the file.
- **Server-side deletes.** Only the company eraser (§20, `eraseDeletedOrganizations`, day 61 after a company deletion) deletes Storage objects, with the admin SDK: everything under `organizations/{orgId}/`.
- **Recovery** is the bucket's Google Cloud Storage soft delete (deleted and overwritten objects are kept for the retention period and can be restored), switched on and checked by the platform owner in the Cloud console (Deploy notes 1.25.0). No app or rule changes bucket settings. **Retention is 30 days** (1.27.0, owner decision 5; `FILE_RECOVERY_RETENTION_DAYS`): deleted and replaced files, and the files of a company the §20 eraser has erased, are kept for up to 30 days, then permanently removed. Both apps' privacy policy (Retention) and data processing agreement (Deletion and Return) say so in one sentence, word for word: `DELETED_DATA_RETENTION_NOTICE` ("A deleted company can be restored for 30 days, after which its data is erased. Deleted files, including the files of an erased company, are kept for up to 30 days so they can be recovered, then permanently removed.").

## 25. PAYE overtime (1.29.0)

Owner-approved spec of 4 Nov ("PAYE overtime, price work and expenses", release 1.29). `lib/canonical/payeOvertime.ts`; the payrun is §4.5 (`payePayrun.ts`). Hours only — no money anywhere for PAYE. Price work, expenses and personal site audits are 1.30.

- **Who sees what.** On Timesheets, a button **Overtime, Expenses & Price Work** (`OVERTIME_HUB_LABEL`) sits below My Timesheets and above User Timesheets (`overtimeHubAccess({ viewer, roster })` → `{ showButton, mine, users }`): **My Overtime, Expenses & Price Work** for a PAYE person (stored `employmentType` `paye`); **Users' Overtime, Expenses & Price Work** for an admin and for anyone who line-manages at least one active person — admins see every active person, a line manager their reports (`overtimeUserSubjects(viewer, roster)`), with a search bar and a filter; tapping a person opens Overtime, Expenses and Price work, read-only except for the approval actions. A self-employed operative who line-manages nobody sees no button. Expenses and Price work are §26 (1.30.0).
- **The Overtime screen** opens on **Current PAYE payrun**, **Past PAYE payruns** (newest first, `payePastPayruns`) and **Custom date range** (start and end, then View Overtime; reaches older records and can generate a report). Until a PAYE payday is set it says `PAYE_PAYDAY_NOT_SET_MESSAGE` and only Custom date range works.
- **What counts.** `payeOvertimeLines({ personIds, bookings, organization, timeZone, range, reviews, requests })`: each booking of the person (every id they go by, without case) in the range, priced by `payrollHoursResult` under the working hours in effect that day (`overtimeForBooking`): weekday hours outside the working day × the weekday multiplier; a weekend set window, the hours outside the window × that day's rate; a weekend paid at a multiplier only, every hour × the rate; an unpaid break stays plain hours (`payrollMultipliedHours`). Only bookings with at least 15 minutes of overtime clock time (`OVERTIME_MIN_MINUTES`) are lines, one per booking (two jobs on one day are two lines). A line's title is the job number and name; under it the day, start–finish and the working day ("day 07:30–16:00"); the figure is `multipliedHoursText` ("1h × 1.5 = 1.5h").
- **The booking's overtime rate** is the stored `otMultiplierOverride` (the field the booking editors and `payrollHoursResult` already used; §8): 1–3 in 0.25 steps (`OVERTIME_MULTIPLIER_STEPS`, `overtimeMultiplierValid`, `OVERTIME_MULTIPLIER_MESSAGE`); missing = the organisation's rate. A stored rate is never clamped or re-priced on read. The cog and Edit Booking edit the same field, so changes go back and forth freely; editing times in scheduling keeps it.
- **States** (`OVERTIME_LINE_STATES`, labels `OVERTIME_LINE_STATE_LABELS`; 1.33.0, owner decisions 6 Nov): **Needs review** (never sent); **Waiting for line manager** (a pending request names the booking and it still matches — the line is locked for the person until it is returned or the request withdrawn); **Changed since you sent it** (a pending request names it but the booking changed, or a Return skipped it for that reason — `staleBookingIds`); **Confirmed** (no line manager: the person's own ✓ ✕ ⚙, a review with no request); **Approved** / **Changed by {name}** / **Declined** (`returnedApproved` / `returnedChanged` / `returnedDeclined`: a Return decided it and the booking is unchanged since; the name comes from `names`); **Booking changed — check it again** (`reopened`: it was decided or confirmed and the booking changed afterwards); **Booking removed — not worked** (`deleted`: an approved red X deleted the booking; drawn from its review, struck through at 0h); **Booking cancelled** (1.29–1.32 data only). The fingerprint is `overtimeBookingFingerprint(booking)` — day, slot, start, finish, rate, the No-unpaid-break toggle and status — so any change to the booking after a decision reopens the line (`overtimeLineIsStale`). 1.46.0: the fingerprint is still **written** exactly as before (the slot upper case with `_` / `-` as spaces: `…|CUSTOM|…` for a booking stored as `CUSTOM`, `…|CUSTOM HOURS|…` for `CUSTOM_HOURS`), so app builds on the 1.45 script write the same strings; it is **compared** only with `overtimeFingerprintsMatch` / `overtimeFingerprintKey`, which read the slot of both sides through `normalizeBookingTimeSlot` (`bookingFingerprintSlot`: `CUSTOM`, `CUSTOM HOURS`, `CUSTOM_HOURS` → `CUSTOM_HOURS`). A booking stored as `CUSTOM` on web and read as `CUSTOM_HOURS` on iOS is therefore one booking, and every fingerprint already stored still matches. Finished states are `OVERTIME_DONE_LINE_STATES`. Declined, deleted and cancelled lines count 0h (`overtimeLineHours`, `overtimeLinesTotal`).
- **✓ ✕ ⚙** (1.33.0, one state machine for the person and the decider: `overtimeChoiceAfter(choice, event)` → `{ mark: none | tick | cross, edit }`, drawn with `overtimeChoiceView({ choice, booking, organization, timeZone, role })`). **✓** fills green; a second ✓ clears it; ✓ after ✕ reverses it. **✕** fills red with the figure struck through; a second ✕ clears it; ✕ drops a cog edit. **⚙** changes start, finish and rate with live hours (`overtimeCogPreview`): the old figure struck through with the new one below, the mark cleared — the line then needs ✓ (or ✕); using the cog again unticks and replaces the figure. Words (`OVERTIME_PERSON_CHOICE_LABELS`, `OVERTIME_DECIDER_LABELS`): the person ✓ "Confirmed", ✕ "Not worked", ⚙ + ✓ "Changed"; the decider "Approved" / "Declined" / "Changed". Choices stay on the screen until sent or returned (Q-A7). **The person's red X** ("I didn't work these overtime hours") is `overtimeStrikeOutcome`: a weekday booking partly outside the working day is trimmed back inside it (06:30–14:00 on 07:30–16:00 → 07:30–14:00); a weekend set-window booking is trimmed to the window; a booking with no hours inside the day or window, or on a weekend paid at a multiplier only, is **deleted** (1.33.0, item f; 1.29–1.32 cancelled it) after `OVERTIME_DELETE_WARNING` — "Remove this booking?", the day · job · times, "This will remove this booking entirely. Your organisation pays a set multiplier for this day. If you still worked this day, use the cog to set the hours you worked and agreed.", Go back / Remove booking, "This cannot be undone. A manager can book it again." (`OVERTIME_CANCEL_WARNING` is the same object, deprecated). With a line manager the delete happens only when the Return approves the line; without one, directly. A manager site booking is trimmed the same way but never deleted (`OVERTIME_CANCEL_NOT_ALLOWED_MESSAGE` says to use the cog). Every booking change is `overtimeBookingWriteFields` → `{ set, remove }` of `workStartTime`, `workEndTime`, `timeSlot` (a Full day becomes `CUSTOM_HOURS` when its times change) and `otMultiplierOverride` only — a partial update; a delete is `overtimeBookingDelete` → the booking and its tombstone (`bookingTombstoneWriteFields`, §2), which carries `overtimeRequestId` when a Return deletes it.
- **Who changes what** (`canEditOwnBookingOvertime`). **1.32.0 (owner decisions 4 Nov), the app passes the person and the roster:** the Super Admin directly; anyone with an active line manager (`hasActiveLineManager`) as a **request** to that line manager — admins included; anyone with none directly where the security rules let them write the booking (an admin, or a manager `canWriteBooking` allows), otherwise as a request. (Before 1.32.0: a PAYE admin directly; a PAYE manager directly where `canWriteBooking` lets them edit the booking, otherwise as a request; a PAYE operative always as a request.) **Which days** (1.32.0, owner option 2): ✓ ✕ ⚙ work on every day up to the end of the current PAYE payrun, future days of it included — `overtimeEditableUntil(organization, todayKey)` (today when no PAYE payday is set) passed as `editableUntilKey`; later payruns stay locked. Online only. A direct change writes the booking and the line's review (`overtimeDirectWrites`); when the booking changes, `booking_hours_changed_by_owner` goes to the job's managers and the person's line managers (§13).
- **Buttons** (1.33.0, owner decisions 6 Nov: each button's readiness is one rulebook rule). **Send for approval** (`OVERTIME_SEND_FOR_APPROVAL_LABEL`, a person with a line manager) is green only when every line in the range is marked ✓, ✕ or ⚙ + ✓ (`overtimeSubmitReady({ lines, choices })` → `{ ready, undecidedCount, bookingIds, message }`); after a Return it stays grey until the person changes a line (✕→✓, ✓→✕ or ⚙ — the baseline is `overtimeLineBaselineChoice`: Approved / Changed → ✓, Declined → ✕) and only changed lines are sent (`overtimeSubmitLines`). **Return overtime** (`OVERTIME_RETURN_LABEL`, the line manager or a deciding admin) is green only when every waiting line of the request is decided (`overtimeReturnReady({ request, decisions, skipBookingIds })`; bookings the decider cannot read, or not the person's, are left out and do not block it). **Generate overtime report** (`overtimeGenerateReady({ lines, hasLineManager, choices, reports })`, `canGenerateOvertimeReport(lines, options)`): with an active line manager every line returned (Approved, Changed by {name} or Declined) or deleted, unchanged since, and no unsent change; without one every line confirmed (✓ ✕ ⚙ write directly); grey while a saved report of exactly this range is still up to date (`OVERTIME_GENERATE_SAVED_MESSAGE`).
- **Requests** (online only). `overtimeRequestLineFields` per line (keep, edit with the proposed start / finish / rate, or strike — a trim, or `cancel: true` meaning "remove this all-overtime booking", 0h after), each with the booking's fingerprint and the hours before and after; `overtimeRequestWriteFields` → `overtimeRequests/{id}` `{ userId, rangeStart, rangeEnd, status: 'pending', lines }`. Sending withdraws, in the same batch, any pending request every waiting line of which is sent again (`overtimeRequestsSuperseded`); the person may withdraw a pending request (`overtimeRequestWithdrawWriteFields`). The line managers and every active admin get the push (`overtimeRequestRecipients`, `overtimeRequestSubmittedMessage`); any of them may decide (`canDecideOvertimeRequest`; never the requester). **A request only ever names and changes the requester's own bookings** (`overtimeRequestLineFields` / `overtimeRequestWriteFields` with `personIds` and `bookings`; the Return re-checks every live booking with `overtimeBookingIsTheirs` and leaves out — and names — any that is not theirs). **A line manager can always decide** (1.33.0 item b, owner 6 Nov, superseding 4 Nov decision A "Needs an admin"): every booking on any id the person goes by (`overtimePersonIds`, `operatives` rows on their email included) — `overtimeDeciderMayWriteBooking({ booking, requesterUserId, requesterIds })`. **Return** (1.33.0, replaces the 1.32 one Approve and partial approval; `overtimeRequestAnyDecided` and `overtimeApprovalWrites` are removed) is the only action that writes the bookings and reviews, in **one transaction** that re-reads the request and every booking: `overtimeReturnWrites({ request, decisions, bookings, organization, timeZone, deciderId, requesterIds, unreadableBookingIds, platform })` → `{ bookingWrites, bookingDeletes, reviewWrites, requestFields, lineOutcomes, message }`; every booking change carries `overtimeReturnRequestId` = the request id (§16: so the person's line manager may change a booking filed under any of the person's ids). Per line: **✓** applies the person's line — keep (review `approved`), their cog (`edited`), their red X as a trim (`trimmed`) or, on an all-overtime day, the booking **deleted** with a tombstone carrying `overtimeRequestId` (`deleted`, listed in `deletedBookingIds`); **✕** means "I don't agree with this line at all" (Q-A2): the booking is left as it was (review `declined`); the person sees it red ✕ and can challenge (✕→✓), ⚙ it and send it again; **⚙ + ✓** applies the decider's own values (review `changed`). **Skip-and-name** (Q-A1): a line whose booking changed since it was sent is not applied and goes back to the person (`staleBookingIds`, "Changed since you sent it"); a booking deleted meanwhile, unreadable or not theirs is left out and named (`droppedBookingIds`). A line applied by a 1.32 partial approval is skipped (`alreadyApplied`). The request becomes `approved` with `decisions`, `decidedBy`, `appliedBookingIds`, `droppedBookingIds`, `staleBookingIds`, `deletedBookingIds`. The message is `overtimeReturnMessage` ("Returned 5 lines. 2 changed since they sent it — they need to send those again."). Then the person hears "Your line manager {name} has returned your overtime request" (an admin deciding: "{name} has returned your overtime request"; `overtimeReturnedMessage`, `overtimeDeciderIsLineManager`, `overtimeReturnedRecipients`). Notices open the payrun of the request (`overtimeNotificationRange`). Price work and expenses keep §26's own review (`reviewApprovalMessage`).
- **Reviews** `overtimeReviews/{userId}_{bookingId}` (`overtimeReviewDocId`): `{ userId, bookingId, collection, decision, fingerprint, reviewedBy, reviewedAt, requestId }`, read with `parseOvertimeReviewDocument`; `decision` is one of `OVERTIME_REVIEW_DECISIONS`: approved | edited | trimmed | cancelled (1.29–1.32) | changed | declined | deleted (1.33.0). A review with no `requestId` is a direct confirmation (no line manager). Old bookings have no reviews, so in a custom range they show as Needs review; nothing old is rewritten or re-priced.
- **Reports.** Generate overtime report saves a copy (`overtimeReportSnapshot` → `overtimeReports/{id}` `{ userId, userName, companyName, rangeStart, rangeEnd, kind: 'overtime', lines, totals, generatedBy, generatedAt }`, written once and never changed, `parseOvertimeReportDocument`) and builds the PDF from it (`overtimeReportRows`): company name and logo, the person, the range, one line per day and job with the final figures (start–finish, overtime hours × rate = total), and the total; hours only. 1.33.0 (owner 6 Nov): a line still red ✕ is listed at 0h, "Declined by {name}" (`declined`, `declinedByName`), and a booking an approved red X deleted at 0h, "Booking removed — not worked"; each line keeps the booking's `fingerprint` and `state`. Overtime is paid only through a generated report (the person sends it on). Several reports per payrun are allowed; Past PAYE payruns and the range's page list them. **Reopen** (1.33.0): if a booking changes after Send, Return or a report (a manager or admin edit, or a delete), its line reopens on the person's page and the cycle starts again for it (`overtimeLineReopened({ before, after, organization, timeZone, reviews, requests })` → `{ reopened, returned }`); the person is told, plus their line manager when the line had been returned (`overtimeReopenedRecipients`, `overtimeReopenedMessage`; sent as `overtime_request_reviewed` / `overtime_request_submitted`). A saved report then reads **Out of date** (`overtimeReportOutOfDate({ report, lines })`, `OVERTIME_REPORT_OUT_OF_DATE_LABEL`; a stored `outOfDate` / `outOfDateAt` is honoured too) and stays as a record; Generate turns green again for a replacement once the line is finished again.
- **Report layout** (1.33.0, owner 6 Nov): the overtime report looks like the invoice / timesheet report. `overtimeReportRows(snapshot, { timeZone, generatedByName, nowMs })` returns the header (`documentTitle`, `companyName`, `personName`, `period`, `generatedAt` / `generatedBy` / `generated` in the organisation zone via `reportGeneratedText` — a report is generated by its own person, so their name is the default), `columns` (Day, Job, Start–Finish, Overtime), `table` (one row per line: `day`, `job`, `time`, `overtime`, `note`, `declined`), `totalLabel` / `totalValue`, `emptyMessage`, `footer` and `outOfDate` / `outOfDateNote` (a report superseded after a line reopened). A line still declined at Generate (`declined` / `declinedByName` on the saved line) shows 0h with "Declined by {name}". The pre-1.33 `title`, `subtitle`, `rows`, `total` stay. Both apps draw only these fields.
- **The rate when booking** (1.32.0, owner decisions 4 Nov, decision A): every custom-hours **create** screen (Book labour, project and small works scheduling, the Daily Overview booking sheet, My Schedule) shows the overtime rate pre-filled with the organisation's rate for that day (`overtimeRateCreateControl({ organization, dayKey, timeZone })` → `{ organizationMultiplier, prefill, options }`), shown to the people who may book (never operatives). `otMultiplierOverride` is stored **only when the person changes it** from the pre-filled organisation rate (`overtimeMultiplierCreateFields({ chosen, organizationMultiplier })`); a booking left at the organisation's rate stores nothing and follows later rate changes, scheduled ones included, and only edited rows are marked on the weekly report (§7). A booking whose day the organisation pays every hour at one rate shows that rate. **Changing or moving a booking** (review fix r274): the Daily Overview booking sheet carries the rate on screen through every save — hours only, another job, Other — and through a move to the other collection (a new document), with `overtimeMultiplierMoveFields({ stored, chosen, organizationMultiplier, newDocument })`; a rate equal to the organisation's is removed, never stored. **Overtime rate on managers' own site bookings** (1.32.0): Scheduling, Daily Overview and My Schedule show a manager site booking's rate with `bookingHoursLabel` like a labour booking (the stored rate, else the organisation's).
- **Edit Booking "Overtime rate"** (Daily Overview and project / small works scheduling, both apps): "Organisation (×1.5)" or "Custom ×__" — `overtimeRateControl({ booking, organization, timeZone })` (shown on a custom-hours booking that has overtime), written with `overtimeMultiplierWriteFields`. Everyone who can edit the booking (`canWriteBooking`: admins, managers on their jobs) may change it, on any person's booking, self-employed included (it changes their timesheet pay); operatives never see Edit Booking.
- **What one change updates.** The booking is the only source: scheduling and My Schedule (times or rate; a booking deleted by an approved red X is gone, and an older `Cancelled` one stays hidden), Daily Overview (`bookingHoursLabel`), warnings (unbooked-labour cover and clashes recalculate; a cog change that makes a clash shows a normal warning and is not blocked), the weekly report PAYE section (§7), self-employed timesheets (§8) and the Overtime list all recalculate from it.

## 26. PAYE price work, expenses and personal site audits (1.30.0)

Owner-approved spec of 4 Nov ("PAYE overtime, price work and expenses", release 1.30; decisions log 4 Nov). `lib/canonical/payeClaims.ts` and `lib/canonical/personalSiteAudits.ts`; the payrun is §4.5 (`payePayrun.ts`); the hub and Users' list are §25 (`overtimeHubAccess`, `overtimeUserSubjects`). Online only. Money is the organisation currency (`resolveOrganizationCurrency`, shown with `formatMoney`). Before 1.32.0 there was no line-manager review; from 1.32.0 there is one (below), and accounts still pay on their side.

- **Where.** Timesheets → Overtime, Expenses & Price Work → My … (PAYE people) → **Price work** / **Expenses**. Each opens on the same three range buttons as Overtime — **Current PAYE payrun**, **Past PAYE payruns** (`payePastPayruns`, with the saved reports of that kind, `payeClaimReportsOfKind`) and **Custom date range** (`payeCustomRange`) — plus **Add** (`PRICE_WORK_ADD_LABEL` "Add Price Work", `EXPENSE_ADD_LABEL` "Add Expense"). Until a PAYE payday is set, `PAYE_PAYDAY_NOT_SET_MESSAGE` and only Custom date range works (and adds). The items of a range are `payeClaimsInRange(items, range)` — an expense by its date, price work when its from–to overlaps the range — oldest first; the list row is `payeClaimListRow` (name and figure; the date and, for price work, the job number and "Approved by …"); the total `payeClaimsTotal`.
- **Price work** (`priceWorkItems/{id}`). The Add Price Work sheet has Cancel at the top and Add Price Work at the bottom. Fields: name (`title`) and description (`details`); **job number** (`jobNumber`, 1.30.1 — see below); dates from–to (`startDate`, `endDate`, organisation-zone day keys; one day allowed); **approved by** — required, picked from `priceWorkApproverCandidates(person, roster, { organizationId })`: the active admins and managers (the people `lineManagerCandidates` offers: never an operative, a pending invite or a deleted profile) whose users document names this organisation and who are admins or carry the manager flag or role (a flagless member is not offered) — the same test as the rules — never the person under any of their ids, no free text; the approver's name is stored beside the id (`approvedByUserId`, `approvedByName`) and the approver is **not notified** (`PAYE_CLAIM_APPROVER_NOT_NOTIFIED_NOTE`); the figure (`amount`, above 0, with `currency`); optional evidence. `priceWorkValidationError` / `priceWorkWriteFields`.
- **Price work needs a job number** (1.30.1, owner decision 4 Nov; `lib/canonical/priceWorkJobNumber.ts`). One rule for PAYE price work and timesheet price work entries (§8): a new item or entry needs a job number — free text like an expense's, trimmed, 1–60 characters (`priceWorkJobNumberError`: blank → `PRICE_WORK_JOB_NUMBER_REQUIRED_MESSAGE` "Add the job number for this price work.", longer → `PRICE_WORK_JOB_NUMBER_TOO_LONG_MESSAGE`; `PRICE_WORK_JOB_NUMBER_MAX` 60). Add Price Work asks for it (`priceWorkValidationError` checks it after the name; `priceWorkWriteFields` writes it trimmed). Items stored before 1.30.1 without one stay valid: `parsePriceWorkDocument` reads `jobNumber` as '' (a stored number is read as text), nothing rejects or rewrites them, and every place the job number is shown — the list row and item detail (`payeClaimListRow`: dates · job number · approver), the report copy (`payeClaimReportSnapshot` now keeps price work's `jobNumber`) and its PDF summary (`payeClaimReportRows`), Users' read-only view — shows `PRICE_WORK_JOB_NUMBER_MISSING_TEXT` ("—") via `priceWorkJobNumberText`. The security rules require it on a new `priceWorkItems` document (string, 1–60); an update of an older item without one passes, and an update that has one keeps 1–60 (§16).
- **Expenses** (`expenseItems/{id}`): the fields of a timesheet expense entry (§8 — `title`, `details`, `jobNumber`, `date`, `amount`) plus `currency` and **at least one piece of evidence** before it can be added (`EXPENSE_EVIDENCE_REQUIRED_MESSAGE`); more are allowed (up to `PAYE_CLAIM_EVIDENCE_MAX`, 20). `expenseValidationError` / `expenseWriteFields`. 1.45.0 (owner decision 14 Nov): the amount field reads `EXPENSE_AMOUNT_LABEL` ("Amount inc VAT") on both apps' expense forms, and so does the expenses report's amount column (`EXPENSES_REPORT_COLUMNS`).
- **Edit and delete** (1.45.0, owner decision 14 Nov): the person edits or deletes their own price work and expenses — `canEditPayeClaim(viewerId, item)` (the item's `userId` is the signed-in account; an item filed under a former id is read-only, as the rules' update and delete hold), online only. Save changes is `priceWorkUpdateWriteFields` / `expenseUpdateWriteFields` → `{ set, remove }`, the changed fields only, the draft checked exactly as a new item (an older price work item without a job number asks for one once edited; an approver must still be a candidate; an expense keeps at least one piece of evidence); `userId`, `createdBy`, `createdAt`, `personUserId` and the currency are never rewritten; the edit sheet opens with `payeClaimEditDraft(item)`. Any change to the name, job number, dates, figure or the number of pieces of evidence moves `payeClaimFingerprint`, so a reviewed item reads Needs review again (`PAYE_CLAIM_EDIT_REVIEW_NOTE`). Evidence the edit removed (`claimEvidenceRemoved`) and a deleted item's evidence are deleted only inside the §24 undo window (`payeClaimEvidenceToDelete`). No security rule change: the rules already allow the person's update and delete of their own item.
- **Evidence** `evidence: [{ path, name, kind, contentType?, auditId? }]` (`parsePayeClaimEvidence`, `payeClaimEvidenceWriteFields`): `kind` `file` | `photo` | `personalSiteAudit`; `path` is a Storage path in the person's own claim folder, `payeClaimEvidencePath` → `organizations/{orgId}/payeClaims/{userId}/{uid}_{ms}.{ext}` (§24 object names; `payeClaimEvidencePathBelongsTo` is checked by the validators). **Readers keep only the person's own claim files**: `parsePayeClaimEvidence(list, { userId, organizationId })`, called by `parsePriceWorkDocument`, `parseExpenseDocument` and `parsePayeClaimReportDocument` with the item's `userId`, drops another person's file, a personal site audit photo, a URL or junk, so a viewer is never offered it. Photos (never SVG) and PDFs, 10MB; web turns a photo into a JPEG so the report can draw it. Apps store the path, never a download URL, so the Storage rules decide who opens it. **Files and §24** (`payeClaimEvidenceToDelete`): when an add is refused or fails after its uploads, the app deletes the files it just uploaded (the uploader's undo window), so no orphaned copy — a frozen personal audit copy included — is left where line managers and admins can read it; the same when a personal audit save fails (its photos). Removing an item deletes its evidence only inside that window; otherwise the files are kept for recovery, as every other removed file (§24).
- **Reports.** **Generate price work report** / **Generate expenses report** turns green once at least one item is in the range (`payeClaimReportReady`). It saves a copy, `payeClaimReportSnapshot` → `payeClaimReports/{id}` `{ userId, userName, companyName, kind, rangeStart, rangeEnd, currency, items: [{ id, title, details, jobNumber, startDate, endDate, approvedByName, amount, evidence }], totals: { amount, itemCount, evidenceCount }, generatedBy, generatedAt }`, written once and never changed (`parsePayeClaimReportDocument`), and builds the PDF from it: the first page is the summary — company name and logo, the person, the range, every item (date · job · name · figure, then its description, approver and evidence) and the total (`payeClaimReportRows`); then **one page per piece of evidence**, its heading (the item's name, date and figure) and details (evidence n of m and its name; the description) at the top (`payeClaimEvidencePages`): a JPEG / PNG drawn as large as the page allows, a PDF's own pages appended after its heading page, anything else listed with `PAYE_CLAIM_EVIDENCE_NOT_DRAWN_NOTE`, and a file that cannot be read with `PAYE_CLAIM_EVIDENCE_UNREADABLE_NOTE` (`payeClaimEvidenceDrawAs`). Several reports per payrun are allowed; the person shares the PDF themselves (web download, iOS share sheet).
- **Report layout** (1.33.0, owner 6 Nov): the price work and expenses reports look like the invoice / timesheet report. `payeClaimReportRows(snapshot, { timeZone, generatedByName, nowMs })` adds the header (`documentTitle`, `companyName`, `personName`, `period`, `generatedAt` / `generatedBy` / `generated`, `reportGeneratedText`), `columns` (`EXPENSES_REPORT_COLUMNS`: Date, Job number, Name, Amount; `PRICE_WORK_REPORT_COLUMNS` adds Approved by), each row's `itemId`, `date`, `jobNumber`, `name`, `approvedBy`, `amount`, `description`, `evidenceNote`, and `totalLabel` / `totalAmount`, `itemCount` / `evidenceCount`, `footer`. `payeClaimEvidencePages` gives one page per piece of evidence headed by its item (`itemName`, `date`, `jobNumber`, `approvedBy` for price work, `amount`) and the evidence's own title (`evidenceTitle`), with the item's description in `notes`.
- **Who sees what** (`canViewPayeClaims(viewer, subject, roster)`): the person, their active line managers (`lineManagerIds`, the users document) and admins outside operative mode — read-only for everyone but the person (`canWritePayeClaim`: only the person writes). In Users' Overtime, Expenses & Price Work → a person → Price work / Expenses: a PAYE person's items as above, read-only; a **self-employed** person's items are their timesheet entries (§8), read-only, in the same ranges — `timesheetClaimRows(sheets, kind, range)` over `parseTimesheetEntries` of their `settings/timesheet_*` sheets (the revised amount when their line manager edited one; each row's `jobNumber`, shown "—" for price work without one, `priceWorkJobNumberText`, 1.30.1).
- **Review** (1.32.0, owner decisions 4 Nov; `lib/canonical/payeClaimReviews.ts`): the Users' → a person → Price work / Expenses screen matches the overtime review — ✓ or ✕ on each item, one **Approve** button, the message `reviewApprovalMessage` ("Approved 2 of 5. 1 rejected; 1 not decided yet."). Reviewers are `canReviewPayeClaims(viewer, subject, roster)`: the person's active line managers and active admins outside operative mode, never the person under any of their ids. Approve writes one document per decided item in one batch, `payeClaimReviewWrites` → `payeClaimReviews/{kind}_{itemId}` (`payeClaimReviewDocId`) `{ userId, itemId, kind, decision: approved | rejected, fingerprint, reviewedBy, personUserId? }` plus `reviewedAt` and write metadata, read with `parsePayeClaimReviewDocument`. An item's state is `payeClaimReviewState(item, reviews)` — Needs review / Approved / Rejected (`PAYE_CLAIM_REVIEW_STATE_LABELS`) — from the review whose fingerprint (`payeClaimFingerprint`: kind, name, job number, dates, figure, currency, evidence count) is the item's current one, so an item the person edits after a review needs review again. **All records for this person:** items are loaded under every id of the person (`overtimePersonIds`). **1.33.0 (owner, 6 Nov; supersedes decision A for claims): a line manager can always review their report's items, whichever of the person's ids an item is filed under** — there is no "Needs an admin". `payeClaimReviewWrites` writes an item under another of the person's ids (a former invite id or a same-email document) like any other and adds `personUserId` (the person's current users id) to its review, so the security rules can match the line manager through that users document and check the item's id is one of the person's (`formerUserIds` / `sameEmailUserIds`, fields the person cannot change on their own document, §16). Who reviews is unchanged (`canReviewPayeClaims`); `payeClaimReviewOutcomeLabel` only names an item left out as not theirs. `personUserId` is fixed once written; a review stored without it (before 1.33.0) may get it from the line manager's next review of that item when it names the person they line-manage whose ids include the item's (an admin's update never adds it). A reviewer never writes the item itself (`canWritePayeClaim`). **Review is optional:** the person may generate a price work or expenses report at any time (`payeClaimReportReady`: at least one item in the range); the review informs the accounts team and never gates the report. Self-employed people's timesheet entries keep the timesheet review (§8).
- **Personal site audits** (`personalSiteAudits/{id}`). Site audits has a **Personal** section below Projects and Small works (`PERSONAL_SITE_AUDIT_SECTION_LABEL`) with **Create Personal Site Audit** (`PERSONAL_SITE_AUDIT_CREATE_LABEL`). Job number and job name are free text, not linked to any job, and print on the audit; the audit has a type, an optional title, a date, the author and 1–100 photos each with a title (`personalSiteAuditValidationError`, `personalSiteAuditWriteFields` → `{ ownerUserId, jobNumber, jobName, type, customTitle, date, authorName, items: [{ id, title, location?, comments?, assignee?, annotations?, imagePath, takenAtMs? }] }`; photos at `personalSiteAuditImagePath` → `organizations/{orgId}/personalSiteAudits/{ownerUserId}/{auditId}/{uid}_{ms}.{ext}`). **Visible only to the person who made it** (`canViewPersonalSiteAudit`, `personalSiteAuditsFor`): no admin, manager or line manager reads the list or its photos, and the company data export leaves them out. **Attached as evidence**, the app builds a frozen PDF copy (`personalSiteAuditPdfRows`) and uploads it into the person's claim folder; the evidence entry is `personalSiteAuditEvidence(audit, path)` (`kind: 'personalSiteAudit'`, `auditId`, named `personalSiteAuditTitle`). Line managers and admins see that copy with the claim, never the private list; later changes to the audit never change the copy. Both apps' privacy policy carries `PERSONAL_SITE_AUDIT_PRIVACY_NOTICE` ("Personal site audits are private to the person who created them. A copy attached to an expense or price work item is visible to that person’s line managers and admins, and is kept for recovery if the item is later removed."); the legal pack version is unchanged.
- **Data.** New collections only, nothing existing changes meaning: `priceWorkItems`, `expenseItems`, `payeClaimReports`, `personalSiteAudits` (`LIVE_COLLECTIONS`, `OPERATIONAL_COLLECTION_PATHS`); Storage kinds `paye_claim_evidence`, `personal_site_audit` (§24). The company data export includes the first three; the data clean-up script has a report-only `paye-claims` check (owners who are not people of the organisation, expenses without evidence).

## 27. Data check (1.38.0)

Owner decision 11 Nov ("Yes do all that"), after a Super Admin's iPhone listed 6 projects for a whole session while the database and web had 7 (a cold restart fixed it). This section decides nothing about what is shown — the rules above do — but checks that each app's loading layer (listeners, offline cache, merges) actually holds what the database holds. `lib/canonical/dataParity.ts`. Read-only: no write, no rule change, no new collection.

- **What is checked.** `DATA_PARITY_CHECKS`: projects, small works, users (the roster query `users` where `organizationId` = the open organisation), operatives, managers, clients, labour bookings and manager site bookings (within the app's loaded window, `bookingLoadStart`, §1), annual leave and tasks. For each list the app is listening to, the server count is a Firestore `count()` aggregation (server source, never the cache) of **exactly the query the listener runs** — the viewer's read plan (§17), so the rules never refuse it; a plan made of several queries counts each and sums them (`dataParitySumCounts`; the parts of `bookingQueryPlan` never overlap). A list the viewer reads one document at a time (an operative's jobs, `jobQueryPlan`) is not counted (`skipped: documentReads`). The device count is the documents the app holds for that query **before any rulebook filtering** (inactive, hidden or out-of-scope rows still count): for operatives, managers, clients, bookings, manager site bookings, annual leave and tasks it is the documents the listener received, **by stored document id with its case kept** (never a typed id that folds case, never the store's list after a reader or placeholder filter); for projects and small works the store's own list (what the screens use) plus the legacy `INITIAL-PLACEHOLDER` job document the readers drop (`workDocuments`), counted from the listener's documents as `readerDroppedCount`; for users the roster query's document ids.
- **The comparison** is `dataParityCheck({ lists: [{ key, serverCount, localCount | localIds, readerDroppedCount?, skipped? }] })` → `{ ok, action: 'none' | 'reload', mismatches: [{ key, label, serverCount, localCount, difference, direction }], reloadKeys, checked, skipped }`. Ids are counted once each. A server count that could not be read (offline, refused, timed out) skips that list; it never fails the check. **Not while editing:** a list whose listener's last snapshot carried this device's unconfirmed writes is skipped (`pendingWrites`) unless every queued write is confirmed within a few seconds (web `waitForPendingWrites`, iOS `waitForPendingWrites`); a list whose listener has not had a server answer for its current query yet is skipped (`awaitingServerSnapshot`) — rows a cache snapshot or an earlier bound left (the bookings window moved) linger until that server answer, so they are never counted. The listeners the check reads run with metadata changes on, so a server confirmation that changes no document still counts as that answer (and clears `pendingWrites`); such a metadata-only snapshot only updates the check's flags and never reaches a store, unless the listener still holds ids the server no longer has (then it is published as the collection). **Two looks:** a difference is acted on only when a second comparison `DATA_PARITY_CONFIRM_DELAY_MS` (5 s) later finds the same list different again: `dataParityConfirm({ first, confirm })` keeps only those, with the second look's figures.
- **Self-heal.** On a mismatch the app silently re-reads that list from the server and re-attaches its listener — only while the organisation the check ran in is still the open one (a company switch during the waits cancels it; iOS also forgets the recently confirmed jobs of a projects / small works list it re-attaches, so a job deleted elsewhere can leave) (web: the listener's query with `getDocsFromServer`, published as a server snapshot — keeping any document this device created or changed whose write the server has not confirmed yet — and a refused listener started again; a read plan's parts are restarted keeping their documents on screen until each new part's server answer, so nothing empties or flickers; iOS: the store's listener removed and attached again), waits a moment, compares again, and records the result: `dataParityLogEntry({ atMs, trigger, first, recheck })` (each mismatch `healed` true / false), kept on this device only (`dataParityLogAppend`, the last `DATA_PARITY_LOG_LIMIT` = 20; web `localStorage`, iOS `UserDefaults`). Nothing is sent anywhere.
- **When.** On open, when the app returns to the foreground and every `DATA_PARITY_MIN_INTERVAL_MS` (10 minutes) while it is open, but never more often than that on one device — every tab of a browser shares the last run through `localStorage`, read at check time — and never offline (`dataParityCheckDue({ trigger, lastCheckedMs, nowMs, online })`); **Check now** on the panel (admins, only while it is shown) runs at once when online. Production builds write nothing about it to the console or device log (debug builds only). A `count()` costs about one read per 1,000 documents counted, so a check is about ten reads. It never blocks a screen.
- **Who sees it** (owner change 11 Nov: nothing may look half-done in the launch app). The check and its self-heal run silently for everyone; **nothing about it is shown** — no panel, row or text in Settings or Data health — and its results stay in the device log only. The admin panel exists behind the `platformConfig/app` switch `dataCheckPanel` (`DATA_CHECK_PANEL_SWITCH_FIELD`; per organisation `dataCheckPanelOrganizationIds`, `DATA_CHECK_PANEL_ORGANIZATION_IDS_FIELD`): `dataCheckPanelEnabled(config, organizationId)` is true only for `dataCheckPanel` exactly `true` or the organisation listed (missing = off, the `ghostAccounts` pattern, §18), and `dataCheckPanelVisible({ viewerIsAdmin, dataCheckPanelEnabled })` shows it to admins only then. The switch never turns the check itself on or off; the security rules do not read it. **Where:** under Data health on both apps — web the Data health page (sidebar foot, admins), iOS Settings → **Data health**, a row shown only while the panel is visible (iOS has no other Data health screen); with the switch off neither app shows the row, the panel or any gap where it would be, and both follow the switch and an organisation switch without a restart. When shown, the panel reads `dataParitySummary({ entry, timeZone })` → "Data check: all lists match (checked 14:05)", "Data check: 1 list was reloaded and now matches (checked 14:05)" or "Data check: 1 list does not match the server (checked 14:05)" with one line per list ("Projects: server 7, this device 6 — reloaded, now matches"), in the organisation zone.
- **The two-app comparison in CI** (test machines only, never production). `lib/canonical/data-parity-fixture.json` (mirrored to iOS as `ProjectPlannerTests/DataParityFixture.json` by `scripts/sync-rulebook-from-web.sh`; its sha256 is `dataParityFixtureSha256` in the manifest) holds raw Firestore documents of a test company — a project and a small works job sharing one id, lower- and upper-case ids, a ghost, inactive and anonymised people, an invite placeholder, the placeholder job document, a numeric job number, a job with no dates, operatives / managers / clients with a placeholder row, a nameless client and two operatives whose ids differ only in case (`rawLists`: held as the listener's documents by stored id, case kept, exactly what the data check counts) — and a sequence of listener events: the first server snapshot, cache snapshots holding only some documents and empty ones, adds / edits / removals from another device, a refused listener with a document added meanwhile, the re-attach, and the removal of the twin's small works copy. Each app feeds every event through its **own** store and merge code (web `mergeOrgCollectionSnapshot`, `jobsFromListenerDocs`, `bookingsFromListenerDocs`, `rosterDocumentsAfter`; iOS `LiveSnapshotMerge`, `DataParityListenerLedger.merge` (the listener's documents the iOS check counts), `ProjectStore.liveWorkSliceResult` with `ProjectWorksMerge.smallWorksList`, `BookingStore.liveBookingsMerge`, `UserStore.liveRosterMerge`) and asserts after every step that it holds exactly `expectedHeld` (ids without case), that `worksListMembership` of the held jobs is `expectedVisible`, and that `dataParityCheck` against the database reports exactly `expectedParityMismatches` (only the refused listener's list, until it re-attaches). Web `lib/dataParity/dataParityFixture.test.ts` (`npm test`) and iOS `DataParityFixtureTests` (rulebook CI) run it; a release that drops or keeps a record wrongly cannot merge. **Cache snapshots** (found by the fixture): a cache snapshot never empties a list or drops a row already held — it only adds and updates; only a server snapshot removes a row (web `retainLoadedRows` with `authoritative: false`, iOS `LiveSnapshotMerge.apply`). Before 1.38.0 a shorter non-empty cache snapshot replaced the web list.

## How the rulebook changes

1. A rule change starts in `project-planner-web`: edit the function in `lib/canonical`, add a test in `lib/canonical/canonical.test.ts`, and edit this file.
2. Bump `Version:` (major: a breaking field or behaviour change; minor: a new rule; patch: wording) and add a `### <version> — <date>` entry under Changelog.
3. `npm run build:canonical` packs the script with this file's version stamped in, writes `lib/canonical/dist/canonical-manifest.json`, and mirrors the files into `../project-planner-ios` when it is checked out.
4. `npm run rulebook:handoff` writes the iOS follow-on prompt to `docs/ios-handoff/v<version>.md` and `docs/ios-handoff/LATEST.md`.
5. Enforcement: the pre-commit hook, CI (`.github/workflows/rulebook.yml`) and the Claude Code Stop hook (`.claude/settings.json`) all run `node scripts/rulebook-sync.mjs check` and refuse a change to `lib/canonical`, `firestore.rules`, `storage.rules`, `firestore.indexes.json` or this file without a version bump, a changelog entry, a fresh pack and a hand-off. A daily CI job compares the iOS repo's mirror with this version and fails when iOS is behind.
6. iOS runs `scripts/sync-rulebook-from-web.sh`, and its `RulebookParityTests` fail when its mirror, manifest or packed script differ from what web published.
7. **A write-shape change updates the cross-platform contract** (1.10.0): when an app changes what it writes to a shared collection, or a reader changes what it returns, regenerate `lib/canonical/cross-platform-contract.json` (`npx tsx lib/canonical/crossPlatformContract.samples.ts --write`; web samples come from the web writers, iOS samples are copied from the iOS payload builders named in `iosWriter`) in the same change. `crossPlatformContract.test.ts` fails until it is, and iOS `CrossPlatformContractTests` fails when its builders no longer produce `iosWritten`.
8. A gap found on iOS is reported to the web agent and fixed here first — never by diverging on iOS.

## Deploy notes

**1.46.0** (rates audit, §7 / §8): **no Firestore rules, Storage rules, index or Functions change**; no migration writes (stored revised amounts are read rounded, never rewritten). New optional fields on `settings/timesheet_*` `payrollLineReviews[lineId]` written by Edit hours: `revisedStandardHours`, `revisedOvertimeHours`, `revisedOvertimeClockHours`, `revisedOvertimeMultiplier`, `revisedStandardAmount`, `revisedOvertimeAmount`, `reviewedBookingFingerprint` (timesheets have no security rule, §8). Signed snapshot lines gain optional `bookingFingerprint`, `bookingHadOvertimeLine`, `appliedReviewKey` and `reviewPartOf`. **An older iPhone that saves a reviewer's sheet drops the new fields** (its `TimesheetPayrollLineReview`, `Project Planner/Models/TimesheetDraftModels.swift`, holds only `decision` and `revisedAmount`; the review then counts as before 1.46.0 — the amount, live hours, never stale), so `minimumIosBuild` is raised to the iOS 1.46 build **before** web 1.46 merges. Merge after 1.45.0 (#322); web deploys from `main` by itself. iOS 1.46 syncs the rulebook and ships the hand-off (`Views/TimesheetManagerReviewSupport.swift` Edit hours → `revisedPayrollLineAmounts`, `Views/WeeklyReportView.swift` → `bookingPaySlices` / `mergeBookingPaySlice`). **Raise `minimumIosBuild`** (`platformConfig/app`) to the iOS build that ships 1.46.0 (platform guardrail 4: a pay change): until then an older iPhone's Edit hours still stores its own hours-ratio amount (the bug this fixes) and its weekly report may differ by pennies; nothing it writes is refused or lost. Order: 1. iOS 1.46 syncs and is on TestFlight; 2. raise `minimumIosBuild`; 3. merge.

**1.45.0** (claim evidence on timesheet entries, edit and delete, "Amount inc VAT", §8 / §26): **no Firestore rules, Storage rules, index or Functions change** (timesheet entries have no security rule, §8; the evidence goes into the existing `payeClaims/{uid}` Storage folder, which the person, their active line managers and active admins already read; the rules already allow a person's update and delete of their own `priceWorkItems` / `expenseItems`). No migration writes; older entries keep their `receiptName` and are never rewritten. **Raise `minimumIosBuild`** (`platformConfig/app`) to the iOS build that ships 1.45.0 before people attach evidence to timesheet entries: an older iPhone saves the entry arrays from its own model and **drops every entry's `evidence`** when it saves that sheet (the files stay in Storage, unlinked). Order: 1. iOS 1.45 syncs the rulebook and is on TestFlight; 2. raise `minimumIosBuild`; 3. merge (web deploys from `main` by itself; it must merge after 1.44.0, #319).

**1.44.0** (paid day always derived, §4.3 / §8; bookings never hidden, §21 / §11): no Firestore rules, index, Storage or Functions change; no migration writes. **This changes pay** for organisations whose stored Monday–Friday `standardPaidHours` (saved before 1.20.0) differs from finish − start − the unpaid break: every unsigned timesheet, invoice, weekly report and Daily Overview figure of those days changes; signed sheets do not. **Raise `minimumIosBuild`** (`platformConfig/app`) to the iOS build that ships 1.44.0 before, or at the same time as, web deploys — an older iPhone would keep dividing by the stored figure and show different pay for the same sheet. Order: 1. iOS 1.44 syncs the rulebook and is on TestFlight; 2. raise `minimumIosBuild`; 3. merge (web deploys from `main` by itself; it must merge after 1.43.0, #318). Then check Settings → Data health → Bookings not linked to a person and link each id.

**1.43.0** (timesheet fixes, owner reports 14 Nov): **Firestore rules publish needed** (the settings read allows a person's own timesheet query, §16) — Firebase console → Firestore → Rules → paste `firestore.rules` → Publish, or `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project project-planner-f986c` — then check the live release (`docs/firestore-rules-notes.md` "After publishing"). Until it is live an operative's own-timesheet history query stays refused (web now opens the period as a new sheet instead of an error). Web deploys from `main` by itself. No index, Storage or Functions change; no migration writes (an empty frozen snapshot is rebuilt by the next reviewer visit, never by a script); `minimumIosBuild` unchanged (wording, gating and display only — no pay figure changes). iOS 1.43 syncs the rulebook and ships the hand-off.

**1.42.0** (the Projects / Small works toggle text; several managers per job): hosting only — merge after 1.40.0 and 1.41.0; web deploys from `main` by itself. No Firestore rules, index, Storage or Functions change (the rules already store and check a manager list; emulator tests added); no migration writes; `minimumIosBuild` unchanged (wording only, and both apps already saved several managers). iOS 1.42 syncs the rulebook and shows the rulebook's toggle text in Change user type.

**1.41.0** (scheduling week one tile per person-day; Replace existing booking; unknown rows dropped on iOS): hosting only — merge after 1.38.0, 1.39.0 and 1.40.0 (all merged); web deploys from `main` by itself. No Firestore rules change: the Replace batch (create, delete, tombstone) passes the live rules as they are (emulator test `tests/bookingReplace.rules.test.ts`). No index, Storage or Functions change; no migration writes; write shapes unchanged (cross-platform contract unchanged). `minimumIosBuild` unchanged: an older iPhone shows no Replace button and keeps its old tiles; a booking a newer device replaced simply disappears from it with its tombstone, as any delete. iOS 1.41 syncs the rulebook and calls `scheduleDayCell`, `bookingReplaceOffer` and `bookingReplaceWrites`.

**1.40.0** (annual leave: Team list, allowance by employment type, manual adjustments; owner steps, in this order, after 1.38.0 and 1.39.0 are merged):
1. **Merge** the pull request; web deploys from `main` by itself.
2. **Publish the Firestore rules right after the merge** — Firebase console → Firestore → Rules → paste `firestore.rules` → Publish, or `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project project-planner-f986c` — then check the live release (`docs/firestore-rules-notes.md` "After publishing"). Until they are live every adjustment save is refused ("Missing or insufficient permissions") and a line manager cannot decide leave filed under an `operatives` row. Static compile cost 240,265 (1.37.0: 236,705; budget 245,000); `firestore.rules` is 145,300 bytes, so `scripts/rules-budget.json` `firestoreRulesMaxBytes` is raised to 147,456.
3. **Deploy the push function** so `annual_leave_adjusted` sends a push (the function ships its own packed rulebook): `firebase deploy --only functions:inbox-push:sendDevicePushOnNotificationCreate --project project-planner-f986c`. Until then the inbox row is written and shown, with no push.
4. **iOS 1.40** syncs the rulebook (`scripts/sync-rulebook-from-web.sh`) and ships the hand-off.
5. **`minimumIosBuild`: yes — raise it to the iOS 1.40 build** (also because "PAYE" in any letter case now counts as PAYE for pay and timesheets — owner decision 13 Nov, item 19 — and an "immediately" type change stores today, item 18: an older iPhone would price those people and save those changes differently; guardrail 4: the allowance a person sees changes — self-employed people and anyone with no stored employment type lose the allowance figures and see days taken; a mid-year PAYE start is pro rata — so an older iPhone would show a different remaining figure for the same person). No index, Storage or migration writes; no data is changed or deleted.

**1.39.0** (booking hours explained and labelled; whole-row selection; My Schedule clash marker; Daily Overview moves in one batch): hosting only — web deploys from `main` by itself. No Firestore rules, index, Storage or Functions change (the batched move writes the same documents the two writes did, each allowed by the live rules); no migration writes; `minimumIosBuild` unchanged (no pay or hours change — an older iPhone keeps the old wording and has no clash marker). iOS 1.39 syncs the rulebook (new `bookingHoursExplanation`, `bookingShortfall` and the `explanation` field) and shows them on its booking pickers.

**1.38.0** (data check and the two-app comparison, §27): hosting only — merge after 1.37.0; web deploys from `main` by itself. No Firestore rules, index, Storage or Functions change (the `count()` aggregations use the queries the listeners already run, which the rules already allow); no migration writes; nothing new is stored in Firestore. `minimumIosBuild` unchanged (no pay, hours or write change). iOS 1.38 syncs the rulebook (new `dataParity*` functions, the fixture and the stamp) and adds its own silent data check (and the Data health card, hidden unless `platformConfig/app.dataCheckPanel` is on); an older iPhone simply does not run the check. To see the panel, the platform owner sets `dataCheckPanel` true (or lists the company in `dataCheckPanelOrganizationIds`) in `platformConfig/app`; leave it missing for launch.

**1.37.0** (annual leave on the scheduling week; one booking colour scheme; display only): hosting only — merge after 1.36.0 (#294); web deploys from `main` by itself. No Firestore rules, index, Storage or Functions change; no migration writes; `minimumIosBuild` unchanged (no pay, hours or write change — an older iPhone keeps its old cell colours and leave display until it updates). iOS 1.37 syncs the rulebook (new `jobScheduleLeaveMarks` and the stamp) and calls it on its Scheduling grid.

**1.36.0** (fixes only: person ids, organisation week, task assignment, wording; plus the owner-requested duplicate-name warning on Add User, app-side only): hosting only — merge after 1.35.0 (#292); web deploys from `main` by itself. No Firestore rules, index, Storage or Functions change; no migration writes. **`minimumIosBuild`: yes — raise it to the iOS 1.36 build before 1.36 web goes live** (guardrail 4: the rate history is now read under every `operatives` row of the person on both apps — a pay figure can change, e.g. 200 → 260 — so an older iPhone would price those people differently; planned with the 13–16 Nov switches). iOS 1.36 syncs the rulebook (its packed script changes in `ghostConversionCandidates`' `memberIds`, the new `usersWithSameName` and `DUPLICATE_NAME_*` copy, wording comments and the stamp; iOS Add User calls `usersWithSameName`). The owner-run ghost conversion script reads more users documents from 1.36.0; re-run its dry run before any `--apply`.

**1.35.0** (My Profile figures, §12): hosting only — merge after 1.34.0 (its rules published); web deploys from `main` by itself. No Firestore rules, index, Storage or Functions change; no migration writes; `minimumIosBuild` unchanged (a new read-only card). iOS 1.35 syncs the rulebook and ships the hand-off.

**1.34.0** (ghost operatives, §11; owner steps, in this order, after 1.33.1 is merged and its rules published):
1. **Merge** the pull request; web deploys from `main` by itself (Add user's Ghost account toggle (hidden until step 5), ghost badges, Give app access, Data health "Ghosts to check").
2. **Publish the Firestore rules** — Firebase console → Firestore → Rules → paste `firestore.rules` → Publish, or `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project project-planner-f986c` — then check the live release (`docs/firestore-rules-notes.md` "After publishing") and that an Operatives manager can still add and edit an operative. The change is R1 and R2 and the ghost self-write review fix (§16), on top of the live 1.33.1 rules; static compile cost 236,705 (1.33.1: 236,475). No index, Storage or Functions change.
3. **iOS 1.34** syncs the rulebook (`scripts/sync-rulebook-from-web.sh`) and ships the hand-off. Until it ships, an iPhone does not read ghosts as people (no email): they are missing from its lists and pickers, and an older iPhone may treat one as a pending invite.
4. **Raise `minimumIosBuild`** (`platformConfig/app`) to the iOS 1.34 build once it is on TestFlight / the App Store — before anyone adds ghosts in earnest.
5. **Show the Ghost account toggle** (owner decision 7 Nov "1b"): only after step 4, set `platformConfig/app.ghostAccounts` to `true` (Firebase console → Firestore → `platformConfig` → `app` → add field `ghostAccounts`, type boolean, value true; or list one company first in `ghostAccountsOrganizationIds`, an array of organisation ids). Until then the toggle is hidden on web and iPhone; existing and converted ghosts work either way. No rules change.
6. **Convert the existing no-login operatives** (owner decision Q-G2): `node scripts/convert-no-login-operatives-to-ghosts.mjs --project project-planner-f986c --all` (dry run: writes `ghost-conversion/ghost-conversion-check-list.csv` and `.md`, nothing in Firestore); fill `lineManagerIds` (and any name or rate the `issues` column asks for) in the CSV; then `node scripts/convert-no-login-operatives-to-ghosts.mjs --project project-planner-f986c --all --apply --line-managers=ghost-conversion/ghost-conversion-check-list.csv`; run the dry run again (0 candidates for the rows converted). Then check Settings → Data health → Ghosts to check.
**1.33.1** (security rules hotfix; no behaviour change): **Firestore rules publish needed.** Owner steps:
1. Open the Firebase console → project `project-planner-f986c` → Firestore Database → **Rules**.
2. Replace the whole text with `firestore.rules` from `main` (after this release is merged) and press **Publish**. (Or, from `~/Developer/project-planner-web` on `main`: `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project project-planner-f986c`.)
3. Check what is really live (`docs/firestore-rules-notes.md` "After publishing"), whatever the console says.
4. Check: the Rules **Monitor** tab's *Errors* count (21 before) stops rising; sending a notification (for example a booking that notifies the person booked) shows in their inbox.
No Functions, index or Storage change; no migration writes; `minimumIosBuild` unchanged; iOS needs no code change (it syncs the rulebook). After publishing, phones stuck at "Pending sync" behind a notification write are **expected** to clear by themselves (the SDK retries the queued write, which now answers) — this is untested, so the owner checks one affected phone: open the app online for a few minutes and confirm "Pending sync" goes away. Why: creating an inbox row with a server-time `createdAt` (rulebook 1.16.0, both apps), and also a notification update written as a merge-set or full set carrying a server timestamp, made the rules engine fail on the combined notifications `allow create, update` (1.32.2); the client never got an answer and every later write from that device waited behind it (`docs/firestore-rules-notes.md` "1.33.1 notifications crash").

**1.33.0** (owner steps, in this order):
1. **Merge** the integration pull request; web deploys from `main` by itself.
2. **Publish the Firestore rules right after the merge** — Firebase console → Firestore → Rules → paste `firestore.rules` → Publish, or `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project project-planner-f986c` — then check the live release (`docs/firestore-rules-notes.md` "After publishing"). Until they are live the new web writes are refused: Return with its booking deletes, reviews `changed` / `declined` / `deleted`, `personUserId` on price work and expenses, My Projects picks. Static compile cost 232,589 (1.32.4: 234,347); `firestore.rules` is 141,866 bytes (1.32.4: 137,867), so `scripts/rules-budget.json` `firestoreRulesMaxBytes` is raised from 140,288 to 144,384 (the compile cost, which is what Google limits, went down). **If 1.32.4 is not live yet, do its steps first** (functions, claims backfill, one hour) — the 1.33.0 rules read the caller's role from the claims too. No index, Storage or Functions change (the notification types are unchanged).
3. **Run the price work / expenses backfill** (one-off, so line managers can read items filed under a former id): `node scripts/backfill-paye-claim-person-user-id.mjs --project project-planner-f986c --all` (dry run: lists every item it would stamp, and any it cannot match with `?`), review the list, then the same command with `--apply`. It writes only `personUserId`, never changes anything else and can be run again safely.
4. **iOS 1.33** syncs the rulebook (`scripts/sync-rulebook-from-web.sh`) and ships the hand-off (`docs/ios-handoff/notes/v1.33.0.md`). Until it ships, a 1.32 iPhone still partially approves overtime, offers Cancel, and calls rulebook functions 1.33.0 removed (`overtimeApprovalWrites`, `overtimeRequestAnyDecided`, `OVERTIME_NEEDS_ADMIN_MESSAGE`, `OVERTIME_NEEDS_ADMIN_LABEL`): those screens fail on an iPhone that has synced 1.33.0 without the iOS work, so sync and the iOS work ship together.
5. **Raise `minimumIosBuild`** (`platformConfig/app`) to the iOS 1.33 build once it is on TestFlight / the App Store, so older iPhones get the update screen.
6. **Phase 2 (a later rules release, after step 5):** the overtime decider writes only `approved` (drop `pending`), the line manager no longer writes `status` → `Cancelled`, and the old booking indexes can go.

**1.32.4** (role and company checks from Auth custom claims, §16; design `docs/rules-claims-design.md`): **Functions deploy, a backfill, then a Firestore rules publish — in this order** (owner steps: `docs/scripts/backfill-auth-claims.md`). 1. `firebase deploy --only functions:inbox-push:authClaimsOnUserWrite,functions:inbox-push:authClaimsOnMembershipWrite,functions:inbox-push:authClaimsOnOrganizationWrite --project project-planner-f986c` (after `npm run build:canonical`; merge and deploy web with or after this step — web waits up to 20 s for claims when founding a company or accepting an invitation). 2. `node scripts/backfill-auth-claims.mjs --project project-planner-f986c` (dry run; stop if it lists anyone over the claims size cap), then `… --apply`, then the dry run again (0 to change). 3. Wait one hour (every signed-in token is refreshed with claims). 4. Publish `firestore.rules` and check the live release (`docs/firestore-rules-notes.md` "After publishing"); then check an operatives manager's operative edit and a booking delete still answer (emulator crash, notes "1.32.4 claims"). Until step 4 nothing changes for anyone. Rollback: publish the 1.32.3 rules again; the claims can stay. No index or Storage change; `minimumIosBuild` unchanged. **iOS:** builds without the 1.32.4 hand-off (marker listener) see a role, permission or company change up to an hour late after step 4; ship the hand-off before or soon after.

**1.32.3** (= 1.32.2 with a third compile-cost cut; no behaviour change): **Firestore rules publish needed** — Firebase console → Firestore → Rules → paste `firestore.rules` → Publish, or `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project project-planner-f986c` — then check the live release (`docs/firestore-rules-notes.md` "After publishing"). Nothing else: no index, Storage or Functions change; no migration writes; `minimumIosBuild` unchanged; iOS needs no change (the packed script differs only in the rulebook stamp). It can go out on its own or with the 1.33 rules, which it makes room for.

**1.32.2** (= 1.32.1 plus a second compile-cost cut; **publish this instead of 1.32.1**, which Google refused: console "unknown error", CLI 409 / 503): **Firestore rules publish needed** — Firebase console → Firestore → Rules → paste `firestore.rules` → Publish, or `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project project-planner-f986c`. No behaviour change from 1.32.1, so everything in the 1.32.1 note below still applies (its order, and raising `minimumIosBuild`). No index, Storage or Functions change; no migration writes; iOS needs no change (the packed script is unchanged apart from the rulebook stamp).

**1.32.1** (= 1.32.0 plus the compile-cost cut; publish this, not 1.32.0): **Firestore rules deploy needed** (`firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project project-planner-f986c`): the new `payeClaimReviews` rules (and the catch-all exclusion) and the overtime request decider writing `status` `pending` after a partial approval (§16). No index, Storage or Functions change; no migration writes. **`minimumIosBuild` is raised before 1.32 switches on** (owner decision 4 Nov; §18): an older iOS build stores `otMultiplierOverride` on every custom-hours booking it creates differently and approves overtime all-or-nothing, so the owner sets `platformConfig/app.minimumIosBuild` to the first iOS build that ships the 1.32.0 hand-off before announcing 1.32. Order: 1. merge; 2. iOS ships the 1.32.0 hand-off to TestFlight; 3. deploy the rules; 4. web deploy; 5. raise `minimumIosBuild`. Until step 3 a line manager's partial approval and every claim review are refused by the rules (the screens show the refusal).

**1.31.3** (includes 1.31.0–1.31.2, which never published): **Firestore rules publish needed.** Google's compiler refused 1.31.0–1.31.2 (CLI 503, console "unknown error"); 1.31.3 is the same rules with about 17% less compile work than the live 1.30.2. Publish in the Firebase console (Firestore → Rules → paste `firestore.rules` → Publish), or `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project project-planner-f986c`. No index, Storage or Functions change; no migration writes. Web is already on 1.31.x. Every 1.31.x behaviour change (rulebook 1.31.0–1.31.2 changelog) takes effect for older iOS builds on publish. If Google still refuses it, the fallback is `docs/rules-cost/1.30.2-plus-critical.rules` (live 1.30.2 plus the critical privilege fixes; see `docs/rules-cost/README.md` for what it leaves open).

**1.30.2**: **Firestore rules deploy needed** (`firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project project-planner-f986c`): `isTimesheetReviewer` never counts the sheet's subject, the self-signed subject's own write, and the self-manage toggles read for this organisation (`orgSelfManageFlag`) in `hbCallerSelfBooks` and `timesheetSubjectSelfSigns` (§16). No index, Storage or Functions change; no migration writes.
