Order: 1. merge; 2. web deploy (User Timesheets no longer lists the viewer; the review page never offers a counter-signature on one's own sheet); 3. deploy the rules; 4. iOS syncs and ships the 1.30.2 hand-off (iOS also needs its own-sheet review and `InvoicingView` fallback `user.hasLineManager` fixed, and its visitor flags read from the membership). **Who notices after the rules deploy:** an admin who is not self-signed (Timesheet self-sign off, not the Super Admin) can no longer counter-sign, export or write review decisions on their own sheet on any build — their line manager (or the other admins) signs it. On an iOS build before 1.30.2 such an admin's own save that carries `extraEntryReviews` (adding an expense or price work entry saves it as the reviewer's) is refused until iOS ships 1.30.2; self-signed admins and the Super Admin are not affected (their own writes stay allowed for older builds). Visitors: an admin whose toggles are on only in their membership of another company can now self-book / self-sign there; a person whose toggles are on only at home loses them in the other company — check Manage Users there if someone reports "Submit request" where they expected "Submit".

**1.30.1**: **Firestore rules deploy needed** (`firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project project-planner-f986c`): `priceWorkItems` creates require `jobNumber` (§16). No Storage, index or Functions change; no migration writes — older price work items and timesheet entries without a job number are left as they are and show "—". Order: 1. merge; 2. web deploy and deploy the rules together (web 1.30.1 sends `jobNumber`; a web 1.30.0 page still open would be refused when it adds price work after the rules deploy — reload fixes it); 3. iOS syncs and ships the 1.30.1 hand-off. **iOS:** if an iOS build that writes `priceWorkItems` without `jobNumber` has shipped, its Add Price Work is refused by the rules until it updates (raise `minimumIosBuild`); iOS timesheet saves are never refused for it (no timesheet rule).

**1.30.0**: **Firestore rules and Storage rules deploy needed** (`firebase deploy --only firestore:rules,storage --project project-planner-f986c`): the `priceWorkItems`, `expenseItems`, `payeClaimReports` and `personalSiteAudits` rules and the catch-all exclusion (§16), and the Storage paths `payeClaims/{userId}/…` and `personalSiteAudits/{ownerUserId}/…` (§24) — without the Storage deploy every evidence and personal audit upload is refused (any other prefix is refused for everyone). No index (the screens query `userId ==` / `ownerUserId ==` only), no Functions change (no new notification types; the approver is not notified), no migration writes. Order: 1. merge; 2. deploy the Firestore and Storage rules; 3. web deploy (Price work, Expenses, Site audits → Personal, the privacy policy sentence, export and clean-up); 4. iOS syncs and ships the 1.30.0 hand-off (until then iOS shows the 1.29 placeholders and no Personal section; nothing it writes changes). Nothing changes for a company until a PAYE person adds an item; until a PAYE payday is set only Custom date range works. The privacy policy gains one sentence with no legal pack version change, so nobody is asked to accept again.

**1.29.0**: **Firestore rules deploy and Functions deploy needed** (`firebase deploy --only firestore:rules,functions:inbox-push --project project-planner-f986c`): the rules are the line manager's overtime booking update and the `overtimeRequests`, `overtimeReviews` and `overtimeReports` rules (§16); the push function ships its own packed copy of the rulebook (`functions/canonical-business.js`), which must carry the new push types (`overtime_request_submitted`, `overtime_request_reviewed`) and the `overtime` toggle. No index or Storage change; no migration writes. Order: 1. merge; 2. deploy the rules and the functions; 3. web deploy; 4. iOS syncs and ships the 1.29.0 hand-off; 5. raise `minimumIosBuild` to that build **before** anyone uses custom overtime rates or operative overtime requests (an older build prices a custom rate correctly — `otMultiplierOverride` was already read — but cannot show, send or approve requests). **Nothing changes for a company until an admin sets the PAYE payday**, except the weekly report, where PAYE people move from the normal hours and pay rows into the PAYE overtime section. **With `enforcedReads` on**, only admins and the job's managers can approve overtime for people whose line manager does not manage the job (the line manager cannot read those bookings, §16 known limits); with it off (the default today) line managers approve their reports' requests, except lines on bookings filed under an `operatives` row, which need an admin or the job's manager. Only new optional fields and new collections: a missing `otMultiplierOverride` is the organisation rate, signed timesheets are never re-priced, and old bookings simply read as Needs review in a custom range.

**1.28.0**: **Firestore rules deploy needed** (`firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project project-planner-f986c`); no index, Storage or Functions change; no migration writes. **Behaviour change for admins who have no line manager:** before 1.28.0 every admin without an active line manager was self-approved (own leave saved approved, own timesheet self-signed). From 1.28.0 only the two self-manage toggles (or being the Super Admin) do that. **Before deploying the rules, the owner checks each company:** 1. open Manage Users → Admins (or, after the web deploy, Settings → Data health → "People who need a line manager") and list every admin who is not the Super Admin and has no line manager; 2. for each, either set a line manager, or have another admin turn on Annual Leave Management and Timesheet self-sign (PAYE: Annual Leave Management) in Edit User — the web deploy must be live first for the Timesheet self-sign toggle; 3. then deploy the rules. Order: 1. merge; 2. web deploy (the new toggle, the Data health list, Submit / Submit request and the counter-signature follow the toggles); 3. the owner's check above; 4. deploy the rules; 5. iOS syncs and ships the 1.28.0 hand-off. Until iOS ships, an iOS build on the 1.27.0 script still shows an admin without a line manager as self-approved. For such an admin (not the Super Admin, toggles off) the rules then **refuse** the older build's approved leave save ("Submit" saving `approved`); a non-admin's `weeklyReportOverride` owner save is refused too. The admin's own timesheet snapshot is still accepted because admins are timesheet reviewers in the rules. So do step 3 before step 4, and tell iOS users to update. An admin left with the toggles off and no line manager is not stranded: their leave and timesheets go to the other active admins, the Super Admin included.

**1.27.0**: **Firestore rules deploy and Functions deploy needed**, no index or Storage rules change: `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules,functions:inbox-push --project project-planner-f986c` (rules: the invitee's own pay-profile copy and the catalogue-operative pay reads, §16; functions: `sendEmail` `material_request` job checks). 1. merge; 2. deploy the rules and the functions; 3. web deploy (Add User passes the invite draft, the material send route sends `jobId` and checks the job, removed variation evidence is recorded and listed for admins, the privacy policy and DPA carry the retention sentence, the invited rate is copied on accept while `privatePay` is on, operatives managers read catalogue operatives' pay while it is on); 4. **owner, Cloud console**: bucket `project-planner-f986c.firebasestorage.app` → **Protection** → **Soft delete policy** → retention **30 days** → Save (Deploy notes 1.25.0 has the path); 5. iOS syncs and ships the 1.27.0 hand-off. Nothing changes for an organisation while `privatePay` is off, except the material job check, which applies only to requests that send `jobId`.

**1.26.1**: no Firestore rules, index, Storage or Functions change. Web deploy; iOS syncs and ships the 1.26.1 hand-off (editor seed and save baseline from `payEditorStartingValues`) **before** the owner turns `privatePay` on for any company — with it off nothing changes on either app.

**1.26.0**: **Firestore rules deploy needed** (`firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project project-planner-f986c`); no index, Storage or Functions change. Nothing switches on by itself: with no `enforcedReadsOrganizationIds` / `privatePayOrganizationIds` and the platform-wide fields missing or false, every read and write is as before. 1. merge; 2. deploy the rules; 3. web deploy (follows both switches live); 4. iOS syncs and ships the 1.26.0 hand-off (plans from the switches, no pay on shared documents while `privatePay` is on, rate history per person, pay profile overlay everywhere pay is shown); 5. raise `minimumIosBuild` to that build; 6. the owner follows `docs/SECURITY_SWITCHES.md`: backfills, then their own company in the lists, checks, then everyone. Rollback: remove the company id from the list (or set the platform-wide field false); no deploy.

**1.25.0**: **Storage rules deploy needed**, no Firestore rules, index or Functions change: `firebase deploy --only storage --project project-planner-f986c`. 1. merge; 2. deploy the Storage rules; 3. web deploy (variation evidence removal deletes the object only when `fileDeletePermission` allows; otherwise it unlinks and keeps it); 4. iOS syncs and calls `fileDeletePermission` before `deleteVariationEvidenceFile` (until then an iOS delete of someone else's evidence is refused and silently ignored — the link is removed, the file kept). 5. **Owner, Cloud console — soft delete** (bucket `project-planner-f986c.firebasestorage.app`): [ ] console.cloud.google.com → project `project-planner-f986c` → Cloud Storage → Buckets → the bucket → **Protection** tab → **Soft delete policy**: confirm it is on and set the retention to **30 days** (1.27.0, owner decision 5; the default is 7, up to 90) → Save. [ ] To restore a file: the bucket → **Objects** tab → switch on **Show deleted data** (or "Show: Live and soft-deleted objects") → find the object by its path (§24) → tick it → **Restore**; or `gcloud storage ls --soft-deleted "gs://project-planner-f986c.firebasestorage.app/organizations/{orgId}/**"` then `gcloud storage restore "gs://…/{path}#{generation}"`. A restored object keeps its path, so the stored download link works again once the record links it. [ ] Optional, longer history of overwritten files: Protection → **Object versioning** on, with a lifecycle rule that deletes noncurrent versions after N days (otherwise every replaced profile photo is kept and billed forever); restore with `gcloud storage cp "gs://…/{path}#{generation}" "gs://…/{path}"`. Soft-deleted and noncurrent bytes are billed as storage. Soft delete also keeps a company's files for the retention period after the §20 eraser runs.

**1.24.0**: no Firestore rules, index or Storage change. **Functions deploy needed** (`sendEmail`: the shared envelope and the optional `jobId` check): `firebase deploy --only functions:inbox-push --project project-planner-f986c`. 1. merge; 2. deploy the functions; 3. web deploy (Add User keeps valid line-manager picks on a type change; the send route uses the shared envelope; email subjects, From, Reply-To and CC are unchanged); 4. iOS syncs: Add User calls `lineManagerSelectionAfterLevelChange` instead of clearing the picks, and the material send may add `jobId`. Until iOS ships, iOS Add User still clears the picks on a type change (the person picks again; nothing is saved differently).

**1.23.1**: no Firestore rules, index, Storage or Functions change. Web deploy only; iOS follows the 1.23.1 hand-off (weekend window for named slots, its create paths, `userMissingLevel` if it shows a Data health screen). A person with no level who has a `managers` row is no longer treated as a manager on web (pickers, unbooked-labour kind) — the same as iOS today; they appear in Data health to be fixed.

**1.23.0**: no Firestore rules, index, Storage or Functions change (lists, counts, badges and clash checks are app-side reads; nothing new is written). 1. merge and deploy web; 2. iOS syncs and switches each screen in the hand-off to the 1.23.0 functions. Until iOS ships, the two apps differ only where the hand-off lists it (people lists, Home and badge counts, day boundaries off GB time, Select Operatives on a day after a working-hours change); no stored data changes either way. The setup wizard's weekend Unpaid break can be chosen by a new company from this release: an iOS build older than 1.19.0 prices it as paid (raise `minimumIosBuild` as the 1.19.0 note says if that build is still in use).

**1.21.0**: no Firestore rules, index, Storage or Functions change (list membership and bookable jobs are app-side reads; writes are still guarded by `canWriteBooking` in the rules). 1. merge and deploy web; 2. iOS syncs and switches every job list, tab, count and picker to the §21 functions (hand-off). Until then iOS still hides inactive small works and counts inactive jobs in no tab.

**1.20.0**: no Firestore rules, index, Storage or Functions change (the rules validate neither `standardPaidHours` nor booking slots). 1. merge and deploy web; 2. iOS syncs and ships the Working hours screen without the Monday–Friday paid-hours box, Full day / Custom booking chips and `leaveHalfDayWindows` for leave. Until then an older iOS build still offers AM / PM (stored and paid as before) and its Working hours save writes a typed `standardPaidHours` without `paidHoursDerived` (that policy is then priced on the typed figure, as before 1.20.0); raise `minimumIosBuild` (§18) once the iOS build is out.

**1.16.0**: 1. merge; 2. `firebase deploy --only functions` (`pushTokenSingleOwner` on users writes; `sendDevicePushOnNotificationCreate` with the shared payload and the actor-token skip) and `firebase deploy --only firestore:indexes` only if `firebase firestore:indexes` does not list `notifications (userId ASC, createdAt DESC)`; 3. `node scripts/dedupe-push-tokens.mjs` (dry run), review, then `--apply`; 4. web deploy (server-time rows, inbox retry, leave conflicts in booking flows); 5. iOS: sync, call `notificationRecipientAccountId` / `bookingCreatedRecipients` with the operatives rows, write `FieldValue.serverTimestamp()` through `notificationCreateFields`, switch company on push tap (`notificationOrganizationSwitch`), and `bookingLeaveConflicts` in its booking flows.

**1.15.0**: 1. merge; 2. `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules,firestore:indexes,functions` — the deletion lock and restore rule, the `orgMemberships.organizationId` collection-group index the eraser needs, the `material_request` sender and recipient rules, and the new daily `eraseDeletedOrganizations` function (needs the Cloud Scheduler API; the default service account deletes Storage files and Auth users). **Deploy it with `platformConfig/app.organizationErasureEnabled` missing or false**: it then only logs what it would erase. After reviewing the first dry-run log (Cloud Functions logs, "organisation erasure (dry run, switch off): would erase"), the platform owner sets it true; 3. web deploy (Delete organisation, Restore, Delete my account paths, the material send route); 4. iOS: call `canSendMaterialOrders` / `materialOrderRecipientCheck` on Send and history, the kept toggles on Change user type, the deleted-organisation screen (`organizationDeletionState`), and later Delete my account (`accountDeletionPath`).

**1.13.0 / 1.13.1** (one merge; full order in `docs/DEPLOY_1.11.0.md`, section "1.13.0"). 1. merge; 2. `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules` — `payProfiles` is private, the lapsed-subscription gate and the email-claim move are live, `privatePay` stays off so pay reads are as before; 3. web deploy (writes billing access fields on the next Stripe sync, writes pay to both places); 4. release iOS 1.13.1 (same), force it with `minimumIosBuild`; 5. `node scripts/backfill-pay-profiles.mjs` (dry run, then `--apply`); 6. a later release of both apps that reads pay, rate history and timesheets per person, then the platform owner sets `platformConfig/app.privatePay` true; 7. `node scripts/backfill-pay-profiles.mjs --apply --strip` — from here no colleague can read another's rate.

**1.12.0 / 1.12.1** (full order and checks in `docs/DEPLOY_1.11.0.md`, which now covers 1.10.0–1.12.1). The read scoping of 1.10.0 / 1.11.0 waits behind `platformConfig/app.enforcedReads`, so the rules deploy no longer waits for the backfills. 1. merge the web change; confirm in the Firebase Auth console that `info@projectplanner.us` is email-verified (the rules and the owner API require it) and that `platformConfig/app.enforcedReads` is not true; 2. straight away `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules,firestore:indexes,functions` — every 1.12.0 security fix and the 1.10.0 / 1.11.0 write rules go live, reads stay as before 1.10.0; 3. release iOS 1.12.0; 4. `node scripts/backfill-job-manager-user-ids.mjs` (dry run, then `--apply`); 5. `node scripts/backfill-booking-job-collection.mjs` (dry run, then `--apply`; again just before step 7); 6. a later release of both apps with `ENFORCED_READS_ACTIVE` true, after steps 4 and 5 report `0 to update` (force iOS with `minimumIosBuild`); 7. the platform owner sets `platformConfig/app.enforcedReads` true — rollback is setting it false, no deploy. Write `platformConfig/app` `{ minimumIosBuild: <build> }` only when a build must be forced.

**1.11.0** (superseded by the 1.12.0 order above; kept for history): `ENFORCED_READS_ACTIVE` stays false in this release. 1. merge the web change (deploys no rules); 2. `node scripts/backfill-job-manager-user-ids.mjs` (dry run, then `--apply`) — before any app build that queries jobs by `managerUserIds` reaches managers; 3. release iOS 1.11.0 (writes `jobCollection` on every booking it saves); 4. `node scripts/backfill-booking-job-collection.mjs` (dry run, then `--apply`; run again just before step 6 to catch bookings older builds saved); 5. a later release of both apps sets `ENFORCED_READS_ACTIVE` true — only after steps 2 and 4; 6. only then `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules,firestore:indexes,functions` (indexes may go earlier). Deploying the 1.11.0 rules while the apps still read whole collections refuses every manager's booking lists.

**1.10.0** (full order and checks in `docs/DEPLOY_1.10.0.md`): 1. merge the web change; 2. `node scripts/backfill-job-manager-user-ids.mjs --project <id> --all` (dry run, the default) and review the per-job diff; 3. the same with `--apply` (writes only `managerUserIds`, partial updates, idempotent, no write metadata — `updatedFrom` takes only `web` / `ios` / `android`); 4. `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules,firestore:indexes,functions`; 5. release iOS 1.10.0. The read rules (§17) refuse whole-collection job and booking queries for managers without a job toggle and for operatives, so app builds that predate 1.10.0 lose those lists for them until they update.

## Changelog

### 1.46.0 — 2026-11-14
- **Edit hours re-prices the booking** (§8, rates audit; new `lib/canonical/timesheetLineEdit.ts`): `revisedPayrollLineAmounts` prices the edited booking with `payrollHoursResult` (policy in effect that day, organisation zone, the booking's `otMultiplierOverride` and break toggle) and `payForHours`, and gives each line of the booking its revised amount (the standard line carries the overtime when the booking has no overtime line). Replaces the hours-ratio scaling on web (`revisedPayrollAmount`, deleted) and iOS (`TimesheetManagerReviewSupport.revisedAmount`). Owner case 07:00–16:00 edited to 07:00–19:00, ×2, £250 → £426.47 (was £250).
- **New optional fields on an edited payroll line review** (`PayrollLineEditReviewFields`): `revisedStandardHours`, `revisedOvertimeHours`, `revisedOvertimeClockHours`, `revisedOvertimeMultiplier`, `revisedStandardAmount`, `revisedOvertimeAmount`, `reviewedBookingFingerprint`. `payrollLineReview(sheet, lineId, live)` reads a review whose fingerprint differs from the live line's `bookingFingerprint` as stale (a booking changed after the edit is never paid twice: the reviewed 602.94 case is 426.47); `timesheetTotals` takes an edited booking's hours and standard / overtime labour from its review; `payrollLineReviewSnapshotParts` signs each part (8.5h + 3h × 2, not one day at the whole amount). A standard-line review carrying overtime is also stale once the booking has its own overtime line (`bookingHasOvertimeLine`); a signed snapshot line keeps `bookingFingerprint`, `bookingHadOvertimeLine` and `appliedReviewKey` (`payrollLineReviewKey`) and a later save tests each review against the line as signed with the same staleness rule (`payrollLineBookingKey` groups lines by collection and booking id); a declined line of an edited booking counts no hours. The fingerprint does not cover the rate (a rate change keeps the edited amount, as before). Older reviews without the fields behave as before.
- **Booking fingerprints compared with one slot spelling** (§8, §25; found by the iOS 1.46 build): every comparison — overtime line states, `overtimeLineIsStale`, the Return, reopen and report checks, `payrollLineReview`, `payrollLineReviewKey` — goes through `overtimeFingerprintsMatch` / `overtimeFingerprintKey`, which read the slot canonically (`bookingFingerprintSlot`, `normalizeBookingTimeSlot`), so a booking stored as `CUSTOM` on web and read as `CUSTOM_HOURS` on iOS is one booking and a review written on one app is not stale on the other. `overtimeBookingFingerprint` still writes exactly the 1.45 string (tested against the 1.45 script's output for every slot spelling), so iPhone builds on the 1.45 script are unaffected. New: `bookingFingerprintSlot`, `overtimeFingerprintKey`, `overtimeFingerprintsMatch` (`lib/canonical/bookingFingerprint.ts`). Parity fixture unchanged.
- **A signed sheet's invoice rows are its own** (§8, fifth review): `timesheetInvoiceRows` no longer drops a signed row because its live line is now a PAYE day, the period now has no self-employed days (`timesheetDays`), or a signed price work / expense row's day is now PAYE; only the signed row's own flags (PAYE / leave), a decline or an amount of 0 leave it out. The 1.10.0 test that dropped a signed row with a live PAYE line is updated.
- **Edit hours refuses blank times and start = finish** (`revisedPayrollLineTimesError`, `PAYROLL_LINE_EDIT_TIMES_MESSAGE`; `revisedPayrollLineAmounts` returns null); the 0.05h overtime cut-off applies with or without an overtime line; a PAYE day (`employment`) pays 0.
- **Revised amounts in pennies** (§8): `timesheetEntryEffectiveAmount` and `timesheetInvoiceRows` round a revised amount (a live review, and an edited signed-snapshot row — rounded, never re-priced); web writes them rounded; the export invoice total is rounded (`invoiceLinesTotal`).
- **Weekly report pay per booking** (§7): `bookingPaySlices` / `mergeBookingPaySlice` — web's per-booking pricing moved into the rulebook (output unchanged) so iOS sums the same pennies.
- **Owner Developer Overview "Timesheet value"** values a fully signed sheet with `timesheetTotals` over its signed snapshot (web `lib/owner/signedTimesheetValue.ts`); a sheet with no snapshot counts its price work and expenses only and is counted as such. Its own calculator (8-hour day, 1.5 cap, GBP; labour always £0) is deleted with its unused backfill script.
- Web fixes: Daily Overview's day hours pass the organisation zone to `bookingPayrollHours`; dead price helpers deleted (`buildPayLinesForDays`, `hoursFromSlot`, `collectSubjectDayEntries`).
- **Owner question, unchanged here:** overtime of 0.05h (3 minutes) or less is dropped from timesheets and invoices (both collectors, `TIMESHEET_OVERTIME_LINE_MIN_HOURS`) but counted on the weekly report.
- No security rules change.

### 1.45.0 — 2026-11-14
- **Evidence on timesheet price work and expense entries** (§8, owner decision 14 Nov; new `lib/canonical/timesheetEntryEvidence.ts`): entries carry `evidence[]` in the PAYE claim evidence shape, stored in the owner's own claim folder (no new Storage rule). `parseTimesheetEntries` reads it (the sheet owner's own files only, `timesheetEvidenceOwnerIds`; `receiptName` kept read-only, `CLAIM_EVIDENCE_LEGACY_RECEIPT_NOTE`); `timesheetOwnerEntriesError` checks the entries the owner added or changed (`timesheetOwnerTouchedEntries`): price work a job number, **a new or edited expense at least one piece of evidence**, at most 20 in the owner's folder; older entries never block. Shown on the sheet, review lists, Users' rows (`timesheetClaimRows` → `evidence`), the weekly report (`claimEvidenceCountText`) and as one page per piece of evidence after the invoice (`timesheetEvidencePages`). Before, both apps stored only a placeholder receipt name and uploaded nothing.
- **Any owner change reopens a timesheet entry review** (§8, review #322): `timesheetEntryFingerprint(row, kind)` of the stored row (no defaults, evidence paths sorted, kind from its array), returned by `parseTimesheetEntries` as `reviewFingerprint`; reviews store `reviewedFingerprint` and `timesheetEntryDecision` reads one that differs as stale; reviews stored before 1.45.0 keep the amount-only rule. `timesheetEntryReviewWriteFields` writes only the reviews a save changes. Readers are given every id of the person (`personIdSet`) so evidence on a sheet filed under an older id is kept.
- **Edit and delete** (§8, §26, owner decision 14 Nov): timesheet entries by the owner until the line manager signs (`canEditTimesheetEntry`, `timesheetEntryEvidenceToDelete`); PAYE items by the person (`canEditPayeClaim`, `priceWorkUpdateWriteFields`, `expenseUpdateWriteFields`, `payeClaimEditDraft`; editing moves `payeClaimFingerprint`, so a reviewed item needs review again).
- **"Amount inc VAT"** (owner decision 14 Nov): `EXPENSE_AMOUNT_LABEL` on every expense form (timesheet and PAYE, web and iOS) and the expenses report's amount column (`EXPENSES_REPORT_COLUMNS`); `PRICE_WORK_AMOUNT_LABEL` ("Amount").
- **One attach control** (owner decision 14 Nov): Add photo / Add site audit / Add file (`CLAIM_EVIDENCE_*` labels), web's one shared component for timesheet entries and PAYE Add / Edit Price Work and Expense; iOS Take photo / Choose photo / Add site audit / Add file.
- The claim evidence rules move from `payeClaims.ts` to `lib/canonical/claimEvidence.ts` (same names and behaviour; `parsePayeClaimEvidence` also takes `userIds`), with `claimEvidenceRemoved`, `claimEvidenceSameList`, `claimEvidenceListError`.
- `timesheetPriceWorkJobNumberError` counts an evidence change as an edit (adding evidence to an older price work entry asks for its job number, as any other edit does).
- Agreed-manager suggestions (1.43.0) confirmed as you type on web; unchanged.
- No security rules change.

### 1.44.0 — 2026-11-14
- **The Monday–Friday paid day is always derived** (§4.3, §8; owner decision 14 Nov, question A option 1): `weekdayPaidHours` is `derivedWeekdayPaidHours` (finish − start − the unpaid break, read into 1–12) for every policy, including the current, prior, scheduled and history policies saved before 1.20.0 without `paidHoursDerived`; the stored `standardPaidHours` is no longer read for pay (still written for older builds, prior and history written back as stored). `validatePayrollTimePolicy` checks the derived day for every policy. A weekend day with no "counts as" of its own reads 8h in the reader and the pay rule alike (`weekendCountsAsHours`, `DEFAULT_WEEKEND_COUNTS_AS_HOURS`; `payrollHoursResult`, `weekendBookingNote`, `weekendBookingBreakdown`, `weekendDayPreview`, `bookingHoursExplanation`, the setup wizard) — never the weekday paid day. Only unsigned timesheets change; signed snapshots and signed invoices are never re-priced, and a signed day-rate line reads from its stored days (web `signedLabourSlice`, the invoice's signed rows and the weekly report). Owner example 07:00–16:00, 30 min unpaid, booking 07:00–19:00 at ×2, £250 → £426.47.
- **No 20-minute half-day snap on weekdays for any policy** (§8): it was tied to `paidHoursDerived` (pre-1.20 policies only); custom weekday hours always pay their clock. Owner to confirm, as part of decision A = 1.
- Web: the 8-hour fallbacks on the paid-day division are gone where the rulebook value is always available — `resolvePayrollRate` / `resolveForTimesheetDay` drop their unused `standardDayHours`, `invoiceLinesForTimesheet` and `buildTimesheetInvoiceHtml` take it as required (the invoice HTML's rate-not-set fallback divided by 8 because the period page never passed it), the unused `estimatedDays` / `estimatedAmount` are deleted, `hoursBreakdown` and My Schedule's total drop their `|| 8`, and every timesheet line's `standardDayHours` is required (`timesheetHoursRateLine` no longer falls back to 8).
- **A booking is never hidden from a job's scheduling week** (§21, §11; owner decision 14 Nov, question H option 1, reverses the 1.41.0 "left off the grid"): `scheduleRowPerson` (an "Unknown person · 6A9632BA" row marked "Not linked to a person", no Quick Add) and Settings → Data health **Bookings not linked to a person** — `unresolvedBookingPeople`, `linkBookingPersonCandidates` (every users document, one per person, never the viewer: `LINK_BOOKING_PERSON_SELF_NOTE`), `linkBookingPersonRefusal` (fresh re-check in a transaction), `linkBookingPersonImpact` (what else the id brings: rate history, leave, timesheets, overtime, claims; a tick when pay may change), `linkBookingPersonConfirmMessage`, `linkBookingPersonDoneMessage` / `LINK_BOOKING_PERSON_ALREADY_MESSAGE`, `linkBookingPersonWriteFields` (an `arrayUnion` of the id onto the chosen person's `formerUserIds`). Web's grid now asks `resolveBookingPerson` through `scheduleRowPerson` (it kept its own `findUserByAnyId` lookup, which did not match `operatives` rows' accounts the same way).
- **One level for every roster shape** (§11, guardrail 1): `lineManagerCandidates` (and so `lineManagerSelectedIds`, `lineManagerSelectionAfterLevelChange`, `ghostCreateError`, the job manager picker's levels) reads a row whose `role` is the resolved `StaffAccountRole` object with `permissionLevelOf`'s rule — a flagless person is manager level, as on the stored document and in `lineManagerIds`. Flagless legacy managers now appear in every line-manager picker on both apps and pass the ghost save; operatives, pending invites and inactive people are unchanged. `priceWorkApproverCandidates` / `agreedManagerSuggestions` still require the stored manager flag or role (the security rules' approver test), so they do not change. Replaces web-only draft #320 (`lineManagerPickerRoster`).
- No security rules change (an admin already writes `formerUserIds` on a users document of their organisation, §16).

### 1.43.0 — 2026-11-14
- **Timesheet line times** (§8, owner rule): `payrollLineClockRanges` — the standard line shows the standard-window times, the overtime line the overtime times (two ranges for an early start and a late finish; weekend windows, every-hour days and overnight bookings from the pay rule's segments); `timesheetLineClockText` reads the clock part of a stored line. Wording only, signed snapshots keep their text.
- **Booking previews price the booking's own overtime rate** (§8, §25): Edit Booking, the Daily Overview booking sheet, Book labour, My Schedule and project / small works scheduling pass `otMultiplierOverride` to the preview, timeline, explanation and weekend rows; `weekendBookingBreakdown` takes it as an optional last argument. Owner example 07:00–19:00 at ×2 = 14.5h.
- **The signed snapshot waits for the bookings** (§8, owner bug): `timesheetSnapshotReady`, `TIMESHEET_SNAPSHOT_LOADING_MESSAGE` ("Loading bookings…"), `TIMESHEET_SNAPSHOT_FAILED_MESSAGE`, `TIMESHEET_PAY_INPUTS_LOADING_MESSAGE` — loaded only on a server answer of the whole query (cleared by a new query or a cache snapshot); Export waits too and builds the snapshot before stamping `exportedAt`; an empty frozen snapshot is rebuilt (`timesheetSnapshotNeedsRebuild`) only when a booking created and last written by the approval instant is missing from it, signatures kept, never a snapshot with labour lines.
- **No extras after the line manager signs** (§8, owner bug, option 1 pending owner confirmation): `timesheetOwnerCanAddExtras`, `TIMESHEET_SIGNED_OFF_EXTRAS_MESSAGE`; no signature is cleared any more. Half-signed sheets already stored read `unsigned` (owner question).
- **Agreed-manager suggestions** (§8): `agreedManagerSuggestions`, `AGREED_MANAGER_SUGGESTIONS_MAX` — the price work approvers' rule, replacing web's own role check.
- **Subcontractor tiles show the working window only** (§21, owner rule): `subcontractorScheduleCell`.
- **Security rules** (§16): a person's own settings query on `userId` is allowed (operatives could not list their own timesheets), never a direct read of a non-timesheet document; no non-timesheet settings document may gain a top-level `userId`; adding a missing `userId` to a sheet needs the sheet's id to name it. Emulator suite `tests/timesheetSettings.rules.test.ts` (privatePay on and off).
- Web: the open timesheet's live listener no longer skips its first snapshot and no longer re-subscribes (or reloads the sheet) on every roster refresh; the booking stores record what their last snapshot covers (`coverage`).

### 1.42.0 — 2026-11-13
- **The Projects / Small works toggle is add & edit** (§11, §16, §17; owner decision 13 Nov, option B — behaviour unchanged): every manager sees every job; with the toggle a manager adds jobs of that kind, edits any job of that kind and chooses its managers; without it they still edit the details of the jobs they manage. The rows now read "Projects — add & edit" / "Small Works — add & edit" with help that says this (`PROJECTS_TOGGLE_LABEL`, `PROJECTS_TOGGLE_HELP`, `SMALL_WORKS_TOGGLE_LABEL`, `SMALL_WORKS_TOGGLE_HELP` in `PERMISSION_TOGGLE_CATALOGUE`). The old help said a manager without the toggle sees only the jobs they manage (wrong since 1.33.0). iOS Change user type showed its own copy of the old text; it now shows the rulebook's.
- **Several managers per job** (§11 Job managers; owner decision 13 Nov): create and edit, projects and small works, on both apps, through `jobManagerCandidates` and the existing `managerIds` / `managerId` / `managerUserIds` (`jobWriteFields`). Both apps already allowed it; web adds `projectManagerIdsWith` (never the same person twice) and tests, iOS tests; emulator tests confirm a toggle manager creates with several managers and changes the list, and a manager without the toggle cannot.

### 1.41.0 — 2026-11-11
- **One tile per person-day on a job's scheduling week** (§21, owner decision 11 Nov, option a): new `scheduleDayCell` — the day total and the number of bookings ("26h · 2 bookings"), standard and overtime underneath, the clock overtime at each rate (`multipliedHoursText`); on a leave day (brown band) the "Std" / "OT" words are dropped (owner yes). Web now combines a person's day into one tile like iOS; a tile with several bookings opens a chooser so every booking stays editable (iOS opened only the longest booking).
- **A booked person is never unknown** (§21): iOS leaves a booking whose id names nobody off the grid, as web does, and logs it (`SCHEDULE_BOOKING_PERSON_UNRESOLVED`); tests lock that former ids, same-email ids, `operatives` rows and ghosts resolve (`resolveBookingPerson`).
- **Replace existing booking** (§5, §2, owner decision 11 Nov, build before launch): a third choice, Replace, on every orange double-booking row, offered only when the viewer may delete every overlapping booking (`bookingReplaceOffer`); the new booking, the old booking's delete and its tombstone are one batch (`bookingReplaceWrites`); `BOOKING_CONFLICT_DECISIONS`, `BOOKING_REPLACE_LABEL`, `BOOKING_REPLACE_HELP`, `BOOKING_REPLACE_CHOSEN_TEXT`, `BOOKING_REPLACE_COLLECTIONS`. Online only on iOS and Android (`booking.replace` in `OFFLINE_ACTIONS`). No rules change (emulator test).

### 1.40.0 — 2026-11-11
- **Annual leave → Team** (§9, owner decisions 11 Nov F18–F23; new `annualLeaveTeam.ts`): `annualLeaveTeamPeople`, `annualLeaveTeamTabVisible`, `annualLeaveTeamPersonIsCurrent`, `annualLeaveTeamEmptyMessage` — admins every current person (self-bookers included, never themselves), anyone else the people they line-manage; never deleted, inactive or pending people; the tab for admins and anyone with a report, whatever the Operatives toggle. Both apps drop their own list builders (web `canManagePersonAnnualLeave`, `canAccessOperativeAnnualLeaveDirectory` deleted). Web matches each person's leave under every id (`leaveRecordsForAccount`); the header year is the organisation's leave year.
- **Allowance by employment type** (§4.4, G25–G27): `annualLeaveBalance` takes `employment` — the allowance applies only on PAYE days (`balanceHasAllowance`, `leaveAllowanceForWindow`); new result fields `allowanceTaken`, `allowancePending`, `allowanceBeforeAdjustments`; no carry-over out of a self-employed year. `annualLeaveBankHolidayRange` and `myProfileSummary` take it too. Guardrail 3: self-employed people (ghosts included) and anyone with no stored `employmentType` see days taken instead of a remaining balance; no record changes.
- **Manual adjustments** (§4.4, H28–H35; new `annualLeaveAdjustments.ts`, collection `annualLeaveAdjustments`): `annualLeaveAdjustmentCreateFields`, `annualLeaveAdjustmentValidationError`, `parseAnnualLeaveAdjustmentDocument`, `annualLeaveAdjustmentsForPerson`, `annualLeaveAdjustmentLabel`, `canAdjustAnnualLeave`, `canViewAnnualLeaveAdjustments`, `annualLeaveAdjustedRecipients`, `annualLeaveAdjustedNoticeText`, `annualLeaveAdjustmentDaysForYear`; `annualLeaveBalance` `adjustments` → `adjustmentDays`. New notice type `annual_leave_adjusted` (push, company activity, destination `annualLeavePerson`). The "set remaining" override is no longer offered (still read until its year ends). `LIVE_COLLECTIONS`, `OPERATIONAL_COLLECTION_PATHS` and the cross-platform contract list the collection.
- **Fixes from the 11 Nov scan**: web writes `holiday_booked_for_you` / `holiday_cancelled_by_manager`; iOS checks `canDecideLeave` before booking leave for someone; leave filed only under an `operatives` row stays admins-only (owner decision 13 Nov, item 17 — an interim row path was removed before release); the 4 Nov "never delete leave" decision is marked done (iOS deletes are gone).
- **Security rules** (§16): `annualLeaveAdjustments` create-only rules and read scope, catch-all exclusion; `holidayBookings` line manager via an operatives row, and a line manager never moves leave to another person. Leave reads are not narrowed (follow-up; see §16).
- **Owner decisions 13 Nov** (same release): (17) leave filed only under an `operatives` row is decided by admins only — new `leaveDecisionRequester`, used by `leaveDecisionQueue` and both apps, and rules `hbCanDecide` without the row path, so repointing a row's email never hands out leave decisions; (18) an "immediately" (or backdated) employment-type change stores today as the effective date (`applyEmploymentTypeChange`), so past timesheets and leave never change; (19) `normalizeEmploymentType` reads "PAYE" in any case or with spaces as PAYE — **pay and timesheets follow it for people stored that way** (owner sign-off 13 Nov; `minimumIosBuild` raised with 1.40); the write paths store the lower-case word and Data health still lists non-exact stored values; (25) new users stay self-employed unless set up as PAYE.
- **Review r306 fixes** (same release): the allowance reads the employment type from the pay record (`annualLeaveEmploymentInput`; the pay profile while `privatePay` is on; unreadable → the whole year counts) and its notes name the type only for viewers who may see pay (`annualLeaveBalanceNotes`, `canViewPersonPay`); Data health lists people whose stored employment type is missing or not exactly `paye` / `self_employed` (`usersMissingEmploymentType`; `normalizeEmploymentType` is unchanged — treating "PAYE" in any case as PAYE is an owner question); the balance returns the shown sums (`shownTaken`, `shownPending`, `notCountedTaken`, `notCountedPending`); the PAYE window comes from the stored change (`employmentTypeTransition`), never a walk over every day; screens show no balance until the pay record and adjustments have loaded, and a refused adjustments read is an error; adjustments store no author name (`annualLeaveAdjustmentAuthorName` from the roster), are never for the writer and only for the current leave year (`allowedYearKeys`); the adjustment push carries no reason (inbox `detail`, new optional notification field). Rules: `createdAt` the request time, a year key in the calendar year before, of or after the request, a reason with a non-space character, no `createdByName`, an admin never adjusts their own operatives row, reads go through `overtimeViewer` (organisation access, billing and deletion first); the holiday update refuses a line manager's change of `userId` / `operativeId` with one `hasAny` and decides through `hbCanDecide`. Compile-cost cuts with no allow/deny change: single-use helpers inlined (`canViewRateRow`, `callerLineManagesUserOrRow`, `orgRootOperativesManagerRemovesMember`, `callerJobToggle`, the adjustment admin check), `hbLineManages` folded into `hbCanDecide`, `isPlatformOwner` one email expression, `lineManagerIdList` with lets, `writeMetadataKeysValid` reads written keys directly. Static compile cost 236,705 → **234,014** with the 13 Nov row-path removal (below main).

### 1.39.0 — 2026-11-11
- **Booking hours explained** (§4.3, owner request 11 Nov): new `bookingHoursExplanation` and `bookingShortfall` (new `bookingHoursExplanation.ts`); `weekendBookingBreakdown` rows gain `explanation: { rule, steps }`. Every booking picker on both apps shows the day's rule and the steps behind each figure (owner examples: "Booked 07:30–10:30 = 3h of the 6.5h window → 8h × 3 ÷ 6.5 = 3.69h"; "07:30–14:00 = 8h (full day) + 14:00–16:00 = 2h × 2 = 4h → 12h"; "Sunday every hour at 2×: 07:30–10:30 = 3h × 2 = 6h"), and weekdays the same (working day, unpaid break, overtime at the multiplier). iOS "Missing to standard day" becomes `bookingShortfall`'s "Short of a full Saturday (8h)", compared with that day's own full day; none on a day paid every hour at a multiplier. Wording only — no figure, write or rule changes; the parity fixture gains the `explanation` field and six cases.
- **Whole-row selection** (owner request 11 Nov, UI only): on iOS a person's whole tile selects or deselects them on Select people and the other people pickers; web rows were already whole-row buttons and now say whether they are selected (`aria-pressed`). No rule change.
- **Hours, overtime and break labels** (§4.3, owner answers 11 Nov): new `overtimeTotalText`, `overtimeIncludedText`, `bookedWithOvertimeText`, `bookingHoursCountText`, `onSiteHoursText`, `saveHoursLabel`, `bookingBreakText`, `bookingHoursTiles` (new `bookingHoursText.ts`). Daily Overview tile "12 OT 1.5×" → "12h OT × 1.5 = 18h"; job line "60.5h booked · +12h OT" → "60.5h booked (incl. 12h OT × 1.5)"; Daily Overview booking rows show the times and the hours pill only; My Schedule total "incl. 6h OT × 1.5 = 9h"; Edit booking tiles "9 Standard (−0.5 break)", "6 OT" (no break tile); "break included" → "30 min unpaid break 11:30–12:00"; "30 mins deducted around midday" → "30 mins deducted 11:30–12:00"; "17.5h paid" → "17.5 hours", "Save · 17.5 hours". Figures unchanged.
- **The break in the hours explanation** (§4.3, owner answer 11 Nov): `bookingHoursExplanation` names the lunch break on the part it falls in, from that day's working hours (unpaid and taken off, paid and not deducted, or removed on the booking), and gains `summary` (one line). Its `steps` change wording: a weekday or set-window break is no longer a separate "− 0.5h unpaid break" step (hours at a multiplier keep it); the parity fixture is regenerated.
- **My Schedule booking clash marker** (§10, owner answer 11 Nov): new `myScheduleClashMarkers`, `MY_SCHEDULE_CLASH_TITLE`, `MY_SCHEDULE_CLASH_APPROVED_TITLE`, `MY_SCHEDULE_CLASH_APPROVED_MESSAGE`, `MY_SCHEDULE_CLASH_ASK_MANAGER`, `MY_SCHEDULE_CLASH_OPEN_WARNINGS` (new `myScheduleClash.ts`), on the Warnings clash rules. Display only.
- **Review fixes (11 Nov)**: `myScheduleClashMarkers` takes `scanDayKeys` and offers "Open Warnings" only for a clash Warnings lists now (`MY_SCHEDULE_CLASH_NOT_IN_PERIOD`, `MY_SCHEDULE_CLASH_DISMISSED`), with the viewer's real scope; `bookingShortfall` takes the day's bookings and measures coverage of the working day (lunch window excluded), never paid hours (breaking for the 1.39.0 draft that took paid hours); new `bookingBreakDeductionText`; `bookingBreakText.toggleLabel`; `bookingHoursExplanation` names a booking's own overtime rate.
- **Daily Overview moves in one batch** (§6, owner answer 11 Nov, web): the new booking, the old booking's delete and its tombstone are one write (was a create then a delete).
- Not in this release (reported to the owner): "Replace existing booking" beside a double-booking row (item 2) — see the PR.
- Merges after 1.38.0 (data parity, merged 12 Nov).

### 1.38.0 — 2026-11-11
- **Data check** (new §27, owner decision 11 Nov): each app compares the server's `count()` of each listener's own query with the documents it holds for that query (projects, small works, users, operatives, managers, clients, bookings and manager site bookings in the loaded window, annual leave, tasks), re-reads and re-attaches a list that differs, checks again and keeps the last 20 results on the device. Nothing is shown in the launch build (owner change 11 Nov): the admin panel in Settings → Data health, with **Check now**, appears only while `platformConfig/app.dataCheckPanel` is on (`DATA_CHECK_PANEL_SWITCH_FIELD`, `DATA_CHECK_PANEL_ORGANIZATION_IDS_FIELD`, `dataCheckPanelEnabled`, `dataCheckPanelVisible`; missing = off). Read-only: no writes, no rules change. `DATA_PARITY_CHECKS`, `dataParityCheck`, `dataParityCheckDue`, `DATA_PARITY_MIN_INTERVAL_MS`, `dataParityLogEntry`, `dataParityLogAppend`, `DATA_PARITY_LOG_LIMIT`, `dataParitySummary`, `dataParitySumCounts`, `dataParityCheckDefinition`, `DATA_PARITY_SKIP_REASONS` (with `pendingWrites` and `awaitingServerSnapshot`), `DATA_PARITY_TRIGGERS`, `dataParityConfirm`, `DATA_PARITY_CONFIRM_DELAY_MS`.
- **Review fixes (before release):** the device count is the listener's documents by stored id with case kept (iOS counted its filtered store lists and case-folded UUIDs, so placeholder rows, nameless clients and unreadable documents read as differences); lists with unconfirmed local writes or no server answer yet are skipped; a difference is re-read only after a second look finds it again (`dataParityConfirm`); a re-read keeps this device's unconfirmed documents and a plan restart keeps its rows until the server answers (no flicker); the throttle is shared by every browser tab; no console or device-log output in production builds; the panel sits under Data health on both apps (iOS Settings → Data health). The fixture gains operatives, managers and clients (`rawLists`). Re-review: listeners run with metadata changes so a quiet list's server answer is seen (metadata-only snapshots stop at the check's flags); a heal never runs after a company switch; iOS forgets confirmed jobs of a re-attached job list.
- **Two-app comparison in CI** (§27): `lib/canonical/data-parity-fixture.json`, mirrored to iOS (`ProjectPlannerTests/DataParityFixture.json`, `dataParityFixtureSha256` in the manifest), fed through each app's own store and merge code by web `lib/dataParity/dataParityFixture.test.ts` and iOS `DataParityFixtureTests`.
- **Web fix found by the fixture:** a cache snapshot shorter than the list on screen no longer replaces it (it only adds and updates rows, like iOS); only a server snapshot removes a row (`retainLoadedRows` with `authoritative: false`). The listener and store merges are now pure modules (`lib/firebase/orgCollectionMerge.ts`, `lib/stores/jobListMerge.ts`, `lib/stores/bookingListMerge.ts`, `lib/staff/rosterDocuments.ts`).

### 1.37.0 — 2026-11-10
- **Annual leave on project and small works scheduling** (§21, owner decisions 10 Nov): new `jobScheduleLeaveMarks`, `JOB_SCHEDULE_LEAVE_LABEL`, `JOB_SCHEDULE_LEAVE_PENDING_LABEL`, `JOB_SCHEDULE_LEAVE_TITLE`, `JOB_SCHEDULE_LEAVE_PENDING_TITLE` (new `jobScheduleLeave.ts`, on `leaveDayKind`, `leaveHalfDayLabel`, `personIdSet`, `standardDayOn`; the emails of the person's roster rows count, AM + PM on one day is a full day). Only people booked on the job are listed; for them, full-day leave is a brown "AL" cell, a half day "AL AM" / "AL PM", pending leave paler "AL?", rejected never; a booking on a leave day keeps its cell with a thin brown "AL" band across the top.
- **One booking colour scheme** (owner decision 10 Nov): `JOB_SCHEDULE_LEGEND` — Standard, Std + OT, OT only, Ann. leave, Half day — and `scheduleCellKind` / `scheduleCellHours` on both apps (iOS `SchedulingV2CellKindBuilder` now calls the rulebook; a 0h booking is a Standard 0h tile on both — iOS showed an empty dashed chip); web booking cells move from role colours to the iOS scheme (role pills unchanged). Display only: no booking figure, count, warning or write changes.
- Web week grid columns are calendar day keys; bookings and leave are both bucketed by them (a browser east of the organisation zone put bookings a column out).
- iOS now matches leave under every id without case (it matched exact ids) and shows half-day, pending and booked-day leave (it showed approved full days on unbooked days only).
- No rules, index, Storage or Functions change.

### 1.36.0 — 2026-11-10
- Fixes only (owner 7 Nov "do all"); nobody sees or is paid differently except where a figure was wrong (below).
- **Person ids (sweep FU-5, guardrail 1).** Web never picks one `operatives` row on the email to match a person's rate history or bookings: new web composer `personRateHistoryIds` (`personIdSet` + `bookingIdsForAccount`). Edit User's previous rates and its "history loaded and empty?" test (no duplicate seed row when the history sits under a former invite id or a second row), the timesheet invoice rate-change notes (former ids, every row on the email, without case; a person with no email no longer picks up every row with an empty email), and the weekly report and timesheet pay rate (`resolvePayrollRate` / `resolveForTimesheetDay` `operativeIds`: a history row filed only under a second `operatives` row of the person now counts) — the live-rate fallback row is unchanged. The schedule's "is this my own booking" (`shouldScopeScheduleToOperative`) is `bookingIdsForAccount` without case. Web's rate-history merge (`mergedDayRateEntries`) matches ids without case.
- **Organisation week (sweep FU-4, guardrail 2).** Web week grids, week navigation, the leave calendar, the materials and H&S weeks, calendar export and Schedule quick-select use the organisation's Monday–Sunday week (`organizationWeekDayKeys`, `isWeekendDayKey`) and the organisation's today, not the browser's; a stored toolbox-talk week commencing is organisation-zone midnight of its Monday. Same result for a browser in the organisation's zone.
- **My tasks** (web): `isTaskAssignedToUser` is `tasksAssignedToViewer` (1.35.0) — tasks under the person's users id, a second `managers` row or another letter case now show; never fewer than before (test against the old helper).
- **Wording**: §6 says `managerCoverageHours.paidHours` is hours worked, capped at the weekday paid day, not pay and not the Daily Overview figure (field name kept); Daily Overview's hours are the pay rule's (`totalPaidHours`) since 1.20.2 (web comments said `workedHours`).
- **Ghost conversion** (§11): `ghostConversionCandidates` takes `memberIds` and reads a stored organisation reference with `documentOrganizationId`, so a person with a login — a member from another company, or a users document storing `organizations/{id}` — is never converted into a second (ghost) person; the script reads those users documents and writes no write metadata.
- Verified fixed earlier (no change): booking clash letter case (#266 / 1.33.0), `preferFinishedAccount` inactive accounts and `userActiveWrites` / `setUserActive` (1.33.0).
- **Duplicate-name warning** (§11, owner decision 10 Nov; owner-requested addition to this release): `usersWithSameName`, `normalizePersonNamePart`, `sameNamePersonContactLabel`, `DUPLICATE_NAME_NO_EMAIL_LABEL`, `DUPLICATE_NAME_WARNING_TITLE`, `DUPLICATE_NAME_WARNING_MESSAGE`, `DUPLICATE_NAME_CONFIRM_LABEL`, `DUPLICATE_NAME_CANCEL_LABEL` (new `duplicateUserNames.ts`). Add User asks before saving when the first and last name match an existing person; Add anyway saves as before. Warning only.
- **Rate history across duplicate `operatives` rows** (owner decision 10 Nov, option (a)): every record on the person's email counts (§8 `payRateOnDay` / `personIdSet`), as built above; no rule change.
- No rules, index, Storage or Functions change.

### 1.35.0 — 2026-11-10
- **My Profile figures** (§12, owner decisions 7 Nov; new `lib/canonical/myProfileSummary.ts`): `myProfileSummary` — annual leave left (`annualLeaveBalance.remaining`, after pending, Q4), hours booked this Monday–Sunday week (worked hours, no multiplier, Q1/Q2), open tasks with overdue and due this week (undated tasks counted, Q3), qualifications expiring soon (`qualificationExpiryCount`) and every line manager by name (`lineManagerSummary`, Q5).
- New helpers it composes: `organizationWeekDayKeys` (the Monday–Sunday organisation week, `dayKeys.ts`), `bookedWorkedHours` (one person's worked hours on a day, overlaps once, `managerCoverage.ts`), `tasksAssignedToViewer` and `homeBookingDayKey` (factored out of `homeOverviewMetrics`, unchanged behaviour).
- Web: My Profile "Your figures" card; Annual leave and Edit User now count a person's leave under every id they go by, with the region's bank holidays (`leaveRecordsForAccount`), instead of one id and no bank holidays.
- Bank holidays for a leave balance (§12, review fix): `annualLeaveBankHolidayRange` and `myProfileBankHolidayRange` — the previous leave year's start to the current one's end, so carry-over sees last year's bank holidays (Easter Monday 2025 in an April leave year). My Profile, Annual leave and Edit User (web) and My Profile and the leave screens (iOS) load bank holidays for these years and wait for them before showing a figure; web My Profile read the organisation's leave defaults from the organisation document instead of `annualLeaveDefaults`.
- No rules, index, Storage or Functions change.

### 1.34.0 — 2026-11-10
- **Ghost operatives** (§11, owner design 6 Nov "ghost: all recommended", answers rounds 1–2; new `lib/canonical/ghostOperatives.ts`): a bookable operative with no app login — no email, no phone; a `users/{id}` document with `ghost: true`, `passwordSet: false`, `email: ''`, the operative level, self-employed, a day or hourly rate above 0 and a line manager. Added by admins and Operatives managers (`canCreateGhost`, `ghostCreateError`, `ghostCreateWrites`) from a **Ghost account** toggle at the top of Add user / Add new operative (`ghostAddFormRows`, `GHOST_OPERATIVE_EXPLANATION`); the admins are told (`operative_created` / `operative_deleted`). New: `isGhostUser`, `isGhostInvited`, `ghostBadgeLabel` / `GHOST_BADGE_LABEL`, `personDedupeKey`, `canBookLeaveFor`, `ghostLeaveBooking`, `canUpgradeGhost`, `ghostUpgradeRefusal`, `ghostUpgradeWrites` (admin-only Give app access, owner option 1), `inviteAcceptPlaceholderFields` / `INVITE_ACCEPT_DROPPED_PLACEHOLDER_FIELDS`, `ghostConversionCandidates`, `ghostConversionWrites` (owner-run `scripts/convert-no-login-operatives-to-ghosts.mjs`, Q-G2), `ghostsToCheck` (Data health).
- Changed for ghosts: `rosterSegment` / `isIdentifiablePendingLeftover` (never Pending or a leftover), `parseUserDocument` and `USER_DOCUMENT_FIELDS` (`ghost`, `inviteEmailHint`; contract regenerated), `unbookedLabourRows` (a ghost counts as signed up; people with no email are one row each and inactive ones are skipped; an `operatives` row whose id is a person's id is that person, so a converted ghost's leave and exclusion cover its row), `unverifiedOperativeRows`, `timesheetDaysForPerson` / `showsMyTimesheets` / `timesheetParticipant`, `userTimesheetSubjects`, `exportedTimesheetHistorySubjects`, `overtimeUserSubjects`, `notificationRecipientAccountId` / `excludeActor` (never a ghost), `userDeleteWrites` (unchanged plan: the review fix removed the ghost short-cut, so tax ids, membership and a listed `members` entry are always removed), `payrunSnapshotRows` (grey "No app login", `PAYRUN_SNAPSHOT_GHOST_CELL`), `inviteAcceptWrites` (`dropPlaceholderFields`).
- Fixes found on the way: pickers and web's annual-leave people list folded people on the email alone, so only the first person with no email was offered (`personDedupeKey`); web Edit User pooled the rate history of everyone with an empty email.
- **Security rules** (§16): R1 — an Operatives manager never changes a ghost's line manager; R2 — a ghost's line manager gets no overtime booking write on it; review fix — nobody sets `ghost` or `inviteEmailHint` on their own users document (update or self-create). Static compile cost 237,518 → 237,676 (on 1.33.0 with its review fixes) → 237,748 with the review fix; 142,948 → 143,099 → 143,357 bytes. Merged onto 1.33.1 (10 Nov): 236,475 → 236,705; 142,916 → 143,325 bytes.
- Review fixes: `isGhostUser` is the boolean `true` only; `ghostConversionWrites` takes the organisation's `roster` and refuses a line manager `lineManagerCandidates` does not offer.
- Emulator finding (not a rules change): the Firestore emulator (1.22.0) crashes on an Operatives manager's users create that carries `isSuperAdmin: false` or no `email` key, so a ghost is written with `email: ''` and no `isSuperAdmin` key. Web Add user's invite placeholder carries `isSuperAdmin: false` (`invitePlaceholderUserWriteFields`); whether production shares the crash is unknown (follow-up).
- **Ghost account toggle hidden until the iPhone update is out** (owner decision 7 Nov "1b", §11, §18): new `platformConfig/app` switch `ghostAccounts` / `ghostAccountsOrganizationIds` (`ghostAccountsEnabled`, `GHOST_ACCOUNTS_SWITCH_FIELD`, `GHOST_ACCOUNTS_ORGANIZATION_IDS_FIELD`); `ghostToggleVisible({ canCreateGhost, ghostAccountsEnabled })` and `ghostAddFormRows` take it (missing = off), so both apps show the toggle only while it is on. `canCreateGhost` stays the permission. Existing ghosts are unaffected. UI gate only: no `firestore.rules` change.
- Wording fix: the 1.32.4 Deploy notes functions command now names the `inbox-push` codebase (`functions:inbox-push:<name>`); `functions:authClaimsOnUserWrite` gave "No function matches given --only filters".
### 1.33.1 — 2026-11-10
- **Security rules hotfix, no allow/deny change** (§13, §16). A notification create carrying a server-timestamp `createdAt` — the shape both apps write (`notificationCreateFields`, 1.16.0) — crashed the rules engine on the emulator (`IllegalStateException: ternary operator predicate is always boolean`) and errored in production (Rules metrics: 21 errors). A notification **update** written with a merge-set or full set plus a server timestamp hung on the 1.33.0 rules too, not only creates. The client got no answer and retried for ever, so on iOS every later write from that phone stayed "Pending sync". The notifications `allow create, update` that 1.32.2 merged with a `request.method` ternary is split back into `allow create` and `allow update` with the same conditions. An invitation create with a server-timestamp field crashed the same way (no app writes one today: both use a client `Timestamp`): its privilege-type check now runs before the ternary as a plain `&&` term (same decision). `orgBillingOpen` reads `accessClosesAt` directly once its type is checked (same value; pays for the split). New permanent suite `tests/methodTernary.rules.test.ts`: every allow that serves more than one method, plus a sweep of every organisation collection and the people documents, written with server timestamps, must answer; with `FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_LOG` set it also asserts no `IllegalStateException`. Static compile cost 237,518 → 236,475.

### 1.33.0 — 2026-11-06
- **PAYE overtime: Send → Return** (§25, §13, owner decisions 6 Nov). One state machine for ✓ ✕ ⚙ (`overtimeChoiceAfter`, `overtimeChoiceView`); each button's readiness is a rule: **Send for approval** (`overtimeSubmitReady`, `overtimeSubmitLines`; after a Return grey until the person changes a line, and only changed lines are sent), **Return overtime** (`overtimeReturnReady`) and **Generate overtime report** (`overtimeGenerateReady`). Return is the only action that writes the bookings and reviews, in one transaction (`overtimeReturnWrites`, replacing `overtimeApprovalWrites`, `overtimeRequestAnyDecided` and the 1.32 partial approval): ✓ applies the person's line, ⚙ + ✓ the decider's values, ✕ means "I don't agree with this line at all" and leaves the booking as it was; a line changed since it was sent goes back to the person (skip-and-name, `staleBookingIds`). New line states (confirmed, returned approved / changed / declined, reopened, deleted); the person hears "Your line manager {name} has returned your overtime request". A booking changed after Send, Return or a report reopens its line and notifies the person (and their line manager when it had been returned); a saved report is then **Out of date** and Generate turns green again. A line still declined at Generate is in the report at 0h, "Declined by {name}". The person's red X on an all-overtime day now **deletes** the booking (with a tombstone naming the request), never cancels it (`OVERTIME_DELETE_WARNING`).
- **A line manager can always approve** (§16, §25, §26, owner 6 Nov): overtime lines and price work / expense items under every id the person goes by (`overtimePersonIds`, `operatives` rows on their email); no "Needs an admin" (`OVERTIME_NEEDS_ADMIN_LABEL`, `OVERTIME_NEEDS_ADMIN_MESSAGE` removed). Review fix: a Return's booking changes carry `overtimeReturnRequestId` (`OVERTIME_RETURN_REQUEST_ID_FIELD`), and the rules accept the line manager's change or Return delete of a booking filed under the person's former invite id or same-email account through that request (`overtimeReturnFor`, `overtimeReturnPersonOk`); a line manager may add `personUserId` to a claim review stored without one. Claim items carry `personUserId` (`payeClaimPersonFields`); older items get it from a backfill (`payeClaimPersonUserIdBackfill`, Deploy notes). `reviewApprovalMessage` moves to `payeClaimReviews.ts` (claims only).
- **Bookings: no Cancel** (§2, owner 4 Nov): Book / Edit / Delete only; every remove control asks `bookingRemoveAction` (exactly the `canDeleteBooking` people); `booking.cancel` leaves the offline table; stored `Cancelled` bookings stay hidden. **Offline delete on iOS / Android** (`booking.delete` offline, `booking.delete_subcontractor` online only, `offlineDeleteReplayDecision`, `bookingDeleteFingerprint`, `bookingDeleteSignedTimesheetWarning`). Tombstones may carry `overtimeRequestId`.
- **Every manager sees every project, small works job and booking** (§17, §21, owner 6 Nov): `seesEveryJob`, `canSeeJob`, `readsEveryBooking` true for managers outside operative mode; the Projects / Small works toggles now govern adding jobs and changing manager lists only. The managers' per-job hide list is gone; the **operatives' hide list stays** (the job's View page shows only "Hide this job from these operatives"). New **My Projects / My Small works** filter, the default for admins and managers (`myWorks*`, picks in `worksListPicks/{uid}`). A manager's **Daily Overview** shows only themselves and their reports (`dailyOverviewScopeFor`, §6). Any manager may send a materials request for any job (owner 6 Nov).
- **Line manager set once, then locked** (§11, §16): `canChangeLineManager`, `lineManagerChanged`; an Operatives manager sets it only when adding an operative or on their own unaccepted invitation; afterwards only admins.
- **Payrun Snapshot** (§7): the Weekly report's admin-only button and period picker; one row per person with Timesheet, Overtime, Expenses and Price work cells (`payrunSnapshotPeriods`, `payrunSnapshotRows`); an overtime report counts only while its live lines still match it. **Report PDFs** look like the invoice report (`overtimeReportRows`, `payeClaimReportRows`, `payeClaimEvidencePages`, `reportGeneratedText`).
- **Smaller fixes**: materials catalogue admins only (`canManageMaterialCatalogue`); `userActiveWrites` never writes another company's users document and never creates a membership; an inactive account is never picked for an `operatives` row in unbooked labour (`preferFinishedAccount`); pay visibility stays option C1 (tests only).
- **Security rules** (§16): every staff member reads every job and booking; overtime Return keys and review decisions; the line manager writes and (on Return) deletes bookings under the person's own `operatives` row; claim items and reviews with `personUserId`; `worksListPicks`; the line-manager lock; materials catalogue admins only. Compile cost 232,589 (1.32.4: 234,347), on top of 1.32.4's custom-claims role checks.

### 1.32.4 — 2026-11-06
- §16: the security rules read the caller's own role and company facts from their Firebase Auth custom claim `pp` (`authClaimsForPerson`, with `parseAuthClaims`, `authClaimsEqual`, `authClaimsRevokeAccess`, `authClaimsUserInputsChanged`, `authClaimsOrganizationAffectedUids`, `authClaimsTokenIsStale`, `authClaimsCoverOrganization`, `authClaimsMarkerPath`), set by three Cloud Functions on `users/{uid}`, `users/{uid}/orgMemberships/{orgId}` and `organizations/{orgId}` writes and by `scripts/backfill-auth-claims.mjs`; the marker `users/{uid}/private/authClaims` tells apps to refresh their token (web listens; iOS hand-off). Same allow/deny for every tested case once a token carries the claims; a change reaches the rules on the next token (at once for apps that listen, else within an hour; deactivation also revokes refresh tokens). Static compile cost 367,346 → 234,347 (−36%), bytes 142,906 → 137,867, inlined document lookups 2,862 → 1,364. Lookups kept: self-create / bootstrap / invite accept, alias ids and names, billing and deletion, the operatives-manager checks (an emulator rules-engine crash, notes "1.32.4 claims"), other documents. Rules suites 225/225; `RULES_STRICT_BUDGET=1` 20 limit refusals, all among 1.32.3's 43; emulator type check 0 issues.

### 1.32.0 — 2026-11-05
- **Overtime multiplier at create** (owner decisions 4 Nov, decision A; §25): every custom-hours create screen shows the rate pre-filled with the organisation's rate for the day (`overtimeRateCreateControl`); `otMultiplierOverride` is stored only when changed (`overtimeMultiplierCreateFields`), so a booking left at the organisation's rate follows later (and scheduled) rate changes.
- **Weekly report multipliers** (§7): PAYE overtime rows per person, job and multiplier, "2h at ×1.5 = 3h" (`overtimeMultiplierText`), rows changed from the organisation rate marked (`changedFromOrganization`, `OVERTIME_RATE_CHANGED_MARK`). Breaking for readers of `weeklyReportPayeOvertime(...).label` (now carries the multiplier).
- **PAYE payday required** (§4.5): saved by the main Save of Payment runs and timesheets, mandatory at organisation setup (`payePaydaySetupError`, `PAYE_PAYDAY_REQUIRED_MESSAGE`), a banner while missing (`payePaydayMissing`, `payePaydayBanner`, `PAYE_PAYDAY_MISSING_BANNER`).
- **Overtime review** (§25): one Approve button (`OVERTIME_APPROVE_LABEL`, `overtimeRequestAnyDecided`); partial approval — `overtimeApprovalWrites` applies what the decider may and returns `lineOutcomes`, `waitingBookingIds`, `appliedBookingIds`, `complete` and `message` (`reviewApprovalMessage`: "Approved 2 of 5…", "Nothing was approved…"); the request stays `pending` until complete; lines under an older operatives-row record show "Needs an admin" (decision A, `OVERTIME_NEEDS_ADMIN_LABEL`, `overtimeLineOutcomeLabel`). A changed booking no longer refuses the whole approval: that line stays waiting. Breaking: the result shape and the `stale` / `needsAdmin` / `refused` / `undecided` refusals are replaced by `nothingApproved` and per-line outcomes.
- **All records for this person** (§25, §26, platform guardrail 1): `overtimePersonIds({ person, roster, operatives })` for overtime loading, sending and approving and for price work and expenses.
- **Future days within the current payrun** (owner option 2, §25): `overtimeEditableUntil` → `canEditOwnBookingOvertime({ editableUntilKey })`; later payruns stay locked.
- **Own overtime edits are requests with a line manager** (§25): `canEditOwnBookingOvertime({ person, roster })` — the Super Admin directly; anyone with an active line manager as a request; anyone without one directly where the rules allow the write.
- **Price work and expenses review** (§26, `payeClaimReviews.ts`): ✓ ✕ per item, one Approve, `canReviewPayeClaims`, `payeClaimReviewWrites`, `payeClaimReviewState`, `payeClaimFingerprint`, `parsePayeClaimReviewDocument`; new collection `payeClaimReviews` (rules §16).
- **Export notice** (§8): `timesheetExportOwnSheetNotice`, `TIMESHEET_EXPORT_OWN_SHEET_NOTICE`.
- **Rules version in Settings** for everyone (§15): `rulebookVersionLabel`.
- **Booking screens follow `canWriteBooking`** (§2, owner decision 4 Nov): `canBookOtherLocationsFor(viewer, personUserIds)` — Office / WFH / Site survey / custom items only for oneself unless admin.
- **Pay visibility narrowed** (§8, owner decisions 5 Nov): `canViewPersonPay(viewer, subject, roster, { context })` — a line manager who is not an admin or an operatives manager sees a report's pay only with `context: 'timesheetReview'`. Read plans keep loading it for the review; firestore.rules pay reads unchanged (needed by the review).
- **Weekly report admin-only** (owner decision 5 Nov, §7, §11): `canOpenWeeklyReport`; `weeklyReports` joins `ADMIN_ONLY_PERMISSION_FIELDS` and its catalogue row is admin-only, so `permissionToggleRows`, `addUserPermissionDefaults`, `lockedPermissionFields` and `effectivePermissions` drop it for managers and operatives; stored values are never rewritten. iOS: hide the Weekly report for non-admins in Home quick actions, the menu and deep links, and drop the toggle from the Manage Users / Add User rows (they come from `permissionToggleRows`).
- **Small shared helpers:** `legalPackAcceptanceWriteFields` (with write metadata), `invitationListConstraints` (both email forms, as stored and lower case, like iOS `InvitationLookup`), `invitationReadErrorIsSkippable` (a clean-up skips only permission-denied invitation reads).
- **Managers' own site bookings** (owner decision 4 Nov): Scheduling, Daily Overview and My Schedule show a manager site booking's overtime rate with `bookingHoursLabel` like a labour booking (the stored `otMultiplierOverride`, else the organisation's). Web already passes it; iOS must pass the stored rate on all three.
- **Review fixes (r274):** decision A holds for keep lines and no-op cogs (`overtimeApprovalWrites` → `needsAdmin` from the booking's person id, whatever the decision); claim reviews are pinned to their item and person in the rules and never written by the person under any id (§16); the weekly report Pay Summary follows `canViewPersonPay` (`reportPayForViewer`, `PAY_HIDDEN_CELL_LABEL`, `PAY_HIDDEN_NOTE`); the Daily Overview booking sheet keeps the rate on every save and move (`overtimeMultiplierMoveFields`). `OVERTIME_APPROVE_REQUEST_LABEL`, `OVERTIME_REQUEST_STALE_MESSAGE`, `OVERTIME_REQUEST_UNDECIDED_MESSAGE` and `overtimeRequestFullyDecided` are deprecated (kept for older iOS builds).
- **Includes 1.31.2** (rules type-check fixes for console/CLI publish; no behaviour change): no function receives a literal `null` (`rosterRowPrivilegeTyped`, `timesheetSame`, `timesheetSameInstant`, `timesheetOwnerLeaves`); see the 1.31.2 entry below.
- **iOS files to change** (hand-off): `Views/Overtime/OvertimeViews.swift`, `Core/FirebaseBackend+Overtime.swift`, `Core/Rulebook+PayeOvertime.swift` (approval result, `overtimePersonIds`, `editableUntilKey`, `person` / `roster`, one Approve); `Views/Overtime/PayeClaimsViews.swift`, `Core/Rulebook+PayeClaims.swift` (claims review); `Core/Rulebook+PayPrivacy.swift`, `Views/ManageUsersView.swift`, `Views/WeeklyReportView.swift` (`canViewPersonPay` context, `reportPayForViewer`, multiplier column); `Views/BookLabourFlowView.swift`, `Views/DailyOverviewView.swift`, `Views/ProjectDetailView.swift` (scheduling), `Views/BookingHoursEditSheet.swift`, `MyScheduleView.swift` (`canBookOtherLocationsFor`, create-time rate, `overtimeMultiplierMoveFields`, managers' site booking rate); `OrganisationInvoicingSettingsView.swift`, `SettingsView.swift` (PAYE payday in the main Save and banner, "Rules x.y.z"); `Core/CustomerLegalPack.swift` (`legalPackAcceptanceWriteFields`); `ManageUsersView.swift` / `UserStore.swift` invitation lookups (`invitationListConstraints`).


### 1.31.3 — 2026-11-05
- Rules restructured for Google's compile limit; no behaviour change. 1.31.0–1.31.2 could not be published (CLI `firebaserules …:test` 503, console "unknown error"): Google's type checker re-resolves every function body at each call site, and 1.31.2 needed about 15% more of that work than the live 1.30.2. 1.31.3 needs about 17% less than 1.30.2 (static cost 431,665 vs 525,898; in-process compile about 13% faster than 1.30.2 and about 30% faster than 1.31.2): booking create / update / delete are one allow per collection, the manager's job is checked twice instead of three times per booking write, organisation-access helpers read each document path once, and 79 small helpers are written out inside their callers (`docs/firestore-rules-notes.md` "Compile cost"). Rules suites 220/220; no new 1,000-expression refusals (`RULES_STRICT_BUDGET=1`: 47 against 49 on 1.31.2); emulator type check clean. Tools and measurements: `scripts/rules-cost/`, `docs/rules-cost/README.md`.

### 1.31.2 — 2026-11-05
- Rules type-check fixes for console/CLI publish; no behaviour change. The Firebase console and the deploy compile reject 1.31.1's `firestore.rules` with three static type errors ("Invalid type. Received one of [null]") because a literal `null` was passed to a function that uses the parameter as a map (`rosterRowPrivilegeTyped`, `managers` and `operatives` rows) or as a time / number (`sameInstantValue` through `timesheetSameInstant`). The `null` branch now sits at the call site (`resource == null ? … : …`), so no function receives a literal `null`; `timesheetSame` and `timesheetOwnerLeaves` follow the same pattern. Every allow / deny decision is unchanged.

### 1.31.1 — 2026-11-04
- firestore.rules slimmed and restructured so it compiles reliably and every `users/{uid}` and `materials` update is decided by the rules, not by Firestore's 1,000-expression limit. The deploy compile (`firebaserules` `:test`) began returning HTTP 503 as the file grew (180 KB at 1.30.2, 188 KB at 1.31.0); it is now 127 KB (comment blocks moved to `docs/firestore-rules-notes.md`, dead and duplicate functions removed, single-use wrappers inlined).
- **Now take effect as 1.31.0 intended** (§11, owner decision 7): an operatives manager's edits of an operative's users document — names and contact, pay (day / hourly rate, pay basis), active flag, employment type and its dates, line manager, leave fields and the operative toggles — and the legacy by-name material claim (an operative claims a material whose `addedBy` is their email or full name). Before, some of these requests were refused because they ran out of expressions, depending on the documents involved.
- **Refused by the rule logic, not the limit:** an operatives manager changing an operative's role, admin / super admin / manager / Operatives flags, self-manage toggles, `hasNoLineManager`, email, organisation or alias ids (`formerUserIds`, `sameEmailUserIds`); a person changing their own pay, line manager, active flag or privileges; anyone editing another person they do not manage; an owned material reassigned or edited by someone else. 1.31.1 also refuses an operatives manager *adding* `email` or alias ids to an operative that had none (1.31.0's field list only looked at changed keys). It also refuses an admin switching their own Annual Leave Management or Timesheet self-sign off through their own profile (§8, 1.28.0: nobody changes their own; the rules' self-edit path let an admin lower them, and in 1.31.0 such requests were refused only by the expression limit). The Super Admin and the organisation's creator still may.
- **Security (second review):** a privilege flag (`isSuperAdmin`, `adminAccess`, `manager`, `operatives`, `annualLeaveSelfBook`, `timesheetSelfSign`) counts as on when it is on in **either** place — the top level or `permissions.{key}` — in every rule that stops a privilege being gained (`privilegeFlag`, `noPrivilegeFlags`), the way `adminLikeUser` already granted admin from either place. Before 1.31.1 an operatives manager could write `permissions.adminAccess: true` onto an operative whose stored top-level `adminAccess` was false (the operative-edit check read the top level first), and an operatives manager could **create** an operative with `permissions.adminAccess` or `manager` / `operatives` set (the create check read only the top-level `adminAccess` and `isSuperAdmin`) — the second was open on the deployed rules. Both are refused, as is a person setting `permissions.isSuperAdmin` on themself. An admin editing their own profile keeps their alias ids (`formerUserIds`, `sameEmailUserIds`) and email link, as everyone else already did. Not changed: the self-edit path still matches a person by their sign-in email without requiring a verified email (an unverified sign-up on an invitee's email can edit the pending placeholder); requiring it would refuse the same-email copies a web profile save writes for every unverified account (no app flow verifies invitees' emails). **Superseded by F5 below** (owner decision 4 Nov: fixed in this release with invitation proof instead of `email_verified`).
- **Security (third review, §16):** (B1) only the organisation's Super Admin creates a users document with `isSuperAdmin` (top level or `permissions`); another admin's create of one is refused. (B2) an operatives manager acts only on a real operative — `managerMayManageOperative` reads the target (the operative level, no admin / Super Admin / manager / Operatives flag in either place, no admin or manager role) for the users update, delete, membership delete, email-claim delete, `operativeDayRateHistory`, `payProfiles` and `operativeProfiles`, and refuses acting on themself. (S1) re-creating your own profile (self-create, re-join, bootstrap) never sets your own pay (`dayRate`, `hourlyRate`, `payBasis`), employment type and its dates, line manager ids or `hasNoLineManager`, unless `members` lists you as admin; listed as an operative you re-create at the operative level only. (S3) an operatives manager creates only a plain operative (no privilege flag, no alias ids, no `hasNoLineManager`, an email with no live claim in the organisation) and writes an email claim only to an operative they manage, replacing only a claim that is empty, names no users document, or names an operative they manage. (S4) an `operatives` row's account link (`userId`, `linkedUserId`, `uid`) is changed only by an admin, or by an operatives manager to an operative they manage; a `managers` row's link by an admin only.
