Self-delete of your own users document: refused from the fourth review (below); `orgRootCallerIsSuperAdmin` keeps reading the stored flag the way the other grant readers do (reading it in either place would widen a grant).
- **Security (F5, owner decision 4 Nov — fixed now, §11, §16):** accepting an invitation needs proof of the emailed invitation, not only its email. The new `users/{authUid}` that copies a pending placeholder carries the invitation's secret `invitationId` (new field in `USER_DOCUMENT_FIELDS`) and the invitation (for that email and organisation, not used) is marked used in the same batch (`inviteBeingAccepted` via `getAfter`); web `completeInviteSetup` writes both in one batch. The later accept steps — taking the email claim from the placeholder, updating or deleting a pending placeholder (`passwordSet` not true) by email, reading or removing its tax ids, copying its invited pay — need the caller's own account created that way in the placeholder's organisation (it lists the placeholder in `formerUserIds`). The invitee marks an invitation used only with the account or membership written with its id. Before 1.31.1 anyone signed in with an invitee's email (an unverified sign-up was enough) could copy the placeholder, take the claim and act at the invited level. No `email_verified` requirement, so web same-email profile sync is unchanged. iOS has no accept flow of its own (it sends invitees to the web link) and needs no change. Organisation updates by someone with no tie to the organisation, an invitation's own mark-used and own-email claims are now refused by the rule logic, not the expression limit.
- **Security (fourth review, §11, §16):** (B-1) an operatives manager lists, deletes and changes only the invitations they sent (`invitedBy` is them; their queries name it) and creates only an operative invitation in their own name for an email no one else holds (`opsManagerEmailFree`), so they can no longer read an admin invitation's secret id or mint an operative invitation for an admin invitee's email. Accepting an invitation never copies a placeholder above the invitation's level: an operative invitation copies no admin, Super Admin, manager or Operatives flag and no admin / manager role; a manager invitation no admin or Super Admin; nobody accepts a Super Admin (`selfCreateWithinInvitationLevel`). A membership from an invitation takes exactly its role and permissions (unchanged, now judged first).
- **Nobody deletes their own users document** (fourth review NIT, §16): both apps delete accounts through `POST /api/account/delete`, which writes with service credentials; `canDeleteUser` never lets anyone delete themself. The dead rules function `managerOfSameOrg` is gone. An invitation's `invitedBy` names the sender as their users id (web) or their sign-in email (iOS); the rules accept either as the caller.
- **Deploy order (A0):** the web release with this change goes live **before** the rules deploy. An older web build creates the invitee's account without `invitationId` and marks the invitation used on its own, which these rules refuse, so a person accepting an invitation in between is stuck until the new web is live.
- **iOS follow-on (no rule divergence):** (1) `ManageUsersView.swift` (about lines 1160, 4393, 4478) marks old invitations used with a query on `email` alone, which the rules refuse for everyone (no `organizationId`); query `organizationId` + `email`, and as an operatives manager also `invitedBy` (the account's id or email as stored). (2) `UserStore.swift` delete clean-up (about line 1928) queries `organizationId` + `email`: as an operatives manager add `invitedBy`. (3) New invitations may keep `invitedBy` = the sender's email (accepted) or move to the users id like web. (4) iOS has no invite-accept write (`mergePlaceholderUserDocOntoAuthUidIfNeeded` stops with `INVITE_SETUP_INCOMPLETE`); keep it that way.
- **Sixth review (§8, §11, §16):** (F1) accepting an invitation never drops a placeholder's role, operative mode (top level or `permissions`), privilege or self-manage flag or line manager; pay may be left out only while private pay is on (F2). (F2) a person never deletes or nulls their own stored `employmentType` (the rules read a missing one as PAYE, the rulebook as self-employed). (F4) a person's own update keeps `role` exactly. (F11 / F12) a person's own update never deletes or nulls a stored top-level `isSuperAdmin`, `manager`, `operatives`, `annualLeaveSelfBook` or `timesheetSelfSign`, which would unmask a `permissions` copy, and the self path reads `isSuperAdmin` the way the grant readers do. (F9) an operatives manager's update of their own invitation that touches no role, permission, flag or sender is judged on the stored invitation alone (iOS-shaped invitations stayed within the budget). Web `deleteUser` also queries `invitedBy` = the users-document email as typed (iOS shape). Known follow-ups: an admin's update of another users document is not type-checked (budget); `membersValuesTyped` accepts a value already stored on another entry; canonical `permissionLevels` reads lenient booleans.
- **Invitations (fifth review S-D, NIT-1, NIT-2, §11, §16):** `invitedBy` names the sender by users id or by sign-in email in any letter case; an operatives manager deletes only operative invitations they sent; on any update an invitation's `organizationId`, `email` and `role` never change and `isUsed` only goes from false to true (nobody reopens one). No app changes these today (both only mark invitations used).
- **Self-delete closed on the email path too (fifth review S-A, §16):** the users delete by email match now also requires another account (`request.auth.uid != userId`); before, a signed-up person (`passwordSet` true) could still delete their own users document that way.
- **Security (fifth review, ROOT, §16): privilege values are typed.** On the write paths to `users/{uid}` (every create, and an operatives manager's update), `users/{uid}/orgMemberships/*`, `organizations/{orgId}.members`, `invitations`, `operatives` and `managers` (when the row carries a privilege key), a privilege key that is written or changed (`isSuperAdmin`, `adminAccess`, `manager`, `operatives`, `operativeMode`, `annualLeaveSelfBook`, `timesheetSelfSign`, top level and in `permissions`) is a bool or null, and a written `role` (users, memberships, invitations) or `members` value is one the apps write (`admin`, `manager`, `operative`, `member`, plus `basic` / `viewer` from iOS's `UserRole`). An untouched stored legacy value is accepted as it is; readers stay tolerant (`truthyBool`), and the deny-side readers count `1` and `1.0` as on. Before, a double `1.0` passed the deny checks (`in [true, 1]` is type-strict) while `truthyBool(1.0)` granted it (B-A): an operatives manager could make an operative an admin with `permissions.adminAccess: 1.0`. On the person's own users update, `adminAccess` and `isSuperAdmin` (top level and `permissions`) must be false or absent unless they are already an admin / Super Admin (the full scan would put the iOS full-merge self-save over the expression limit). An admin's update of another users document is not type-checked (it already holds every privilege, and the check would put the admin type-change batch over the expression limit); both apps write bools there. (S-B) Accepting an invitation copies the placeholder's privilege flags, role, line manager (`assignedManagerUserId(s)`, `hasNoLineManager`) and self-manage toggles exactly, and its pay (`dayRate`, `hourlyRate`, `payBasis`, `employmentType` and its dates) exactly or not at all (private pay leaves it out) — a person never sets their own pay or line manager (§8).
- **Invite accept writes the membership with the account (fourth review S-1, §11):** web `completeInviteSetup` writes `users/{authUid}/orgMemberships/{orgId}` in the accept batch in the `inviteMembershipWrites` shape. Before, a non-admin invitee's separate membership write was refused after the account was created ("Missing or insufficient permissions"), and a retry then found the invitation used. A later fallback write never stops the setup.
- `&&` does not short-circuit on a false left side that reads document data; expensive branches are gated with a ternary on a cheap condition (`docs/firestore-rules-notes.md` "Expression budget"). Rules tests can assert a logical denial (`tests/rulesAssert.ts`).

### 1.31.0 — 2026-11-04
- **People and access** (§11, access audit, owner decisions 4 Nov; new `peopleAccess.ts`): no rights carry over on a level change — an operative is nobody's line manager (`lineManagerIds`, `rosterMemberIsOperativeLevel`; rules for leave decisions, timesheet review, pay, overtime, price work, expenses and claim evidence). A line manager cannot be made an operative, deactivated or deleted while anyone reports to them (`lineManagerReports`, `lineManagerChangeBlock`, `LINE_MANAGER_REPORTS_BLOCK_MESSAGE`).
- **Warnings scope** (§5, §6, §17): admins see every warning; a manager only themselves, their reports and the jobs they manage, whatever their toggles (`warningScopeFor` rewritten; `isWarningRowInScope`, `managerBookingJobId`). Counts and Daily Overview's unbooked people use the same scope.
- **Job managers**: admin ↔ manager keeps jobs; made an operative, deactivated or deleted removes the person from their jobs (`jobManagerRemovalWrites`, `changeRemovesJobManager`) and tells the admins (`job_manager_removed`); a new job needs a manager (`jobManagerRequiredError`; rules refuse a create without one).
- **Same-email documents**: Change user type writes only changed fields, clears `hasNoLineManager` when leaving admin and updates `members` for a listed same-email document (`accountTypeChangeWrites` `members`); deactivate writes every same-email document (`userActiveWrites`); `rosterListFor` folds before it classifies.
- **One flag reader** (`userPermissionFlag`; rules `staffFlag`): the top-level value when stored, else `permissions.{key}`.
- **Change Super Admin** is one batch (`superAdminTransferWrites`); the outgoing Super Admin without a line manager keeps self-approval (both self-manage toggles written on — owner to confirm).
- A demoted admin keeps Annual Leave Management (`accountTypePermissions`). "No line manager" is refused by the rules on anyone but an admin.
- **Operatives managers** add, edit, deactivate and delete operatives (`userDeleteRefusal` `actorManagesOperatives`, `operatives_only`; rules widened for exactly those documents); they delete only the operative's bookings dated after today, never today's or past ones, which they may only cancel (`operativesManagerMayDeleteBooking`, `bookingsToRemoveOnOperativeDelete` `futureOnly`; rules: the booking `date` after the request time and no users document). Admins get `operative_created` and the new `operative_deleted` (company activity).
- **Exported timesheets** (§8): `exportedTimesheetHistorySubjects` — the Exported list and hub count include the viewer's own exported sheets (read-only) and inactive people in scope; `userTimesheetSubjects` unchanged; `timesheetDocumentSubjectId` keeps sheets stored without `userId`.
- **iOS follow-up**: pass `jobs` (with stored manager fields) and `roster` to `warningScopeFor` (a caller without `jobs` gives a manager no job rows); call `lineManagerChangeBlock` before Change user type to operative, deactivate and delete; write `jobManagerRemovalWrites` + `job_manager_removed`, `operative_created` / `operative_deleted`; use `superAdminTransferWrites`, `userActiveWrites`, the unfolded roster for `accountTypeChangeWrites` (with `members`), `userDeleteWrites` with `actorManagesOperatives`, `jobManagerRequiredError` on job create and `jobTypeStoredFields`. replace `InvoicingView.usersForExportedHistory()` and the hub Exported count with `exportedTimesheetHistorySubjects`; an operatives manager's delete removes only future bookings (`operativesManagerMayDeleteBooking`). iOS currently blocks the Super Admin from editing their own profile — the rules allow it (only their own self-manage toggles are fixed), so remove that block.
- Web: Edit User and Add User saves are one batch; unbooked labour reads same-email ids; job edits (§21): `jobTypeStoredFields` / `jobTypeDisplayName`, manager group not rewritten by unrelated edits, clearing managers removes all three fields, pin → address removes the coordinates, organisation-zone job dates, derived client ids kept; `parseJobDocument` reads legacy `assignedManagerUserIds`.

### 1.30.3 — 2026-11-04
- **Job managers** (owner bug 4 Nov, §11): one picker list for projects and small works, create and edit, on every platform — `jobManagerCandidates(roster)` (active admins and managers, folded per person by the live document, never operatives, inactive, pending or deleted). A job's stored managers read through `jobManagerAssignments(job, roster, catalogue)` (current / former with `JOB_MANAGER_FORMER_NOTES` / unknown); a former manager is shown as such with a remove action and is not named as the job's manager. New module `jobManagers.ts` (`JOB_MANAGER_LEVEL_LABELS`, `JOB_MANAGER_NO_CANDIDATES_MESSAGE`). Root cause: web judged a person on the one users document the email fold kept (admin first), so a stale admin copy — an invite not accepted, an inactive duplicate — left by repeated Change user type saves hid a live manager; web Change user type passed the folded roster to `accountTypeChangeWrites`, so the duplicate never got the new level. iOS (`ProjectManagerPickerSupport`) offered any active catalogue row with an active users document on its email, whatever their level — it should adopt `jobManagerCandidates` / `jobManagerAssignments`.

### 1.30.2 — 2026-11-04
- **Nobody reviews their own timesheet** (audit 4 Nov, §8, §16). `timesheetWriterIsReviewer` is false for the subject under any of their ids (`writerFormerIds`, new `writerAliasIds`, and `personIdSet` over the new `roster` argument) before the admin test, so an admin with Timesheet self-sign off can no longer counter-sign or sign off their own sheet; `userTimesheetSubjects` never lists the viewer (id, former ids, alias ids, roster rows on the same email). The owner path decides self-sign with `timesheetSelfSigned` (`timesheetOwnerWriteFields` with `subjectHasLineManager: !timesheetSelfSigned(...)`, as 1.28.0 intended). Web: the review page offers a counter-signature only to a reviewer (`TimesheetPeriodPage`).
- **Self-manage toggles per organisation** (§8, §9, §16): new `organizationSelfManageFlags({ organizationId, userId, user, membership, creatorUserId })` — the users document of this organisation, else the membership here, else off; the creator both on. Web never carries the home company's toggles into another company while the membership read is outstanding (`applyDeviceOrgMembership`, `sessionUserForOrganizationProbe`).
- **Security rules** (§16): `isTimesheetReviewer` excludes the subject (`timesheetCallerIsSubject`); the subject writes their own sheet only as the owner — the whole sheet when `timesheetSubjectSelfSigns` (older iOS builds save a self-signed admin's own sheet as the reviewer's), otherwise without sign-off, export, review or snapshot fields. New `orgSelfManageFlag` / `membershipSelfManageFlag` / `membershipFlag`: `hbCallerSelfBooks` and `timesheetSubjectSelfSigns` read the toggles for this organisation. The 1.28.0 "Known limit" (admins reviewers of their own sheet) is removed.
- Review fixes (same release): the self-signed owner write needs the sheet's `userId` to be one of the caller's ids and the doc id to name it (`timesheetSelfSignedOwnerWrite`) — the doc id prefix alone never borrows another person's self-signing; the owner never files a sheet under someone else or changes its `userId` (`timesheetOwnerKeepsSubject`), and deletes only an unsigned sheet filed under them (no self-signed exception); the caller's `sameEmailUserIds` count as theirs in the subject test. Web passes the open organisation's creator to `organizationSelfManageFlags` while the membership read is outstanding.
- **iOS follow-on**: pass the roster (and `writerAliasIds`) to `timesheetWriterIsReviewer` and never offer the counter-signature on one's own sheet; `TimesheetApprovalPolicy.requiresLineManagerCounterSign` must not fall back to `user.hasLineManager` when the script does not answer (InvoicingView) — treat the sheet as needing a counter-signature, as having no line manager no longer self-signs anyone; the signed-in user's self-manage toggles in another company come from the membership there (`organizationSelfManageFlags`), never the home users document.

### 1.30.1 — 2026-11-04
- **Price work needs a job number** (owner decision 4 Nov), in both places (§8, §26): PAYE price work (`priceWorkItems`) and timesheet price work entries (`priceWorkEntries`). New shared module `priceWorkJobNumber.ts`: `priceWorkJobNumberError` (trimmed, 1–60, `PRICE_WORK_JOB_NUMBER_REQUIRED_MESSAGE` "Add the job number for this price work.", `PRICE_WORK_JOB_NUMBER_TOO_LONG_MESSAGE`, `PRICE_WORK_JOB_NUMBER_MAX`), `priceWorkJobNumberText` ("—", `PRICE_WORK_JOB_NUMBER_MISSING_TEXT`, for items and entries stored before 1.30.1), `timesheetPriceWorkJobNumberError(previous, next)` (an owner's new or edited entries only; untouched older entries and reviewer decisions never ask).
- PAYE price work: `PriceWorkDraft.jobNumber`, `priceWorkValidationError` (after the name), `priceWorkWriteFields` (writes `jobNumber`), `parsePriceWorkDocument` (`jobNumber`, '' when none), `payeClaimListRow` (dates · job number · approver), `payeClaimReportSnapshot` (keeps price work's `jobNumber`), `payeClaimReportRows` ("—" for none).
- Timesheets: `timesheetInvoiceRows` rows carry `jobNumber`; web shows it on the sheet, the review list, the invoice, the weekly report price work rows and exports, and Users' read-only rows (`timesheetClaimRows`).
- **Security rules** (§16): `priceWorkItems` create requires `jobNumber` (string, 1–60); update: if present 1–60. No timesheet rule — older iOS builds save entries without it.

### 1.30.0 — 2026-11-04
- **PAYE price work and expenses** (new §26, owner decisions 4 Nov): the same three range buttons as Overtime plus Add; Add Price Work (name, description, from–to, a required approver from `priceWorkApproverCandidates` — active admins and managers, never operatives or the person, no free text, not notified — the figure, optional evidence) and Add Expense (the timesheet expense fields, at least one piece of evidence). `priceWorkWriteFields`, `expenseWriteFields`, `priceWorkValidationError`, `expenseValidationError`, readers `parsePriceWorkDocument` / `parseExpenseDocument`, `payeClaimsInRange`, `payeClaimsTotal`, `payeClaimListRow`, evidence `payeClaimEvidencePath` / `parsePayeClaimEvidence` / `payeClaimEvidenceWriteFields` / `payeClaimEvidenceDrawAs`.
- **Reports**: Generate price work / expenses report (green after the first item) saves a frozen copy `payeClaimReports/{id}` (`payeClaimReportSnapshot`, `parsePayeClaimReportDocument`, `payeClaimReportsOfKind`) and builds the PDF from it — a summary page, then one page per piece of evidence (images full size, PDF attachments appended; other files listed with a note) — `payeClaimReportRows`, `payeClaimEvidencePages`.
- **Who sees what**: the person, their line managers and admins, read-only for all but the person (`canViewPayeClaims`, `canWritePayeClaim`); a self-employed person's items under Users' are their timesheet entries, read-only (`timesheetClaimRows`).
- **Personal site audits**: Site audits → Personal → Create Personal Site Audit, free-text job number and name, owner only (`personalSiteAuditWriteFields`, `personalSiteAuditValidationError`, `parsePersonalSiteAuditDocument`, `canViewPersonalSiteAudit`, `personalSiteAuditsFor`, `personalSiteAuditImagePath`, `personalSiteAuditTitle`); attached as evidence as a frozen PDF copy (`personalSiteAuditPdfRows`, `personalSiteAuditEvidence`). Privacy policy sentence `PERSONAL_SITE_AUDIT_PRIVACY_NOTICE` (legal pack version unchanged).
- **Uploaded files** (§24): kinds `paye_claim_evidence` and `personal_site_audit` (`storedFileKind` with the folder's owner); `fileDeletePermission` refuses admins on a personal audit file (`owner_only`).
- Review fixes (same release): evidence readers keep only the person's own claim files (`parsePayeClaimEvidence` with the owner); a refused add deletes the files it just uploaded and removing an item deletes evidence only inside the §24 undo window (`payeClaimEvidenceToDelete`), the privacy sentence says the copy is otherwise kept; approvers must have a users document of this organisation and the admin or manager flag or role, in `priceWorkApproverCandidates` and the rules alike; the rules check the first five evidence paths and refuse SVG; Storage evidence reads need an active line manager or active admin; Firestore `callerLineManagesUser` honours `hasNoLineManager` (also for §25); personal audit edits keep the create shape; report copies hold at most 500 items.
- **Security rules** (§16): the four collections, the catch-all exclusion, and the two Storage paths, with emulator tests (`tests/payeClaims.rules.test.ts`, `tests/storage.rules.test.ts`). `LIVE_COLLECTIONS` and `OPERATIONAL_COLLECTION_PATHS` list the collections; the company export reads the first three and leaves personal audits out; the data clean-up script gains the report-only `paye-claims` check.

### 1.29.0 — 2026-11-04
- **PAYE payday** (§4.5): `invoicing.payePayday` with the append-only `invoicing.payePaydayHistory`; a payrun runs from the day after last month's payday to this payday, 29–31 the last day of shorter months, never moved off weekends; a change applies from the next payrun and past payruns keep their dates. `payePayrunPeriod`, `payePayrunSummary`, `payePastPayruns`, `payePaydayHistory`, `payePayday`, `payePaydayWriteFields`, `payePaydayValidationError`, `PAYE_PAYDAY_NOT_SET_MESSAGE`.
- **PAYE overtime** (new §25): the Overtime screen, lines and states (`payeOvertimeLines`, `overtimeForBooking`, `overtimeBookingFingerprint`, `overtimeLineIsStale`), the cog (`overtimeCogPreview`), the red X (`overtimeStrikeOutcome`, `OVERTIME_CANCEL_WARNING`), booking writes (`overtimeBookingWriteFields`), who changes what (`canEditOwnBookingOvertime`), operative requests and their approval in one transaction (`overtimeRequestLineFields`, `overtimeRequestWriteFields`, `overtimeRequestsToReplace`, `overtimeRequestWithdrawWriteFields`, `overtimeRequestFullyDecided`, `overtimeApprovalWrites`, `canDecideOvertimeRequest`), direct changes (`overtimeDirectWrites`), reviews and reports (`overtimeReviewDocId`, `overtimeReportSnapshot`, `overtimeReportRows`, readers `parseOvertimeRequestDocument` / `parseOvertimeReviewDocument` / `parseOvertimeReportDocument`), the hub (`overtimeHubAccess`, `overtimeUserSubjects`).
- **The booking's overtime rate** is the existing `otMultiplierOverride` (1–3 in 0.25 steps; missing = the organisation's rate, so nothing is re-priced), now also read on manager site bookings (`parseManagerSiteBookingDocument`), with Edit Booking's control `overtimeRateControl` / `overtimeMultiplierWriteFields` (§8, §25). The spec named the field `overtimeMultiplier`; the stored field the apps already wrote and priced is kept so there is one rate per booking and older builds price it the same.
- **Weekly report** (§7): PAYE days leave the normal hours and pay rows; a live PAYE overtime section per person per job (`weeklyReportPayeOvertime`, `weeklyReportPayeDay`).
- **Notifications** (§13): `overtime_request_submitted` and `overtime_request_reviewed` (push), `booking_hours_changed_by_owner` (inbox only); recipients `overtimeRequestRecipients`, `overtimeReviewedRecipients`, `bookingHoursChangedRecipients`; a new **Overtime** toggle (`overtime`, on by default); destinations `overtimeUser` / `overtimeMine`; `relatedId` kinds `overtimeRequest` and `booking`.
- Review fixes (same release): the line-manager booking write no longer trusts an `operatives` row's link (members can change it) — only a booking whose person id is a users id listing the caller as line manager, with text clock times and a known slot; a request can only name and change the requester's own bookings (`overtimeBookingIsTheirs`, re-checked in `overtimeApprovalWrites`, which also leaves out unreadable bookings and refuses lines the decider may not write, `overtimeDeciderMayWriteBooking`, `OVERTIME_NEEDS_ADMIN_MESSAGE`); the weekly report drops PAYE days from the timesheet feed and from office / working-from-home rows too; manager site bookings get Edit Booking's overtime rate (Daily Overview).
- **Security rules** (§16): a person's line manager may change only the overtime fields of their bookings (and cancel them); `overtimeRequests`, `overtimeReviews`, `overtimeReports` rules; the catch-all read never re-opens them. `LIVE_COLLECTIONS` and `OPERATIONAL_COLLECTION_PATHS` list the three collections.

### 1.28.0 — 2026-11-04
- Admin self-manage toggles (§8, §9, §11, owner decisions 4 Nov). New admin-only toggle **Timesheet self-sign** (`timesheetSelfSign`, in `USER_PERMISSION_FIELDS` and the membership keys; "Sign your own timesheet and generate the invoice without a line manager's signature."). With **Annual Leave Management** (`annualLeaveSelfBook`) it makes the two self-manage toggles (`ADMIN_EDITABLE_PERMISSION_FIELDS`), independent of each other and of the line manager. `selfManageToggles`, `isSuperAdminAccount`, `isPayeAccount`, `ADMIN_ONLY_PERMISSION_FIELDS`.
- Self-approval follows the toggles: `timesheetSelfSigned` is Timesheet self-sign on (or the Super Admin), `canSelfBookAnnualLeave` is Annual Leave Management on (or the Super Admin). Having no line manager no longer self-approves an admin (replaces the 1.3.0 rule). The `roster` argument of both is kept and not read.
- When an admin needs a line manager: `lineManagerRequired` is true for an admin unless Annual Leave Management is on and Timesheet self-sign is on (or the admin is PAYE). `lineManagerRequiredError` says which (`ADMIN_LINE_MANAGER_REQUIRED_MESSAGE`, `PAYE_ADMIN_LINE_MANAGER_REQUIRED_MESSAGE`); `usersMissingLineManager` lists such admins (Manage Users, and Settings → Data health on web). `lineManagerOptions` still offers "No line manager" to every admin.
- The Super Admin always reads both toggles on and locked (`effectivePermissions`, `lockedPermissionFields`, `permissionToggleRows` note `SUPER_ADMIN_SELF_MANAGE_MESSAGE`). Timesheet self-sign is never shown and reads off for a manager or operative; a demotion keeps the stored value underneath (`lockedPermissionFields` locks it for those levels, so saves keep it). A PAYE admin's row is greyed out (`TIMESHEET_SELF_SIGN_PAYE_NOTE`); the viewer's own rows are not editable (`permissionToggleRows({ viewerIsSubject })`, `OWN_SELF_MANAGE_MESSAGE`).
- Review fixes (same release): the organisation's **creator is its Super Admin** even without the `isSuperAdmin` flag, and a Super Admin of another company working here as an admin is not — judged per organisation with `isOrganizationSuperAdmin(viewer, org)` and passed as `organizationSuperAdmin` (`isSuperAdminAccount`, `effectivePermissions`, `permissionToggleRows`). Rules: invite accept copies the toggles only as set on the placeholder (`noMorePrivilegeThan`); re-creating one's own profile or the bootstrap path never switches them on except for the creator (`selfCreateSelfManageOff`); the creator's own document is exempt from the self-toggle lock. A save leaving admin never writes Timesheet self-sign (`keepStoredLockedPermissions`), so the stored value stays underneath.
- Security rules (§16): `hbCallerSelfBooks` is the self-book flag or the organisation's Super Admin (no "admin with no current line manager"); the owner writes `weeklyReportOverride` only when `timesheetSubjectSelfSigns` (Super Admin, or an active admin of the organisation with `timesheetSelfSign`); a person never changes their own self-manage toggles (admins included, the Super Admin exempt); `timesheetSelfSign` joins the toggles a non-admin cannot raise. `hasCurrentLineManager` is removed. See Deploy notes for the check before deploying.

### 1.27.0 — 2026-11-04
- Add User: the invitee is never offered as their own line manager (§11, owner decision 1). `inviteDraftLineManagerSubject` / `INVITE_DRAFT_PERSON_ID`: web Add User passes the invite draft (typed email, chosen level) as `person` to `lineManagerCandidates`, `lineManagerSelectionAfterLevelChange` and `lineManagerRequiredError`, as iOS `AddUserView` already does. Before, web passed no person, so a roster row on the invitee's email was offered and counted.
- Material email job checks (§16, owner decision 2): with `jobId`, `material_request` (email function) and the web send route also require that the sender sees the job (`canSeeJob`, §17) and that the job number is the stored one — `materialRequestJobViewer`, `materialRequestJobCheck`, `MATERIAL_REQUEST_JOB_NOT_FOUND_MESSAGE` / `_NOT_VISIBLE_MESSAGE` / `_NUMBER_MISMATCH_MESSAGE`. Web's send sheet now sends `jobId`. Requests without `jobId` are still accepted.
- Admins can still delete any file (§24, owner decision 3): documented, unchanged.
- A deleted company is restorable for 30 days, not 60, the same as file recovery (§20, owner decision): `DELETION_RESTORE_DAYS` 30, the account-delete and Delete organisation copy, and `DELETED_DATA_RETENTION_NOTICE` ("A deleted company can be restored for 30 days, …"). A deletion already scheduled keeps its stored `eraseAfter` (`organizationEraseDue`), so it is not erased sooner.
- Removed variation evidence is recorded (§24, owner decision 4): `variationEvidenceRemovalWriteFields` appends `{ path, name, removedBy, removedAt }` to the variation's `removedEvidence` in the same partial update that unlinks it; `parseVariationDocument` reads it; admins see "Removed evidence (n)" (`canViewRemovedEvidence`, `removedEvidenceHeading`). The cross-platform contract's variation sample now reads `removedEvidence: []`.
- Soft delete retention is 30 days (§24, owner decision 5): `FILE_RECOVERY_RETENTION_DAYS`, `DELETED_DATA_RETENTION_NOTICE` — added word for word to the privacy policy (7. Retention) and the DPA (14. Deletion and Return); Deploy notes 1.25.0 / 1.27.0 set the bucket to 30 days.
- The invited rate is copied on accept while `privatePay` is on (§8, §16, owner decision 6): `invitePayProfileCopyWrite`; rules `inviteeCopiesOwnInvitedPay` let the accepting person create their own `payProfiles/{uid}` once, only as an exact copy of their pending placeholder's pay profile, while the email claim still names it.
- Catalogue operatives' pay for operatives managers while `privatePay` is on (§8, §16, owner decision 7): `catalogueOperativePaySubjects`; `canViewPersonPay` takes `catalogueOperative: true`; `payProfileReadPlan` reads their `payProfiles/{rowId}` and `rateHistoryQueryPlan` adds `operativeIds` (`operativeId ==` + `userId == ''`). Rules: the operatives-manager branch of `canViewPayOfRow` is limited to catalogue rows (`payOperativeRowIsCatalogue`), so a row on an account holder's email is no longer read through the row. Web lays `payProfiles/{rowId}` over each catalogue `operatives` row for those managers (the overlay iOS found missing).

### 1.26.1 — 2026-11-04
- Pay editors start from the pay shown (§8): `payEditorStartingValues(person, profile, { privatePay })`. With `privatePay` on, web's people profile showed pay from `payProfiles` while Edit User started from `users/{uid}`, which can hold an old rate, so an admin could see one rate, edit another and save the old one back, and the rate-history previous row and "rate changed?" test used the wrong value. Now the display and every pay editor (web Edit User, the catalogue operative form and the save baseline read; iOS Manage Users editor seed and save baseline, operative and rate editors) take the same record, which is also the save baseline. With `privatePay` off nothing changes: the person's document as stored, no pay-profile read.

### 1.26.0 — 2026-11-04
- Security switches per organisation (§16, §17, §18): `platformConfig/app` takes `enforcedReadsOrganizationIds` and `privatePayOrganizationIds` beside the platform-wide `enforcedReads` / `privatePay`, so the owner can switch one company on first. The rules read them as `enforcedReadsOn(orgId)` / `privatePayOn(orgId)`; the shared test is `securitySwitchOn` / `securitySwitches` (`SECURITY_SWITCHES`, `SECURITY_SWITCH_ORGANIZATION_FIELDS`).
- The apps follow the switches (§17): job and booking plans take `enforced: securitySwitchOn(...)` instead of waiting for a release that flips `ENFORCED_READS_ACTIVE` (now only the default). Web re-plans its listeners live when the switch changes.
- Private pay in the apps (§8): `sharedDocumentPayWrite` keeps pay off `users` / `operatives` / `managers` writes while `privatePay` is on (before 1.26.0 a web pay save, Add User with a rate and an invite acceptance were refused as a whole once it was on); `rateHistoryQueryPlan` reads rate history per person for non-admins (the whole-collection read was refused); `payProfileReadPlan` + `overlayPayProfile` lay pay profiles over the roster so pay still shows after the strip.
- Owner guide `docs/SECURITY_SWITCHES.md` (order: backfill → rules → iOS build → `minimumIosBuild` → own company → test → everyone; rollback).

### 1.25.0 — 2026-11-04
- Uploaded files are never deleted outright or lost (§24, launch checklist 2.2): `fileDeletePermission`, `storedFileKind`, `storedFileUploader`, `storedFileObjectName`, `FILE_UNDO_WINDOW_MINUTES`, `FILE_KEPT_ON_REMOVE_COPY`. `storage.rules`: any member could delete or overwrite any certificate, variation evidence, site audit photo, task file and health & safety file of the organisation, and any staff member any timesheet export; now a delete is the uploader within 15 minutes of the upload or an admin of that organisation outside operative mode, an overwrite only the uploader within 15 minutes, logos and timesheet exports admin only; profile photos stay the person or an admin. Creating files is unchanged.
- Every upload is a new `{uid}_{unixMs}.{ext}` object (`storedFileObjectName`; web `generatedStorageFileName` and iOS `StorageUploadPolicy.generatedObjectName` already wrote this shape), so replacing a file keeps the old one and its links.
- Web variation evidence removal deletes the Storage object only when `fileDeletePermission` allows it; otherwise it removes the link and keeps the file.
- Recovery is Google Cloud Storage soft delete, set up by the platform owner (Deploy notes 1.25.0).
- iOS hand-off: `Project Planner/Views/Variations/VariationEditorSheet.swift` `removeEvidence` (~1548) calls `fileDeletePermission` before `FirebaseBackend.deleteVariationEvidenceFile` and shows `FILE_KEPT_ON_REMOVE_COPY` when it is not allowed; `StorageUploadPolicy.generatedObjectName` may call `storedFileObjectName`. `uploadHealthSafetyFile`'s clean-up delete (uploader, seconds after upload) keeps working.
- Web docs: the iOS booking recalibrator references (`docs/ios-parity/04-business-logic.md`, `IOS_APP_BLUEPRINT.md`, a note on the 1.19.0 hand-off) now say it is retired.

### 1.24.0 — 2026-11-04
- Line-manager picks on Add User when the account type changes (§11): `lineManagerSelectionAfterLevelChange` keeps the picks that are still `lineManagerCandidates` and drops the rest; "No line manager" stays only for an admin. Before, web kept every pick and iOS cleared them all. Web Add User and Edit User now call `lineManagerCandidates` directly (the web `lineManagerChoices` wrapper is removed).
- Material quote / order email envelope (§16): `materialRequestEmailEnvelope` / `materialRequestEmailSubject` / `MATERIAL_REQUEST_FROM_SUFFIX` — one subject, From name, Reply-To and CC for the web send route and the email function `material_request`. Subjects are unchanged. `material_request` accepts an optional `jobId` and refuses a job outside the caller's organisation; requests without it are still accepted.
- iOS hand-off: `Project Planner/Core/AddUserPermissionSeeding.swift` `AddUserLineManagerSelection.afterAccountTypeSelection` and `Project Planner/Views/AddUserView.swift` (~337–349, the account-type card) call `lineManagerSelectionAfterLevelChange` with the invite draft as `person` instead of clearing the picks; `MaterialRequestEmailClient.send` may send `jobId` (the job's document id) with `material_request`.

### 1.23.1 — 2026-11-03
- Owner decisions on 1.23.0: a person with no level flag is on Managers (1A) and their clashes stay High (2A). Web no longer sets `manager: true` from a `managers` row (3A, `withManagerListHint` removed from the roster store), so web and iOS follow one list rule (§11).
- Every user is created with a level (owner): `userMissingLevel`, `usersMissingLevel`, `USER_MISSING_LEVEL_MESSAGE`; Settings → Data health lists people with none, each linked to Manage Users. Add User / re-invite refuses an invite with no level (§11).
- Weekend named slots (§5, §8): a Saturday or Sunday Full day / AM / PM with no clock times is that day's own window (`weekendFullDayWindow`) and lunch break for clashes, the overlap flag, leave cover and booking conflicts (`bookingStandardDayOn`), never the weekday day. Pay is unchanged (`payrollHoursResult` already used the weekend window).
- One clash rule for every booking flow (§5): Book labour, the Daily Overview booking sheet, Quick Add and booking edit use `bookingRequestClashIds` on the booked day's working hours, as the Schedule wizard does since 1.23.0.
- Setup wizard (§4.3): the working hours are checked with `validatePayrollTimePolicy`, weekend breaks included, before the step and setup finish.
- iOS hand-off: `Core/ScheduleBookingConflictSupport.swift` / `Models/OperativeBookingInterval.swift` (a weekend named slot is `bookingStandardDayOn`'s window — call `bookingRequestClashIds` and pass the policy state); create paths to confirm always write a level — `FirebaseBackend.swift` `signUp` (~700, write ~793), `createOrganization` (~4883), `createUserInvitation` (~7035, placeholder ~7408) and `Core/UserStore.swift` `acceptInvitation` (~1807); refuse an invitation with no level (`userMissingLevel`); `Models/AppModels.swift` `placedByManagersRecord` must not set the manager flag or a list.

### 1.23.0 — 2026-11-03
- People, Home and per-day hours: the last audit gaps (findings 9, 12, 16–22) and the older one-standard-day gaps are shared rules; nothing changes in Firestore, the security rules or Cloud Functions.
- People lists (§11): `rosterListFor(kind, roster, claimedEmails)` / `rosterListKind` — each person is on exactly one list by level (`permissionLevelOf`): Admins (never Managers, admin role included), Operatives (operative mode or role), Managers (manager flag, manager role, or no level flag). A `managers` row never moves a person between lists. Web listed a flagless person on Operatives; iOS listed them nowhere unless a `managers` row placed them.
- High vs Medium clashes (§5): `clashManagerIds(roster)` — active admins and manager-flag people, the rule both apps used, written once.
- Home (new §23): `homeOverviewMetrics` on the organisation day, each person once under all their ids; people on site counted once when they have both a labour and a manager booking; leave by list (§11) and leave days (`leaveRecordDayKeys`). Tasks `isTaskOverdue`, deadlines `deadlineUrgency` / `deadlineDaysRemaining`, due dates `dueDayKey`, all on the organisation day.
- Qualification counts (§5): `qualificationExpiryCount` from `qualificationExpiryRows` (one calendar month, expired included), never a device 30-day window.
- Leave calendar (§9): `leaveDayKind` — leave days only (weekends and given bank holidays inside a multi-day record are not leave days), rejected never, a pending cancellation still approved, fixed order pendingFull > approvedFull > pendingHalf > approvedHalf.
- Site map, job tiles, variations (§21, §22): `siteMapPins` (cancelled never counts; organisation day), `jobTileBadges` (Tentative bookings, open tasks, active users each person once), `mergeVariationSources` (ids without case) and `openVariationCount`.
- Working hours by day (§5, §8): `overlappingBookingIds`, `managerClashGroups`, `operativeClashGroups`, `leaveCoverageRows` and `bookingLeaveConflicts` take the working-hours state and read each day's own standard day (`standardDayOn`); a booking being made is checked with `bookingRequestClashIds` under the booked day's hours (web Schedule wizard; iOS Select Operatives), never today's.
- Setup wizard (§4.3): Saturday and Sunday lunch break Paid / Unpaid, length, start and end, from `weekendBreakFor` on 15-minute steps; the Monday–Friday break length steps by 15 minutes.
- Push tokens (§13): every iOS sign-out, forced ones included, goes through one path (iOS PR #166, `FirebaseBackend.signOutRemovingPushToken`, 5-second bounded wait); a forced sign-out removes the token where the app is still signed in to do so, and a Firebase-initiated sign-out relies on `pushTokenSingleOwner`.
- iOS hand-off — each file calls the functions above through `CanonicalBusinessEngine` and deletes its own copy: `Models/AppModels.swift` (~791-808 `appearsOnOperativesList` / `appearsOnManagersList` / `appearsOnAdminsList` → `rosterListKind`, the lists → `rosterListFor`; `placedByManagersRecord` no longer decides a list); `Core/WarningsComputation.swift` (~501-505 `managerOrAdminUsers` → `clashManagerIds`; ~1444-1454 leave cover and ~1778 the clash payload `day` → the policy state); `Views/HomeView.swift` (~2125-2229 `HomeOverviewMetrics.compute` → `homeOverviewMetrics`; ~1503-1517 the 30-day count → `qualificationExpiryCount` with the operative's ids); `Navigation/MainMenuCatalog.swift` (~524-534 → `qualificationExpiryCount`); `Views/OrgSitesMapView.swift` (~191-208 device day and `status != .cancelled` → `siteMapPins` on `dayKeyInOrganizationZone`); `Views/DLModels.swift` (~274-292 `daysRemaining` / `urgency` → `deadlineDaysRemaining` / `deadlineUrgency`); `Views/TasksDetailView.swift` (~756-759 `isOverdue` → `isTaskOverdue`); `Views/OperativeAnnualLeaveViews.swift` (~1124-1150 `dayKind` → `leaveDayKind`; approved leave with a pending cancellation now shows as approved); `Core/FirebaseBackend+Variations.swift` (~545 `mergeVariationSources`, ~379-380 the open filter → `mergeVariationSources` / `openVariationCount`); `Views/ProjectDetailView.swift` (~420-426 and ~553-575 → `jobTileBadges`); `Core/TimesheetPayrollCollector.swift` (~234-238 `overlappingBookingIds` day → the policy state); `Views/SelectOperativesView.swift` (~41-43 today's policy) and `Core/ScheduleBookingConflictSupport.swift` (~375 `OperativeBookingInterval.bookingsOverlap`) → `bookingRequestClashIds` and `bookingLeaveConflicts` with the policy state.

### 1.22.0 — 2026-11-03
- Money and leave-report gaps from the cross-app audit (findings 6, 7, 8, 15) are shared rules; nothing changes in Firestore, the security rules or Cloud Functions.
- Timesheet totals are `timesheetTotals` (§8): the sum of the invoice rows (`timesheetInvoiceRows`), so every list row, detail screen and sign sheet equals the invoice — declined entries 0, revised entries at the revision, stale reviews pending at their own amount, PAYE days and leave never. `timesheetInvoiceRows` takes `applyLabourReviews` for a reviewer before signing. Web list rows summed raw entries (a declined expense counted); web `payrollTotal` / `expensesTotal` / `priceWorkTotal` / `grandTotal` / `draftAdditionalTotal` are removed.
- Holiday Report is `leaveReportRows` / `leaveReportDayCount` / `leaveReportYears` (§9): organisation leave year, working days only on multi-day records, half day 0.5, ids without case; the total equals `annualLeaveBalance.taken`.
- Weekly Report approved-timesheet feed is `weeklyReportTimesheetFeed` (`mergeTimesheetFeedWeeks`, `timesheetFeedCovers`, `timesheetFeedLabourLines`, `timesheetFeedMoneyLines`), moved unchanged from web (§7).
- Variation numbers are `nextVariationNumber` / `variationNumberTaken` / `variationNumberValue` / `formatVariationNumber` (new §22): trailing digits only ("V2-003" → "V2-004", owner decision); deleted variations count for the next number and the duplicate check, so a number is never reused.
- A person's booked cover on a day is `managerCoverageHours` (§6): `covered` = `standardDayCoverage` (Book labour on both apps), `paidHours` = merged blocks priced once by `payrollHoursResult`.
- iOS hand-off — each file calls the functions above through `CanonicalBusinessEngine` and deletes its own copy: `Views/InvoicingView.swift` (~3045-3048 the "Summary total" row and ~4065-4066 the list summary → `timesheetTotals` with the same `sheet` / `payrollLines` / `extrasMode` / `timesheetDays` it passes to `timesheetInvoiceRows`; pass `bookingPaidHours` / `bookingOvertimeHours` on each booking's standard line); `Views/TimesheetManagerReviewSupport.swift` (~148-170 the hand-written effective amounts → `timesheetTotals`, a reviewer before signing with `applyLabourReviews: true`); `Views/HolidayReportView.swift` (~105-143 `yearBookings` / `totalDaysTaken` / `daysCount` and the calendar-year `availableYears` → `leaveReportRows` and `leaveReportYears` with `annualLeaveDefaults.startMonth`, the region's bank holidays and the person's ids from `personIdSet` plus their operative ids; "N days approved in YYYY" names the leave year); `Core/WeeklyReportTimesheetFeed.swift` (~32-250 → `weeklyReportTimesheetFeed`, `timesheetFeedCovers`); `Models/VariationModels.swift` (~172-187 `VariationNumbering.numericValue` / `nextFree` → `variationNumberValue` / `nextVariationNumber`); `Core/VariationStore.swift` (~100-108 `voNumberIsDuplicate` → `variationNumberTaken`, deleted variations included); `Models/ManagerScheduleInterval.swift` (~78-115 delete `combinedPaidBookedHours`) and `Core/StaffEmailIdentity.swift` (`paidHours` ~156-188 and `BookLabourCandidateSelection.choices` ~369 / ~446 → `managerCoverageHours(...).covered` over the person's labour and manager site bookings that day, instead of `paid + 0.08 >= required`).

### 1.21.1 — 2026-11-03
- Bookable jobs never check the job's dates (owner decision on 1.21.0): `bookableJobs(jobs, viewer, context)` is visible + not inactive + `canWriteBooking`; trailing `dayKeys` / `timeZone` are accepted and ignored. A job past its end date and a job with no end date are bookable on any day. Book labour, My Schedule self-booking, the Daily Overview booking sheet, Quick Add and the Schedule wizard no longer filter or block on the job's dates (§21). iOS: `Views/BookLabourFlowView.swift` and `Views/MyScheduleView.swift` call `bookableJobs` and never filter on the job's dates.

### 1.21.0 — 2026-11-03
- Job lists are one shared rule (new §21, `lib/canonical/worksLists.ts`): list membership `worksListMembership` (every document of the collection, inactive included; collection decides the kind), per-viewer visibility `visibleJobs` / `operativeJobIds` / `worksListPersonIds`, tabs and counts `jobWorkStatus` / `worksTab` / `worksInTab` / `worksTabCounts` (inactive → Completed; All = every row), booking pickers `bookableJobs`, Home `activeJobsCount`. Stores keep every job: web's shared `projectsIncludeInactive` flag (Projects, Clients and Site Audit hid inactive projects, and changed the store for every other screen by load order) and iOS `ProjectStore.smallWorks` (live only) are removed.
- Operatives see jobs through tasks as well as bookings and deadlines on web too (§17 already said so); web read their job documents from bookings only.
- Booking pickers offer only jobs that are visible, not inactive and writable (`bookableJobs`; web Book labour skipped `canWriteBooking`; My Schedule and the Daily Overview sheet offered inactive jobs under Completed / All). Owner decision: the job's start and end dates are never checked — a job past its end date or with no end date stays bookable.
- A job's status day is the organisation zone everywhere (`jobWorkStatus` takes the zone as a required argument; web defaulted to London).
- Web Daily Overview decides small works by collection (`jobKind`), not by `jobType` text.
- `parseJobDocument` reads a numeric `jobNumber` as text.
- iOS hand-off (audit findings 1–5, 10, 13, 14), every file below calls the §21 functions through `CanonicalBusinessEngine` and keeps no list rule of its own: `Core/ProjectStore.swift` (`smallWorks` keeps every document — drop the `isLive` filter at ~984-986; lookups use the full list); `Views/ProjectSmallWorksRevampTokens.swift` (`WorksListStatusCounts` → `worksTabCounts`, inactive under Completed, All = rows); `Views/ProjectsView.swift` and `Views/SmallWorksView.swift` (membership `worksListMembership`, visibility `visibleJobs` instead of their own code and the name fallback, chips `worksInTab`); `Views/TasksDetailView.swift`, `Views/WeeklyReportView.swift`, `Views/ScheduleOperativeView.swift` and the `TimesheetPayrollCollector` call sites (`Views/InvoicingView.swift`, `Core/WeeklyReportTimesheetFeed.swift`) look jobs up in the full list; `Navigation/MainMenuCatalog.swift` ("N open" → `worksTabCounts(...).active` over `visibleJobs`); `Core/WorkAccess.swift` (`visibleWorks` / `operativeVisibleProjectIds` → `visibleJobs` / `operativeJobIds`; home count at ~128-171 → `activeJobsCount`, used by `Views/HomeView.swift`); `Views/BookLabourFlowView.swift` and `Views/MyScheduleView.swift` (job pickers → `bookableJobs`; never filter on the job's dates); `Views/SiteAuditView.swift` (counts `worksTabCounts`); `Core/ProjectStatusRulebook.swift` / `Models/ProjectModels.swift` (status → `jobWorkStatus` with the organisation zone, no `Calendar.current` fallback). Delete dead `Models/ProjectWorksMerge.swift` `dedupeByJobNumber`.

### 1.20.4 — 2026-11-03
- Pay screens show hours at a multiplier as clock hours with the break as its own deduction — one text, `multipliedHoursText` ("6.5h × 2 − 0.5h break = 12.5h"), from `payrollMultipliedHours(result)` — on timesheet lines, invoices, the weekly report (screen, PDF, XLSX, HTML) and Daily Overview; never the back-calculated base ("6.25h × 2"). A signed line keeps its stored text and amount (`parseMultipliedHoursText` reads it back). Pay totals are unchanged (§8).

### 1.20.3 — 2026-11-03
- Time steps (owner, final): day start and finish (weekday and each weekend window) stay on 30 minutes; lunch break start, end and length are on 15 minutes (`PAYROLL_BREAK_TIME_STEP_MINUTES`, `payrollBreakTimeOnStep`, `payrollBreakMinutesOnStep`, `validatePayrollTimePolicy(policy, previous)`: "Lunch break times and length use 15-minute steps (e.g. 10:45)."); e.g. 10:00–10:45, 45 min. A value stored off its step passes until changed. iOS: 30-minute day pickers, 15-minute break pickers and a 15-minute length stepper (§4.3).
- An unpaid break is plain hours, never multiplied (owner, final): every hour at N× pays clock × N − break (Sunday 07:30–14:00 × 2, 30 min unpaid: 12.5h, was 12h); a break in a set window's outside hours comes off after the multiplier (Saturday 07:30–11:00 counts 4h, ×2, booked 07:30–14:00: 9.5h, was 9h); inside the window and Monday–Friday unchanged. `payrollHoursResult`, `weekendBookingBreakdown` ("6.5h × 2 = 13h − 0.5h unpaid break = 12.5h"), `bookingHoursLabel` ("12.5h (6.5h × 2 − 0.5h break)"), new `payrollOutsideClockHours` (§4.3, §8).
- Working hours weekend preview: `weekendDayPreview(draft, weekday)` gives the example hours, a summary with the break minutes taken off and the paid hours, and the rows — from the draft, so Paid / Unpaid, length and times change it at once; `unpaidBreakAppliesOnDay` judges a weekend day on its own break length, not the weekday length (§4.3).

### 1.20.2 — 2026-11-03
- A new booking never replaces or hides another: "already booked" is the same person, day, slot, place and — for custom hours — the same start, finish and break (`bookingRequestIdentity`, `isSameBookingRequest`). Web My Schedule and Book labour compared without the times, so a second weekend booking at the same place was silently dropped after the double-booking row was ticked, and My Schedule showed two such bookings as one. Creates always use a fresh id and a plain create write (§2).
- Booking rows on every list show the times ("Custom 07:30–14:00") and the pay rule's hours for that day with the multiplier sum (`bookingHoursLabel`): Saturday 07:30–14:00 on an 8h window → "8h", Sunday every hour × 2 → "13h (6.5h × 2)". Daily Overview's day total is the paid hours, split into standard hours and hours at the multiplier (§6, §10).
- Working-hours clock times are on 30-minute steps (`PAYROLL_POLICY_TIME_STEP_MINUTES`, `payrollPolicyTimeOnStep`, `validatePayrollTimePolicy(policy, previous)`: "Working hours use 30-minute steps (e.g. 07:30)."); break length stays any minutes 0–120; a time stored off the step passes until changed. iOS: offer 30-minute pickers (§4.3).

### 1.20.1 — 2026-11-03
- Leave cover (owner rule): on a half-leave day the lunch-break window never needs covering, as for unbooked labour — `leaveCoverageRows` takes the day's break window out of the other leave half before it counts what is missing. AM leave on 06:30–14:30 with lunch 10:00–10:45 plus a booking 11:00–14:30 → 10:45–11:00 (0.25h) missing; plus 10:45–14:30 → covered. 1.20.0 required the whole half (0.5h) (§5, §9).
- Web Home dashboard "Hours logged" counts each booking's paid hours by the pay rule under the working hours in effect that day (`payrollHoursResult`, `weekdayPaidHours`), not a fixed 8h / 4h.

### 1.20.0 — 2026-11-03
- The Monday–Friday working day is start, finish and the lunch break only (owner decision): no manual paid-hours figure for Monday–Friday on any platform; the paid day is finish − start − the unpaid break (`derivedWeekdayPaidHours`, `weekdayPaidHours`), shown live as "06:30–14:30 = 8h − 45 min unpaid break = 7h15m" (`weekdayPaidDayPreview`). Every 1.20.0 save writes `paidHoursDerived: true` and `standardPaidHours` = the derived value (`withDerivedWeekdayPaidHours`, inside the apply-now and schedule write fields); a policy without the flag keeps its stored `standardPaidHours`, so nothing saved earlier is re-priced. `validatePayrollTimePolicy` checks the derived day is 1–12 hours. Saturday / Sunday "counts as" hours are unchanged (§4.3, §8).
- Weekday FULL DAY pay, day-rate units, the weekly-report day unit, invoices, Daily Overview, My Schedule and Home read `weekdayPaidHours`. Under a derived policy custom weekday hours are paid on the clock and never snapped to a half day (owner example: FULL DAY 7.25h, 06:30–12:00 4.75h, 11:00–14:30 3.5h, 06:30–16:30 7.25h + 2h OT) (§8).
- Monday–Friday bookings offer Full day or Custom only (`WEEKDAY_TIME_SLOTS` = FULL DAY, CUSTOM; `allowedTimeSlotsOnDay`, `timeSlotAllowedOnDay`); stored AM / PM bookings are never rewritten, shown as stored and paid as before on the `halfDayWindows` split (§4.3, §8).
- Annual leave halves split the working day at its exact middle, ignoring the break (`leaveHalfDayWindows`, `leaveHalfDayLabel`, `leaveComparableInterval`), for every leave display, `bookingLeaveConflicts`, `leaveCoverageRows` and timesheet leave lines (`timeRange`); leave day counting stays 0.5 / 1. In leave cover the lunch-break window never needs covering (AM leave + 11:00–14:30 on 06:30–14:30, lunch 10:00–10:45 → 0.25h missing). This applies to every company, not only policies saved from 1.20.0 (owner accepted): on 07:30–16:00 with lunch 12:00–12:30 the leave halves move from 07:30–12:00 / 12:30–16:00 to 07:30–11:45 / 11:45–16:00, so existing leave-clash and leave-cover rows can change (§5, §9).
- Weekday unbooked labour covers the whole working day; the lunch-break window never needs covering (§5).

### 1.19.0 — 2026-11-03
- Working-hours pay history: `payrollTimePolicyHistory` (append-only `{ effectiveFrom, policy }[]`) and `payrollTimePolicyRevision` on the organisation; `effectivePayrollTimePolicy` / `payrollTimePolicyForDayFromState` / `payrollTimePolicyTimeline` read history first (`payrollTimePolicyHistoryEntries`, `parsePayrollTimePolicyHistory`, `PAYROLL_POLICY_HISTORY_START`), falling back to prior / current / scheduled for a company with none. Apply now, promotion, Schedule and Cancel append to it (seeding it from the flat fields on the first write) and never remove an entry; a third change no longer re-prices days before the second (§1, §4.3).
- Two-admin guard: working-hours saves run in a transaction and are refused when the working hours changed since the screen loaded them (`payrollTimePolicySaveConflict`, `PAYROLL_POLICY_STALE_SAVE_MESSAGE`) (§4.3).
- Every consumer prices a day under the policy in effect on THAT day from the whole state: timesheets and invoices (incl. a change scheduled inside the pay run), weekly report pay rows, feed and day units, unbooked-labour warnings (`unbookedLabourRows` takes `payrollPolicy` as a state and reads each day), booking previews and timelines, Daily Overview and subcontractor edit sheets (prefill `customHoursPrefillForDays`), Home "Up next". Signed timesheets are never re-priced (§4.3, §5, §6, §7, §8).
- Weekend lunch breaks are fully per day: `saturday` / `sunday` `breakPaid` (missing = paid), `breakMinutes`, `breakWindowStart`, `breakWindowEnd`, each missing field the weekday one (`weekendBreakFor`, `payrollBreakOnDay`, `unpaidBreakAppliesOnDay`, `weekendLunchBreakCopy`, `payrollDayWindows`; weekday limits in `validatePayrollTimePolicy`); owner example: Saturday break 45 min 11:30–12:15 → 7.25h; `payrollHoursResult` takes it off by the weekday rule (set window and every-hour modes) and returns `unpaidBreakHours`; `customHoursBreakWarning` and the "No unpaid break" toggle on a weekend day only when its break is unpaid; `weekendBookingBreakdown` rows gain `unpaidBreakHours` and `lines`. Raise `minimumIosBuild` before any company switches a weekend break to Unpaid (§4.3, §8).
- Weekend unbooked labour (owner design): `warningDetection.weekendCoverage` — Saturday / Sunday toggles and "Hours to cover" windows under include-weekends (`parseWeekendCoverage`, `resolveWeekendCoverageDay`, `windowCoverage`; time inside that day's lunch-break window never needs covering); `unbookedLabourRows`, `unbookedLabourRowsOnDay` and `unbookedScanDays` use it instead of the weekend "counts as" hours; Daily Overview follows (§4.6, §5, §6).
- Working hours shows a live calculation under Saturday and Sunday, as Monday–Friday has (`weekendBookingBreakdown`) (§4.3).
- Booking flows warn about a double booking only when the times overlap (My Schedule self-booking fixed on web); a double-booked manager or admin may book anyway with the orange row (§5).

### 1.18.0 — 2026-11-03
- Custom hours follow the selection kind (§4.3, owner bug): `customHoursPrefillForDays(policyOrState, dayKeys, timeZone)` → `{ start, end, kind: 'weekday' | 'weekend' | 'none' } | null`. A multi-day picker that pre-filled weekend times (`weekendCustomHoursDefault`) kept them after the weekend days were unticked and weekdays picked. Now, when the selection changes kind, start and finish reset to that kind's default straight away: weekend → the first selected weekend day with a set window, Saturdays first (Sunday's when Saturday pays every hour at N×), else the editor's usual times; weekday → the standard day (`halfDayWindows`) in effect on the first selected day (`effectivePayrollTimePolicy` when given the policy state). Within the same kind the times are left alone, so a manual edit survives adding a day. iOS: call `customHoursPrefillForDays` (through `CanonicalBusinessEngine`, passing the policy state) in every multi-day booking picker and reset start/finish whenever the returned `kind` changes (ignore `none` and null).
- Per-day hours for weekend bookings (§4.3, owner decision): `weekendBookingBreakdown(policyOrState, dayKeys, start, end, isBreakRemoved, timeZone)` returns one row per selected day (`dayKey`, `weekdayLabel`, `start`, `end`, `workedHours`, `countsAsHours`, `standardHours`, `overtimeHours`, `multiplier`, `paidHoursEquivalent`, `line`), each from `payrollHoursResult` for that day under the rules in effect on it (no second calculator; weekends take no unpaid break off, as pay). Owner's example: Saturday window 07:30–14:00 counts as 8h, Sunday every hour at 2× → "Saturday 07:30–14:00 = 8h (counts as a full day)", "Sunday 07:30–14:00 = 6.5h × 2 = 13h". Saturday and Sunday stay bookable together whatever their rules. Web shows these rows in place of the standard-day summary in the custom-hours picker, Quick Add, booking edit, Daily Overview booking sheet and the Schedule wizard review. iOS: call `weekendBookingBreakdown` (through `CanonicalBusinessEngine`, passing the policy state) for every weekend booking confirm / hours breakdown and render its rows; never compute weekend hours in Swift.
- Clash rows (§5, owner decision, no rule change): every booking flow shows a double booking as an orange row in the same list and style as the purple annual-leave rows (✓ keep, ✕ drop, under `BOOKING_CONFLICTS_HEADING`) — never a separate alert, banner or confirm. iOS: route every booking flow's clash prompt through the same conflict rows.

### 1.17.0 — 2026-11-03
- Weekend booking note for a selection (§4.3, owner decision): `weekendBookingNotes(policy, dayKeys, timeZone)`. Saturday and Sunday selected with the same rule give one "Saturday/Sunday is paid on the hours you enter: …" line; different rules give the Saturday line then the Sunday line; one weekend day gives that day's `weekendBookingNote` (kept). `policy` may be the policy state so each day uses the rules in effect on it. Each booked day keeps its own `weekendCustomHoursDefault`; a single-pair picker opens with the first Saturday's. iOS: call `weekendBookingNotes` (through `CanonicalBusinessEngine`, passing the policy state) in every weekend booking picker and render each line.
- Working hours by day (§4.3): every screen that prices or labels a day uses `effectivePayrollTimePolicy` for that day; booking pickers and Daily Overview no longer use today's rules for another day. Tests lock a change scheduled to start on a Saturday, the October and March clock changes, schedule-then-schedule (replaces, as written), partial writes and stored Timestamp shapes.
- Push tokens (§13): `pushTokenDedupePlan` no longer keeps a token on an invite placeholder users document (UUID-shaped id, `isPushTokenPlaceholderUserId`) — the owner's dry run would have kept tokens on a placeholder and removed them from three real accounts because the placeholder's `updatedAt` was newer. Placeholders always lose tokens; signed-in accounts compare `pushTokenUpdatedAt` (else `updatedAt` only when none has it); a token only on placeholders is removed everywhere. `pushTokenSingleOwner` never treats a placeholder as the owner (`pushTokenOwnerWrites`). iOS must never write `pushTokens` to any users document other than the signed-in Auth uid's.

### 1.16.1 — 2026-11-03
- Health & safety file links (§1 partial writes): a blank `fileURL` / `fileName` never overwrites a stored one (`healthSafetyDocumentWriteFields`, `HEALTH_SAFETY_FILE_FIELDS`); two copies read as one keep the copy with a link (`mergeHealthSafetyRowCopies`). Web PR #227 found the web serializer wrote `fileURL: ''` over stored download URLs (RAMS lost their View link). iOS: `uploadHealthSafetyFile` must not store a `gs://` URL when `downloadURL()` fails — retry, or keep the storage path in a separate field and fail the upload visibly.
- Ships in the same merge as 1.16.0 (neither deployed before).

### 1.16.0 — 2026-11-03
- Notification centre reliability (§13). A push could reach a phone for a row no inbox lists: the token sat on another users document, or the row was addressed to a non-account id. Tokens now belong to one account (`addedPushTokens`, `pushTokenOwnerWrites`, `pushTokenDedupePlan`, `pushTokensExcludingActor`; Cloud Function `pushTokenSingleOwner`, `scripts/dedupe-push-tokens.mjs`). Recipients resolve to the signed-in account, one per person (`notificationRecipientAccountId`; `excludeActor` and `bookingCreatedRecipients` take a `catalogue`; an id the roster cannot resolve is dropped — the parity fixture case changes). Due-now rows carry server time (`notificationCreateFields` `serverTimestamp`, `notificationCreatedAtIsServerTime`); only `SCHEDULED_NOTIFICATION_TYPES` hide until `createdAt`, others allow `INBOX_CREATED_AT_SKEW_MS`. One push payload (`pushPayloadFields`). Rows in another company: switch on tap when a member (`notificationOrganizationSwitch`, interim).
- Annual leave when booking (§5, §9): `bookingLeaveConflicts`, `ANNUAL_LEAVE_BOOKED_LABEL`, `ANNUAL_LEAVE_REQUESTED_LABEL`, `BOOKING_CONFLICTS_HEADING` — booking flows list leave on the chosen days as purple rows beside the orange double bookings; tick or drop each before saving.

### 1.15.0 — 2026-11-03
- Owner decisions round 6. A user type change keeps every saved toggle (§11): `accountTypePermissions` keeps the stored toggles and changes only the level flags; `accountTypeWritePermissions` takes the screen's values only for toggles the new level can change. Admins still read every toggle on and locked except Annual Leave Management; the stored values return on demotion.
- Material quotes and orders (§16): `canSendMaterialOrders` (admins, super admins outside operative mode, managers); `materialOrderRecipientCheck` (saved wholesaler contacts, the sender's copy, at most 3 typed addresses — `MATERIAL_ORDER_TYPED_RECIPIENTS_MAX`). Enforced by the email function `material_request` and the web send route; `materialSendRecords` stays admins and managers in the rules.
- Company deletion (new §20, §16): `organizationDeletionState`, `organizationDeletionWriteFields`, `DELETION_RESTORE_DAYS` (60), `organizationDeletedMessage`, `canRestoreOrganization`, `organizationDeleteConfirmationError`, `organizationEraseDue`, `erasedOrganizationAuditFields`. Rules lock a scheduled organisation (`orgBillingOpen`) and allow only the super admin's restore; a daily function erases organisations past `eraseAfter`, only while `platformConfig/app.organizationErasureEnabled` is true (otherwise a logged dry run).
- Delete my account (§11): `accountDeletionPath` → `delete` | `handover_required` | `delete_with_company`; web `/api/account/delete` replaces 409 `sole_admin` with a super admin handover and company deletion (restorable only by the platform owner).

### 1.14.0 — 2026-11-03
- Line manager options are one per person (§11): `lineManagerCandidates` folds roster rows by `personIdSet` (same id ignoring case, `formerUserIds`, `sameEmailUserIds`, same email ignoring case) and offers the live users document's id; `lineManagerSelectedIds` maps a stored id on the other duplicate onto that option; `lineManagerSummary` counts a person once. Owner bug: after admin → manager → admin → manager, `accountTypeChangeWrites` set every same-email users document to manager and the picker listed the person twice (the web live roster listener also re-added the folded-away document).
- A self-employed person must have a pay rate (§8, §11, §16 note): `payRateRequiredError`, `PAY_RATE_REQUIRED_MESSAGE`, `usersMissingPayRate`; Add User and Edit User refuse the save when the writer may set pay (`canChangePersonPay`). Not enforced by the security rules.
- New-user permission defaults (§11): `addUserPermissionDefaults` starts every toggle the adder can change off; locked toggles keep their value. A user type change never applies them.
- Lapsed subscription on iOS (§19, rule text only): everyone, admins included, sees only `SUBSCRIPTION_LAPSED_MESSAGE`; the admin billing notes are web only and never shown on iPhone (owner decision, App Store guideline 3.1.3).
- Email function (deploy note): the `EMAIL_SERVER_KEY` secret and the `X-Project-Planner-Server-Key` header are trimmed before they are compared, so a secret set from a file with a trailing newline no longer fails every web email with "Server key is not valid".

### 1.13.2 — 2026-11-03
- Brings the deployed 1.12.2 fixes into the 1.13 line (one atomic user type change with the membership, admins read a membership in their organisation, `accountTypeSyncState`, same-email sync within the organisation, My Schedule leave for self-booking screens). No new rule beyond 1.12.2 and 1.13.1; 1.13.0, 1.13.1 and 1.13.2 ship in one merge.

### 1.13.1 — 2026-11-03
- Owner decision revises Q2 before release (1.13.0 was never deployed; both ship in one merge). Weekends (Q2, §4.3, §5, §6, §8): Saturday and Sunday bookings are custom hours only on every platform (`allowedTimeSlotsOnDay`, `WEEKEND_TIME_SLOTS` = CUSTOM, `timeSlotAllowedOnDay`); the editor prefills a set-window day's window (`weekendCustomHoursDefault`); the note is `weekendBookingNote`; one request never mixes weekdays and weekend days (`bookingDaysMixWeekdayAndWeekend`, `WEEKEND_WEEKDAY_MIX_MESSAGE`, the iOS wording). Stored weekend FULL DAY / AM / PM rows are never rewritten; a stored set-window FULL DAY pays the window at its "counts as" hours (`weekendFullDayWindow`), an all-hours FULL DAY the pre-1.13.0 calculation. `weekendFullDayNote` is removed (replaced by `weekendBookingNote`); `WEEKEND_TIME_SLOTS` is CUSTOM only.

### 1.13.0 — 2026-11-03
- Owner decisions round 4. Pay visibility (Q1, §8, §16): `canViewPersonPay` — the person, their active line managers, admins and super admins, managers with the Operatives toggle for operatives. Pay moves to the private `organizations/{orgId}/payProfiles/{subjectId}` (`PAY_PROFILE_FIELDS`, `payProfilePath`, `parsePayProfileDocument`, `overlayPayProfile`, `payProfileWriteFieldsFromProfileWrite`, `payProfileBackfillWrites`, `scripts/backfill-pay-profiles.mjs`); rules `canViewPayOf`; behind `platformConfig/app.privatePay` (`privatePayOn`) rate history, `operativeProfiles` and timesheets are limited to pay viewers and no pay value goes back on `users` / `operatives` / `managers` (`payStaysPrivate`). The catch-all read no longer re-opens `payProfiles`, `operativeDayRateHistory`, `operativeProfiles` or `settings`.
- Weekends (Q2, §4.3): no AM / PM on Saturday or Sunday (`allowedTimeSlotsOnDay`, `timeSlotAllowedOnDay`); a weekend FULL DAY is that day's set window at its "counts as" hours (`weekendFullDayWindow`; it was the weekday standard day plus weekend overtime); the picker note is `weekendFullDayNote`. Existing weekend AM / PM keep their pay. (Superseded by 1.13.1 before release.)
- Lapsed subscription (Q3, new §19, §16): both apps blocked with `SUBSCRIPTION_LAPSED_MESSAGE` (`subscriptionAccess`); admins reach billing only; nothing deleted; the webhook writes `billing.accessOpen` / `accessClosesAt` (`billingAccessFields`) and the rules close organisation data (`orgBillingOpen`).
- Carry-over (Q4, §4.4): pro rata in the year joined from `users/{uid}.employmentStartDate` (`proRataLeaveAllowance`; `annualLeaveBalance` `employmentStartDayKey`, `yearEntitlement`, `proRata`); optional cap `annualLeaveDefaults.maxCarryOverDays` (`parseMaxCarryOverDays`, null = no cap, suggested 5). `leaveYearStartMonth`.
- Overnight bookings (Q5, §5, §8): `slotInterval` runs past 24:00; clashes and timesheet overlaps carry an overnight booking into the next morning (`overnightCarryInterval`, `intervalCrossesMidnight`, `nextCalendarDayKey`); paid at the start day's rules (it was plain hours at 1×).
- Countries (Q6, §4.1): pickers offer `LAUNCH_COUNTRY_CODES` (GB, IE) via `launchCountryOptions`; a stored other country is kept and shown.
- Lunch warning (§8): `customHoursBreakWarning` / `CUSTOM_HOURS_BREAK_WARNING` confirm step before saving custom hours that cover the unpaid break with Unpaid break on. Pay unchanged.
- Self email change (§14, §16): `selfEmailChangeAction`, `selfEmailChangeSentMessage`, `signInEmailSyncWrites`; the rules let a person delete their own old email claim once their verified token email is another.

### 1.12.2 — 2026-11-03
- Change user type is one atomic batch (§11): users documents, `members.{uid}` and the membership together. Before 1.12.2 web wrote the membership after the batch (a refusal was swallowed), and iOS read the membership first, which only the person could read, so an admin → manager change stopped after `users/{uid}` (iOS: "the organisation membership could not be updated"). Rules: an admin of the organisation reads a membership there (get only; list stays the person's). Super Admin and founder protections (1.12.0 B4) unchanged.
- Web profile save (§11): the same-email name sync queries this organisation's users only (`organizationId` + `email`); the email-only query was refused by the rules, so every admin save of someone else's profile — including a user type change — ended in "Missing or insufficient permissions" after the profile had been written. The sync is best effort.
- Half-applied user type (§11): `accountTypeSyncState`, `ACCOUNT_TYPE_OUT_OF_SYNC_MESSAGE` — Manage Users lists them, Edit User repairs on Save. One save at a time; a saved profile returns to the people list.
- My Schedule (§10): web's self-booking My Schedule (admins, timesheet managers) shows the person's own annual leave, matched by `leaveRecordsForAccount` with the roster's same-email ids.

### 1.12.1 — 2026-11-03
- Read scoping switched by data (§17, §18): the rules apply the 1.10.0 / 1.11.0 job and booking read scoping only when `platformConfig/app.enforcedReads` is true; until then every member reads them as before 1.10.0. The rules and functions deploy right after this merge; the backfills, the `ENFORCED_READS_ACTIVE` release and then the `enforcedReads` flip come later (Deploy notes, `docs/DEPLOY_1.11.0.md`).
- 1.12.0 and 1.12.1 ship together in one merge (neither deployed before).

### 1.12.0 — 2026-11-03
- Security rules hardening (§14, §16; audit B1–B4, H2–H5, M3 part). Platform owner is the verified token email only (`isPlatformOwner`, `isVerifiedPlatformOwner`; the users document `email` no longer counts, and a person changes their own `email` only to their Auth email). Managers with operatives access invite operatives only (`invitationIsOperativeOnly`). `formerUserIds` / `sameEmailUserIds` are never self-written (self-create only from the invite placeholder) and count only on the caller's own company's users document. `isSuperAdmin` and ownership change only by the Super Admin; an admin cannot demote the founder. Deactivated people lose organisation access (`organizationMemberActive`). Roster row emails change only by admins (operatives rows: operatives managers too). Inbox rows only for members, and the push function pushes only to active members. `accountConfirmations` are not listed and are marked used only by their signed-in owner. The `qualifications` and `skills` catalogues are staff only. Deployed right after the merge (Deploy notes).
- Forced app update (§18): `platformConfig/app` `{ minimumIosBuild, minimumWebVersion? }`, `appUpdateRequired`, `PLATFORM_CONFIG_APP_PATH`; readable signed out, written by the platform owner.
- Day keys east of UTC+12 (§1): `dateFromDayKeyInZone`, `midnightInZone` and `addDaysInZone` work from the zone offset at local midnight. New Zealand (and Chatham, Kiritimati) day keys were one day out: a Friday paid at Saturday rules, a Mon–Fri leave request counted 2 days, rate changes and payment runs started a day late.
- The unpaid break (§8): only the covered part of the break window comes off, up to the break length; a booking entirely inside the break pays 0, never a full standard day (`payrollHoursResult`).
- Policy limits on read (§4.3): `parsePayrollPolicy` clamps to `PAYROLL_POLICY_LIMITS` (now defined beside it); `payForHours` pays 0 for a day rate with a paid day below 1 hour (`PAY_STANDARD_DAY_MIN_HOURS`), never up to 800× the rate.
- Leave year (§4.4): always 12 months (`leaveYearEndMonth`); a mismatched save is refused (`validateAnnualLeaveDefaults`, `validateLeaveYearMonths`); a stored mismatch reads as 12 months from the start (`leaveYearBounds`).
- Not changed (owner decisions pending): who reads pay, billing enforcement, weekend AM/PM, carry-over cap, overnight bookings.

### 1.11.0 — 2026-11-03
- Owner decisions round 3 — PAYE (§8): a PAYE day never produces a timesheet line; My Timesheets, reminders, the owner sheet, the invoice and User Timesheets rows exist for a period only when the person is self-employed on at least one day of it, and only those days count (`timesheetDaysForPerson`, `showsMyTimesheets`; `timesheetParticipant` takes a day or a period and no longer counts PAYE; `timesheetInvoiceRows` `timesheetDays`). PAYE reviewers keep User Timesheets.
- Invites (§11, §14, §16): accepting applies exactly what the invitation stores — role, permissions, pay and line manager (`invitationRole`, `invitationProfileFields`, `inviteMembershipWrites`; `invitationCreateFields` stores `role`). The rules let the person accepting a genuine, unused invitation to their email create that membership and their `members.{uid}` with exactly those values, in the batch that marks it used.
- Permission toggles by level (§8, §11): `effectivePermissions`, `lockedPermissionFields`, `permissionLevelOf`. Admins: every toggle on and locked except Annual Leave Management; no operative mode (a stray flag is ignored by the approval rules and the security rules too). Managers: Materials and Site audit on and locked; Wholesalers editable. Saves never write a locked toggle (`keepStoredLockedPermissions` in `userProfileWriteFields`); no document is rewritten. One row set for Add User, its summary and Edit User (`permissionToggleRows`); `canManageOperatives` / `canScheduleOperatives`.
- Change user type writes every document that decides it (`accountTypeChangeWrites`, `accountTypePermissions`): users documents of this organisation, `members.{uid}`, the membership (a profile of another company: the members entry and membership only); refused for a super admin who would stop being an admin (§11).
- Roster (§1, §11, §16): `rosterMembersForOrganization` / `organizationLevelFor` — the members keys and the creator are always on the roster, with their level in this organisation (never another company's flags); a denied read never removes anyone. Rules: a co-member reads a users document anchored on their company (`members` key or creator) even when it names another company (`anchoredInCallerCompany`).
- Line managers (§11): `lineManagerCandidates`, `lineManagerOptions` ("No line manager" only for admins), `lineManagerSummary` (`{ label, ids: [{ id, name }] }`, "Unknown person" for an unknown id, "+N more" only for N ≥ 1). Pay copy is the rulebook's (`PAY_BASIS_SAVE_NOTE`; one basis, saving one removes the other).
- Job kind (§16, §17): the collection decides (`jobKind`); bookings carry `jobCollection` (`bookingJobCollectionFields`, `jobCollectionForJobId`, `normalizeBookingJobCollection`; written by every booking writer, returned by the readers). Rules: a toggle manager reads that kind's bookings by `jobCollection`; a booking without it only admins, its job's managers and its people; `jobCollection` must match the job's collection on a manager's write. `bookingQueryPlan` adds `jobCollection` queries; `readsEveryBooking` (enforced) is admins only. Still behind `ENFORCED_READS_ACTIVE` (false). `scripts/backfill-booking-job-collection.mjs`; composite indexes `jobCollection` + `date`.
- Cross-platform contract v2: iOS samples built by rulebook writers carry `builder` (writer + exact input → exact `iosWritten`); the iOS membership sample is the nested shape (the flat one is an `ios-legacy` read-only case); full iOS job samples (`managerUserIds`, `addressLine2`, `siteAddress`, `manager`, `description`, `hidden*UserIds`, `usesMapPinForLocation`); tasks `createdByUserId`; bookings `jobCollection`.

### 1.10.1 — 2026-11-03
- Enforced reads (§17) are off until the backfill has run: `ENFORCED_READS_ACTIVE` = false.
